### Infrastructure / Ops
- [ ] **VPS root filesystem is at 84% (7.7G free of 49G)** and every deploy builds two images on it. Noticed 2026-08-03 while releasing #440/#441. Nothing has failed yet, but a Docker build that runs out of space fails in confusing, half-finished ways rather than with a clear error. `docker image prune -af --filter until=168h` plus a look at what else is on the volume is the cheap first move; folding a prune into `scripts/deploy.sh` is the durable one. **Re-measured 2026-08-15 before the #499 deploy: 75% (12G free of 49G)** — better than 2026-08-03, so something was reclaimed in between, but `docker system df` shows **14.34GB of build cache, 12.03GB of it reclaimable**, which is most of the headroom. Still unfixed and still the same failure mode; a prune was deliberately skipped that day to keep the build cache warm.
- [ ] **Voucher and project attachments are undeletable and leak Cloudinary storage forever** — `AssetsService.uploadFile()` returns only `secure_url`, and a URL cannot be turned back into a `public_id`, so `deleteFile()` can never be called for anything uploaded through it. `VoucherAttachment` and `ProjectAttachment` both store `file_url` with no `public_id` column, and both cascade-delete their rows with the tenant/voucher/project — the rows vanish, the files stay, billed indefinitely. Found while adding `crm_contact_attachments` (2026-08-02), which avoids the trap by storing `storage_key` and going through the new `uploadBuffer()`. Fix is the same shape for both: add a `storage_key`, switch their upload paths to `uploadBuffer()`, and purge assets before the cascade. Existing rows cannot be recovered this way — their `public_id`s are only derivable by listing the Cloudinary folder and matching URLs, so a one-off reconciliation script is needed for anything already uploaded.
- [ ] **The four `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites fail even against a live, seeded Postgres** — long assumed to be "needs a database", which is only half the story. Reproduced 2026-07-30 on a local PG16: build the schema, run `prisma/seed.ts` (succeeds, exit 0), and `PostingRuleEventType` holds all 17 values; run `jest test/integration.spec.ts` against it and the type is left with only **10** — `supplier_payment`, `depreciation`, `cash_transaction`, `salary_accrual`, `salary_payment`, `asset_acquisition` and `fund_transfer` are gone. Every subsequent signup then 500s on `db.postingRule.findFirst()` with `22P02 invalid input value for enum`, which is why 69 of 79 cases fail. Something in the test run drops and recreates the type from a stale definition; nothing in `seed.ts`, `bootstrap-accounting.ts` or the backend source issues `DROP TYPE`/`db push`, so the culprit is still unidentified — `integration.spec.ts` does `dotenv.config()` against the repo-root `.env`, which is the first thing to rule out. Until this is found, the suites cannot pass in CI no matter how the database is provisioned, so fixing the `db:seed` step above is necessary but not sufficient.
- [ ] **An unidentified backend suite fails intermittently** — running `npm test --workspace=apps/backend` three times in a row on `957af54b` (2026-08-15, pre-#499 release check) gave **5 failed suites / 70 failed tests** on the first run and exactly the documented baseline of **4 / 69** on both subsequent runs, with no code change in between. The 4 are the known `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites; the intermittent 5th was not captured, because the first run's output was tailed rather than saved and the re-runs no longer reproduced it. That makes the suite's pass/fail signal unreliable as a release gate — "4 failed" is the baseline you compare against, and a genuine new breakage that happened to be the 5th suite would look identical to this. Next time it appears, capture the full run to a file (`npm test --workspace=apps/backend > /tmp/t.log 2>&1`) before re-running, so the suite gets a name. Suspect anything time- or date-sensitive given the CRM crons and `startOfToday()` logic that just landed
- [ ] **`packages/shared-types/*.test.ts` is run by no jest project** — the backend config matches `*.spec.ts` under `apps/backend`, the frontend matches `<rootDir>/src/**`, and the package itself has no `test` script, so `phone.test.ts` (and anything added beside it) has never run in CI. Found 2026-08-08 while adding `countryCodeFromE164` tests there, which had to be run through a hand-written jest config to confirm they pass. The cheap fix is a `test` script on the package with its own small config, since the root `npm test` already fans out with `--workspaces --if-present`.
- [ ] Fix the E2E job's `db:seed` step — `.github/workflows/deploy.yaml` runs `npm run db:seed` without ever building `@erp71/shared-types`, which `prisma/seed.ts` imports. `packages/shared-types/dist/` is gitignored and the package has no `prepare` script, so on a fresh CI checkout the import resolves to a non-existent `dist/index.js` and the seed dies with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Fix is a `npm run build --workspace=@erp71/shared-types` step before the seed. Pre-existing and unrelated to the seed split (the failing import is unchanged at `HEAD~1`); went unnoticed because the job is gated on the `run-e2e` label and is effectively never triggered — surfaced 2026-07-23 by adding the label to PR #394. Worth also reconsidering the label gate, since a job nobody runs rots silently.
- [ ] Move production schema changes from `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` to `prisma migrate deploy` — `packages/database/prisma/migrations/` holds 20+ real migrations that production never runs. The backend `CMD` (`apps/backend/Dockerfile`) and `scripts/deploy-erp71.sh` both reconcile the live schema with `db push --accept-data-loss`, which **drops columns and tables without asking** to reach the target schema. Benign so far only because the schema has only grown; the first rename or removal silently discards production data. Do this as deliberate, separate work — after months of `db push` the live schema has likely drifted from the migration history, so `migrate deploy` will fail until that's reconciled. Start with a drift check (`prisma migrate diff` between the live DB and the migration history), then backfill a baseline migration if needed. (Local dev DB is already visibly drifted — `Tenant.onboarding_dismissed_at` missing — found while fixing the seed bug.)

## HIGH PRIORITY — Ship within first 2 weeks of launch

- [ ] **`useServerList` swallows a failed load into an empty table — every list in the app looks "empty" when it is actually broken.** The hook catches, sets `items: []` and `total: 0`, and exposes an `error` that **no page in the codebase reads** (`grep` for a destructured `error` across every `useServerList` call site returns nothing). A 403, a 500 and a genuinely empty result are indistinguishable on screen. This is what hid the Project Management module's missing permission grants, and before that made an api-client shape bug look like "no projects exist" — twice in one day, from the same blind spot. Fix at the hook so it covers every list at once: surface the failure in the table body (an inline retry beats a toast for a table that owns its own region) and keep `emptyMessage` for the genuinely-empty case. Care needed on rollout — ~40 pages consume it, some of which already toast their own errors, so an unconditional toast in the hook would double up.
- [ ] **Fold `APPROVE_VOUCHER` into `sync-role-permissions.ts` and retire its one-off script.** `prisma/sync-approve-voucher-permission.ts` (called from `sync-accounting.ts`) predates the general reconciler and was left alone deliberately: it grants unconditionally where the general one skips a role that already holds part of its group, so folding it in would stop re-granting on tenants where an owner has since removed it — a behavior change on already-deployed code, not a refactor. Do it as its own change: add an `approve-voucher` group to `PERMISSION_BACKFILL_GROUPS`, delete the script and its `syncPermissions()` call in `sync-accounting.ts`, and confirm against a real Postgres that a tenant which already has the grant stays a no-op. Leaving both in place means the next new permission has two plausible homes.
- [ ] **Account codes phase B — tighten to NOT NULL + UNIQUE.** Phase A shipped the columns as loose as `prisma db push` would accept, because production reconciles its schema that way and never runs migrations: `account_groups.code` and `account_subgroups.code` are `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, and `accounts.code` is still **nullable** (db push refuses to make an *existing* column required whether or not it has a default, and that column had NULL rows). The unique indexes are absent for the same reason — `db push` runs *before* the backfill in the boot chain, so at that moment every new row still holds `''` and creating a unique index would fail and take the site down. So today the prefix/uniqueness invariants are enforced by the service and the backfill, **not by the database**. Once every environment is confirmed backfilled (`SELECT count(*) FROM accounts WHERE code IS NULL OR code = ''` returns 0, same for the two group tables), add: `@@unique([tenant_id, code])` on all three, `accounts.code` to `String @db.VarChar(6)`, and drop the `@default("")`. That change is *not* db-push-safe either — it will need the same staging, or for the `migrate deploy` switch below to land first.
- [ ] **`sync:accounting` reports "nothing to do" while it rewrites codes** — found during the 2026-08-01 production deploy. Its per-tenant delta is built by `snapshot()` from account **names** and posting-rule keys only, so a run that re-coded all 503 accounts across 21 tenants printed `All 21 tenant(s) already up to date. Nothing to do.` The write itself was correct (verified against the database), but the message actively misleads the operator reading the deploy log — the one place a code backfill is visible. Add code changes to `Snapshot`/`Delta`, or have `backfillAccountCodesForTenant`'s `AccountCodeBackfillResult` (it already returns `{groups, subgroups, accounts}` counts, currently discarded by `applySync`) surface in the report.
- [ ] **Drop `legacy_code` a release after phase B** — `accounts.legacy_code` holds each account's old flat number (1010, 5020 …) so tenants can still find an account by the code on their existing paperwork; it is searchable and shown as muted `was 1010` subtext on the CoA list. It is transitional by design.
- [ ] **`html` and `body` render in Times on every page — Inter only ever arrives via an explicit `font-sans` class, and 18 files remember to add it.** Found while fixing the blog typography on 2026-08-10. `app/layout.tsx` puts `inter.variable`/`notoSansBengali.variable` on `<body>`, but Tailwind 3.4's preflight emits `html { font-family: var(--font-inter), var(--font-bengali), … }` — and on `html` those custom properties are **not in scope**, so the declaration is invalid at computed-value time and `html` falls back to the UA serif, which `body` then inherits. Measured in a headless browser against a production build: `getComputedStyle(document.body).fontFamily` is `Times` on `/`, `/blog`, `/pricing`, `/demo` **and** `/store/demo-shop`. Pages look right only because their root `<div>` carries `font-sans`, which resolves the variable correctly one level down. **Fix is one line — move the two `variable` classes from `<body>` to `<html>`** — but it is not in the blog's scope because it changes every page that currently *lacks* `font-sans` from serif to Inter, and that set includes the tenant storefront (`/store/[slug]`, its shop and product pages), `/demo`, `/status` and the short-link routes. Those are customer-visible surfaces, so the change wants its own before/after pass rather than riding along with a typography fix. Once it lands, the per-page `font-sans` classes become redundant and can be swept.
- [ ] **Five pre-existing typecheck errors block a clean `tsc --noEmit` across the repo** — 4 in backend (`accounting.service.spec.ts` ×2, `billing-scheduler.service.spec.ts` ×1, `short-links/is-safe-target.spec.ts` ×1) and 1 in frontend (`contact/page.test.tsx` missing `@testing-library/user-event` declaration). Unrelated to blog/ai modules; worth consolidating into a single fix pass.
- [ ] **`TaskDetailPanel.test.tsx` "renders what is there as markdown rather than as source" is flaky in CI** — failed once on PR #494 (`await screen.findByText('Isolate')` timed out at line 630) and passed on a re-run of the *same* commit, 1 failure out of 2505. Ran 6× locally against the same tree, green every time, and the file is untouched by that branch — so it is a timing flake, not a regression: the assertion waits on `getProjectTask` resolving and the markdown renderer mounting, which is tight under a loaded runner. Worth raising that one `findByText`'s timeout or awaiting the panel's loaded state before asserting, since a flake here fails the whole frontend job and blocks unrelated PRs. Surfaced 2026-08-13.
- [ ] **AI usage logging on platform blogs — tenant side works, platform side does not** — `tenant-blog.service.ts`'s `draftWithAi` calls `ai.logUsage()` after `completeUnbilled()`, so a shop's draft is billed against its credits. The platform path in `blog.service.ts` never calls `logUsage` at all — it has no `tenantId` to log against, and `AiUsageLog.tenant_id` is a required FK (`schema.prisma:4644`), so there is no row it could write. Platform staff AI spend is therefore invisible. Making it trackable means a nullable `tenant_id` on the usage log plus a logging call on the admin path.
- [ ] **Shared draft prompt wastes tokens on a nonexistent column** — `buildBlogDraftPrompt` asks the model for `author_title` on every generation, even on the storefront (tenant blog) path which has no such column in `TenantBlogPost` and will discard it. Tokens are cheap but the fix is cheaper: check the context (admin or tenant) and omit the field from the prompt on the path where it doesn't exist.
- [ ] **`TenantPostEditor` reads `useTenantPlanFeatures()` without its `ready` flag** — the storefront AI Assistant button is gated on `premiumAi`, but the hook's initial state is `{ planCode: null, features: {}, ready: false }`, so `canUseAi` is false on first paint and the button pops in once `getMe()` resolves. Fails closed, so it is cosmetic, but every other consumer (`accounting/page.tsx`, `settings/page.tsx`, `settings/dashboard/page.tsx`, `purchases/page.tsx`) destructures `ready` and holds render until it is true. Follow that pattern. Surfaced 2026-08-08 in the blog AI review.
- [ ] **The AI overwrite confirm still under-names what it replaces** — `ai.overwritePrompt` in both editors lists title, excerpt, body, slug, category, audience and featured, but `applyDraft` also overwrites author name, author title, cover alt and both SEO fields. The widened `hasContent` guard even counts `authorName`/`authorTitle`/`coverAlt` as content, so the dialog can be raised by a field it never mentions. Also worth aligning the phrase "featured flag" with the checkbox's own label ("Feature at the top of the index" / `তালিকার শীর্ষে দেখান` / `Paparkan di atas senarai`) — the Malay `tanda keistimewaan` is a particularly loose rendering. Surfaced 2026-08-08 in the blog AI review.
- [ ] **No newsletter list, so the blog index cannot capture an email** — the reference design the blog home was restyled from closes with an email-capture band, and `BlogSubscribeBand` offers the RSS feed and a trial signup instead, because there is no subscriber model, no `POST /blog/subscribe` and no send path: a form there would collect addresses and drop them. Making it real means a `BlogSubscriber` table (email, locale, confirmed_at, unsubscribe token), a double opt-in confirmation through the existing `EmailService`, and a per-post or digest send — none of which is a design change, which is why the band ships without it. Surfaced 2026-08-13 while restyling the blog home.

### Express Retail Pro import — LANDED (2026-07-25), provider contract verified 2026-07-29
Platform-admin-only importer that pulls a tenant's sales/purchase history out of an Express Retail Pro account: `apps/backend/src/external-sync/` (client, mapper, service, nightly scheduler, controller, DTOs), the admin page `apps/frontend/src/app/(app)/admin/tenants/[tenantId]/external-sync/`, `api.ts` helpers, three Prisma models, and migration `20260725120000_add_external_sync`. Credentials are AES-256-GCM encrypted; an id-mapping table makes a re-pull update in place instead of duplicating. Backend tests (39) and both typechecks pass, but nothing below has been exercised against a real database or a real provider.
- [ ] Apply migration `20260725120000_add_external_sync` to a database — it has only ever been `prisma generate`d, never run
- [ ] Write `external-sync.service.spec.ts` — the 907-line import/write path (window resolution, run lifecycle, product/customer/supplier/sale/purchase upserts, mapping writes) has **no** test coverage; only the mapper and HTTP client are tested
- [x] ~~Verify the Express Retail Pro contract against a live instance~~ — **confirmed 2026-07-29** against the Careforce account (org `262`). The XSRF login flow and all seven endpoint shapes match what `express-retail.client.ts` already assumes; no client changes needed
- [ ] Confirm the two deliberate omissions in the service header are the intended end state: imported sales are not posted to the ledger, and customer/supplier due balances are not recomputed (both to avoid double-counting against tenant opening balances)
- [ ] The nightly `0 2 * * *` scheduler re-pulls every enabled connection's full `window_days` range; no connection is enabled by default, so confirm the load is acceptable before flipping the first one on
- [x] ~~Decide the migration model~~ — **done 2026-07-29: full replay, opt-in.** `ExternalSyncConnection.post_impacts` (migration `20260729130000_add_external_sync_post_impacts`, default **off**) makes every imported document produce what a natively entered one would: stock movements, party due balances and dated ledger vouchers, all in `external-sync.impacts.ts`. Off, the import stays inert exactly as before. No cutoff field — the operator is responsible for the tenant having no opening balances over the imported range, and the UI warns before the toggle is saved
- [x] ~~Import ordering / stock guard / opening balances~~ — **fixed 2026-07-29** after a posted import of Towfiq Traders left 943 sales unimported and 214 customer balances negative. Three causes, all now addressed: purchases ran *after* sales within each chunk so a replay from zero stock was always short; `applyInventoryMovement` refused the shortfall, dropping the whole sale while its payments still imported; and the provider's per-party `previous_due` opening balance was never imported, so payments settling pre-history debt had nothing to settle. Imported sales now pass `allowNegative` (a replayed sale is a fact, and its stocking purchase may predate the window), and a party created under posting gets its opening balance as a dated ADJUSTMENT — not posted, since it is the previous system's revenue
- [ ] **Re-import Towfiq Traders on the fixed code.** Its current books are wrong: 943 sales missing, 214 negative customer dues, ledger short by those sales. Posted documents are immutable so a re-run cannot repair them — the imported data and its mappings need clearing first. Schedule is already disabled
- [ ] Stock will legitimately go negative for products whose stocking purchases predate the imported window. Decide whether to seed opening stock before the replay or accept negative balances as the honest record of a partial history
- [ ] **Preconditions before enabling `post_impacts` on any tenant** — none are enforced in code: (a) no opening balances/stock covering the imported range, or every document counts twice; (b) opening stock as of the window start, or replayed sales drive stock negative; (c) posting rules configured for `sale`, `purchase`, `customer_payment`, `supplier_payment`, `sale_return`, or postings silently skip; (d) no locked fiscal period over the range — `autoPostFromRules` rejects those with `FISCAL_PERIOD_LOCKED`, and historical periods are exactly what tends to be locked. Consider a preflight check on the connection page that reports all four before the toggle can be turned on
- [x] ~~Expose the import to tenant admins~~ — **done 2026-07-29.** New `externalImport` platform feature (per-tenant overridable, off by default) plus `tenants/external-sync` controller and a page under Settings › Data Management. Two gates: the platform operator enables the feature for a tenant, and only the workspace OWNER may reach it. `post_impacts` is deliberately **not** tenant-editable — the tenant page shows its state read-only and the controller strips the field from the request — because enabling it needs the preconditions above checked. The Data Management card and the platform-admin per-tenant toggle both appear automatically from `PLATFORM_FEATURE_KEYS`
- [ ] The tenant import page duplicates most of the platform-admin one (`admin/tenants/[tenantId]/external-sync`). They have diverged deliberately for now — different capabilities and wording — but a shared component would stop the two drifting as steps are added
- [x] ~~Make the import observable and runnable in pieces~~ — **done 2026-07-29.** The run row is now rewritten as work proceeds (`phase`, `progress {done,total,warnings}`, live `stats`) instead of only at the end, so the admin page's existing 5s poll shows a progress bar, the current step and per-entity counts while a long import runs — and a crash keeps whatever it had counted. `steps` on the run request (`MASTERS | SALES | PURCHASES | CUSTOMER_PAYMENTS | SUPPLIER_PAYMENTS | SALE_RETURNS`, default all) lets one phase be retried without re-fetching everything; steps are independent because cross-references resolve through the mapping table rather than the current run. `POST /runs/:runId/cancel` stops a run at the next chunk boundary with status CANCELLED, keeping what already imported. Migration `20260729140000_add_external_sync_run_progress`
- [ ] Progress is per step-chunk, not per record: a single month of sales is one unit, so the bar can sit still for a while on a big chunk. Consider emitting a record-level counter inside `syncSalesWindow` if that proves too coarse in practice
- [ ] A cancelled or failed run has no resume: re-running repeats the whole selected range. It is cheap and safe because the mapping table makes re-imports updates rather than duplicates, but a high-water mark per step would avoid the rework
- [x] ~~Rebuild the re-import path~~ — **done 2026-07-29** via immutability rather than reverse-and-repost: once a document has posted, a later re-pull skips it with a `POSTED_IMMUTABLE` warning instead of rewriting rows whose voucher and stock movement have already landed. Upstream corrections to a posted document therefore need manual handling — revisit if that becomes common
- [ ] Add a real `source`/`external_ref` marker to `Sale`/`Purchase`. Today the only thing identifying an imported row is the `XR-` serial prefix plus its `ExternalSyncMapping` row, which is too weak for "exclude imported from X" or "reverse this import"
- [x] ~~Import customer/supplier payments~~ — **done 2026-07-29.** Endpoints verified (they were not in the original build): `GET /get-customer-payments` and `GET /get-supplier-payments`, both taking `page`, `per_page`, `name`, and the same `dateFrom`/`dateTo` window the sale/purchase endpoints use (`from`/`to` are ignored). Unlike the other endpoints these return a **Laravel paginator** — rows are under `data.data`, with `data.total` / `data.last_page` / `data.per_page` — so the client needs a paging loop, not a single fetch. Row shape: `id, invoice (TR#####), date, customer_id|supplier_id, type, method, bank_account_id, amount, note, status, organization_id` plus nested `customer`/`supplier`, `bank_account`, `add_by`; customer rows also carry `previous_due`. Live volumes: 2,044 customer payments (2025-08-03 →) and 185 supplier payments (2025-10-08 →). Targets are `CustomerCreditTransaction` (`postsVia: 'customer_payment'`) and `SupplierCreditTransaction` (`postsVia: 'supplier_payment'`)
- [ ] **Confirm CR/CP with the vendor.** The import reads CR as cash received and CP as cash paid, which is consistent across both lists (a customer CP is a refund out, a supplier CR is a refund in) and matches the observed mix — over 2026 the live account has 1,213 CR / 6 CP on the customer side and 179 CP / 1 CR on the supplier side. It is still an inference from the data, not documentation, and amounts are always positive, so if the codes mean something else those minority rows are booked backwards. `resolvePaymentDirection` skips any *other* code rather than guess
- [ ] Payments record `method` (`cash|bank`) only in the transaction note, because `CustomerCreditTransaction`/`SupplierCreditTransaction` have no payment-method column and `bank_account_id` has no mapping to our accounts. Harmless while imports do not post; must be resolved before B/cutoff, or every receipt hits one default account. `GET /get-bank-accounts` supplies the master list
- [ ] Imported payments store `balance_after` from the provider (`previous_due ∓ amount`) for customers and `0` for suppliers, whose rows carry no `previous_due`. It is the source system's balance, not ours — the importer does not maintain `due_balance`. Revisit with the migration-model decision
- [ ] Consider importing the sale's payment split. `/get-sale` rows carry `cashPaid`, `bankPaid`, `bank_account_id` and `previous_due` alongside `paid`, which the mapper currently ignores in favour of `paid` alone — enough to post a sale's own settlement correctly without the payment list
- [ ] `/get-sale` rows have their own `reference` field (null throughout the sampled data). We map `invoice` into `reference_number`; revisit if a tenant actually populates `reference` upstream
- [x] ~~Import sale returns~~ — **done 2026-07-29.** `POST /get-sale-return`, mapped to `SalesReturn` + `SalesReturnItem` with the provider number in the new `reference_number` column (migration `20260729120000_add_sales_return_reference_number`, which also closes the last document model that had no such field). Returns run after every sale in the range so a parent in an earlier chunk resolves; a return whose parent sale is missing, or whose line does not match a line of that sale, is skipped whole with a warning rather than imported with a total that does not add up. Verified live: 16/18 returns import, 2 skip because their parent sales predate the window, and every line of the 16 matched
- [x] ~~Return parents are now optional~~ — **done 2026-07-29.** `SalesReturn.sale_id` and `PurchaseReturn.purchase_id` (and the matching line-level ids) are nullable, migration `20260729160000_optional_return_parents`. A parent is still validated when supplied — lines must belong to it and stay within what remains returnable, and the refund is priced from it so a return can never give back more per unit than was charged. Without a parent the caller supplies product and unit price/cost, and the return settles in cash, the only defensible default when the original tender is unknown. The importer no longer discards a return whose sale is outside the window; it imports it unlinked and warns
- [ ] The return UI still requires picking a sale/purchase. The standalone path exists only through the API — add a "no original document" mode with a product picker and price entry if operators need it
- [ ] **Purchase returns: header-only — decide whether to import at all.** `POST /get-purchase-return` → `data.purchasereturns[]` (same `searchType`/`recordType`/`dateFrom`/`dateTo` body) exposes **no line items anywhere**: no details array on the return, none on the nested `purchase`, and the page references no details endpoint. So `PurchaseReturn` could only be imported header-only — `total_amount` with no `PurchaseReturnItem` rows, hence no per-product detail and nothing to reconcile against. Parent linking works the same way sale returns do (nested `purchase.id`, resolved via the PURCHASE mapping). 12 rows across 2026. Recommendation: skip — a return with no lines reads as data but cannot be used
- [ ] Sale returns skip when their parent sale is outside the imported range — 2 of 18 on a full-2026 window, and proportionally more on the default 90-day one, since returns run 1–3 months behind their sale. The import warns with `PARENT_SALE_UNRESOLVED` and tells the operator to run a full-history resync. Consider a parent-lookup fallback that fetches just the missing sale instead of requiring a full resync
- [ ] Importing sale returns will freeze the parent sale's line items. `writeSale` refuses to rewrite items when `salesReturnItem` rows reference them (the `ITEMS_LOCKED` warning), so once a sale has an imported return, later re-pulls stop updating that sale's lines and only its header changes. Confirm that is acceptable, or teach the update path to rebuild lines around existing returns
- [ ] `/get-damage` exists (damage/wastage, `data.damages[]`) but returned 0 rows for this tenant across 2026 — no target model on our side either. Ignore unless a tenant actually uses it
- [ ] `writePurchase`'s update path does not refresh `created_at`, so an upstream purchase-date correction never propagates on re-pull (the create path does set it from `mapped.purchaseDate`). `Purchase` has no separate business-date column — `created_at` is doing double duty
- [ ] `Purchase.reference_number` is import-only for now: it is written and shown on the invoice, but there is no field to type one on the purchase create/edit form (`Sale.reference_number` is user-editable). Add it if manual entry is wanted

- [x] ~~`api.getSales()` full-history callers~~ — **done 2026-07-29.** The returns parent-picker now searches server-side (`search` + `limit=20`, exact serial matched among the results), onboarding asks only for the server total with `limit=1`, and the dashboard takes five rows for the activity panel plus a count-only probe for the delivery tile — which also made that count exact across all sales rather than whatever had been downloaded. `status` accepts a comma-separated set so the probe is one request. No app code calls `getSales()` any more; it remains only as a helper.

### Server-side pagination rollout (foundation shipped for Leads 2026-07-14)
- [x] ~~sales/list~~ — **done 2026-07-29**, prompted by an imported tenant with 2,238 sales showing an empty list. `GET /sales` moved from cursor to page/limit with a `SALE_SORTABLE` allowlist, server-side search (serial, reference, status, customer) and a server-side status filter; the page uses `useServerList`. One page is now 0.16 ms and 20 rows instead of ~23 requests pulling the whole history.
- [ ] Migrate the remaining ~15 pages that hand a server-capped slice to `DataTable` onto server pagination + sorting (same pattern as Leads: per-endpoint `SORTABLE` allowlist + `sortBy`/`sortDir`, page/pageSize/sort state, `serverPagination` prop). Step 3 (100-limit): crm/customers, crm/tasks, accounting/expenses, accounting/loans, admin/users, hr/leaves, hr/salary-payments, purchases/supplier-payments, sales/customer-payments. Step 4 (20/50/200/500-limit): accounting/reconciliation, accounting/vouchers, crm/campaigns, hr/attendance, sales/customer-ledger, purchases/supplier-ledger, sales/price-lists/[id]. `DataTable`/`resolveOrderBy` need no further changes. Documented limitation: server-mode export/print covers the current page only.
- [ ] `DataTable` client-mode pagination is structurally non-functional (pre-existing, surfaced during the server-pagination review): `state.pagination.pageIndex` is hardcoded to `0` with no `onPaginationChange`, so Next/Prev/page-number buttons never change the visible slice for any table NOT using `serverPagination`. Either fix client mode (back `pageIndex` with state) or migrate all such tables to server mode. Verify which currently-client tables actually let users reach page 2.

## IMPORTANT — First month after launch

### Social publishing

- [ ] **Instagram (and the other untested networks) will reject a push the way Facebook did** — `REQUIRED_POST_TYPE` in `buffer.service.ts` maps only `facebook: 'post'`, because that is the one network with a channel connected to test against. `InstagramPostMetadataInput` needs **both** `type: PostType!` and `shouldShareToFeed: Boolean!` — the latter decides whether a reel or story also lands in the main feed, which is a publishing decision rather than a default to guess at. TikTok, YouTube, Pinterest, Threads and Google Business are unchecked and likely have their own required fields. Whoever connects one of these should read its `developers.buffer.com/types/<Network>PostMetadataInput.html` page and extend the map, ideally exposing type/feed choices in the composer rather than hard-coding them.

### CRM activity unification

- [x] **R1 — expand + backfill** — done 2026-08-15, see COMPLETED. `CrmActivity` is the write path; every legacy table and endpoint still serves reads untouched.
- [x] **R2 — cutover** — built 2026-08-18, PR #502, see COMPLETED. Every screen reads `CrmActivity`; legacy endpoints remain but are unreferenced, which is what starts R3's clock.
- [ ] **R3 — contract — CODE WRITTEN, NOT SHIPPABLE YET.** Implemented and verified locally on 2026-08-18 (drops the three tables, the two `legacy_*` columns, the three backend modules and `sync-crm-activities.ts`; `prisma migrate diff` against R2 shows exactly 3 `DROP TABLE`, 2 `DROP COLUMN`, 1 `DROP INDEX` and their FK constraints, nothing else; verified against a local Postgres that `db push` applies it cleanly and the container start chain still runs with the tables gone). **The gate is not met and cannot be until R2 has been deployed for seven days**, so it must not merge: production applies schema with `db push --accept-data-loss` on container start, and R2 is not even released yet. Gated on legacy row counts matching their mirrored activities and seven days of no legacy writes. Drops `LeadConversation`, `CustomerInteraction`, `CrmFollowUp`, the `CrmActivity.legacy_*` columns and the three legacy modules. **Must stay additive-free-of-DROP until then**: production reconciles schema with `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` on container start and never runs the migrations directory, so a `DROP` merged early destroys data on the next restart rather than at a deliberate migration.
- [ ] **Observe backfill idempotency on production across two runs** — supersedes the pre-R1 rehearsal item, which was overtaken by the #499 deploy. The backfill has now run once against real data (115 rows, 1 of 21 tenants) and its numbers were checked against the live database, but idempotency there is still proven by construction only: nobody has compared row counts across two runs on prod. The second run happens on the next container restart. **R3 deletes this script**, so if it is going to be checked, it has to be before that release — after R3 the legacy tables are gone and any backfill error is frozen permanently.
- [x] **Three of the four bugs the spec documents** — fixed in R1 (see COMPLETED): converting or losing a lead now cancels its planned activities and clears the rollup; completing an activity records an outcome and stamps `last_contacted_at` on both leads and customers, so the reorder cron no longer re-fires at customers the team already called; assigning an activity to someone other than yourself notifies them.
- [x] **The fourth bug is now fully fixed** — the CRM dashboard's due/overdue cards read `PLANNED` activities as of R2, so a materialised next step finally reaches them. Was: **the dashboard cards are still blind to `next_step`** — an overdue next step is now a real `PLANNED` activity and `GET /crm/activities/summary` counts it, but the CRM dashboard still reads `GET /crm/follow-ups/summary`, which counts only the legacy table. Closes with the R2 cutover; until then the due/overdue cards under-report by however many next steps the backfill materialised.

### Referral system — robustness and product gaps

- [ ] **Bangladesh payout realities are unmodelled** — commission to individuals attracts AIT withholding, and `RefereePayment.method` is free text rather than a real bKash/Nagad payout. Worth a gross/withheld/net split before this scales past a handful of partners.
- [ ] **No admin UI for the `referral_accounting` settings** — the four keys (enabled, house tenant, expense account, payment account) have to be set through the platform-settings API or directly in the table. If commission posting is switched on, this is the next step.
- [ ] **A reversed-after-paid commission posts no reversing journal entry** — the clawback updates the referral ledger but not the platform books, so the two disagree after a refund on an already-paid commission. Needs a credit-side entry mirroring the payout.
### UI rules

- [ ] **The shared `DataTable` header itself breaks the CLAUDE.md UI rules** — every `<th>` it renders carries `font-black uppercase tracking-widest`, which the guidelines ban in app code. Found while asserting the referral portal no longer hand-rolls a status pill: the page-level test had to be scoped to `<td>` because the shared component trips the same check. Fixing it is one line in `DataTable` but changes the header look of **every table in the app**, so it wants a deliberate visual call rather than a drive-by.
- [ ] **Open the print template editor and a real print in a browser** — the whole feature is verified by unit tests, typecheck and the in-app iframe preview only. Nothing has gone through an actual print dialog, and the two things most likely to be wrong there are exactly the two that jsdom cannot exercise: whether the repeating `<thead>` letterhead really repeats on page 2 of a long A4 invoice in Chrome, and whether a Cloudinary logo is reliably decoded before `print()` fires on a slow connection.
- [ ] **Bangla headers need an embedded font** — `fontFamily: 'bengali'` resolves to `Noto Sans Bengali`/`SolaimanLipi`/`Nikosh` from the *print host's* installed fonts. On a Windows POS without them the header silently falls back to Arial and renders Bangla as boxes. Fix is a base64 WOFF2 subset inlined into the print CSS (~100–300KB per print window), which is worth measuring before committing to.
- [ ] Print **footer** templates — signature blocks, terms & conditions, "computer-generated document" lines. `PrintHeaderConfig` is versioned JSON specifically so this lands as a type change, not a migration; `openPrintWindow` already takes `footerHtml`.
- [ ] Page numbers ("Page 1 of 3") on multi-page documents — needs the same `<thead>`-style trick as the header, or a CSS `@page` counter that Chrome does not currently support in the margin box.
- [ ] Thermal logo quality at 58mm — the renderer caps the logo at 10mm high there, but no real 58mm roll has printed one yet. If it smudges, flip `showOnThermal` to default off for `Thermal58` via `perPaper`.
- [ ] The remaining printed surfaces still have no tenant header: `BarcodeLabel`/`inventory/labels` (deliberate — labels are not documents), the three React invoice pages (`sales/[id]/invoice`, `purchases/[id]/invoice`, `purchases/orders/[id]/invoice`), which print via CSS rather than `openPrintWindow` and should render `renderHeaderHtml` output through a small `<PrintHeader />` component.

### Accounting dashboard

- [ ] **Print a long trial balance and a multi-page P&L for real** — the statement printers are verified only by asserting on the HTML handed to `document.write`. What jsdom cannot show is whether the column header repeats on page 2 of a 200-row trial balance in Chrome (`repeatHeader` uses the `<thead>` trick, same as the invoice header) and whether the seven columns fit A4 portrait without the amounts wrapping.

Every module's Overview now answers "what is happening in this module now", the way CRM > Overview does. Six of the seven were a link grid repeating the sidebar. No new routes, no new nav nodes, no new plan feature — each dashboard inherits its own module's existing guard stack, and each degrades to the plain hub rather than a 403.

### Sales entry parity follow-ups
- [ ] **Sale lookup on the return entry page fetches every sale to resolve one serial** — `api.getSales()` then a client-side `.find()` on `serial_number`, carried over verbatim from the deleted `IssueReturnModal` and marked with a TODO in `sales/returns/new/page.tsx`. Needs a lookup-by-serial endpoint (or a serial filter param on the existing list endpoint).
- [ ] **Returns entry seeds every line at its full returnable quantity** — a deliberate change from the old modal, which seeded 0 and made the user type each amount. Faster for the common "return the whole sale" case, but a careless user can now over-return by accepting the defaults. Revisit if it causes real mistakes; the per-line cap (`maxQuantity`) still prevents returning more than was sold.
- [ ] **Quotations and orders cannot store a money breakdown** — the same gap as the `Sale` one above, on two more models. `CreateQuotationDto`/`CreateSalesOrderDto` take only `totalAmount`, and their item DTOs only `productId`/`quantity`/`price`, so document-level discount/VAT/transport/labour/rounding and per-line discount % are **hidden** on those screens (`showAdjustments`/`showDiscount={false}`) rather than silently dropped. `CreateSalesOrderDto` also has no notes field, so the order screen has no note box. Persisting any of it means Prisma columns + DTO fields on both modules, and a decision about what a quotation total means when it converts to a sale.
- [ ] **No edit route for quotations, orders or returns** — the three entry pages create only. Their `[id]` detail pages still use their own UIs, so an edited quotation looks nothing like the one just created — exactly the split `/sales/[id]` just fixed for sales. The update DTOs do accept items, so an `/edit` mode on `DocumentEntryLayout` (mirroring what `SaleEntryLayout` does with `readOnly`) is feasible.

### Sidebar UX
- [ ] **No HR Reports group and no payroll UI** — both deferred out of the HR menu restructure (see COMPLETED) because they have nothing behind them. There is no HR report page anywhere in the frontend, and the backend `payroll` module (`PayrollRun`, `PayrollLine`, `SalaryComponent`, `EmployeeSalaryStructure`, `EmployeeBankAccount`) has **zero** frontend pages, so Payroll ships as a group holding only Salary Payments. Both groups were designed to absorb these later without a second restructure
- [ ] **The HR restructure has had no browser pass** — verified by unit tests, typecheck and lint only. jsdom cannot show whether four nested groups make the HR module scroll awkwardly at 360px, or whether an accordion in front of Payroll's single child feels right. Also note the id rename resets `sidebar-open-groups` in localStorage for anyone who had HR groups open — harmless, but it is why the module may appear collapsed on first load after the deploy
- [ ] **Platform-admin menu restructure has had no authenticated browser pass** — unit tests cover the tree (nav-resolver, Sidebar, nav-layout-merge). Local `/login` is up, but the seeded platform-admin login returned 500, so the nested groups were not clicked through at 360px / desktop. Same class of gap as the HR item above.
- [ ] **A saved `platform_admin_layout` will not pick up the new grouping.** `addNavNodesToLayout` only inserts missing ids; it does not reparent `admin.support` into Inbox or `admin.referrals` into Growth. An unset layout already uses the new default. If someone has customised the platform-admin sidebar, reset it from Platform → Navigation (or leave it unset). The 2026-08-18 node ids are listed on `DEFAULT_PLATFORM_ADMIN_NAV_LAYOUT` for a future `sync-nav-layout.ts` run.
- [ ] **Expenses module phase 2 — move the URLs.** The Expenses split (2026-07-20) is nav-only: the pages still live at `/accounting/expenses/*`, so breadcrumbs still read "Accounting" and `page-breadcrumbs.ts` has no `expenses` module root. Moving to `/expenses/*` means the page dirs, `routes.ts`, `accounting-only-paths.ts`, `voice-nav-routes.ts`, the layout redirect at `layout.tsx:326`, and redirects for existing deep links.
- [ ] **Expenses access is still `canAccessAccounting`.** Deliberate (same gate it had inside Accounting, and the pages are still under `/accounting`), but a real Expense module probably wants its own `VIEW_EXPENSES`/`MANAGE_EXPENSES` in `packages/shared-types/index.ts` so a shop can let a manager file expenses without opening the whole ledger. Backend `expenses.controller.ts` currently declares no permission decorators at all — check that first.
- [ ] **Saved nav layouts silently reset when registry ids are removed.** `parseNavLayoutJson` falls back to the code default when a saved layout references an unknown id, so the Expenses split discards any custom tree a tenant/platform admin had saved (`validateNavLayout` → "Unknown nav id"). Acceptable one-off here, but a nav restructure has no migration path today — consider a reconcile step that drops unknown nodes and grafts new ones instead of resetting wholesale.
- [ ] Sidebar expand/collapse: respect manual collapse (auto-expand effect currently re-opens the active route's group)
- [ ] Platform admin menu flash: derive `inPlatformAdminMode` optimistically from `active_context` before `getMe()` resolves
- [ ] Scope `sidebar-open-groups` localStorage per workspace (tenant vs platform-admin)

### Accounting posting correctness — fabricated vouchers + dead rules (2026-07-16) — CRITICAL — LANDED, follow-ups below

Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-16-accounting-posting-correctness-design.md`. Found while planning the demo-data work. All items originally tracked here are now done and consolidated into `## COMPLETED` below (see the "Accounting posting correctness" entry). Remaining open threads:

- [x] **Verify CI + quarantine flaky e2e suites** (2026-07-17) — pushing `dev` confirmed CI red: the three DB-backed e2e suites failed on PR #380 **and on the prior `dev` HEAD `9f64bde` before this work**, so they are pre-existing, not a demo-data regression. Two distinct failure modes, neither fixable by a timeout bump: `integration.spec.ts` blows a 20s `beforeAll` AppModule-boot timeout under coverage; `inventory-operations.spec.ts` + `sales-returns-orders.spec.ts` get **400 on signup** — parallel e2e suites colliding on the shared `retail_test` DB (a test-isolation bug). Quarantined all three individually via `--testPathIgnorePatterns` in `deploy.yaml` (same pattern the frontend job already uses); `database-exports.spec.ts` **and** the new `demo-data.integration.spec.ts` keep running (both green in CI). Real fix (proper per-suite DB isolation / raised boot timeout) is deferred below. `src/` unit run is 98 suites / 1283 green.
- [ ] **Un-quarantine the 3 backend e2e suites** — `integration.spec.ts`, `inventory-operations.spec.ts`, `sales-returns-orders.spec.ts` are excluded from CI (`deploy.yaml` `--testPathIgnorePatterns`). They fail from a shared-DB isolation collision (concurrent signups 400) and a too-tight AppModule-boot timeout under `--coverage`, not from product bugs. Fix by giving each e2e suite its own tenant/email namespace (or `--runInBand` for `test/`) and raising the boot hook timeout, then drop the ignore patterns.
- [x] Wire `PaymentMethod.account_id` into posting — done 2026-07-18 (additive `overrideDebitAccountId`/`overrideCreditAccountId` on `autoPostFromRules` + `resolvePaymentMethodAccountId`; wired into sales cash + paid-portion legs). See COMPLETED. **Follow-up:** apply the same override to the other cash-touching callers (customer/supplier payments post the mode leg on the opposite side; expenses on the credit leg) — the mechanism is in place, they just need to pass the override
- [x] Wire per-asset `FixedAsset.asset_account_id` / `depreciation_account_id` into posting — done 2026-07-18 via the same override mechanism: `asset_account_id` overrides the debit (Fixed Assets) on acquisition, `depreciation_account_id` overrides the debit (Depreciation Expense) on the monthly run
- [ ] Perpetual inventory accounting — needs multi-leg vouchers or a second COGS event per sale (`autoPostFromRules` writes exactly two `VoucherDetail` rows). Would make Stock on Hand / COGS real; the periodic model chosen here leaves inventory off the balance sheet entirely (folds in the previously-separate "inventory model is incoherent" item — nothing ever debits Stock on Hand under periodic, so a perpetual-style COGS rule would only ever go negative)
- [ ] `purchase_return` posts Dr Purchase Payable / Cr Purchases unconditionally (`condition_key: 'none'`) — wrong for a cash purchase's return, which should credit cash
- [ ] Purchases cannot record a payment: `CreatePurchaseDto` has no `paidAmount`, `purchases.service.ts:75` never writes `Purchase.paid_amount` (schema default 0), and the full total is always booked as `CREDIT_PURCHASE`. So every purchase is a payable and `purchase/cash`|`purchase/bank` rules are unreachable. Adding `paidAmount` would need the supplier credit transaction to book only the unpaid remainder
- [x] **Purchase Payable never clears in the ledger** — fixed 2026-07-17 by the `supplier_payment` event type; see COMPLETED below

### Follow-ups from the voucher-edit fix (2026-07-31)

- [ ] `VoucherDetail` has **no line-order column** — lines are read back `orderBy created_at asc`, and because every line of a voucher is inserted in one transaction they all share the same `now()`, so Postgres was free to return them in a different order on each read. Patched by adding an `id asc` tiebreak (order is now stable, but arbitrary rather than the order the user typed). The real fix is a `line_number Int` on `VoucherDetail`, written by `createVoucher`/`updateVoucher` **and by `posting.utils.ts`** (which every auto-posting module goes through), with a backfill migration for existing rows. Until then, a voucher's debit/credit lines may display in an order the user never entered — on the detail page, in the list expansion, and on printed vouchers
- [ ] `getVoucher` returns `store_id`/`attribution` but not the store's **name**, so an edited voucher whose branch was deleted (or belongs to a store the current user cannot see) silently falls back to the first store in the list rather than saying which branch it had. Worth surfacing once branch-scoped visibility exists

Compare scope needs nothing: the server already drops zero-total rows there

Add a flip once there is a browser to check it in

### Follow-ups from the chart-of-accounts UI work (2026-07-31)

- [ ] Renaming a bootstrap-seeded account is now possible from the UI, but `bootstrap-accounting.ts` resolves accounts **by name** (`accountByName.get(rule.debit_account)`) when wiring `DEFAULT_POSTING_RULES`. Safe at runtime today because the bootstrap runs only at tenant creation (`auth.service.ts`, `admin-tenants.service.ts`), but an explicit `sync-accounting` run against a tenant that renamed, say, "Cash in Hand" will not find it and will recreate a second account under the original name. Either give `Account` a stable `system_key` for the bootstrap to match on, or block renames of rule-referenced accounts
- [ ] `Account.party_type` (set on AR / Purchase Payable / Salary Payable control accounts, driving the per-party subsidiary ledger) is not exposed anywhere in the chart-of-accounts UI — a hand-created control account can never be marked as one. Decide whether it belongs in the account modal or should stay bootstrap-only
- [ ] `apps/frontend/src/app/(app)/admin/tenants/page.test.tsx` fails 9/9 with `api.getAdminAddonModules is not a function` — the suite's `@/lib/api` mock was never extended when the addon-modules work added that call. Pre-existing and unrelated to the CoA work; noticed while running the full frontend suite
- [x] Run `repair:vouchers` against production — done 2026-07-18. Dry-run reviewed, then live: **1 fabricated voucher deleted** (Grocery Demo `JV-00001`, Dr General Operating Expense / Cr Cash in Hand, 490, 2026-04-17 — the `inventory_adjustment` fingerprint, not the `fund_movement` Main Bank/Cash one) + **38 harmful `none`-fallback rules removed** (2/tenant × 19; `event_type IN (fund_movement, inventory_adjustment)`, distinct event_types from what `sync:accounting` adds, so no oscillation). No renamed-account preservations. See COMPLETED.
- [ ] The repair fingerprint matches accounts by NAME, so a tenant who renamed a fingerprinted account (e.g. "Cash in Hand") keeps their fabricated vouchers. Fails safe (preserves rather than wrongly deletes), and the dry-run output now prints account names so an operator can spot the mismatch before running live
- [ ] `customers.service.spec.ts` still never asserts a voucher is actually created — its only posting assertion is `expect(autoPostFromRules).toHaveBeenCalled()`, which checks the call happened, not what it returned. Worth a test asserting `postingStatus`/`voucher_id` on a real credit payment

### Voucher coverage & party ledgers (2026-07-17) — CRITICAL — spec written, not started

Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`. Goal: every transaction involving an item of monetary value creates a voucher, by default, for every tenant; every customer/supplier/employee gets a ledger *derived from* the GL rather than parallel to it. Found by auditing which modules *should* call `autoPostFromRules` rather than which ones do — `POSTING_CONTRACT` only validates calls that already exist, so a module that never posts is invisible to it. Phases are ordered; Phase 0 gates the rest.

- [x] **Phase 0 — `npm run sync:accounting`** — built 2026-07-17, see COMPLETED below. **Not yet run against production**, so the live bKash/Nagad bug persists there until the item below is done
- [ ] **Run `npm run sync:accounting --workspace=@erp71/database -- --dry-run` against production**, review the per-tenant report with the user, then run live. This is what actually fixes the "some Skipped, some Posted" split on prod. (The Dockerfile CMD now runs it on every container start, so the next deploy applies it automatically — run the dry-run first to see what it will do before that happens.)
- [ ] Phase 1 — chart of accounts: new **Non-Current Assets** group (none exists); accounts 1045 Advances to Suppliers, 1050 Fixed Assets, 1055 Accumulated Depreciation, 1060 Staff Advances, 2030 Customer Advances, 5020 Salary & Wages, 5030 Depreciation Expense, 5040 Cash Shortage/Overage (2050 Salary Payable gated on D4). Add `Account.party_type` and mark 1030/2010/2050 as control accounts
- [x] Phase 2 — event types + default rules **landed incrementally** (supplier_payment, depreciation, cash_transaction, salary_accrual/payment, asset_acquisition, fund_transfer). **Lazy `ensureCustomerPaymentPostingSetup`/`ensureLoanPostingSetup` folded into `DEFAULT_POSTING_RULES` and deleted 2026-07-18** (single provisioning mechanism; both now `expectation: 'rule'` in POSTING_CONTRACT). Still pending: `supplier_advance`, `order_deposit`, `advance_settlement` (deposits — Phase 5)
- [x] Phase 3 — party dimension — done 2026-07-17, see COMPLETED below. **`SalesOrder.customer_id` is nullable** — taking a deposit must require a customer or the advance line is unattributable (enforced in Phase 4 deposits). Historical `party_id` backfill onto existing AR/AP lines deferred to Phase 8; this phase tags new postings only
- [ ] Phase 4 — wire the gap modules (each passes party). **suppliers: DONE 2026-07-17** (see COMPLETED) — and now passes `partyType: 'SUPPLIER'` as of the Phase 3 work, so supplier payments appear in the GL-derived supplier ledger. Remaining: **sales-order deposits** (`sales-orders.service.ts:119`). **salary-payments: DONE 2026-07-18** (accrual + payment settlement + party ledger, see COMPLETED), **cashier PAYOUT/LOAN: DONE 2026-07-18**, and **depreciation: DONE 2026-07-18** (see COMPLETED — now wrapped in a `$transaction`, posts Dr Depreciation Expense / Cr Accumulated Depreciation, and the fiscal-period guard comes free via `autoPostFromRules`' date). Add `voidAutoPostedVoucher` to update/remove paths (`salary-payments:124`, `sales-orders:223` hard-delete paid amounts with no reversal). **fund-transfers: DONE 2026-07-18** (converted to `autoPostFromRules` via the `fund_transfer` event, both legs emit PostingEvents). Add every tuple to `POSTING_CONTRACT`
- [ ] Phase 5 — **SalesOrder → Sale conversion + `advance_settlement`.** Required by D2 (advances are real posting accounts). No conversion flow exists anywhere — `sales.service.ts` never references `salesOrder`/`orderDeposit` — so without this, advances accumulate and never clear. Books stay net-correct meanwhile (Advances + AR are both real, just grossed-up), but this is not deferrable indefinitely
- [x] Phase 6 — sales partial-payment idempotency collision — fixed 2026-07-18 via `legKey`; see COMPLETED
- [x] **Phase 7 — `money-model-contract` (the anti-recurrence guard)** — done 2026-07-18, see COMPLETED below. (The supporting party invariants — every control line has a `party_id`; party_ids resolve to real parties — already landed with Phase 3 in `demo-data.integration.spec.ts`.)
- [x] **Fixed-asset acquisition posts a voucher** — fixed 2026-07-18; `createFixedAsset` now posts Dr Fixed Assets / Cr <mode> via the `asset_acquisition` event (optional `paymentMethod`, defaults cash). `FixedAsset` reclassified `gap`→`postsVia` in `money-model-contract.ts`. See COMPLETED
- [x] Phase 7b — **fix the read paths** — done 2026-07-17, see COMPLETED below
- [x] Phase 8 — **backfill script** `npm run backfill:vouchers` (sales + supplier-payment reposters) — done 2026-07-18, see COMPLETED. **Built + locally verified, NOT run against prod.**
- [x] **Run `backfill:vouchers` against production** — done 2026-07-18. Deploy auto-ran `sync:accounting` (19 tenants brought up to date; verified with a follow-up `--dry-run` → "All 19 tenant(s) already up to date"). Then backfill dry-run → live: **posted 1373 rows, 0 failed, 0 in locked periods** (Grocery Demo 1027 sales + 66 supplier-payments + 139 customer-payments; the rest seed/demo tenants, 15–18 `SL-SEED-*` sales each). Re-run dry-run → 0 (idempotent). ⚠️ **Deploy/ops bug found — see new item below:** the `npm run backfill:vouchers` command in this instruction FAILS in the prod image; had to run the compiled `node apps/backend/dist/scripts/backfill-vouchers.js` instead.
- [ ] **BUG: `backfill:vouchers` npm script is unrunnable in the prod Docker image.** `apps/backend/scripts/backfill-vouchers.ts` (run via `tsx`) imports `../src/accounting/posting.utils` (+ `classify-payment-mode`, `customer-credit.utils`), but the runner stage of `apps/backend/Dockerfile` copies only `dist` and `scripts/` — **not `apps/backend/src`** — so tsx throws `Cannot find module '../src/accounting/posting.utils'`. (The other three scripts live in `packages/database/prisma/` and are self-contained, so `sync`/`reconcile`/`repair` work fine.) Workaround used on prod: `docker compose … run --rm --entrypoint sh backend -c "node apps/backend/dist/scripts/backfill-vouchers.js …"` — the build already emits `dist/scripts/backfill-vouchers.js` whose relative `../src/…` resolves to the present `dist/src/…`. **Fix options:** (a) point the npm script at the compiled JS, (b) `COPY apps/backend/src` into the runner stage, or (c) rewrite the script's imports to not reach into `src`. Update `docs` / runbook to match whichever.
- [x] Phase 8 cont. — **more reposters** (customer payments, salary, cashier cash-outs, depreciation) added to `backfill-vouchers.ts`, each reconstruction checked against its caller; depreciation links its voucher back to the entry. E2e-verified (depreciation + cashier); done 2026-07-18
- [x] Phase 8 cont. — **reconciliation** `npm run reconcile:balances` (diffs `due_balance` vs GL-derived party balance; `--rebuild` overwrites from GL) and **ledger UI repointed** at the GL-derived view (`buildPartyLedger` + `GET /customers|suppliers/:id/gl-ledger`; both ledger pages swapped data source) — done 2026-07-18, see COMPLETED
- [ ] Phase 8 final — **retire the parallel tables**: once `reconcile:balances` shows GL and `CustomerCreditTransaction`/`SupplierCreditTransaction` agree on prod, drop the parallel tables + the credit-transaction endpoints. Deferred until the diff is clean on prod. **Prod reconcile ran 2026-07-18 (report only, NO `--rebuild`): 48 party balances differ — NOT clean.** Root cause (verified by querying prod `voucher_details`, **not** a purchase-posting gap — purchases DO post): **historical control-account lines created before the Phase 3 party dimension (2026-07-17) have no `party_id`**, so the per-party GL omits them. On Grocery Demo's `Purchase Payable`: all 157 `purchase` lines (Cr 2,105,800) + 32 `purchase_return` lines are `party_id`=NULL, while all 66 `supplier_payment` lines (Dr, re-posted by `backfill:vouchers` which tags party) are tagged → per-supplier GL sees only payment debits ⇒ negative. AR reconciles because sales+customer-payments got tagged (freshly re-posted by backfill, or already tagged). Pattern (A) — 9 seed/demo tenants, 4 seed customers each (GL small positive vs `due_balance=0`) — is separate stale seed denorm. **Fix = a one-time `party_id` retag/backfill** (the deferred Phase-8 historical backfill): for each untagged control line, set `party_id`/`party_type` from the source doc. **Built 2026-07-18: `npm run retag:parties --workspace=@erp71/database` (`--dry-run`, `--tenant=`)** — `packages/database/prisma/retag-party-ledger.ts`, self-contained (runs in the prod image, unlike `backfill:vouchers`). Only stamps the leg whose `Account.party_type` matches the source's party type (never the cash/revenue leg), only `party_id`=NULL lines (idempotent). Resolvers: `sale`→`Sale.customer_id`, `sale_return`→`SalesReturn.sale.customer_id`, `customer_payment`→`CustomerCreditTransaction.customer_id`, `purchase`→`Purchase.supplier_id`, `purchase_return`→`PurchaseReturn.supplier_id`, `supplier_payment`→`SupplierCreditTransaction.supplier_id`. NOTE this is a **retag**, not a re-post — a `backfill:vouchers` purchase reposter would no-op since the vouchers already exist. Sequence: retag → re-run `reconcile:balances` (should come clean) → only THEN consider `--rebuild`. **Do NOT `--rebuild` before the retag** (it would overwrite Grocery Demo's correct `due_balance` from the still-lopsided per-party GL). **RAN ON PROD 2026-07-18** (PR #383, deployed): dry-run counts matched the known untagged lines exactly (Grocery Demo 156 purchase + 32 purchase_return retagged, 1 supplier-less purchase correctly skipped), then live retagged 188 lines. Re-run `reconcile:balances` (oracle) → **Grocery Demo suppliers 12/12 → 3/12 mismatched** (from 100k+ negative balances down to small diffs). The 3 residuals are **legitimate purchase-return/overpayment divergences, not a retag miss** (verified: all lines now tagged): Green Marketing GL=−1,334.85 (truly overpaid, `due_balance` floors at 0); Dhaka Marketing (+2,626.91) & Sunrise (+3,701.54) `due_balance` > GL because a purchase return exceeded the outstanding due (GL debits the full return, the app caps the `due_balance` reduction). **`--rebuild` still NOT run and NOT warranted** — it would push these to nonsensical payables. Retag only sets `party_id`/`party_type`, never amounts, so the trial balance is unchanged. Remaining reconcile noise = 35 seed-tenant customer rows (stale seed `due_balance`, separate from this work).
- [ ] Phase 5b — **payroll accrual + employee payment (D4 = IN).** Makes all three party types symmetric: Customer→AR/Customer Advances, Supplier→AP/Advances to Suppliers, Employee→Salary Payable (2050)/Staff Advances (1060), so the Phase 3 party dimension covers employees with no extra machinery. New `SalaryAccrual` model mirroring `AssetDepreciationEntry` (`employee_id`, `pay_period`, `amount`, `voucher_id`, unique per period) to give `autoPostFromRules` a real `sourceId`. Monthly runner modelled on the depreciation runner (`accounting.service.ts:2269`) but inside a `$transaction` + `assertFiscalPeriodOpen`: `salary_accrual` → Dr Salary & Wages / Cr Salary Payable \[party\]; `salary_payment` settles it → Dr Salary Payable / Cr \<mode\>. Employee ledger = `voucher_details WHERE party_type='EMPLOYEE'`; payable = accrual − Σ payments, derived from the GL. **Blocker: drop `@@unique([tenant_id, employee_id, pay_period])` on `SalaryPayment`** (+ the `ConflictException` at `salary-payments.service.ts:66-70`) — it permits exactly one payment per employee per month, forbidding the partial payments/advances this design exists for. Scope limits: no salary structure exists (flat nullable `Employee.basic_salary` only, skip+report employees without one); no attendance→pay link, so loss-of-pay deductions are out of scope

### Six-month demo data generation (2026-07-16) — LANDED, follow-ups below

Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-16-six-month-demo-data-design.md` (approach B — primitive-level generator in the backend). Built and verified end-to-end: the full CLI run (`npm run seed:demo`) generates ~2,972 sales spanning six backdated months in ~19s, and the trial balance balances exactly (Σdebits == Σcredits, difference 0.00) because it is *derived* from the tenant's own `PostingRule`s. All items consolidated into `## COMPLETED` below (see the "Six-month demo data generation" entry). Remaining open threads:

- [ ] **Spec correction (accepted, not fixable here):** cashier sessions **cannot** reconcile to sales — there is no FK from `Sale` to `CashierSession`. The generator creates plausible standalone sessions with opening/closing floats; tying them to sales needs a schema change (out of scope).
- [x] Gap — supplier payments never call `autoPostFromRules` — fixed 2026-07-17 in `suppliers.service.recordCreditPayment`; see COMPLETED below. The demo-data generator's `paySuppliers` now also posts a `supplier_payment` voucher (done as part of the Phase 3 party work), so a freshly generated dataset no longer understates Purchase Payable movement
- [ ] `inventory-shrinkage.service.ts` uses `product.price` (selling price) as `unitCost` on the movement — a real modelling flaw (the generator sidesteps it by passing `product.cost`, but the production service is still wrong).

The other three catalogs (`PHARMACY`, `SURGICAL_MEDICAL`, `COMPUTER_HARDWARE`) are authored and unit-selected by `catalogForBusinessType`, but have not been exercised by a full generation run against a tenant of that type — worth a smoke run each.
- [ ] `apps/backend/test/demo-data.integration.spec.ts` is DB-backed (like the other `test/` suites) and needs a reachable `DATABASE_URL` with the schema pushed. It passes against a clean DB; verify it is green in CI (see the "Verify CI" item above) and quarantine only if genuinely flaky.

### Voucher approval — follow-ups (2026-08-02)
- [x] ~~No per-report "approved only" toggle~~ / ~~no bulk approve~~ / ~~no sidebar queue badge~~ / ~~never opened in a browser~~ — **all four done 2026-08-02**, see COMPLETED
- [ ] **The deploy path applies no migrations — remember this for the next backfill.** `apps/backend/Dockerfile` CMD runs `prisma db push` then the sync scripts, so anything in `prisma/migrations/` reaches production never. The APPROVE_VOUCHER backfill therefore lives in `prisma/sync-approve-voucher-permission.ts`, called from `sync-accounting.ts`. This is the second time it has bitten (first: account codes, commit `409234e`). Worth either making `sync-accounting.ts` the documented home for all role-permission drift, or teaching the container to run `migrate deploy` — the current split means every backfill has to be written twice and kept in step by hand.
- [ ] **Party ledgers and customer/supplier due balances deliberately ignore approval** (`party-ledger.util.ts` is untouched) — a customer's outstanding due should not move because an accountant has not signed off yet, and those lines are almost all system-posted anyway. AR/AP *aging*, being financial statements, DO follow the setting. Worth a decision if a tenant ever turns `auto_approve_system_vouchers` off, because then the two views can disagree.
- [ ] Editing an approved voucher silently resets it to PENDING (by design — an edit invalidates the sign-off) but the editor gives no warning before saving.
- [ ] **The approved-only toggle is remembered per browser, not per user** (`localStorage['report_approved_only']`, matching how scope/level/hide-zero already work). Two people on one machine share it, and it survives a tenant switch — so an accountant who flips it on for tenant A sees it on for tenant B. Same latent problem as the three existing keys; fix them together if it ever bites.
- [ ] **Bulk approve acts on the current page only** (20 rows, capped at 200 server-side). There is no "select all N matching this filter" — a tenant that turns off `auto_approve_system_vouchers` and lets a backlog build will page through it.
- [ ] The sidebar badge polls every 60s and refreshes on an in-app `erp71:voucher-approval-changed` event; an approval made in *another tab* still takes up to a minute to show. Fine for now — worth revisiting only if the app grows a real push channel.
- [ ] Approving does **not** re-check the fiscal period. A voucher entered into an open month, left pending, and approved after that month is locked will still land. Arguably correct (the voucher already exists and its date never changes), but it is an unstated assumption — decide explicitly.

### Gross-profit reporting — follow-ups from the weighted-average costing work (2026-08-07)

Nine of the ten landed on 2026-08-07 (see COMPLETED). The tie-out is the one left.

- [ ] **Build the Cost Coverage page, or delete `getCostCoverage`** — the backend `GET /sales-reports/gross-profit/cost-coverage` endpoint and the `api.getCostCoverage` client wrapper are both live, but the page they were written for was never built. The frontend route pointing at it was removed on 2026-08-11; the endpoint is deliberately left in place because it is the list a tenant works through to make the other four reports defensible. Either build the page or drop both.
- [ ] **Operational-vs-ledger tie-out** — the accounting P&L (`accounting/reports/pl`) has no gross-profit section and no COGS grouping, so sales-report gross profit and the trial balance cannot be reconciled. Under periodic inventory they legitimately differ — stock is expensed at purchase, so the P&L shows purchases where the sales reports show COGS of what actually sold — and a tie-out report would state that difference explicitly instead of leaving two numbers to argue. Deferred deliberately: it is a reconciliation *between* two correct-but-different bases, not a bug in either, and it needs a decision about which one the owner is meant to trust for tax.

### Weighted-average costing — follow-ups (2026-08-07)

- [ ] **Run the backfill against production before trusting any margin report** — `cd packages/database && npm run backfill:product-costs` (dry run) then `-- --apply`. Until it runs, pools are empty and sales silently fall back to `ProductPrice.cost`, i.e. the old behaviour, with no indication on screen that they have.
- [ ] **The pool is read-then-written inside the caller's transaction** — quantity uses an atomic increment so it cannot drift from the movement ledger, but the average is computed from the quantity as read, so two receipts of the same product landing together can blend against a slightly stale basis. Rounding-scale on a rare event; a `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` would fix it at the cost of serialising the till. Revisit if a high-volume tenant shows drift.
- [ ] **Product create still writes no `ProductPrice` row** — the new optional `cost` on `CreateProductDto` seeds the pool through INITIAL_STOCK but does not reach the price list, so a product created with a cost and no opening stock has no basis under LATEST_COST. Write the row, or drop the field from that path.
- [ ] **`getStockOnHand` recomputes its own weighted average at report time** (`inventory-reports.service.ts:157-180`) from purchase movements, excluding INITIAL_STOCK because it used to carry retail. Now that a maintained pool exists and that bug is fixed at source, the report should read `ProductCost` instead of deriving a second, slightly different answer.
- [ ] **Migration chain cannot be replayed from scratch** — `prisma migrate deploy` on an empty database fails at `20260510182137_warranty_claims` ("relation ProductSerial does not exist"); `db push` is what actually builds the schema. Pre-existing and unrelated to costing, but it means migrations are not a usable disaster-recovery path.

- [ ] Customer-facing invoice/receipt email after a sale
- [ ] **Persist the sale entry form's adjustment breakdown.** `Sale` stores only `total_amount`, so discount %, VAT, transport, labour and rounding are lost the moment the sale is saved — `/sales/[id]` can only show their *net* as one "Adjustment" row, and per-line `discount` is likewise dropped (`SaleItem` has no column for it, so every reloaded line reads 0%). Needs columns on `Sale` (+ `SaleItem.discount_percent`), the create/update DTOs carrying them, and the detail page rendering the real rows instead of the residual. Adding the relation (mirroring `CustomerCreditTransaction.creator`) is a one-line schema change plus a migration

### Performance
- [ ] Implement Redis caching for hot data (product catalog, active pricing) — Redis is provisioned but unused
- [ ] Switch large list endpoints to cursor-based pagination
- [ ] Run `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on the 10 most frequent queries
- [ ] Decide the timezone contract for date-range filters — every `from`/`to` (or `dateFrom`/`dateTo`) filter in the codebase treats a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` as a **UTC** day boundary: purchase-reports, sales-reports, inventory-reports, warehouse-transfers, accounting and now CRM conversations. The tenants are Bangladeshi (Asia/Dhaka, UTC+6), so a user picking "5 July" gets 05 Jul 06:00 → 06 Jul 06:00 local, and anything logged between midnight and 6am on the 5th lands in the previous day's bucket. Each endpoint is individually self-consistent and matches every other report, which is why the CRM conversations filter was deliberately built the same way rather than diverging — but the whole set is quietly off by six hours for the business it serves. Fixing it is one shared `buildDateWindow` helper that takes the tenant timezone (the AI chat prompt already resolves `Asia/Dhaka`), applied everywhere at once; fixing one endpoint alone makes two reports of the same day disagree.
- [ ] Fix the load-sensitive flake in `AiChatWidget.test.tsx` ("offers starter questions and sends one when picked") — it uses `findByText` with the default 1s timeout, and failed once in a full-suite run while passing in isolation and on three subsequent full runs. Adding the 144th suite was enough extra worker contention to trip it, so it will keep resurfacing as suites are added. Raise the timeout or await the specific mock settling rather than the rendered text.
- [x] Add DB query timeout to prevent runaway queries
- [x] Enable Next.js Image optimization for product images
