## OPERATIONAL — Pre-launch or ongoing

### Deployment
- [ ] **The saved platform `tenant_layout` in production is invalid, so every nav-layout customisation there is silently dead** — found 2026-08-05 while running the post-deploy `sync-nav-layout --nodes=inventory.reports.stock-on-hand` step for the stock-on-hand release. The script **refused to write** because the merged layout fails validation: the stored layout still references `accounting.transactions` (plus its four children `expenses`, `expense-categories`, `expense-reports`, `loans`) and `admin.tenants`, none of which exist in `NAV_REGISTRY` any more. **No harm to that release** — verified in code, not assumed: `parseNavLayoutJson` returns `getDefaultNavLayout(scope)` whenever `validateNavLayout` fails (`packages/shared-types/navigation.ts:579`), so the invalid layout is discarded at read time and every tenant sees the full code default including the new report. **But that is exactly the problem:** the platform-level sidebar customisation has been ignored on every request for some time, and nobody would notice because the fallback looks correct. It also blocks the `--nodes=crm.setup` item below, which will refuse to write for the same reason. Fix is to prune the six dead ids from the stored layout (or reset the platform layout to the default and re-apply any wanted customisation), after which both sync runs go through. Worth adding a startup or health-check warning when a stored layout fails validation, since the silent fallback is what hid this. **Still invalid on 2026-08-11**, re-checked against production during the #489 nav deploy — same 138 nodes, same six dead ids — and it did the same favour again: the four gross-profit entries reached every tenant only because the stored layout is discarded at read time. Two releases in a row have now depended on this bug being present. The one row in `TenantNavLayout` has `layout = null` (pinned to default), so per-tenant overrides are not a factor yet
- [ ] **Run `sync:nav-layout --nodes=crm.setup` against production** — the CRM Setup release (PR #445, deployed 2026-08-03) collapsed the `crm.lead-taxonomy` and `crm.custom-fields` sidebar nodes into one `crm.setup`, and `resolveTenantSidebarLayout` returns a saved layout verbatim, so any tenant who customised their sidebar before this release has **no CRM Setup entry at all** — the two old pages still redirect into it, so the feature is reachable by URL but invisible in the menu. Dry-run first: `docker exec erp71-backend-1 npx tsx packages/database/prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=crm.setup --dry-run`, then without the flag. Re-running is a no-op. Not run during the 2026-08-03 deploy — the command was blocked by the local permission classifier, not by anything on the VPS
- [ ] Make `scripts/deploy.sh` fail loudly when the new image never starts — it fast-forwards the repo before building, so a failed build leaves `/opt/erp71` on the new commit while the containers keep serving the old image, and the site stays up and healthy throughout. A deploy should end by polling `/api/v1/health` until `commit` equals the SHA it just checked out, and exit non-zero (ideally after `git reset --hard` back to the previous SHA) if it does not. Found 2026-07-25 when a frontend build OOM'd and the deploy reported nothing amiss.
- [ ] **Move the backend container off `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` to versioned migrations** — the container CMD (`apps/backend/Dockerfile:105`) runs `db push` on every boot, which **cannot add a required column to a table that already has rows**. This took production down for ~25 minutes on 2026-08-07 during the HRIS release (PR #468): `LeaveType.updated_at` is `DateTime @updatedAt`, which Prisma emits as NOT NULL with no database default, and prod `LeaveType` had 4 rows. `db push` refused, exited non-zero, and the backend crash-looped — the frontend kept serving 200s the whole time, so the site looked alive while every API call failed. **CI could not have caught this**: it tests against a fresh empty database, where `db push` on 0 rows always succeeds. The failure mode is structural and will recur on the next required column added to any populated table. Fix is `prisma migrate deploy` against checked-in migrations, so the same SQL runs in CI and production and a non-backfillable change fails the pipeline instead of the box. Note `db push` is *also* invoked with `--accept-data-loss` in `scripts/deploy-erp71.sh:99`
- [ ] **`LeaveType.updated_at` was backfilled by hand in production — no migration exists for it** — recovering the 2026-08-07 outage meant applying `ALTER TABLE "LeaveType" ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "updated_at" TIMESTAMP(3)`, backfilling from `created_at`, then `SET NOT NULL` directly against the prod database. Production is now consistent with the schema, but **that SQL lives nowhere in the repo**, so any other environment restored from an older dump, or any new environment brought up against existing data, will hit the identical crash-loop with no record of the fix. Needs a real migration with a backfill (add nullable → backfill → set not null) committed alongside the schema change. Pairs with the `db push` item above — a versioned migration is what makes this reproducible
- [ ] **Renumber `20260811090000_add_recruitment` — it collides with `20260811090000_add_attendance_punches`** — both landed 2026-08-11 (PRs #486 and #485) and were written against the same timestamp. Nothing is broken today: Prisma sorts by full directory name so the suffixes break the tie deterministically, the two migrations create disjoint table sets with no cross-references, and the production deploy path is `db push`, which never reads either file. It becomes real the moment someone runs `migrate deploy` against a fresh database — which is exactly the fix the two items above call for, so clear this first. Rename to `20260811100000_add_recruitment`; the careers-portal migration at `20260811120000` already sorts after both and depends on the recruitment tables, so it stays put. Same class of bug as `fix(db): renumber the labels migration to 20260803130000`
- [ ] Give the VPS more headroom, or stop building on it — 1.9GB RAM is thin for building two Node images on the same box that also runs them (plus scrum71, hermes and the portfolio site). The frontend build already needed its V8 heap raised to 3072 to spill into swap, which makes deploys slow and leaves no margin for the next dependency that grows. Options: build images in CI and pull them on the VPS, add a persistent BuildKit cache, or resize the box.
- [ ] **Complete one real Google sign-in against production, then one Google signup** — shipped and configured 2026-08-08 (PR #473), and everything observable from outside is green: the button renders, `/auth/google/config` returns `enabled: true`, no console errors. **But no ID token has ever reached `POST /api/v1/auth/google` from a real Google account**, so three things remain unproven and all three fail *only* at the moment a user clicks: whether the **consent screen is actually published** (left in "Testing" it works for listed test accounts and silently fails for everyone else — the single most likely misconfiguration), whether `https://app.erp71.com` is on the **authorised JavaScript origins** list (a missing origin errors on click, not on render), and whether the **account-resolution branches** behave — signing in with a Google account whose email matches an existing password user should *link* to that workspace, not create a second empty one. Test in this order: (1) sign in from `/login` with a Google account that already has an ERP71 password account, confirm it lands in the existing workspace and `/auth/me` reports `google_connected: true` with `has_password: true`; (2) sign in from `/login` with an unknown Google account, confirm `requires_workspace: true` routes to the onboarding wizard rather than erroring — this path was **broken until PR #469 fixed the wizard's `planCode: 'FREE'`** and has never run in production; (3) sign up from `/signup` with another unknown account and confirm the org name and plan carry through in the same request. Until (1) is done, the email-linking rule — the one security-relevant decision in the feature — has never executed against real data
- [ ] **Configure Firebase phone auth and complete one real mobile sign-in against production** — the code shipped (see COMPLETED below) but the feature is **entirely inert until two env vars are set on the VPS**: `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` and `FIREBASE_API_KEY`. Nothing in it has ever run against Firebase — every test drives the verifier with locally-generated RSA keys and a stubbed JWKS endpoint, and the frontend tests stub the SDK, so the SMS leg has never been exercised at all. Setup order matters and each step fails at a different moment: (1) enable **Authentication → Sign-in method → Phone** in the `erp71-709cf` Firebase console — without it the browser gets `auth/operation-not-allowed` on *send*, not on render; (2) add `app.erp71.com` to **Authentication → Settings → Authorised domains** — a missing domain fails the invisible reCAPTCHA (`auth/captcha-check-failed`), again only on click; (3) set both vars in `.env.production` **before** the deploy build, not after, so one build brings the feature up (same lesson as `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` on 2026-08-08); (4) check the SMS quota — the Spark plan's is small and Firebase throttles hard per number and per IP. Then test in this order: sign in from `/login` with a number that already sits on exactly one ERP71 account (proves the adoption branch, and `/auth/me` should report `mobile_connected: true` with `has_password: true`); sign in with an unknown number and confirm `requires_signup` collects an email and the new account lands in the onboarding wizard; sign up from `/signup` with another unknown number and confirm the org name and plan carry through in one request. Also worth confirming deliberately: a number that sits on **two** accounts is refused with the "linked to more than one account" message rather than signing anyone in — that is the one branch where guessing wrong would hand someone another tenant's workspace. Full runbook: `docs/ops/mobile-sign-in-setup.md`
- [ ] **Exercise the blog end-to-end before announcing it** — deployed 2026-08-08 with only route mounting and public page rendering verified (`/blog`, `/whats-new`, `/blog/rss.xml`, `/sitemap.xml` all 200, but all of them against an **empty** blog). No post has been authored, published, edited or read through either admin UI. Worth checking specifically: that `/blog` and the RSS feed render correctly with real content rather than in their empty state, that a tenant with `TenantBlogSettings.enabled = false` (the default for all 21 tenants) sees the storefront blog stay dark, and that the 63 freshly granted blog permissions actually open the right screens for a non-owner manager rather than 403-ing

- [ ] Campaign recipient preview (`GET /crm/campaigns/:id/preview`) only shows name/phone in the sample list — add email for EMAIL-channel campaigns
- [ ] Uploaded campaign rows resolve against Customer/Lead/CrmContact with `email: { in: [...], mode: 'insensitive' }`, which Postgres compiles to `LOWER(email) IN (…)` — a hash probe per row, not a pattern match, but still a sequential scan because no plain btree index on `email` can serve a function of the column. Three scans per upload, bounded by the 1,000-row cap and down from the 3N they replaced, so this is not urgent. Case-insensitivity is required (none of the three tables normalises email on write, and an exact match would mint a duplicate contact for a stored `Rahim@Example.com` on every upload). **A `LOWER(email)` functional index on each of the three tables would make this index-backed with no other code change** — that is the cheap fix if these tables get large. Normalising email on write across the three tables would also work but needs a backfill and a write-path change.
- [ ] **A WhatsApp campaign reports 100% delivered even when nothing was sent** — `WhatsAppService.sendMessage()` swallows every failure: missing credentials log a line and return, and a non-2xx from Meta logs and returns. Nothing throws, so `dispatchCampaign` marks the recipient `SENT` and increments `delivered_count`. SMS and EMAIL both throw and are counted honestly; WhatsApp is the odd one out. Fix is a `{ sent: boolean }` return threaded into the dispatch loop. Noticed 2026-08-06 while adding per-tenant sender identity; deliberately left alone there because it changes delivery accounting, not sender resolution.
- [ ] WhatsApp campaigns send `type: 'text'`, which Meta only accepts inside the 24-hour customer-service window — a business-initiated marketing blast needs an approved message *template*, which isn't implemented. Combined with the swallowed-failure item above, a cold WhatsApp campaign is rejected by Meta and still reported as delivered.
- [ ] Per-tenant sender identity covers CRM campaigns only — every other tenant-originated email (sale receipts, low-stock alerts, reports) still goes out from the platform address. `EmailService.send()` takes a `tenantId` now, so extending it is a per-call-site decision rather than new plumbing; platform mail (password reset, verification, billing) must stay on the platform sender.
- [ ] No per-tenant SMS sender ID — `SmsService.getCredentials()` still reads the single platform `sms.sender_id`. Same shape as the email/WhatsApp identity if a tenant ever gets its own approved mask.
- [ ] CRM analytics dashboard (Epic 84 Stories 1–2: top customers, retention rate, loyalty liability) still not built — only the AR aging report (Story 3) shipped
- [ ] Profile photos on Leads and Contacts — spec written 2026-08-11 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-crm-lead-contact-photos-design.md`), not started. `photo_url` + `photo_storage_key` on `Lead` and `CrmContact`, a new `POST /crm/photos` upload endpoint returning both (so replaced photos can actually be deleted, unlike the voucher/project attachments), a `PhotoField` control in both create/edit forms reusing `AvatarCropModal`, and the avatar rendered inline in the first column of both list pages.

Run one external question (e.g. "what is the NBR VAT rate on packaged food") and check that citations come back and land in the sources line. Add web questions to `ai-chat-eval.md` while there.

If eval shows it searching for things the report tools cover, the next step is a router check on the *question* rather than the query.
- [ ] **Confirm `web_search_enabled` is actually `true` in production.** Reported 2026-07-26 as "web search is not working": the assistant answered a product-research question without mentioning the web at all. That is the exact signature of the flag being off — `resolveTools` strips both web tools before the request is built and `buildSystemPrompt` omits the whole `THE WEB` section, so the model never learns the capability exists and cannot say it is unavailable either. The local dev DB has **zero** rows in `platform_settings` (no `ai` group at all), so locally it is certainly off; production was not checked (a read-only psql over SSH was blocked by tooling permissions). Check Platform Settings → AI, then `GET /ai/chat/tools` — if `web_search` is missing from that array the flag is not reaching `resolveTools`; if it is present, the problem is downstream in the OpenRouter call and this is the first live exercise of the plugin request/annotation shapes.

### Transaction anomaly detection (started 2026-07-25, `feat/ai-transaction-anomalies`)

- [x] **`transaction_anomalies` tool + `AnomalyDetectionService`** — 14 deterministic SQL checks over sale lines, purchase lines, duplicates, entry timing and refunds — see the COMPLETED entry — done 2026-07-25
- [ ] **Weekly scheduled scan → notifications.** The tool only runs when somebody opens the chat panel and asks, which is not how anyone finds fraud. `AnomalyDetectionService.scan()` already takes a tenant id and a window and is exported from `AiModule` for exactly this. Wants a `@Cron` job through `JobTrackerService` (like the low-stock alert), a `high`-severity-only filter, and one in-app notification per tenant per week rather than one per flag. Decide whether it emails as well — the owner already gets low-stock and expiry mail, so the pattern exists.
- [ ] **DB-backed tests for the SQL itself.** `anomaly-detection.service.spec.ts` covers the half that decides what a flag *means* (rule precedence, impact, severity, partial failure) against a stubbed `$queryRaw`. Nothing has executed the queries: `percentile_cont` returning what we think, the `created_at::date - sale_date::date` gap sign, the over-refund `HAVING` aggregating across multiple returns, and the `string_agg` duplicate grouping are all unverified. Belongs with the `test/*.integration.spec.ts` suites — which currently cannot run locally (the dev DB is missing `feature_overrides`).
- [ ] **Execute the SQL once, anywhere.** Shipped to production 2026-07-25 (PR #400, `2b72804`, health-verified) with the six raw queries never having run against a real database — the suite stubs `$queryRaw`. Safe to ship only because a failed detector now names itself rather than reporting a clean audit, so the worst case is a check reporting itself unavailable. Still the highest-value 10 minutes available: one scan against any real dataset proves or disproves `percentile_cont` on a `numeric` column, the `created_at::date - sale_date::date` gap sign, the over-refund `HAVING`, and the `string_agg` grouping all at once. A read-only psql run against the prod DB was attempted and correctly blocked by tooling permissions; the local dev DB cannot run it either (missing `feature_overrides`, pre-existing).
- [ ] **Run it against the demo tenant and tune the thresholds.** The `normal` preset (40% price deviation, 5× quantity, ≥5 baseline samples, ৳100 minimum impact) is a considered guess, not a measured one. The number that matters is flags-per-month on a real six-month dataset: a scan that returns 300 rows is the same as one that returns none. Check the duplicate-invoice rule hardest — it excludes walk-in sales precisely because it would otherwise bury everything else, and that exclusion is the assumption most likely to be wrong for a shop that names every customer.
- [ ] **No REST endpoint or page.** Chat-only, like the Phase 5 report endpoints. If it survives eval, `GET /ai/anomalies` + a review page (dismiss a flag, mark it explained) is the natural next step — and a dismissal store is what would stop the weekly scan re-reporting the same known-good transaction forever.
- [ ] **Index review before this runs on a large tenant.** The baseline CTEs join `SaleItem`/`PurchaseItem` to their parent over 90 days and group by `product_id`. `SaleItem` has an index on `sale_id` only; `PurchaseItem` has **no indexes at all** beyond its PK. Deliberately not added here — an index on `SaleItem(product_id)` taxes every sale line insert, which is the hottest write path in the app, and that trade wants a measurement rather than a guess. Measure the scan on the largest tenant first.
- [ ] **Detectors not built, and why.** Per-line discounts: `SaleItem` has no discount column, so "who is giving away the biggest discounts" is unanswerable without a schema change (the deviation-from-median price rule is the closest proxy). Backdating on purchases: `Purchase` has only `created_at`, no separate document date, so there is nothing to compare. Per-cashier return concentration: needs a rate against that cashier's own volume, not a per-row rule, so it does not fit the current shape. Expenses and manual vouchers are not scanned at all.
- [ ] **Watch whether the model actually reaches for it.** The prompt names the trigger phrases ("is anything wrong", "check for mistakes"), but the failure mode is the opposite one — reaching for `list_documents` and eyeballing invoice totals, which cannot work because that tool returns no line items. Add anomaly questions to `ai-chat-eval.md`, including one that should *not* trigger it.

## ROADMAP — Post-launch features

- [ ] Reconcile local dev DB schema drift blocking `prisma db push` for the URL-shortener migration — `Customer` is missing a `@@unique([tenant_id, user_id])` constraint that has been in `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma` since commit `5665ab9` (2026-05-22, "Session 10 — refund/SLA pages, DB indexes, NID encryption"); no actual duplicate rows exist (`SELECT tenant_id, user_id FROM "Customer" WHERE user_id IS NOT NULL GROUP BY 1,2 HAVING count(*)>1` returns zero), so applying it is safe, but it's an unrelated schema mutation out of scope for the URL-shortener task that added it. Once resolved, run `cd packages/database && npx prisma db push` to actually create the `ShortLink` table and `Quotation.share_token`/`share_token_at` columns locally (currently only the generated Prisma Client types exist, not the live table/columns). Found 2026-08-04 during URL-shortener Task 2.
- [ ] Move the remaining ~55 list pages from "fetch everything" to true server pagination (`useServerList`) — the count bug is fixed everywhere and every row is reachable, but only products, customers, suppliers, the inventory ledger and CRM leads actually page against the server; the rest call `fetchAllPages`, which walks every page on load. That is strictly better than the previous behaviour (a wrong total *and* missing rows) but it trades a wrong number for a larger payload, so each page is worth converting as its table grows — sales, purchases, sales orders, quotations, returns and warranty claims first, since those grow per transaction. **The paging is the small half.** Each conversion must also move that page's search box, column sorts and filter presets server-side, or they silently apply to one page of N and look like they worked — see the products (`stockStatus`) and customers (`segment`/`customerType`) conversions for the pattern, plus a `SortableMap` in the service so `sortBy` stays allowlisted. `useServerList` already handles the page-1 reset and the stale-response guard.
- [ ] Decide whether table preferences should be per tenant (or per user) rather than per browser — `data-table-preferences` is one `localStorage` key shared by every tenant signed in from the same browser profile, so column layout, widths and rows-per-page carry across a tenant switch, and a genuinely new tenant on a previously-used machine inherits whatever that browser had stored. Keying by tenant id is a small change; moving it server-side per user is the fuller answer and would make the setting follow a person across devices. Either way it resets everyone's saved preferences once, so it wants a deliberate call rather than a silent migration.
- [ ] Give the remaining lookup dropdowns a searchable async picker instead of loading every row — `getSuppliers`, `getCustomers`, `getProducts`, `getEmployees` and friends now fetch the complete set so nothing is silently missing from a picker, but a tenant with thousands of products pays for all of them on page load to render a `<select>`. The POS customer typeahead (`api.searchCustomers`) is the shape to copy.
- [ ] Unify lead conversations and customer interactions into one relationship timeline — `LeadConversation` and `CustomerInteraction` are near-identical models (type, direction, summary, outcome, created_by, created_at) split either side of conversion, and nothing joins them. **A lead's entire conversation history disappears from view the moment it converts to a customer**, which is precisely when that history becomes most valuable: `Lead.converted_customer_id` records the link, but `/crm/conversations` reads only `LeadConversation` and the customer profile reads only `CustomerInteraction`. Options are a union query behind one endpoint, a Postgres view, or collapsing both onto one polymorphic `Interaction` table with a nullable `lead_id`/`customer_id` pair (the shape `CrmTask` already uses). The last is the cleanest and the only one needing a data migration. Deliberately out of scope of the conversations list, which was asked for and built lead-only.
- [ ] Make `CustomerInteraction.type` tenant-editable too, from the same channel list — the customer profile at `/sales/customers/[id]` still hardcodes `['CALL','SMS','WHATSAPP','EMAIL','VISIT','NOTE']` and its own `typeIcons` emoji map, and the backend still validates it against the `InteractionType` enum. So a tenant that adds "Telegram" to CRM Setup sees it on a lead and not on a customer — the same conversation, classified two different ways depending on which side of conversion it happened. `ConversationChannel` is already the right table for both; the work is a `channel_id` on `CustomerInteraction` plus the same resolve-on-write the conversations service now does. Best done together with the timeline unification above, since that would collapse both models anyway. Found 2026-08-03 while making the lead conversation channel list dynamic.
- [ ] Reconsider the `/crm/lead-taxonomy/:kind` route name now that it serves conversation channels as well — the module handles three lists and the URL still says "lead". Renaming it is a one-line controller change plus the five call sites in `api.ts`; deliberately not done in the same pass as adding channels, so that change stays reviewable as a feature rather than a rename. Found 2026-08-03.
- [ ] Add a controller spec for `crm-lead-conversations` — `crm-leads` has both a service and a controller spec, this module now has only the service one. The untested surface is real rather than nominal: the DTO→opts mapping in `toOpts`, the `mine=true` → `tenant.userId` resolution (the one place a user could otherwise spoof whose conversations they read), and the `@Get('summary')`-before-`@Get(':id')` declaration order, which is currently guaranteed by nothing but line position and would regress silently into a 404 on any reordering.
- [ ] Run `npx tsx prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=inventory.demands` after the product-demands deploy — `resolveTenantSidebarLayout` returns a saved layout verbatim and never merges `NAV_REGISTRY` into it, so any tenant or platform layout saved through the nav admin will not show Inventory → Demands until this runs. Deliberately not on boot (see the script's header); it is a no-op on re-run and reports what it skipped. Found 2026-08-07 while adding the demands nav entry.
- [ ] Let an approved product demand raise its warehouse transfer or purchase order directly — today fulfilment is a free-text note naming the transfer or PO, which is honest but means the link between the request and the movement lives in a string nobody can query. The approved quantities are already per line and already exactly the shape `CreateWarehouseTransferDto.items` wants, so "create transfer from demand" is mostly plumbing plus deciding the source warehouse; the reason it was left out is that it makes a demand able to move stock, which is the property the module was deliberately built without. Worth doing once there is evidence of how tenants actually fulfil them. Found 2026-08-07.
- [ ] Notify an approver when a demand is submitted, and the raiser when it is decided — the module has a full approval flow and sends nothing, so a demand sits in SUBMITTED until somebody happens to open the page. `Notification` and the low-stock email path already exist; the missing piece is deciding who "the approver" is, since `APPROVE_PRODUCT_DEMAND` is a per-store grant and may be held by several people or nobody. Found 2026-08-07.
- [ ] Translate the `/careers` pages into bn/ms — the public board, job detail, sign-in, register and the three portal screens are English-only, unlike every other public page on the platform, which goes through `useI18n`. Roughly 70 strings. The hiring-side recruitment screens are already fully translated. Found 2026-08-11.
- [ ] Notify on careers events — nothing is emailed at any point: a candidate learns their stage changed only by opening the portal, and a workspace learns it has a board application only by opening the applicants list. `JobApplicationEvent` already records exactly what would be sent. The open question is who "the hiring workspace" means for an email, since `MANAGE_HR` may be held by several people or nobody — the same question left open for product-demand approvals above. Found 2026-08-11.
- [ ] Let a company write a note the candidate can actually read — the portal timeline is stage-and-date only, because `JobApplicationEvent.note` is where interviewers write internal remarks and publishing it would leak deliberation. A `visible_to_applicant` flag on the event plus a "share this with the candidate" tick in the stage modal would give "interview on Sunday 10am at the Gulshan office" somewhere to live. Deliberately not done in the same pass as the portal, since it changes what an existing field means for the hiring side. Found 2026-08-11.
- [ ] Verify a careers signup's email or mobile so claiming works for ordinary users — `resolveApplicant` will only link a candidate to a record a company already holds when the platform has verified the matching identifier, and a careers signup verifies neither, so anyone whose number the employer already has is refused instead of linked. The platform already has both mechanisms (email verification tokens, Firebase phone auth); the missing piece is offering them in the careers flow. Until then the refusal message is the whole answer. Found 2026-08-11.
- [ ] Give a hiring workspace its own branded careers page — the board is platform-wide and filterable by company, but there is no `/careers/c/<slug>` a shop could link to from its own site, and no company logo anywhere on the board. `Tenant.brand_logo_url` and `storefront_slug` both already exist. Found 2026-08-11.
- [ ] E-commerce storefront (Standard/Premium tier feature per PRD)
- [ ] Delivery / fulfillment management
- [ ] Offline-capable POS (service worker + IndexedDB sync)
- [x] Multi-branch consolidated reporting
- [ ] Extend the reporting detail level (account/subgroup/group) to the remaining COA-grained reports — `comparative-pl`, `budget-vs-actual` and `cost-center-pl` still render account rows only. The roll-up primitives are already generic (`report-level.utils.ts` + `buildLevelledGroups`/`buildLevelledCompareSection` in `accounting.service.ts`), so each is a DTO field plus swapping its group builder. Deliberately deferred from the first pass to keep that change reviewable.
- [ ] Expandable drill-down rows on the statement reports — the level selector switches the whole report at once; expanding a single group to see its subgroups/accounts in place is the natural follow-up. The payload already carries group→row nesting and `is_unassigned`, so this is a UI-only change.
- [ ] Support partial payment of an investor's monthly profit share — `payShare` settles a share in full because the payout posting's idempotency key is `tenant:investor_profit_payout:<shareId>`, so a second partial against the same share would collide with the first and silently no-op. `InvestorProfitShare.paid_amount` already exists for it. The fix is either a `legKey` derived from a payout sequence or a separate `InvestorPayout` row per payment with an allocation to the share; the latter also gives a payout a date and method of its own, which a part-settlement needs anyway.
- [ ] Decide how an investor who joins or exits mid-month should be pro-rated — `eligibleInvestors` includes anyone active for any part of the period and gives them their full agreed percentage, so someone who joined on the 28th takes a whole month's share. Pro-rating by days is the obvious alternative but it is a policy call, not a bug: for a partner who put capital in at the start of the month and formalised late, the current behaviour is the right one. Whichever way it goes it should be written down in the module rather than left implicit.
- [ ] Run the investor profit run on a schedule instead of by hand — it is a month-end action someone has to remember, and every other monthly accrual in the system (`salary_accrual`, depreciation) is already cron-driven. The run is idempotent on `(tenant, year, month, scope_key)`, so a scheduled invocation is safe to retry; what it needs first is a per-tenant opt-in, since declaring a share automatically is a money decision.
- [ ] **Investor portal — phase 1, login and identity** — plan written up in `docs/investor-portal-plan.md`. `Investor` has `name`/`email`/`phone` but no `user_id`, so a partner has no way to authenticate as themselves; every figure about their own money is currently visible only to staff. Phase 1 is the migration (`user_id`, `portal_access`, `@@unique([tenant_id, user_id])` — scoped per tenant like `Customer`, not globally like `Referee`, because one person may back several shops), an `InvestorPortalGuard` modelled on `RefereeGuard`, `GET /investor-portal/me` + `/summary`, invite/revoke endpoints behind `MANAGE_INVESTORS`, and the `active_context === 'investor'` wiring through `auth-session.ts` / `select-account` / `(app)/layout.tsx` / `Sidebar.tsx`. **The security tests belong in this phase, not after it** — in particular the one pinning the invariant the whole design rests on: an investor has no `TenantUser` row, so `TenantInterceptor` never sets `request.tenantId` and `@Tenant()` throws, which is what makes an ordinary `app`-scope token inert against staff endpoints. Audited as sound today — 75 controllers take tenant via `@Tenant()`, only `auth`/`navigation`/`invitations` read `req.tenantId` directly — but nothing currently fails if that changes.
- [ ] **Investor portal — phase 2, the numbers** — capital ledger, profit-share history, statement, PDF/CSV. The statement is nearly free: `Investor Profit Payable` is already a `PartyType.INVESTOR` control account, so `buildPartyLedger` produces a per-investor statement with no new query code, and `InvestorsService.get()` already returns capital txns and shares with run detail. Mostly projection and field-stripping.
- [ ] Decide whether an investor sees the company's net profit in the portal — their share is `basis x pct`, so hiding the basis while showing the share is only nominally private, and a capital partner arguably has a right to the figure; but an owner with a 5% partner may not want total profit disclosed. Suggested resolution is a per-tenant `investor_portal_show_profit_basis` toggle defaulting on. A policy call, not a bug — it needs deciding before the phase 2 UI is built, since it changes what the summary screen is.
- [ ] Third-party accounting exports (Tally/QuickBooks format)
- [ ] Public API + API key management for enterprise customers
- [ ] White-label option

### HR (Epic 60–63)

- [x] Attendance & Leave management (Epic 61) — AttendanceRecord, LeaveType, LeaveBalance, LeaveRequest models + migration; AttendanceModule with full CRUD API (upsert attendance, leave type management, leave balance set/query, leave request create/review/cancel, attendance summary); registered in AppModule — done 2026-06-09
- [ ] Payroll & Salary management (Epic 62) — remaining: salary profiles beyond basic salary, loan/advance management, monthly payroll generation, PDF payslips
  - [x] Salary payment option — `SalaryPayment` model (employee, amount, pay_period YYYY-MM, payment_date, method, notes) + `basic_salary` on Employee; migration `20260615000000_add_salary_payments`; `SalaryPaymentsModule` (GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE `/salary-payments` + `/salary-payments/summary`) with one-payment-per-employee-per-period guard; salary field on add/edit employee forms; Salary Payments page at `/dashboard/salary-payments` (pay form prefilled from basic salary, period/date/employee filters, period total) + sidebar link under HR; i18n en/bn/ms — done 2026-06-15

#### HRIS — all 14 phases built (2026-08-06)

Plan: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-hris-completion-design.md`. Every gap from the Shomvob comparison is now implemented; the entries below record what shipped and, more usefully, what each phase deliberately did **not** do.

- [x] **Phase 0 — permission lockdown.** `EmployeesController` guarded with `JwtAuthGuard` alone, so any authenticated tenant user could read every salary. New `MANAGE_HR`; reads need `VIEW_HR`, writes `MANAGE_HR`, salary needs `VIEW_PAYROLL` to *read or write* — the write half matters because the edit form posts `basic_salary` on every save and would have round-tripped a stripped read back as null. `hr-manage` backfill group (the existing `hr` group has already reconciled and would skip a new permission forever) — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 1 — employee self-service portal.** `EmployeeGuard`, `/employee-portal/*`, `Employee.portal_access`, `/my` with tabs, employee context in the account chooser and sidebar. Security spec pins the invariant the design rests on: an employee IS a real tenant member, so `active_context` is a UI switch, not a boundary — only the absence of store permissions keeps them out of staff screens — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 2 — holiday calendar and work schedules.** `Holiday`, `WorkSchedule`/`WorkScheduleDay`, `EmployeeSchedule`; `LATE`/`EARLY_LEAVE`/`ON_LEAVE` statuses; pure arithmetic in `schedule.util.ts`. Times are minutes from local midnight, not `DateTime`. `sync-work-schedules.ts` boot-time sync — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 3 — attendance capture.** Employee check-in/out with geofencing off `Store` coordinates, status derived from schedule, `AttendanceSettings` per tenant, and **approving leave now writes `ON_LEAVE` attendance rows** — the two models previously shared no data, so an approved leave left the attendance report showing the employee absent — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 4 — overtime and the frozen month snapshot.** `OvertimeRecord` is the decision, distinct from the observation on the attendance row; `AttendanceMonthSnapshot.frozen_at` is what stops a payroll re-run producing different pay — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 5 — salary structure.** `SalaryComponent`, dated `EmployeeSalaryStructure`, encrypted `EmployeeBankAccount`. `sync-salary-components.ts` seeds components but deliberately does **not** backfill structures — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 6 — payroll runs and payslips.** `PayrollRun`/`PayrollLine`/`PayrollLineItem`, `PayrollAdjustment`, every input copied onto the line, `PAYSLIP` print doc type, payslips on the portal — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 7 — disbursement.** Emits `salary_accrual` + `salary_payment` per line through the existing events; disbursement file grouped by method with an explicit `unpayable` list — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 8 — expense claims.** `ExpenseClaim` with the `LeaveRequest` approval shape; receipts on `uploadBuffer()` + `storage_key` from the start; reimbursement via a `PayrollAdjustment` — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phases 9 & 10 — assigned assets, policies, employee documents.** `AssetAssignment` is not `FixedAsset`-only; `Policy` + acknowledgement tracking; `EmployeeDocument` with expiry sweep — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 11 — leave policy engine.** Accrual modes, capped carry-forward, half-day rules, multi-level approval (`LeaveRequestApproval`), team calendar — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 12 — lifecycle and final settlement.** `EmployeeStatus` beyond ACTIVE/INACTIVE, exit reason and last working day, onboarding/offboarding checklists, settlement as a *preview* with blockers reported rather than enforced — done 2026-08-06
- [x] **Phase 13 — Bangladesh statutory registers.** PF register, tax deduction statement (July–June income year), wages register, employee register, service book — all from frozen runs — done 2026-08-06

**Open follow-ups this work leaves:**

- [ ] **Nothing here has been opened in a browser.** All 14 phases are verified by unit test, typecheck and lint only — 187 backend suites / 3,134 tests. The `/my` portal is the one that most needs a real device: whether the check-in button is thumb-sized, whether the browser geolocation prompt behaves on a mid-range Android, and whether the four-tab strip works at 360px. Same standing gap the dashboards and the project board carry.
- [ ] **Payroll accrues net pay, not gross.** The textbook entry splits gross into net payable plus one liability per deduction, and `autoPostFromRules` writes exactly two `VoucherDetail` rows — the same multi-leg limit already logged against perpetual inventory. Net keeps Salary Payable reconciling to zero after payment rather than carrying a phantom balance, but **the employer's obligation for withheld PF and tax is not modelled at all**. Closing it needs multi-leg vouchers, which is the same prerequisite perpetual inventory is waiting on.
- [ ] **Frontend is thin from Phase 4 onward.** Phases 0–3 have real UI (`/my`, `/hr/schedules`, the employee detail changes). Phases 4–13 are API + tests only: overtime approval, the payroll run screen, expense claims, assets, policies, documents, lifecycle and the statutory registers all need pages. The endpoints and their shapes are settled, so these are presentation work rather than design work.
- [ ] **No i18n for phases 4–13.** The three locales carry `employeePortal` and `workSchedules`; nothing else. Adding a page from the list above means adding its keys to en/bn/ms together or the catalog parity test fails.
- [x] **Recruitment is now in scope and built** — see the COMPLETED entry dated 2026-08-11. Appraisals and training remain out of scope per the plan's §2.
- [ ] **Recruitment has not been opened in a browser** — the same standing gap the rest of HRIS carries. The pipeline columns on a job post are the part most worth looking at on a real device: they are a 4-across grid that has only ever been rendered by jsdom, and 360px behaviour is unverified. Surfaced 2026-08-11.
- [ ] **CVs are pasted URLs, not uploads** — `Applicant.resume_url` takes a link, so getting a CV into ERP71 means uploading it somewhere else first, which is the one step in the flow that requires leaving the page. `AssetsService.uploadBuffer()` already returns a public URL with a `storage_key` (the delete-safe path `CrmContactAttachment` uses), so an upload control here is small and avoids the undeletable-asset trap logged under Infrastructure. Surfaced 2026-08-11.
- [ ] **No public application intake** — candidates are entered by whoever is holding the CV. A storefront careers page listing `OPEN` job posts and writing straight into `Applicant`/`JobApplication` is the obvious next step, and the storefront already has the public-route and slug machinery for it. Needs a rate limit and a spam story before it ships. Surfaced 2026-08-11.
- [ ] **Nothing notifies anyone about a hiring pipeline** — no alert for an application sitting in a stage for weeks, no reminder as a post's closing date passes, no email to the hiring manager when somebody applies. `stage_changed_at` and `closing_date` are both stored precisely so a daily cron could answer these; the notifications cron is the precedent. Surfaced 2026-08-11.
- [ ] **Biometric attendance devices** are out of scope. Phase 3 ships geofenced phone check-in, which covers retail and field staff; a factory floor needs per-vendor hardware integration and should be its own phase on evidence of demand. The `AttendancePunch` log added 2026-08-11 is the shape such an import would write into — `source: 'IMPORT'` is already accepted by the DTO — so the remaining work is per-vendor, not structural.
- [ ] **A punch edit does not re-run the month it lands in.** `rebuildDay` fixes the day, but `AttendanceMonthSnapshot` and any `OvertimeRecord` generated from the old figures are left stale until somebody rebuilds the month by hand, and nothing on the in/out screen says so. Worse, a punch can be edited into a **frozen** month, which is exactly the immutability `frozen_at` exists to provide — `upsertAttendance` has the same hole, so this is a pre-existing gap the new screen widens rather than one it introduces. Fix is a frozen-month guard on the punch mutations plus either an automatic snapshot rebuild or a visible "this month needs rebuilding" flag.
- [ ] **Worked minutes ignore the gaps between paired punches.** `rebuildDay` measures first-IN to last-OUT and subtracts the schedule's `break_minutes`, so an employee who punches out for two hours midday is credited as if they took the scheduled one-hour break. Consistent with what portal check-in has always computed, and correct for the common case of one in and one out, but wrong for the split-shift case the punch log exists to describe. Summing paired intervals is the fix; it needs a decision on whether `break_minutes` is then still deducted, or becomes a floor.
- [ ] **The in/out screen has no per-day view.** `GET /attendance/punches/day` is built and returns a day's punches with the record they produce, but nothing calls it — the page is a flat list across a date range. An employee-day drill-down showing the punches beside the derived clock-in/clock-out is the natural place to explain a disputed figure, and the endpoint is already there for it.
- [ ] **`apps/mobile` is still empty and should stay that way for now.** The PWA manifest and service worker already existed, so Phase 3 added a `/my` shortcut rather than starting a Flutter app. Revisit only if push notifications or offline check-in become real requirements.
- [ ] **Run the two new boot-time syncs against a real database before the first deploy** — `sync:work-schedules` and `sync:salary-components`. Both are unit-tested for idempotency and both are in the container start chain, but neither has been exercised against Postgres. `--dry-run` on each is the cheap first check.
- [ ] **`TaskDetailPanel.test.tsx` "renders what is there as markdown" is timing-fragile and now flakes.** It uses `await screen.findByText('Isolate')` on jsdom's 1s default after an async `getProjectTask` mock resolves. Seen failing once in five full frontend runs on the HRIS branch; four subsequent runs and three runs on `dev` were green, so this is pre-existing fragility rather than a break — the HRIS work added 23 test files, which raises worker contention and the odds of missing that timeout. Deliberately **not** fixed as part of the HRIS branch: silently editing another module's test to make an unrelated PR green hides the signal, and the projects owner should decide. The fix is a longer `findByText` timeout, or asserting on the section rather than racing the markdown render. Worth doing soon — a flaky red blocks merges.
- [ ] **`Field` still does not associate its label with its control unless given `htmlFor`.** Hit again while testing `/hr/schedules`; worked around there by passing explicit ids, same as the project date inputs. The `useId()` fix in `Field` remains the better trade and is still unmade.

- [ ] Clean up the stale platform `tenant_layout` nav setting — the saved layout in `platform_settings` (group `navigation`) still references `accounting.transactions`, `accounting.transactions.expenses`, `.expense-categories`, `.expense-reports`, `.loans` and `admin.tenants`, none of which exist in `NAV_REGISTRY` any more. `validateNavLayout` therefore fails it and `parseNavLayoutJson` silently substitutes the code default — so **the saved customisation has no effect at all and nobody has been told.** That is currently load-bearing in a good way (it is why every registry addition still reaches the sidebar), but it is luck rather than design: the moment someone saves a valid layout through the nav admin, that escape hatch closes and any nav entry added afterwards silently stops appearing — which is what `npm run sync:nav-layout --workspace=@erp71/database -- --nodes=<id>` exists to fix. Either reset the setting (discarding whatever customisation was intended) or strip the dead ids and re-save. Worth also making the resolver log when it discards a layout, since today that is completely silent. Found 2026-07-28 while adding the CRM Sources & Categories sidebar entry.

- [ ] Build customer editing — there is no way to edit an existing customer anywhere in the UI, only create one (`AddCustomerModal` has no edit counterpart, and `api.updateCustomer` is called from nowhere in the frontend despite the backend fully supporting it). Found 2026-07-29 while adding a birthday field to the customer create form for the CRM follow-ups birthday trigger: the field could only be added to *creation*, so no existing customer can ever pick up a birthday, and any other data-entry mistake on a customer record is uncorrectable short of a database edit.
- [ ] Run `npm run sync:nav-layout --workspace=@erp71/database -- --nodes=crm.contacts` after the CRM Contacts release deploys — the new sidebar entry only reaches tenants whose saved `tenant_layout` predates it via that script. Currently masked by the stale platform layout described above (an invalid saved layout falls back to the code default, which already has the node), so this is insurance for the moment that setting is repaired.
- [ ] Extend contacts beyond the address book — no custom fields (`CustomFieldEntity` still has only `LEAD`), no conversation/follow-up log, and no "promote to lead" or "link to customer" action, so a scanned card is currently a dead end you have to re-key into a lead by hand. The Lead ⇄ Contact link is the one worth doing first: the business-card scanner's most common real use is meeting a prospect, and today that means creating the contact and then typing the same nine fields into `/crm/leads/new`. Found 2026-07-30 while building the contacts module.
- [ ] Notify on follow-ups going overdue, not just on auto-creation — `crm-follow-ups.service.ts`'s birthday/reorder crons now send an in-app notification the moment they create a follow-up, so the two automated triggers are never created into a void. A manually-created follow-up (GENERAL/COLLECTION, from the customer or lead detail page) still gets none — it only surfaces if someone opens the CRM hub or the follow-ups list. A daily due-today digest (mirroring the low-stock alert cron's dedup-by-day pattern in `notifications.service.ts`) would close this; deliberately left out of the 2026-07-29 rename/fix pass to keep it reviewable.

### Project Management Phase 3 — Trello-style task cards (scoped 2026-08-02; 3A + 3B landed 2026-08-03)

Full scope, decisions and sequencing: `docs/projects/project-management-phase-3.md`.

**Both open questions were decided 2026-08-03.** (1) **Board columns are per project** — 3L is the literal Trello translation, not the tenant-wide-plus-overlay middle path I had recommended. The constraint that must survive it: every per-project column still has to carry a TODO/IN_PROGRESS/DONE category, because Phase 2's tenant-level sprints and the Tasks page filter across projects on `statusCategory`, not on column identity. (2) **Single assignee stands** — no `ProjectTaskMember`, 3G deferred until someone asks for two people on a card.

- [x] **3A — board cards open, and say something** — done 2026-08-03, see COMPLETED
- [x] **3B — checklist UI** — done 2026-08-03, see COMPLETED
- [x] **3M + 3N — inline add-card, filters, and pointer-based dragging that works on a phone** — done 2026-08-03, see COMPLETED
- [ ] **3E — subtasks on the card. POSTPONED on request 2026-08-03, not cancelled.** `parent_task_id` exists and `findOne` already returns subtasks with their statuses; nothing displays them. The card badge for it shipped with 3A, so only the detail-panel section is outstanding.
- [x] **3F + 3H — labels and dates (one migration)** — done 2026-08-03, see COMPLETED
- [ ] **Audit the `@IsOptional()` + `''` clearing pattern across every PATCH DTO.** Found while making a task's due date clearable: `@IsOptional()` skips only `null` and `undefined`, so an empty string falls through to `@IsDateString()`/`@IsUUID()` and 400s — while PATCH reads `undefined` as "leave alone", so `''` is the only way to express "remove this". Without a `@ValidateIf` the field simply cannot be cleared. `UpdateTaskDto`'s date fields are fixed, and its `assigneeId`/`assigneeEmployeeId`/`milestoneId`/`sprintId` were fixed the same way on 2026-08-08 while making the card's assignee editable. **`UpdateProjectDto` almost certainly has the same bug**: it declares `@IsOptional() @IsUUID() customerId`, and the Phase 2 project edit form deliberately sends `''` to clear a customer link, which by this reasoning returns 400 rather than clearing. Not verified against a running server — worth 10 minutes with curl before assuming either way, then a sweep of the other optional-UUID/date PATCH fields.
- [ ] **`Field` does not associate its label with its control unless given `htmlFor`.** The wrapper renders a bare `<label htmlFor={undefined}>`, so a `Field`-wrapped input has no accessible name — screen readers announce it as unlabelled, and `getByLabelText` cannot find it in tests (which is how this surfaced). The new task date inputs pass `htmlFor` + `id`; roughly every other `Field` call site in the app does not. Fix is mechanical but wide: either thread ids through, or have `Field` generate one with `useId()` and clone the child, which fixes every call site at once and is the better trade.
- [x] **3C + 3I — comments, activity feed and watchers** — done 2026-08-03, see COMPLETED
- [ ] **`@mentions` in task comments.** 3I built the notification path (watchers fan out to the in-app `Notification` table), so a mention is now just parsing `@name` out of the body and adding those users as recipients. Deliberately left out of 3C to keep it reviewable.
- [ ] **3L — per-project board columns + WIP limits**, per decision 1 above. `ProjectTaskStatus` is `@@unique([tenant_id, name])` with no `project_id` today. Carries a data migration: give every existing project a copy of the current tenant column set so nothing moves on the day it ships, then let boards diverge. Keep `category` mandatory on each column — see the constraint above.
- [ ] **3D — task attachments. Has a prerequisite outside this phase.** `ProjectAttachment` has a model and no API. Building the upload path means building it on `uploadBuffer()` + a `storage_key` column — i.e. doing the Cloudinary leak fix logged at the top of this file, not extra scope. Doing it the old way deepens a liability already written down. Also the module's first tenant-supplied file surface: type allow-list + size cap needed.
- [ ] **3J + 3K — archive, then cover colour.** `deleted_at` soft-deletes but nothing can un-delete and no screen lists archived tasks; Trello's archive is reversible and users assume it. Cover colour is cosmetic and belongs last.
- [ ] **An estimate change leaves no trace in the activity feed either — same enum, same migration as the description item above.** Making the estimate editable on the card (2026-08-08) means `estimate_hours` can now move from the UI, and `recordUpdateActivity` has no branch for it because `ProjectTaskActivityType` has no value for it. The remaining-hours history covers *remaining*, not the estimate, so a revised estimate is currently invisible. Fold `ESTIMATE_CHANGED` in alongside `DESCRIPTION_CHANGED` when the next Projects migration is taken — one enum value, one block in `recordUpdateActivity` with `{from, to}`, one i18n line in each of en/bn/ms.
- [ ] **`assigneeId` is never checked against the tenant.** `UpdateTaskDto`/`CreateTaskDto` validate it as a UUID and the service writes it straight to `assignee_id`; nothing confirms the user is in this tenant or the employee in this workspace, so a hand-made request can park a task on a stranger. Pre-existing since Phase 1 and not widened by the card's new picker (which only offers the project roster), but it is now a field with a UI, which makes it likelier to be poked at. Fix is an `assertAssignee` beside `assertStatus`/`assertSprint`: `tenantUser` for the user id, `employee` scoped by `tenant_id` for the employee id, on both create and update.
- [ ] **A description edit leaves no trace in the activity feed.** `ProjectTaskActivityType` has `RENAMED` but nothing for the description, so retitling a task shows in the timeline and rewriting its whole description does not. Adding a value to that enum is a migration, which is why it was left out of the in-place-editing change; worth folding into the next Projects migration rather than taking one on its own. `recordUpdateActivity` is already shaped for it — one more block beside the title one, with no before/after text in `data` (a 5,000-character diff does not belong in an audit row; "changed the description" is the whole signal).
- [ ] **The board's inline add-card still creates title-only tasks.** The New Task modal on the project page now takes a description; the column composer on the board deliberately does not, since its whole point is typing a title and moving on. If that turns out to be the path people actually create tasks by, the description will have to be reachable from it — most likely by opening the new card's panel straight after creating it rather than by growing the composer.

- [x] **3L — per-project board columns + WIP limits** — done 2026-08-03, see COMPLETED
- [x] **3D — task attachments, on `uploadBuffer()` + `storage_key`** — done 2026-08-03, see COMPLETED
- [x] **3K — cover colour** — done 2026-08-03, see COMPLETED
- [ ] **3J — archive. The last item of the phase.** `deleted_at` soft-deletes but nothing can un-delete and no screen lists archived tasks; Trello's archive is reversible and users assume it.
- [ ] **3G — card members (plural)** — **deferred by decision, not dropped.** Revisit only on a real request.
- [ ] **`ProjectTasksService.move()` re-numbers the whole column one `UPDATE` at a time** inside a transaction on every drag. Fine at 20 cards, not at 200, and two people dragging in the same column will contend — and 3M/3N add filters and inline creation, which makes columns longer. Batch the renumber or switch to fractional ordering while touching this code; not worth a phase on its own. (The new checklist reorder endpoint already takes the batched shape — `$transaction` over an array of updates — so there is a pattern to copy.)
- [ ] **Watch the first board load per project after the 3L deploy.** Production applies the schema with `prisma db push`, never `migrate deploy` (`apps/backend/Dockerfile`), so `20260803160000_per_project_board_columns` — the phase's only data migration — **will not run there**. The backfill it describes is done at runtime instead: `seedProjectColumns` adopts a project's tasks off the tenant template, and `board()` self-heals if it still finds an orphan. That is tested and mutation-checked, but the *production* shape it defends against has not been exercised. After deploying, open one board per tenant and confirm the cards are there; then check `SELECT count(*) FROM project_tasks t JOIN project_task_statuses s ON s.id = t.status_id WHERE s.project_id IS NULL` returns 0. A non-zero count means a board somewhere is rendering empty.
- [ ] **The migration/`db push` split is a standing trap, not a projects problem.** Every migration file in `prisma/migrations/` is documentation as far as production is concerned — the schema is applied by diff, and any migration that *moves* data silently does nothing. Phase 3L is the second time this has bitten (the conversation-channel backfill was the first, logged in the #445 release entry). Worth either switching the container to `migrate deploy`, or writing down in `CLAUDE.md` that data migrations must be written as idempotent boot-time syncs — there are already four `sync:*` scripts doing exactly that.
- [ ] **Open the board on a real phone, and the rest in a browser.** Everything shipped so far is verified by unit tests, typecheck, lint and `next build` only. What jsdom cannot exercise is exactly what is most likely to be wrong: (a) whether the **grip is big enough to hit with a thumb** and whether a touch drag over a *scrolling* column behaves — the column is `touch-pan-y` and the grip `touch-none`, which is the right theory and untested against a real finger; (b) whether the drag ghost tracks acceptably on a mid-range Android; (c) whether click-to-edit on a checklist item survives a touch keyboard, where blur fires on every scroll. Also worth a look: dragging a card to a column that is off-screen needs **edge auto-scroll**, which is not implemented — on a narrow screen you currently have to scroll first, then drag.

### Cross-project Boards — follow-ups (2026-08-09)

Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-09-cross-project-boards-design.md`. The feature itself is complete and reviewed (`## COMPLETED` below); these are gaps the review rounds surfaced but didn't block shipping.

- [ ] **Production will not show the Boards nav item until a script runs.** `NavigationService.resolveTenantSidebarLayout` returns a tenant's *saved* sidebar layout verbatim — it never merges in new entries from `NAV_REGISTRY`. Any tenant (or the platform-admin layout) that has ever customised its sidebar through the nav admin screen has a saved layout frozen before Boards existed, so the new menu item is invisible there with no error, even though the code fully supports it. Fix: after the Boards deploy, SSH to the VPS and run `cd packages/database && npx tsx prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=projects.boards` (drop `--dry-run` once the printed diff looks right) against production. This is a deploy step, not something to run from a laptop against prod.
- [ ] **A board has no "create a new task here" path — only "attach an existing one."** The old per-project board had an inline per-column composer that created a title-only task straight into that column; it was deleted because it depended on a single project id and a real status id, and a cross-project board has neither by default (a column can map to many projects' statuses, or none). `AddBoardTasksModal` (`apps/frontend/src/components/projects/AddBoardTasksModal.tsx`) only lets you search for and attach tasks that already exist somewhere. Someone who wants to jot down a new to-do while looking at a board today has to leave it, go create the task on a project page, then come back and attach it. Needs a "new task" flow that asks which project it belongs to (and, once picked, which status/column) before creating — the same missing-context problem the old composer's removal was about, just needing an explicit answer instead of an implicit one.
- [ ] **Neither boards nor columns can be reordered by hand.** The boards list (`BoardsService.list`, `apps/backend/src/projects/boards.service.ts:39`) is fixed at `created_at desc` — newest board always on top, no way to pin or reorder. Columns have a `sort_order` field, but the only way to change it is opening board settings and editing each column's row one at a time; there's no drag-and-drop. Both need a drag-reorder UI backed by a reorder endpoint (the checklist-item reorder endpoint added earlier in the Projects work is a batched-update pattern to copy from).
- [ ] **Dead locale keys left over from deleting the old per-project board page.** In `apps/frontend/src/lib/localization/messages/{en,bn,ms}/projects.ts`: `t.projects.tabs.board` (the old page's sidebar tab label) now has zero references anywhere in the frontend. Inside the `board` block, the keys `title`, `kanban`, `scrum`, `allTasks`, `noSprint`, `createFailed`, `addCard`, and `newCardPlaceholder` are also unreferenced (`moveFailed` is no longer dead — the cross-project board's own failed-drop toast now reuses it, see the final fix wave, 2026-08-09) — they belonged to the old page's kanban/scrum toggle and inline card composer, neither of which the new cross-project board has. The block can't be deleted wholesale, though: `emptyColumn`, `noMatches`, `columnTotal`, and the whole `filters.*` and `card.*` sub-objects are actively read by the new board detail page (`apps/frontend/src/app/(app)/projects/boards/[id]/page.tsx`). Needs a careful line-by-line prune across all three locales, not a block delete.
- [ ] **Board settings saves inconsistently on the same page.** On the board columns/settings screen (`apps/frontend/src/app/(app)/projects/boards/[id]/columns/page.tsx`), editing a column's name, category, or WIP limit stages the change locally and requires clicking an explicit Save button (`saveColumn`, around line 347). Right below it, the per-project status-binding dropdowns in the same column's row save immediately on every selection change with no button (`setBinding`, line 231, wired to the `<Select onChange>` at line 412). Same page, two different mental models for "did my change take effect yet" — worth picking one pattern and applying it to both.
- [ ] **A board cannot be renamed.** `api.updateBoard` exists and `PATCH /projects/boards/:id` is live and works, but nothing in the frontend calls it — there is no rename UI anywhere. A board's name and description are effectively write-once, set at creation and fixed forever after. (This supersedes a narrower known issue that a board's description specifically can never be cleared back to NULL once set, because `UpdateBoardDto.description` is `@IsOptional() @IsString()` — `null` fails the `@IsString()` check and `undefined` is skipped by `@IsOptional()`, so there is no value that means "clear it". A rename UI needs to fix that validation too, not just add a form.)
- [ ] **A task in a deactivated status is permanently stuck in Unsorted.** `BoardColumnsService.bindProject` only binds *active* statuses (`listTaskStatuses(tenantId, false, projectId)`), but the board settings page's per-column status dropdowns list every status on the project, including inactive ones. Deactivating a status that already has tasks in it does not move or rebind anything, so those tasks' cards sit in Unsorted from then on — nothing auto-binds a deactivated status, and the only way out is binding it by hand in board settings.
- [ ] **`setBindings` will accept a tenant-template status that no binding can ever resolve.** `BoardColumnsService.setBindings` validates a status id only against `tenant_id`, not against `project_id`, so a `ProjectTaskStatus` row with `project_id = null` (a tenant-wide template row, not a real per-project status) can be saved as a column binding. `resolveStatusId` matches bindings by `project_id`, so a binding to a template row can never match a task's actual status — it just sits there doing nothing. Not reachable from the current UI (the settings page only offers per-project statuses), so low priority, but the service method itself has no guard against it.
- [ ] **Board reads are unpaginated, and every card drop re-fetches the whole board three times over.** `assertBoard` is duplicated byte-for-byte in both `boards.service.ts` and `board-columns.service.ts` instead of shared. A single card drop (`BoardsService.moveCard`) calls it via `assertBoard`, then again inside `listColumns`, then again inside the closing `findOne` — three separate `board.findFirst` queries for one drag. Both `moveCard` and `addTasks` end by returning the *entire* board — every card on it, the six-way `CARD_TASK_INCLUDE`, no pagination — just to reflect one card's change back to the client that already has the rest of the board in memory.
- [ ] CRM campaigns: unsubscribe link and suppression list for uploaded email lists
- [ ] CRM campaigns: open/click tracking for email campaigns
- [ ] Local dev DB is schema-drifted (missing `User.google_id`, `User.tenant_id`) — login 500s and 69 backend integration tests fail. Needs a `prisma db push` reset or a fresh seed.

- [x] **Saving an edited Lead 400s when the lead already has a next step** — same `forbidNonWhitelisted` trap as the R2 conversation form. R1 dropped `next_step` / `next_step_date` / `next_step_assigned_to` from `UpdateLeadDto` (they are a read-only rollup of the earliest PLANNED `CrmActivity`), but `leadFormToPayload()` still sent them on every edit. The global pipe rejects the whole PATCH, so changing name/status/etc. on a lead that already had a next step failed with `property next_step should not exist`. Fix (branch `fix/lead-save-next-step-payload`): `leadFormToPayload(form, { mode: 'update' })` omits those keys; the edit form hides the next-step inputs (`showNextStep={false}`) because reschedule is through the activity panel. Create still sends them so a new lead can open with a planned activity. Tests: payload unit test (RED then GREEN), detail-page save test (counterfactual RED without the page change), `UpdateLeadDto` pipe contract. — done 2026-08-18
- [x] Unify tenant Support Chat + Feedback into one composer and one thread per knock — shop users send Help / Bug / Feature / Other from `/support` or the header button; help stays conversation-only; feedback types still create a `Feedback` row so automation is unchanged, and admins can reply in the same chat. Admin inbox is `/admin/support` (type filter + Actions panel); `/admin/feedback` redirects. Feature flags stay separate: the surface shows if either is on. Tests: support.util/service 19, SupportComposer 8, Sidebar/catalog/nav-resolver green. Local DB was empty/drifted so the authenticated UI was not browser-driven this session — done 2026-08-18
- [x] **CRM activity unification R2 — cutover** — every screen moves onto `CrmActivity`; legacy tables and endpoints are untouched and merely unreferenced, which is what starts R3's seven-day clock. **No schema change in this release.**

  **It fixes a live production bug R1 shipped.** The lead detail conversation form still sent `next_step` / `next_step_date` / `next_step_assigned_to`, which R1 removed from `CreateLeadConversationDto`; the global `ValidationPipe` runs `forbidNonWhitelisted: true`, so since the #499 deploy **logging a conversation with a next step filled in returned 400** (surfaced as a raw `alert`). Without a next step it still worked, which is why nobody noticed. R2 deletes that form. It also closes the other R1 gap: the in-app notification for cron-created activities linked to `/crm/activities`, a route that did not exist until now.

  **One panel replaces three.** `CrmActivityPanel` takes over from `FollowUpPanel`, the lead detail conversation list and the customer detail interaction list — three components over three tables that could not refer to each other. Planned (due ascending, overdue in `red-600`) and History (completed descending), with *Schedule* and *Log activity*. Completing opens a `ModalShell` with channel/summary/outcome plus a **"Schedule the next one"** block that is present by default and skippable — the closed loop is the entire reason the tables were merged. The `next` object is omitted rather than sent half-filled, since the API validates the nested shape and would 400.

  **Nav nodes are repointed, not replaced.** `resolveTenantSidebarLayout` returns a saved layout verbatim, so a new `crm.activities` id would have left every tenant who customised their sidebar with **no entry at all** — precisely the trap the `crm.setup` rename hit, which is *still* an open TODO because its sync was never run. Both existing nodes now point at `/crm/activities` under one "Activities" label, and `crm.conversations` leaves the default layout so a fresh tenant sees one item. **No `sync-nav-layout` run is needed for this release** — that was the point of doing it this way.

  **The dashboard was a backend change, not a frontend one.** The CRM dashboard read `crmFollowUp` and `leadConversation` directly in `crm-dashboard.service.ts`, not through the summary endpoint. Its due-today and overdue cards now count `PLANNED` activities, which is what finally makes a lead's next step visible there — the fourth of the four bugs the design set out to fix. Both dashboard blocks read one table now, so they need filters that keep them measuring different things: `subject` non-null is planned work, `channel_id` non-null is a touch that actually went out; they overlap where a planned call was completed and logged, which is the closed loop counted once in each. Lead scoring also moves to counting `DONE` activities, matching `CrmActivitiesService.rescoreLead` — the two must agree or a lead's score would depend on which path last touched it.

  **One R1 backfill gap fixed, and it had to be now.** The spec requires a `PENDING` follow-up on an already-`CONVERTED`/`LOST` lead to backfill as `CANCELLED`, not `PLANNED`, or the merge re-imports the stale-overdue bug it exists to fix. R1 shipped without it, and `skipDuplicates` never revisits an already-mirrored row, so it lands as a **correction pass** that repairs what R1 already wrote. R3 drops the source tables, which would freeze any backfill error permanently — so this was the last release in which it could be fixed. Verified against a local Postgres: 2 cancelled on the first run, 0 on the second, closed leads left with empty rollups.

  Smaller things: `/crm/activities` replaces `/crm/follow-ups` and `/crm/conversations`, both kept as redirects for saved bookmarks (the `/sales/crm/tasks` precedent); CRM Setup gains an Activity Purposes tab (the backend kind already existed, so `CrmListPanel` just needed to treat purposes like channels); the customer page's AI Draft button moves off `purple-600` onto the one-accent rule while it was being rewritten; both AI drafters now seed the panel's log dialog through a `draft` prop rather than writing into forms that no longer exist, so the feature is preserved rather than dropped; `FollowUpPanel` deleted as orphaned. All new copy in en/bn/ms, parity-checked by key count.

  **Verified:** backend 233/237 suites (the 4 failures are the documented integration suites needing a seeded live Postgres, none referencing CRM code); frontend **229/229 suites, 2531 tests** green, including 7 new `CrmActivityPanel` tests covering the closed loop, the half-filled-`next` omission, target scoping and draft seeding; `nest build`, `next build` and both typechecks clean; `next lint` 0 errors; `/crm/activities` emitted in the build with the two legacy routes now small redirect stubs. **Deployed** with PR #505, merge `383464e3`. **Not done: nothing here has been opened in a browser** — these are new screens and jsdom cannot show whether the complete dialog fits at 360px or whether the two-section panel reads right against real data — built 2026-08-18

- [x] **Brand logo assets wired into the frontend — favicon, PWA icons, social card and an in-app brand mark** — the logo artwork had been dropped into `apps/frontend/public/logo/` and referenced by nothing. **Three things were outright broken, not merely missing:** `manifest.ts` pointed its PWA icons at `/icon-192.png` and `/icon-512.png`, neither of which existed, so installing the app produced an iconless launcher; `layout.tsx` declared `manifest: '/manifest.json'` while Next's `app/manifest.ts` serves at `/manifest.webmanifest`, a second 404 (the property is now simply absent — Next injects the correct link itself); and there was no favicon and no `og:image` anywhere, so every marketing, blog and careers link shared to Facebook or WhatsApp rendered bare. The generator dump `favicon_io/` was also mode `700`, which would have failed a Docker build running as non-root. Assets are now flattened into Next's file conventions — `src/app/favicon.ico`, `src/app/apple-icon.png`, and `public/logo/icon-{192,512}.png` — with `fabicon.png` (a typo duplicate) and `site.webmanifest` (which would have fought `manifest.ts`) deleted. The 512 is declared twice, `any` and `maskable`, because Android crops an icon with no maskable entry into a circle and clips the mark. **The social card is generated, not checked in:** `app/opengraph-image.tsx` composes 1200×630 from the lockup and `BRAND_TAGLINE` via `ImageResponse` at build time, so a logo change cannot leave a stale card behind, and `app/twitter-image.tsx` re-exports it because Twitter needs its own tag rather than inheriting `og:image`. `metadataBase` is set from the same `siteOrigin()` the sitemap and robots.txt use — without it Next resolved the card against `http://localhost:3000` and every shared link would have pointed at an unfetchable image. **`components/BrandLogo.tsx` is the single door to the artwork** (lockup/mark × default/reverse, width derived from the 2600×1024 and 1024×1024 aspect ratios), replacing the wordmark-as-text in `MarketingNav`, `MarketingFooter`, `CareersShell`, login, signup and the `Sidebar` fallback — the same reason brand strings live in `lib/brand.ts`. It renders through `next/image` with `unoptimized`: serving SVG through the optimizer needs `dangerouslyAllowSVG` globally, which is not a trade worth making while tenants can supply their own `brand_logo_url`. **Tenant branding still wins everywhere it applies** — the sidebar shows an uploaded `logoUrl` and `businessName` first, and the ERP71 mark only fills the un-branded case. **Two design corrections came out of actually looking at the rendered pages:** the mark is outline artwork that turns to mush reversed out small, so the login/signup blue tile was replaced by the full lockup at 40px, and the sidebar's coloured tile is now dropped in the fallback case in favour of the default-tone mark at 28px (the tile is kept for tenant logos). The three now-dead `brand: 'ERP71'` i18n keys were removed from all three locales. **Verified:** frontend suite 227 suites / 2539 tests green including 9 new `BrandLogo` tests and 3 new `Sidebar` branding-fallback tests; production build clean with the `metadataBase` warning gone; generated OG card confirmed a valid 1200×630 PNG and inspected visually; login, home, pricing and careers loaded in a headless browser with every `/logo/*` image resolving at the expected size (remaining console errors are `localhost:4000` backend calls, unrelated). **Fixed in passing:** `Sidebar.test.tsx` mocked `@/lib/branding` with `{ virtual: true }`, which registers the mock under the literal specifier and left the component importing the real hook — the mock had been a no-op, and its defaults happened to match, which is why nobody noticed. **Not deployed** — done 2026-08-15

- [x] **CRM activity unification R1 — expand + backfill** — plan at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-12-crm-activity-unification-r1.md`, spec at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-crm-activity-unification-design.md`. Ships the `CrmActivity` table, its purpose taxonomy, the module that owns it and a re-runnable backfill, so the new model becomes the write path while every legacy table and endpoint keeps working untouched. **Additive only, and that is the release gate, not a nicety:** production reconciles schema with `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` on container start (`apps/backend/Dockerfile`) and never runs the migrations directory, so a `DROP` or `ALTER COLUMN` in the diff would destroy data on the next restart. `prisma migrate diff` against `origin/main` emits exactly **2 `CREATE TABLE`, 3 `ADD COLUMN`, zero `DROP`, zero `ALTER COLUMN`** — checked at the start and re-checked against the final tree.

  **`status`, not a second table, separates planned from logged.** One `CrmActivity` row is polymorphic over lead and customer (nullable `lead_id`/`customer_id`, exactly one set, asserted on every write path) and carries `PLANNED` / `DONE` / `CANCELLED`. `complete()` is why the merge exists: marking done, recording what happened, stamping `last_contacted_at` and optionally scheduling the next activity are now one transaction, where before they were two writes to two tables with nothing linking them. The next activity inherits the closed one's purpose and assignee — chasing the same invoice is still a `COLLECTION`. Completing an already-`DONE` activity is a 400 rather than a no-op, so a double-submitted form cannot create two "next" activities for one completion.
