  **`Lead.next_step*` became a read-only rollup with exactly one writer class.** `CrmActivitiesService.recalculateRollup()` owns all five columns (`next_step`, `next_step_date`, `next_step_assigned_to`, `next_activity_id`, and `Customer.next_activity_id`/`next_activity_date`), and every hand-written path was closed: dropped from `UpdateLeadDto` and from the conversations DTO, and stripped in `mapLeadData` as well — `forbidNonWhitelisted` strips unknown keys *before* the DTO, not after, so removing the field alone would not have stopped a stale client. The columns keep their names deliberately, so the leads list, sort allowlist, `myActionsToday` filter, lead scoring and `ai/chat-data.service` needed no changes. Two documented exceptions write them inline rather than through the service, both to avoid closing an import cycle (`CrmActivitiesModule` → `CrmLeadsModule` → `CrmActivitiesModule`): lead create and the CSV importer materialise an opening `next_step` as a `PLANNED` activity and derive the rollup from it, and lead close clears all four. The grep the plan specifies for "no unexpected writer" comes back clean — every remaining hit is a read, a sort key, a scoring input, or one of those two.

  **Rollup ordering has a trap worth naming:** `NULLS LAST` is not expressible in a Prisma `orderBy` shorthand, so an undated activity would sort ahead of a dated one and become the "next step". `recalculateRollup` asks for dated rows first and only falls back to any planned row, covered by a test for each branch.

  **Purposes joined the existing generic taxonomy machinery** rather than getting their own module — `LeadTaxonomyKind.PURPOSE` inherits the service, controller and settings API for free. Two consequences the plan called for and one it did not: the `CHANNEL` consumer was repointed from `leadConversation` to `crmActivity` (the backfill mirrors every conversation into an activity ahead of the API starting, so activities are the superset, and counting the old table would let an in-use channel be deleted); and **the plan's version of that change would have 500'd on channel deletion** — `CrmActivity`'s denormalised column is `channel_code`, not `type`, and more importantly `LeadConversation.channel_id` is `onDelete: Restrict` with its rows kept through R2, so reassigning only the activities leaves the FK to block the delete. Channel removal now moves both tables and counts `max(activities, legacy conversations)` so a partially-backfilled tenant still gets the friendly reassign prompt instead of a raw foreign-key error.

  **The two daily crons moved onto `CrmActivity`** (birthday, reorder-reminder), keeping the `$queryRaw` `EXTRACT` birthday query, `REORDER_DORMANT_DAYS = 60` and the null-tolerant dormancy `OR` exactly as they were — those carry hard-won reasoning. Two changes on top of a straight port, both because these sweeps run across every tenant on the platform: the purpose is resolved **once per tenant** rather than once per customer, and a tenant whose purposes were never seeded is skipped with a warning rather than throwing and taking the whole run down. Cron rows still notify the tenant owner when they have no assignee, which a literal port to `notifyAssignee` would have silently dropped.

  **The backfill is idempotent by construction**, not by filtering: `@@unique([tenant_id, legacy_source, legacy_id])` plus `createMany({ skipDuplicates: true })`, with Postgres treating NULLs as distinct so the index constrains only backfilled rows and never touches user-created ones. `Lead.next_step` is materialised **only** where it is not already a same-day duplicate of a mirrored follow-up — that duplication is the whole reason for the migration, and importing it anyway would manufacture a second copy of one task on day one — and never for a converted or lost lead, whose next step is stale by definition. Unmatched channel codes keep `channel_code` and leave `channel_id` null, so nothing is silently lost and the residual is reported. The rollup pass runs only when something was actually mirrored, so a settled tenant does not get its whole lead and customer base rewritten on every container start. It catches its own errors and exits zero: it sits in the Dockerfile `&&` chain ahead of `node main.js`, where a non-zero exit is a full outage rather than a failed script.

  **Verified against a real Postgres, not only mocks.** Stood up a local server, pushed the schema, seeded a tenant with three lead conversations, two customer interactions (one carrying a deliberately unmatchable channel code), three follow-ups, an open lead with a next step, a lost lead with a stale one, and a lead whose next step duplicates a same-day follow-up. First run mirrored 9 rows, materialised 1 next step and skipped 1 as a same-day duplicate; **three consecutive runs left the count at 9**; `--dry-run` wrote nothing. Inspected the resulting rollups directly in SQL: the duplicate-lead's rollup points at the earlier follow-up rather than its own next step, and the lost lead's stale next step was cleared. Backend suite **233 of 237 suites green** — the 4 failures are the documented `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites that need a seeded live Postgres, and none of the four reference CRM code. `nest build` clean, `prisma validate` clean.

  **Not done, and not defects:** no frontend change (R2), no legacy table or endpoint removed (R3), and **the backfill has never run against production data** — the fixture was hand-built, so real tenants' interaction types and next-step text have not been through it. A restore-a-prod-dump rehearsal is the honest gate before this deploys; it is filed above — done 2026-08-15

  **Deployed (PR #499, merge commit `7166a712`).** Released without the prod-dump rehearsal the entry above calls the honest gate — deployed on explicit instruction, with the risk accepted rather than retired. The pre-merge gate that *was* run is the one that matters most: `prisma migrate diff` from `main` to `dev` emitted exactly **2 `CREATE TABLE`, 3 `ADD COLUMN`, zero `DROP` and zero `ALTER COLUMN`**, which is what makes the container's `db push --accept-data-loss` safe. Backend suite at the documented baseline (233/237 suites, 3967/4036 tests; the 4 failures are the known `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites), `nest build` clean, `prisma validate` clean. **Verified against production:** VPS `HEAD` and `/api/v1/health` both report `7166a712` with `db: ok`, frontend 200.

  **The backfill has now run against production data, and its numbers check out.** `sync:lead-taxonomy` seeded `GENERAL`/`COLLECTION`/`BIRTHDAY`/`REORDER_REMINDER` for all 21 tenants, then `sync:crm-activities` mirrored **115 rows across 1 of 21 tenants** — only one tenant has CRM data at all. By source: 56 `LEAD_CONVERSATION`, 7 `CRM_FOLLOW_UP`, 52 `LEAD_NEXT_STEP`, with **4 `next_step`s skipped as same-day duplicates** (the `shouldMaterialiseNextStep` dedup doing exactly the job it exists for) and 57 rollups recalculated. Queried the live database directly to confirm rather than trusting the log: 57 `DONE` + 58 `PLANNED` = 115, the per-source counts reconcile against it (56 conversations + 1 completed follow-up = 57 `DONE`; 52 next-steps + 6 pending follow-ups = 58 `PLANNED`), and the `CrmActivity_tenant_id_legacy_source_legacy_id_key` unique index is present — that index plus `skipDuplicates` is the whole idempotency mechanism, so its existence in prod is the load-bearing check.

  **Still open.** Idempotency has been *proven by construction, not observed on prod* — the second run happens on the next container restart, and nobody has compared row counts across two runs against real data. No screen reads any of this yet (R2), so the 115 rows are invisible to users and none of the new endpoints has been exercised against real tenant data — the write path is live but untravelled. The single-tenant footprint also means the backfill's harder cases (unresolvable channel codes, multi-tenant batching) got no exercise here despite 21 tenants existing — verified against production 2026-08-15

- [x] **Blog home restructured around a lead story, borderless cards and a closing CTA band** — asked as "take inspiration from this design, however our color and font-style should remain as is", against a reference (The Founder's Folio) whose look is carried by a serif face and a dark-green accent. Both were the parts explicitly held back, so what was taken is the **layout**: a split lead story (cover left, headline/excerpt/byline right) above a rule, a section header with a right-hand feed link, a three-up grid, and a full-width conversion band at the foot. Everything visual stayed on the existing system — `font-sans` (Inter/Noto Sans Bengali), `blue-600` as the only accent, the gray text ramp — and the `text-5xl md:text-6xl font-black tracking-tighter` masthead was left untouched rather than re-scaled, since "font-style as is" is the whole constraint. **The lead story needed no new data:** `BlogListPost` already carries `author_name`, `author_title` and `featured`, and `blog.service.ts:99` already orders `[{featured: desc}, {published_at: desc}]`, so `rows[0]` is the editor's pick when there is one and the newest post otherwise — `FeaturedPost` takes a post rather than looking one up, and the index promotes it on **page 1 only** (on page 2+ the top row is just whatever fell there). The byline avatar is a two-letter monogram because there is no author image column and a stock silhouette is worse than initials. **`PostCard` lost its border box** — the cover image is now the card edge and the text sits on the page, which is what makes the reference's grid read as an index rather than a row of outlined rectangles; the meta line keeps `mt-auto` so dates still align across a row despite ragged excerpts. That card is shared with `/blog/category/[slug]`, which was checked in a browser and inherits it cleanly. **`BlogSubscribeBand` deliberately omits the email form the reference has there** — there is no subscriber model and no subscribe endpoint anywhere in the backend, so the field would have collected addresses and discarded them; it offers the two things that do exist (the RSS feed, a trial signup) and the gap is filed under HIGH PRIORITY. The eyebrow labels use `uppercase tracking-wide` at `font-semibold`, which is the sanctioned eyebrow recipe in `docs/ui-design-guidelines.md` §2.3 — not the deprecated `font-black uppercase tracking-widest` voice. **Verified in a real browser, not by inspection:** ran the actual page against a stub API on `:4000` serving seven posts with covers, and screenshotted `/blog` at 1280 and 390 and `/blog/category/operations` at 1280 — lead story, grid alignment, band and mobile stacking all confirmed, and `scrollWidth > innerWidth` measured **false** at both widths (no horizontal scroll at 390px). `tsc --noEmit` clean apart from the known pre-existing `contact/page.test.tsx` missing-module error; `next lint` 0 warnings on all four files; `npx jest src/components/blog` 4 suites / 28 tests green. **Not verified: never rendered against the production database** — every post in the check was fabricated, so real titles, excerpt lengths and cover aspect ratios have not been through the new grid, and no post on `app.erp71.com` currently has an `author_title` to prove the byline's second line — done 2026-08-13

  **Deployed (PR #497, merge commit `78f26125`).** All five required checks green on both trigger runs; E2E skipped as usual. The release carried only this blog work plus two docs-only TODO commits — the URL shortener changes were already on `main` from #495. **Verified against production:** VPS `HEAD` and `/api/v1/health` both report `78f26125` with `db: ok`, and `/blog` returns 200. **Partially closes the "never rendered against the production database" caveat above:** the live page now renders real posts through the new layout — the `Latest posts` section header, three `<article>` cards and six Cloudinary cover URLs are in the served HTML, and `BlogSubscribeBand` is there with its RSS and trial links and no email field, as designed. **Still not verified: nobody has looked at it in a browser since the deploy.** These are layout changes, so served markup is weak evidence — the lead-story promotion, the borderless card edges and mobile stacking against *real* titles and cover aspect ratios are exactly what HTML inspection cannot confirm — verified against production 2026-08-13

- [x] **URL shortener: interstitial removed, full short URL in the list, and per-click analytics captured** — three changes to the shortener, done together because they all touch the `/s/<code>` path.

  **1. The "You are leaving ERP71" interstitial is gone.** `/s/<code>` now 302s straight to an external target instead of bouncing through `apps/frontend/src/app/s/[code]/leaving/page.tsx` (deleted). A shortener that stops to ask is not a shortener — every link pasted into WhatsApp or an ad cost the recipient an extra tap on a page they did not ask for. **What replaces that safety is upstream, not gone:** only a signed-in user holding `MANAGE_SHORT_LINKS` can mint a link at all, and `isSafeTarget` still refuses non-http(s) schemes, private/loopback hosts, credentials-in-URL and our own auth pages. The route also re-checks the scheme locally before emitting an absolute `Location`, so a `kind: 'external'` claim carrying `javascript:` or `data:` still fails closed to `/not-found` rather than becoming script execution under our own domain. **Accept knowingly: `app.erp71.com/s/<code>` is now a silent redirector to whatever an authorised user shortened** — that is inherent to a shortener, and it is what was asked for.

  **2. The first column shows the complete URL** (`https://app.erp71.com/s/<code>`), not the bare `/s/<code>` path. Reading a code out to someone, or selecting the row text by hand, previously produced something that goes nowhere — the copy button was the only working exit from that screen. The origin comes from `window.location.origin` rather than an env var, because that is by definition the domain the user reached these links through and it is the same value the clipboard has always copied; it is held in state and set in an effect since `window` does not exist during the server render, falling back to the bare path until it resolves so no wrong domain ever flashes.

  **3. Every click is now recorded with its context, not just counted.** New `ShortLinkClick` table (migration `20260813090000_add_short_link_clicks`) storing referrer + referrer_host, a channel bucket, all five utm_* tags plus the raw query string, user_agent + browser/os/device_type/is_bot, ip_address, country/city and language. `click_count` stays as the denormalised counter the list renders, so no report has to `COUNT(*)` over a table that grows without bound. **The context has to travel explicitly:** `/s/[code]` is a server-side route handler, so its fetch to the API carries none of the visitor's headers — they go in the request body, except the IP, which is forwarded as `X-Forwarded-For` so the API reads it the same way it does for every other request instead of trusting a body field. Derived columns sit beside the raw values they came from so parsing can be improved and re-run later. **The whole tracking write is swallowed on failure and logged** — someone is mid-redirect, and a tracking error must cost a row of analytics, never their destination; that was already the route's stated intent and is now actually implemented (the counter update used to be able to 500 a resolvable link). Channel classification deliberately lets a recognised referrer host outrank `utm_medium`, since the other order files a Facebook ad tagged `utm_medium=cpc` under SEARCH.

  **Verified:** backend `src/short-links` 5 suites / 166 tests green, including 40 new `click-context.spec.ts` cases (channel buckets, substring-vs-label host matching, iPad-is-not-macOS, Edge-is-not-Chrome, bot flagging, bounds and control-character stripping) and service tests pinning that a failed click write still returns the destination. Frontend `src/app/s` + `src/components/short-links` 6 suites / 84 tests green, with new cases for the direct external redirect, the `javascript:`/`data:`/protocol-relative rejections, and the forwarded visitor context. Both typechecks at their known pre-existing baselines. **Not verified: nothing has been run against a real browser or a live database** — the click table has never been written to, so the first real click is the first check that the schema and the Prisma write agree — done 2026-08-13

  **Deployed (PR #495, merge commit `21fe9209`).** All five required checks green on both trigger runs; E2E skipped as usual (label-gated). **Verified against production:** VPS `HEAD` and `/api/v1/health` both report `21fe9209` with `db: ok`. The `ShortLinkClick` table exists in the live database with all 23 columns, its three indexes and the cascade FK to `ShortLink` — the container's `db push` created it, which was the main open risk. The running backend carries `dist/src/short-links/click-context.js` with `buildClickContext` and `shortLinkClick.create` wired into `recordClick`, and the frontend image has no `leaving` route left. Live smoke test: `/s/<unknown>` 302s to `/not-found`, and `/s/<code>/leaving` now 404s. **Still open: no click has been recorded end to end.** Proving that needs a real click on a real link, and reading the link table on the VPS was blocked, so the first genuine visit is still the first check that a row actually lands — verified against production 2026-08-13

- [x] **Facebook pushes were rejected for not declaring a post type** — with the mode fixed, Buffer accepted the mutation and rejected the post on its own rules: `Buffer rejected the post: Invalid post: Facebook posts require a type (post, story, or reel).` — a `MutationError` union member, not a GraphQL error, so the transport was finally working. `FacebookPostMetadataInput.type` is `PostTypeFacebook!` (`post | reel | story`), and nothing was sending it. `createPost` now takes the channel's `service` and attaches `metadata: { facebook: { type: 'post' } }` for Facebook only; `post` is the ordinary feed post, and reel/story would need composer support since their asset rules differ. **LinkedIn and Twitter are deliberately absent from the map** — neither has a required `type`, and sending metadata Buffer did not ask for is its own way to be rejected. **Instagram is deliberately absent too:** its metadata needs `shouldShareToFeed` as well, which changes where the post appears, so it is left to whoever connects an Instagram channel and can test it — filed under FOLLOW-UPS. Types read off `developers.buffer.com/types/*`, not guessed. Also corrected a `schema.prisma` comment that still called `socialMediaPostPush.mode` "Buffer's `mode` verbatim", which stopped being true with the ShareMode translation (doc comment only — no migration). **Verified:** three new `buffer.service.spec.ts` tests — Facebook metadata, case-insensitive service matching, and no metadata for LinkedIn/Twitter — the first confirmed red first (`Received: undefined`); `src/social-media/` 3/3 suites / 49 tests green, typecheck at its 4-error baseline. **Not verified: no post has been published end to end yet, and the Facebook `type` value has not been exercised against the live API** — done 2026-08-12

  **Deployed (PR #493, merge commit `66230cf8`).** All five required checks green on both trigger runs; E2E skipped as usual. The PR was opened as the docs-only TODO note and retitled when the Facebook fix landed on `dev`, so it carried both. **Verified against production:** VPS `HEAD` and `/api/v1/health` both report `66230cf8` with `db: ok`, and the running container's `dist/src/social-media/buffer.service.js` holds `REQUIRED_POST_TYPE = { facebook: 'post' }` at line 30 with the `payload.metadata` assignment at line 116. **Open:** whether the LinkedIn and Twitter pushes from the ShareMode round actually published is unknown — reading `social_media_post_pushes` on the VPS was blocked, so only the push history in the UI can say, and re-pushing a channel that already sent would duplicate the post — verified against production 2026-08-12

- [x] **Push to Buffer failed on every channel — `mode` was not a `ShareMode` value** — with the channel list working, pushing a post failed three for three with `Variable "$input" got invalid value "now" at "input.mode"; Value "now" does not exist in "ShareMode" enum.` Buffer's enum is `addToQueue | shareNext | shareNow | customScheduled`; `BufferPostMode` used `now` and `next` for two of the four, so **Publish now and First-slot pushes could never have worked** — only the two modes that happen to spell the same, Next-free-slot and At-an-exact-time, ever reached Buffer. The wire value is now translated by a `SHARE_MODE` map in `buffer.service.ts` **rather than renaming `BufferPostMode`**, because that constant is not just an internal name: `BUFFER_POST_MODES` is the DTO's `@IsIn` allow-list (renaming breaks the API contract), the value is persisted on `socialMediaPostPush.mode` (existing rows would keep the old spelling), and the composer's dropdown builds its labels from `p.modes[value]` in three locales (renaming orphans the keys). Translating at the boundary keeps Buffer's vocabulary a Buffer detail. **The enum came from Buffer's schema reference, not from guessing** — `developers.buffer.com/reference.html`, which also confirms `mode: ShareMode!` and that the `assets`/`tagIds`/`needsApproval` non-nulls carry defaults, consistent with the push reporting `mode` as its only coercion error. **Verified:** two new `buffer.service.spec.ts` tests confirmed red first (`Expected "shareNow", Received "now"`) — one per differing mode, plus one pinning the two pass-through modes so a future edit cannot quietly rewrite them — `src/social-media/` 3/3 suites / 46 tests green, typecheck at its 4-error baseline. **Not verified: no post has actually been published through Buffer yet** — done 2026-08-12

  **Deployed (PR #492, merge commit `056541a6`).** All five required checks green before merge across both trigger runs; E2E skipped as usual (opt-in via the `run-e2e` label). **Verified against production:** VPS `HEAD` and `/api/v1/health` both report `056541a6`, `db: ok`, and the running container's compiled `dist/src/social-media/buffer.service.js:24-29` carries the `SHARE_MODE` map with `shareNow` / `shareNext`. **Still open, and only a real push can close it:** no post has been published end to end. Retrying the three failed pushes will publish for real to the live LinkedIn, Twitter and Facebook accounts — the earlier failures published nothing, so nothing is duplicated by retrying — verified against production 2026-08-12

- [x] **Buffer → Test connection was failing on a GraphQL type mismatch** — Platform Settings → Buffer → Test connection returned `Buffer request failed: Variable "$organizationId" of type "String!" used in position expecting type "OrganizationId!"`. `POST /admin/social-media/buffer/test` runs `BufferService.listChannels()`, whose `CHANNELS_QUERY` declared `query Channels($organizationId: String!)`. Buffer types that argument as the **custom scalar `OrganizationId!`**, and GraphQL validates variable declarations by exact type with no coercion between distinct scalars — so the document was rejected at validation time, before execution, even though the value being sent is an ordinary string. One-line fix: the declaration is now `$organizationId: OrganizationId!`. **This broke every read path, not just the test button** — `listChannels` also backs `GET buffer/channels` and the channel picker in the composer; `createPost` was unaffected since it declares `$input: CreatePostInput!`. **`request()` now reports every GraphQL error rather than `errors[0]`** — validation fails once per bad position, so surfacing only the first hides the rest and costs a deploy per mismatch to find them; the messages are joined with ` | `. **Verified:** two new `buffer.service.spec.ts` tests, both confirmed red against the old code first, and `src/social-media/` is 3/3 suites / 44 tests green with backend typecheck at its known 4-error baseline (accounting/billing specs, unchanged from HEAD). **Not verified: not exercised against the live Buffer API** — the tests stub `fetch`, so only Buffer itself can confirm the scalar name is right; re-run Test connection to close that out — done 2026-08-12

  **Deployed (PR #491, merge commit `00713892`).** All six required checks green before merge; E2E skipped, since `deploy.yaml:121` runs it only for PRs carrying the `run-e2e` label. The release also carried the two docs-only CrmActivity commits (spec + R1 plan, no code). **Verified against production rather than assumed:** VPS `HEAD` and `/api/v1/health` both report `00713892`, and the running container's compiled `dist/src/social-media/buffer.service.js` line 25 reads `query Channels($organizationId: OrganizationId!)`. **Still not closed out: the live Buffer round trip.** Auth is checked before validation on Buffer's side — an unauthenticated probe of both spellings returns `UNAUTHENTICATED` either way — so nothing short of pressing Test connection with the real token proves the scalar name. If it errors again the message now lists *every* mismatch at once — verified against production 2026-08-12

  **Closed out 2026-08-12:** the Push to Buffer composer now lists all three live channels (`nayeemahmad`/linkedin, `nayeem_ahmad`/twitter, `Engr. Nayeem Ahmad`/facebook), which only `listChannels` can populate. `OrganizationId!` was the right scalar and the read path is confirmed working against the real Buffer API

- [x] **HR sidebar restructured from eleven flat links into four groups** — HR was the last large module hanging every link straight off its module node, while Sales, Accounting and Inventory all group theirs. Now: Overview and Employees at the top, then **Attendance & Leave** (attendance records, in/out records, leaves), **Payroll** (salary payments), **Recruitment** (job posts, applicants, applications) and **HR Setup** (departments, designations, calendar & schedules). `NAV_REGISTRY` had defined `hr.organization` / `hr.operations` / `hr.recruitment` since before the module was built, but **no layout node ever placed them**, so all three were inert and the sidebar had never rendered an HR subgroup; the first two are now retired and the ids renamed to match their new parent. **Nav-only — every href is unchanged**, so `/hr/employees/departments` keeps its URL under HR Setup and no redirect is needed. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-hr-menu-restructure-design.md`. **Two live defects fixed in passing:** `/hr/attendance/punches` had no `NAV_REGISTRY` entry at all — the page was reachable only from the HR hub, in no menu, since it shipped in PR #485 — and `hr.operations.schedules` asked for a `CalendarDays` icon that was missing from `NAV_ICON_MAP`, so `resolveNavIcon` had been silently returning the `LayoutDashboard` fallback. `hr.attendance.records` carries `exact: true`, or the sidebar's prefix-matching `isActive` lights it up alongside In/Out Records. The HR hub (`hr/page.tsx`) was regrouped to the same five sections — it had been a *third* structure agreeing with neither the sidebar nor itself — and gained the Calendar & Schedules tile it never had. New copy in en/bn/ms: four `hr.hub.*` group labels, `hub.links.schedules`, and `sidebar.items.attendanceRecords` / `attendancePunches` (a child labelled "Attendance" inside a group labelled "Attendance & Leave" reads as a mistake, so `sidebar.items.attendance` was left alone rather than reused). **Why the ids were renamed rather than just reparented:** `validateNavLayout` rejects unknown ids and `parseNavLayoutJson` then serves the code default, so a rename *pushes* every tenant onto the new tree — whereas keeping the ids leaves a saved layout valid and returned verbatim, i.e. flat HR forever, with no way to repair it (`addNavNodesToLayout` appends, it cannot reparent). That only works while production's platform `tenant_layout` is still invalid and discarded at read time, which is the OPERATIONAL item above. **Verified:** frontend 226/226 suites / 2518 tests green, backend `src/` 224/224 suites / 3796 tests green (the four `test/*.spec.ts` DB-backed e2e suites fail locally without a database, as they do in CI where three are quarantined), backend navigation suites 20/20, `next lint` clean, frontend typecheck at its known baseline (the pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` import in `contact/page.test.tsx`), and `validateNavLayout` valid for both default layouts with no HR layout node missing from the registry. The two new `nav-resolver` tests were confirmed red against the old structure before turning green. **Not verified: not opened in a running browser** — done 2026-08-11

  **Deployed (PR #490, merge commit `49d9419f`).** All five required checks green before merge; E2E skipped, and worth recording correctly because the previous entry phrased it backwards — E2E is **opt-in**, `deploy.yaml:121` runs it only when a PR carries the `run-e2e` label, so no label is needed to skip it. The release shipped the two HR commits **plus five CRM profile-photo commits** (spec, plan, `photo_url`/`photo_storage_key` on `Lead` and `CrmContact`, the `parseImageUpload` move to `common/image-upload.util`, and `POST /crm/photos`), deliberately half-wired: the code attaching a photo to a contact was still uncommitted and stayed behind, so the endpoint and columns are live but unreachable from the UI. **Verified against production rather than assumed:** VPS `HEAD` and `/api/v1/health` both report `49d9419f`; `/`, `/hr`, `/hr/attendance/punches` and `/hr/schedules` all 200 — the punches route being the one that had never been in a menu; boot-time `db push` reported "in sync" in 313ms and all four `photo_*` columns exist on `Lead` and `CrmContact` (nullable, so the #473 required-column failure mode never applied); and the saved platform `tenant_layout` is **still** 138 nodes carrying the same dead `accounting.transactions*` / `admin.tenants` ids and no `hr.*` group node, so it is still discarded at read time and every tenant is served the code default with the new HR tree. **Three releases have now depended on that bug being present** — verified against production 2026-08-11

- [x] **The gross-profit reports were unreachable — wired into the sidebar and the Sales hub** — the five reports shipped in PR #471 on 2026-08-07 with backend, permissions, i18n and four working pages, and **no link to any of them**. `NAV_REGISTRY` in `packages/shared-types/navigation.ts` is what the sidebar is built from, and nothing gross-profit was ever registered, so the pages existed only for whoever typed the URL — on `dev` and on `main`, in production, for four days. Four new registry entries under the existing `sales.reports` subgroup (by product, by salesperson, margin exceptions, margin bridge), matching their siblings' `advancedOnly: true`, plus the same four as tiles in the Sales hub's Reports section, plus `sidebar.items.*` and `hub.links.*` copy in en/bn/ms reusing the page titles already translated in `sales.ts`. The by-product entry carries `exact: true` — the other three sit under its href and the sidebar's `isActive` falls back to a prefix match, so without it all four highlight at once. **The dead Cost Coverage route was dropped:** `routes.sales.reports.grossProfitCoverage` pointed at `/sales/reports/gross-profit/coverage`, a page that was never built, and the by-product page linked to it — a 404 one click from the report. The link and the route constant are gone, and `coverageFix` ("Open Cost Coverage to see which products need a cost") was reworded in all three locales since it was directing people at the same missing screen; the backend endpoint and `api.getCostCoverage` are left in place with a follow-up filed. **Tenants with a saved nav layout will not see these until the sync script runs** — `resolveTenantSidebarLayout` returns a saved layout verbatim and never merges the registry into it, so `cd packages/database && npm run sync:nav-layout -- --nodes=sales.reports.gross-profit,sales.reports.gross-profit-salespeople,sales.reports.gross-profit-exceptions,sales.reports.gross-profit-bridge` is part of this deploy (dry-run first; re-running is a no-op). **Verified:** frontend 226/226 suites / 2516 tests green, shared-types 33/33, backend navigation suites 20/20, `next lint` clean, frontend typecheck at its known baseline (the pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` import in `contact/page.test.tsx`), and `validateNavLayout(DEFAULT_TENANT_NAV_LAYOUT)` valid with the four nodes resolving to the right hrefs in the right order. **Not verified: not opened in a running browser** — done 2026-08-11

  **Deployed (PR #489, merge commit `7404f362`).** All five required checks green before merge (Build, Lint, Dependency Audit, Test Backend, Test Frontend; E2E skipped by the labelled-PR rule). `deploy.sh main` rebuilt both images, VPS `HEAD` is `7404f362`, `/` and `/sales/reports/gross-profit` both 200. No migrations in this batch. **The nav sync script turned out not to be needed, and it is worth writing down why rather than rediscovering it:** the platform `tenant_layout` in `platform_settings` is *still* invalid — 138 nodes, six stale ids (`accounting.transactions*`, `admin.tenants`) left over from removals that predate this — so `parseNavLayoutJson` fails validation and serves the code default at read time, which is where the four new entries live. The script would have refused to write anyway: merging into an already-invalid layout leaves it invalid, and it declines rather than persist something that would be silently discarded on read. The one row in `TenantNavLayout` has `layout = null`, i.e. pinned to default, so there is no per-tenant override to sync either. **This is still luck, not design** — the moment someone saves a valid layout through the nav admin, every registry addition after it becomes invisible and `npm run sync:nav-layout` becomes mandatory. Filed under OPERATIONAL: repair the stale platform layout, which currently masks that failure mode — verified against production 2026-08-11

- [x] **Deployed the HR batch to production (PR #488, commit `d09c80f2`)** — merged 10 commits from `dev` to `main`, 84 files, ~12,100 insertions, covering four features: the careers portal (#487), HR recruitment (#486), employee in/out records (#485), and whole-year holiday management. CI on the release PR was green on all five required checks before merging (Build, Lint, Dependency Audit, Test Backend, Test Frontend; E2E skipped by the labelled-PR rule).

  **The migration files in this batch never ran, and that is the normal path here — worth writing down because it keeps getting rediscovered.** Production has **no `_prisma_migrations` table**; the backend container's `CMD` ends with `prisma db push --skip-generate --accept-data-loss`, so the schema is derived from `schema.prisma` on every boot and the `migrations/*/migration.sql` files are **inert on the deploy path**. They still matter for local work and for reading intent, but "apply this via the migration path, not `db push`" is not an option that exists on this VPS — the only pre-deploy question that can be asked is the #473 one: *does this batch add a required column to a populated table?* This batch does not. Six of the seven objects are new, empty tables (`attendance_punches`, `job_posts`, `applicants`, `job_applications`, `job_application_events`, `job_seekers`); the one change to a populated table is `User.applicant_token_version INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, which `db push` accepts because the default backfills it. Push reported `Your database is now in sync with your Prisma schema. Done in 439ms`.

  **Two migrations in this batch share an identical timestamp prefix** — `20260811090000_add_attendance_punches` and `20260811090000_add_recruitment`, from #485 and #486 landing the same day. Harmless in practice twice over: Prisma orders by full directory name so the suffixes break the tie deterministically, the two create disjoint table sets with no cross-references, and on this deploy path neither file executes anyway. It is still the same smell that `fix(db): renumber the labels migration` fixed once before, and it will bite whoever first runs `migrate deploy` against a fresh database — **renumbering the recruitment one is filed under OPERATIONAL**.

  **Verified:** VPS `HEAD` at `d09c80f2` matching the merge commit; all three containers up (`erp71-db-1` healthy, backend and frontend recreated and started); `Nest application successfully started` with **zero** lines matching `error|failed` in the boot log; all six new tables and `User.applicant_token_version` present in `retail_saas`; the new routes live and mapped — 5 under `/api/v1/attendance/punches`, 10 under `/api/v1/careers/*` (auth, public board, portal), and the `/api/v1/hr/recruitment/*` set; `app.erp71.com` `/api/v1/health`, `/`, and the new public `/careers` board all 200. **Not verified:** nothing was clicked through in a browser, and no applicant signup or job application was exercised end to end against production — the careers portal's claim logic (the four `resolveApplicant` branches) is covered by unit tests only — done 2026-08-11

- [x] **Careers portal — one job-seeker login, spanning every company on the platform.** Asked as "a basic portal for job applicants; one login for each applicant allowing to handle all job applications to various companies". Built as the **applicant-facing half of the recruitment module that landed the same day** (PR #486, the entry below): a public board at `/careers` listing vacancies from every workspace, sign-up/sign-in, and `/careers/portal` where one person sees every application they have sent, to any company, with its stage and progress. Nothing was duplicated — applying writes an ordinary `Applicant` + `JobApplication` pair inside the hiring tenant, so a careers-board candidate turns up on that workspace's existing recruitment screens with no import step.

  **The identity is the interesting part, and it is the first of its kind here.** Every other portal — employee, referee, investor — belongs to exactly one workspace. A `JobSeeker` belongs to **none**: it hangs off `User`, is unique per user, and holds the profile and CV that get snapshotted into each company's `Applicant` row at apply time. That makes the token **scope**, not permissions, the boundary that matters, so it is closed in both directions: `CareersJwtGuard` refuses anything not `applicant`-scoped, and **`JwtAuthGuard` was rewritten from a deny-list to an allow-list** — its previous form named `storefront` explicitly and would have admitted applicant tokens to the entire ERP API. Sessions revoke independently through a third `User.applicant_token_version`; a password change bumps all three.

  **Claiming is where the care went.** `Applicant` is unique on `[tenant_id, phone]`, so when a workspace has already typed in a walk-in candidate with the same number, creating a second row is not untidy — it is impossible, and the row has to be reused. Reusing it is also a way to hand somebody another person's interview history, because a phone number typed into a signup form proves nothing. So a claim requires the platform to have **verified** the matching identifier (`email_verified_at` or `mobile_verified_at`); when it cannot and the number is taken, the application is refused with a message saying how to fix it, rather than guessed at. Four explicit branches in `resolveApplicant`, each tested.

  **What the candidate never sees:** `JobApplication.notes`, `rating`, `rejection_reason`, `source`, `hired_employee_id`, and the `JobApplicationEvent.note` an interviewer writes. The portal's timeline is stage and date only. Enforced by an allow-list `select` rather than by omission, so a column added to the model later is not published by default.

  **Two additive changes to the hiring side, both deliberate.** `JobPost.publish_to_board` (default false) — OPEN already meant "we are hiring" before a public board existed, and reusing it would have advertised every existing OPEN post to the internet on deploy, so advertising is a per-post tick-box with its own copy in en/bn/ms. And `JobApplication.cover_letter`, kept out of the workspace's editable `notes` so an interviewer cannot overwrite the candidate's own words or be mistaken for their author; it renders read-only in the stage modal, which is the screen where somebody decides whether to advance them.

  **Verified:** 82 new backend tests (25 careers service — board predicate, the ownership walk through `Applicant.user_id`, all four claim branches, the private-column allow-list; 15 security — both guard directions, token-only resolution, the public board treating a non-applicant token as anonymous; 14 DTO; plus 2 pinning `publish_to_board` on the hiring side). Backend **228 suites / 3819 green**, frontend **226 / 2516 green**, locale parity intact across en/bn/ms, both typechecks at their pre-existing baselines, `next build` clean with this branch's 7 careers routes and #486's 4 recruitment routes coexisting. Migration `20260811120000_add_careers_portal` is additive only.

  **Not done, deliberately:** the `/careers` pages are **English-only** — they are a standalone public surface outside the `(app)` shell, and wiring ~70 strings through three locales is worth its own pass rather than inventing translations here. **Nothing has been clicked through in a browser** — no database on this machine, so every screen is pinned by unit tests and a production build only. **Nobody is emailed at any point.** This branch's first attempt built a parallel recruitment module before #486 merged; it was discarded and rebuilt on #486's models rather than kept alongside, because two applicant tables that never talk to each other is worse than either one alone — done 2026-08-11

- [x] **HR → Recruitment: job posts, applicants and applications.** Asked as "add an option to manage job posts, applicants, applications", which supersedes the "recruitment is out of scope" line the HRIS plan carried. **Three tables, not one, because they have three different lifetimes:** a `JobPost` outlives everyone who applies to it, an `Applicant` outlives any one application, and a `JobApplication` is the only thing that moves. The consequence worth stating is that somebody who applies twice in two years is **one applicant with two applications** — the interviewer sees the earlier round instead of re-discovering it — which is why `Applicant` is keyed on `@@unique([tenant_id, phone])` and why creating an application from a phone number already on file reuses that row rather than colliding on the index. A fourth table, `JobApplicationEvent`, is append-only: `JobApplication.stage` is where a candidate is now, the events are how they got there, which is what an unsuccessful candidate asking "why" needs answered. **The one decision that constrains everything else: `HIRED` is not reachable through the stage endpoint.** A hire creates an employee record, and letting the stage be set without one would leave the two halves of the same event disagreeing — so `POST /applications/:id/hire` does both together, going through `EmployeesService.create()` (module now exports it) rather than writing the `Employee` row locally, so employee-code generation, phone uniqueness and the payroll-visibility rules stay in one place. The post is marked `FILLED` only when hires reach `openings`, not on the first hire: a post for three cashiers is still hiring after one. **Permissions are the existing `VIEW_HR`/`MANAGE_HR` pair, deliberately not a new one** — whoever runs employees runs hiring, and a new permission would have to be granted to every existing HR role before the module did anything for anyone. Guards on the hire endpoint match what creating an employee needs. Soft deletes throughout, with two refusals that are data-integrity rather than politeness: a post with live candidates cannot be deleted (close it instead), and a hired application cannot be removed at all, because it is the record of how that employee joined. Migration `20260811090000_add_recruitment` is hand-written and was **verified statement-for-statement identical to what `prisma migrate diff` generates**, then applied to a real Postgres built from the pre-change schema, after which `migrate diff --from-url` reported no drift. Frontend: `/hr/recruitment/{job-posts,applicants,applications}` plus a per-post pipeline view that groups candidates into stage columns — an empty OFFER column beside a full SCREENING one says something a date-sorted list does not. The applications list opens on live stages only, because the rejected pile grows without bound and is the one thing nobody opens that screen to see. Nav nodes `hr.recruitment.*` added to the registry and the default layout; i18n complete in en/bn/ms; user manual §9.4. **Verified:** backend `npx jest src/recruitment src/employees src/navigation` 6 suites / 116 tests green (24 new: code generation, the reopen-keeps-its-date rule, the salary-band guard, soft-deleted-applicant restore, duplicate refusals, the stage-log, rejection-reason clearing, HIRED-via-hire-only, the fill-at-headcount arithmetic, and the two delete refusals); frontend `npx jest src/lib src/app/(app)/hr` 38 suites / 642 tests green including 10 new page tests; locale parity 3/3; both typechecks at the known pre-existing baseline; lint 0 errors. Additionally smoke-tested against a live Postgres: grouped stage counts, both unique constraints biting with P2002, the hire link reading back through the detail include, and the tenant cascade taking all four tables with it. **Not done: never opened in a browser** — there is no running backend on this machine, so every screen is verified by unit test and typecheck only. **Deploy step: existing tenants with a customised sidebar need `npx tsx prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=hr.recruitment.job-posts,hr.recruitment.applicants,hr.recruitment.applications`** (see that script's header for why it is not run on boot) — done 2026-08-11
- [x] **Employee in/out records — attendance days are now derived from a punch log rather than being the only copy of what happened.** Asked as "add an option to manage employee in/out records; first in, last out should create/edit attendance for the day". `AttendanceRecord` holds exactly one `clock_in` and one `clock_out` per employee per day, which cannot describe a midday errand, a split shift, or a time typed at the wrong hour — and left nowhere to record *why* a figure was changed. New `AttendancePunch` (`attendance_punches`, migration `20260811090000_add_attendance_punches`) is the raw log: one row per tap, with `direction`, `source`, free-text `notes` and the geofence fields mirrored from the day row. `AttendancePunchService.rebuildDay()` re-derives the day from it on **every** write — **first IN becomes `clock_in`, last OUT becomes `clock_out`** — then runs the same `assessDay`/`deriveStatus` the portal check-in uses, so status, lateness, early leave, worked and overtime minutes all move with a correction. **The rule is a pure exported function (`derivePunchWindow`), not buried in the service**, because it is the whole feature and a database is a poor place to read a policy from. Three judgement calls worth stating: an OUT before the first IN is **ignored**, not used, since it belongs to the previous night's shift and taking it would give the day a negative span; a day of OUT punches with no IN gets **no clock-out at all** rather than a lone departure that reads as a day worked; and `rebuildDay` **refuses to clear a day it did not create** — a punchless day whose row is `ADMIN` (typed by hand) or `LEAVE` (written by an approval) survives untouched, so deleting one punch cannot silently erase an unrelated record. Moving a punch across midnight rebuilds **both** days, the one it left and the one it joined. Portal check-in/check-out now write punches too (`source: 'SELF'`, log first then summary, so a crash leaves recoverable evidence rather than a figure with nothing behind it) — without that, an employee who only ever taps the portal would show an empty in/out screen. `direction` is a plain string, not an enum, matching `AttendanceRecord.source` and costing no migration the first time a tenant wants `BREAK_START`. Frontend: `/hr/attendance/punches` with employee/date/direction filters, add-edit-delete, a stated explanation of the derivation rule on the page (it is not discoverable from the table), a confirm step before delete, `hideOnMobile` on the two secondary columns, and en/bn/ms keys. The punch time is sent as a **zone-less local wall-clock string** on purpose — schedules are stored as minutes from local midnight, so a zone suffix would shift every judgement against them. Reachable from the attendance page header, the HR hub, and the AI navigation list. **Verified:** 47 new tests — 30 on the punch service (the derivation rule including inverted and orphan punches, status/minute derivation, the delete-only-what-I-made guard, the cross-midnight double rebuild, tenant scoping on every mutation, page-size cap) and 8 on the page, plus a controller test pinning that `DELETE /attendance/punches/:id` reaches the punch service rather than being read as a day-record id by the `:id` route below it. Backend **224 unit suites green** (the 4 `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites still need a live database — pre-existing), frontend **224 suites / 2,499 green**, i18n parity green, both typechecks at their pre-existing baseline, lint clean on every new file. **Not done: never opened in a browser** — no dev database on this machine, so the derivation has not been watched happening against real rows, and the table has not been looked at on a phone — done 2026-08-11
- [x] **Manage holidays a year at a time — HR → Calendar & schedules.** Per-date holiday CRUD already existed; setting up a new year still meant thirty-odd trips through a two-field modal, which is why the calendar tends to stay empty and every attendance calculation quietly runs against a working-day baseline. Three whole-year operations added behind `MANAGE_HR`: **`POST /hr/holidays/bulk`** (up to 366 dates in one call — a date that already has a holiday is *skipped*, not rejected, so re-running an import is a no-op rather than a 409 that discards the other 40 rows; `overwrite: true` turns those skips into renames, and a name that is already correct counts as skipped rather than updated); **`POST /hr/holidays/copy-year`** (same day of the month, not same weekday — 29 February is reported back as `unmapped` rather than slid onto the 28th, which would invent a holiday nobody declared); **`DELETE /hr/holidays/year/:year`**. Reading the new **`GET /hr/holidays/suggestions?year=`** needs only `VIEW_HR`; it returns the six Bangladesh holidays that fall on a fixed Gregorian date (21 Feb, 26 Mar, 14 Apr, 1 May, 16 Dec, 25 Dec), each flagged with whether the tenant already has it. **Deliberately fixed-date only** — the Eids, Durga Puja and Buddha Purnima are lunar and announced year by year, so a table shipped in code would be wrong within a year and silently wrong after; dates that successive governments have added and removed (17 Mar, 15 Aug, 7 Nov) are left out for the same reason. The UI hint says so rather than leaving the gap to be discovered. Frontend: a **Manage year** button on the holidays tab opens a three-tab modal (Suggested / Copy a year / Clear year) with the missing suggestions pre-ticked, a holiday count beside the year picker, and a batch summary toast that reports created/renamed/unchanged instead of a bare "saved". The year picker is now **anchored on today** (±2) instead of on the selected year, which used to let you walk to 1997 one click at a time with no way back. Copy shares the bulk path, so its skip/overwrite semantics are identical by construction. **Verified:** 202 backend tests green across `work-schedules`/`attendance`/`hr-dashboard` (18 new, covering skip-vs-overwrite, unchanged-name accounting, duplicate dates within one payload, midnight-UTC storage, the leap-day case, year-range rejection and tenant-scoped clearing) and 56 frontend tests green including 7 new page cases; `tsc --noEmit` clean on both apps (the 5 remaining backend errors and 1 frontend error are pre-existing, in `accounting`/`billing`/`short-links` specs and a missing `@testing-library/user-event` dev dep); catalog parity holds across en/bn/ms. **Not verified: never opened in a browser** — this container has no database, so no request has actually reached Postgres and the modal has only been exercised through jsdom — done 2026-08-11
- [x] **Blog and blog-post typography now match the marketing site — including the font itself, which was the actual complaint.** Asked as "on Blog page and Blog Details the font look different; make the font size, style same as the home page". Two separate causes, and the first was invisible from the source: **the blog was rendering in Times, not Inter.** Every page's `html`/`body` computes to the UA serif (see the standing item under HIGH PRIORITY for why — the font variables sit on `<body>` while preflight's `font-family` sits on `<html>`), and Inter only arrives through an explicit `font-sans` class that 18 files carry and the three blog pages did not. Adding `font-sans text-gray-900` to the blog wrappers — the same string `HomeClient`, `PricingClient` and `ContactClient` already use — is what fixed the "different font"; the root-cause fix was left out deliberately because it would restyle the tenant storefront and `/demo` in the same deploy. **Second, the scale was the `(app)` compact density rather than the marketing display scale**: h1 `text-3xl font-bold` → `text-5xl md:text-6xl font-black tracking-tighter` on the index and category pages and `text-4xl md:text-5xl` on a post, lead copy `text-sm leading-7` → `text-lg leading-relaxed`, `PostCard` titles `text-sm font-semibold` → `text-lg font-bold` with `text-sm` excerpts. **`ArticleMarkdown` gained a `variant` prop instead of being enlarged in place**, because it is shared by three surfaces that do not agree on a type scale: the marketing post, a shop's storefront, and `(app)/whats-new` — which lives inside the app shell where the compact-density UI rule applies. `compact` is the **default**, so the storefront and the in-app page render byte-identical and cannot be enlarged by someone adding a caller; only `/blog/[slug]` passes `variant="article"` (h2 `text-2xl`, h3 `text-xl`, body `text-base leading-8`). The two class maps are built once at module scope rather than per render. **Verified:** frontend 219 suites / 2462 tests green including 5 new variant cases (compact default pins `text-sm leading-7`, article pins `text-base leading-8`, heading sizes both ways, and raw HTML still refused in the article variant — the safety posture is unchanged, still `skipHtml` with no `rehype-raw`); `next build` clean at 215 static pages; lint 0 errors. Measured in a headless browser against the production build: `/blog` h1 is now **Inter 60px/900/-3px** against home's **Inter 72px/900/-3.6px** (same family, same weight, one step down the scale), lead copy 18px vs home's 20px, 0 console errors, and no horizontal scroll at 360px where the h1 drops to 48px. **Not verified locally: `PostCard` and the article body were never rendered with real content** — there is no backend on this machine, so `/blog` showed its empty state and `/blog/<slug>` its "not available" fallback; the card and prose classes are pinned by unit tests only, and want a look on production where real posts exist — done 2026-08-10
- [x] CRM: create an email campaign from a CSV/Excel upload with per-row subject and message, plus scheduled delivery, cancel and reschedule — done 2026-08-10 (not yet clicked through in a browser; the local dev DB is schema-drifted and cannot log in)
- [x] CRM campaign upload — final review fixes: cancelling an in-flight send now actually stops the drain and cannot be overwritten by a COMPLETED write; orphaned claims are recovered via `CrmCampaignRecipient.claimed_at` + a 15-minute stale threshold (migration `20260810090000_campaign_recipient_claimed_at`); SMS/WhatsApp segment recipients no longer carry an email, which the new `@@unique([campaign_id, email])` was silently deduping away; uploaded rows resolve in a fixed number of queries (three, still case-insensitive) instead of 3×N; upload creation is transactional; plus the UI minors (null message, DRAFT reschedule, explicit null unschedule, detail-fetch error toast, keyboard-reachable dropzone, recipient page ordering) — done 2026-08-10

- [x] Cross-project Boards — Boards is its own submenu under Project Management; a Board owns columns that bind to per-project `ProjectTaskStatus` rows, so one board holds hand-picked tasks from any number of projects. Per-project board route removed. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-09-cross-project-boards-design.md` — done 2026-08-09

- [x] **Mobile-number sign-in via Firebase phone auth** — new `POST /api/v1/auth/mobile` takes the Firebase ID token the browser gets after an SMS one-time code and resolves it in four steps: known `firebase_uid` → sign in; **exactly one** account carrying that E.164 number → adopt the Firebase identity onto it and stamp `mobile_verified_at` (so a password user switching to the SMS code doesn't get a second, empty workspace); **several** accounts carrying it → refused outright; nobody → `{ requires_signup: true, mobile }` with **nothing written**, and the page then posts the same token back with an email address to create the account. The multi-account refusal is the interesting case: `User.mobile` has never been unique here (`auth.service.ts` says so explicitly — one person may own several businesses), so unlike Google's email match there is no honest way to pick a row, and guessing would hand someone another tenant's workspace. **The email supplied at signup is never used to link an existing account** — the SMS code proves the number and nothing at all about the address, so linking on it would let anyone with a phone claim any account whose email they can guess; a taken address is a 409 instead. Unlike the Google path the address is *not* pre-verified, so a verification email goes out and `email_verified` stays false. 2FA is unaffected: TOTP accounts still get `requires_2fa` after the SMS step. **Token verification is hand-rolled against `node:crypto`** (`firebase-token.service.ts`), deliberately mirroring `google-token.service.ts` rather than pulling in `firebase-admin` — which is a very large dependency *and* would need a service-account key on the server, where verifying an ID token needs neither. It reads Google's `securetoken@system` keys in **JWK** form (same import path as the GSI keys), pins `alg: RS256` before touching the signature, and checks `iss`/`aud` against the configured project — the load-bearing check, since every Firebase project on earth is signed by the same Google key, so without it any project's token would verify. `auth_time` is required (a token minted before the sign-in is not from that sign-in) and `firebase.sign_in_provider` must be exactly `phone`, so an anonymous or email/password token — perfectly valid for this project — carries no proof of a number and is rejected. **Schema:** `firebase_uid` (nullable unique) and `mobile_verified_at`, plus a non-unique index on `mobile` for the lookup (migration `20260808120000_add_firebase_mobile_sign_in` — two nullable columns and an index, additive on a populated table, which is what `prisma db push` will accept). `/auth/me` gained `mobile_connected` and `mobile_verified` alongside `google_connected`. **Configured by two backend env vars**, `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` and `FIREBASE_API_KEY` (both public client identifiers, not secrets — Firebase's access control is the authorised-domains list, not key secrecy); the frontend reads them at runtime from `GET /auth/firebase/config` for the same reason as Google's, so enabling this is a backend restart rather than a frontend rebuild. Unset means the option never renders. **The Firebase JS SDK is loaded from `gstatic.com` on demand** (pinned 10.14.1 compat build) rather than added to `package.json`: it is only needed by the visitors who choose mobile sign-in, it keeps the config a runtime value, and it keeps ~150KB of auth SDK out of a frontend build that already spills into swap on a 1.9GB VPS. `MobileSignInPanel.tsx` owns the whole flow — number → code → session, plus the extra email step — so the login and signup pages stay thin; the signup page passes its own form fields in, so a filled-in form provisions the workspace in the same round trip. New `countryCodeFromE164()` in shared-types maps a verified number back to a country for `mobile_country_code` (longest dial prefix wins, so `+91` never shadows `+971`). **Verified:** 14 cases in `firebase-token.service.spec.ts` (valid token, foreign project by issuer and by audience, foreign signing key, `alg: none`, HS256 confusion, expired, future `iat`, future/missing `auth_time`, non-phone provider, missing and non-E.164 number, malformed without a JWKS fetch, cache hit + rotation, JWKS outage as 503), 10 in `auth.service.spec.ts` (requires_signup writes nothing, passwordless creation with the number verified and the email not, workspace provisioning, refusing a taken email, returning user by uid, adoption onto the single matching account, a number changed at Firebase following through, the multi-account refusal, 2FA still challenged, no DB touch on a bad token), 7 in `MobileSignInPanel.test.tsx` (hidden when unconfigured, silent on a failed config request, full send→verify→exchange, invalid number rejected before any SMS, the email step reusing the same token without a second SMS, signup fields carried through, a wrong code kept recoverable), 3 in `phone.test.ts`. Backend `npx jest src` 3322/3322 green, frontend `npx jest` 2413/2413 green, locale parity intact across en/bn/ms; both `tsc --noEmit` at their known pre-existing baselines. Setup guide in `docs/ops/mobile-sign-in-setup.md`. **Not done: nothing has run against Firebase** — no project configured, no SMS sent, no real token verified; see the open item under OPERATIONAL → Deployment — done 2026-08-08

- [x] **Board task cards name their project, backed by a new `Project.short_name`** — asked as "in board - task card, show the project name also. We need a short name new field for that." `short_name` is a nullable `String` on `projects` (migration `20260808120000_add_project_short_name`), optional by design: every existing project has one and only `code` to fall back on, so `projectLabelOf()` in `board-tasks.ts` resolves short name → code → name, and a whitespace-only value counts as unset rather than rendering a blank chip. The card shows it under the title as muted text with a `FolderKanban` glyph, the full project name in the `title` attribute and an `sr-only` "Project:" prefix (an `aria-label` on a `<p>` is not reliably announced), so it can never compete with the task title in an 18rem column. Backend: `shortName` on `CreateProjectDto`/`UpdateProjectDto` capped at 20 characters, written through create and update (`undefined` still means "leave alone", `''` clears it), added to `TASK_INCLUDE`'s project select so every task-shaped response carries it, and folded into the projects list search alongside code and name. Frontend: a Short name field beside Type in `ProjectForm` with a hint saying where it shows up, `maxLength={20}`; en/bn/ms all gained `fields.shortName`, `fields.shortNameHint` and `board.card.project`. 5 new tests — the two fallback rules and the null case on the helper, plus board-card rendering of the short name and of the code when none is set; frontend projects suites 8/189 green, backend `src/projects` 11/187 green, both apps typecheck at their existing baselines (the same 3 backend spec errors and the frontend `user-event` import). **Not done: not opened in a browser, and the sprint planning rows and Tasks list still print the project `code`** — they could use the short name too, but that changes columns nobody asked about — done 2026-08-08

- [x] **The task card is two columns now, the assignee and estimate are editable, and logging hours no longer needs a second form** — asked as three things: (1) task edit should allow changing assignee and estimate, (2) merge the "log hours" and "change remaining hours" forms, (3) give the card a right-hand column for the secondary fields, Trello-style. **(1) Neither field had a UI, and the backend needed one line for each.** `UpdateTaskDto` already took `estimateHours`, `assigneeId` and `assigneeEmployeeId`, and `update()` already wrote all three — but `assigneeId` was `@IsOptional() @IsUUID()`, so **unassigning was impossible**: PATCH reads `undefined` as "leave alone", so only `''` can mean "nobody", and the empty string fell through to `@IsUUID()` and 400'd. `ProjectTasksService` had stored `dto.assigneeId || null` since Phase 1 and `project-tasks.service.spec.ts` had a passing test for the unassignment path, so the service was ready for a value the API refused to accept. Added `@ValidateIf((_, v) => v !== '')` to `assigneeId`, `assigneeEmployeeId`, `milestoneId` and `sprintId` — the same fix the date fields already carried, and the next four items on the standing `@IsOptional()`-audit item below. **The assignee picker offers the project's roster, not every user in the workspace** (`getProject(projectId).members`, loaded separately from the task so a roster that cannot be read still leaves an openable card), which is also the only way an *employee with no login* is pickable — Phase 2 made them assignable and there has never been a screen that did it. Both assignee columns are sent on every change: PATCHing only the one that gained a value would leave a card holding a user *and* an employee. Whoever currently holds the card is kept in the list even if they have left the project, or the select would fall back to its first option and the card would read as assigned to somebody it is not. **The estimate saves on Enter or blur, not on change** — a save-on-change number input commits an estimate of `1` while you are typing `12` — and an emptied box puts the stored figure back rather than being read as zero, which would move the burndown without anyone asking. **(2) Hours and the revised remaining figure are one form.** They were two sections with two Save buttons and two notes: logging an afternoon *and* saying the job had grown was two round trips. Now one form takes hours, date, remaining-after and a note; with hours it calls `logProjectTime` (passing the remaining figure through, blank still meaning "accept the server's `max(0, remaining - hours)` suggestion"); **with no hours it PATCHes `remainingHours` + `remainingNote`, so a plain re-estimate is still possible** — the merge would otherwise have removed the only way to revise an estimate without inventing time to log. Save is disabled until one of the two is filled in. Backend untouched here: both paths already existed and already record the right `RemainingSource` (`TIME_LOGGED` when the figure is derived, `RE_ESTIMATED` when it is stated). **(3) The layout is Trello's**: description, checklist, attachments, the hours form, the time and remaining-hours histories and the activity feed in a wide left column; status, assignee, estimate, logged/remaining tiles, dates, labels and cover in a narrow right one, with the modal widened `lg` → `2xl` to hold both. **Placed with explicit `md:col-start`/`md:row-start` rather than by source order**, so the sidebar reads *first* on a phone — where status and assignee are what you open a card to change, and where burying them under the activity feed would have been worse than the single column it replaced — and on the right on a desktop. The dates card stacks its two inputs now that it lives in a 1/3-width column. Also added: the project code and name as the modal subtitle, and `htmlFor`/`id` on the fields the sidebar introduced (`Field` gives no accessible name without them — the standing item below). **Verified:** frontend 210 suites / 2426 tests green, 20 new on the panel (roster options including the no-login employees, the holder shown and kept after leaving the project, both assignee columns cleared in one PATCH, unassign as `''`, a roster failure leaving the card usable; estimate on Enter, on blur, unchanged not saved, emptied box restored, Escape discarding, a failed save restoring the server's figure; hours+remaining in one save, blank remaining left to the server, remaining-only as a re-estimate with no time entry, Save disabled until one is filled, the form cleared after a save, a failed log keeping it); new `project.dto.spec.ts` — 7 cases pinning the `''` clearing and that a non-UUID assignee is still rejected; backend `src/projects` 12 suites / 194 green. en/bn/ms catalog parity green (added `task.details`, `task.unassigned`, `task.saveFailed`, `time.logHint`; removed `remaining.note`, whose section no longer exists). Lint clean; both apps typecheck at their existing baselines. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — the two-column proportions and the sidebar-first order on a phone are exactly what jsdom cannot answer — done 2026-08-08

- [x] AI Assistant button on both blog post editors — a prompt fills every field for review; platform side unbilled, tenant side charges AI credits — done 2026-08-08

- [x] **Deployed Google sign-in and the blog release to production (PR #473, commit `2e180ccf`)** — merged 7 commits from `dev` to `main`, 118 files. **This deploy was clean, unlike #468 the day before**, and the reason is worth recording: the pre-deploy check was not "did CI pass" but "does this batch add a required column to a populated table", which is the specific thing `prisma db push` refuses. It does not — across both merged features the only change to an existing table is `User.passwordHash` NOT NULL → nullable (a *relaxation*) plus a new nullable-unique `User.google_id`; the other 8 tables are new and empty. `db push` reported `Your database is now in sync with your Prisma schema. Done in 438ms`. **That check is the reusable part** — it can be made from the schema diff alone, before deploying, and would have caught #468. **`GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` was set on the VPS before the deploy, not after**, so one build brought the feature up rather than a deploy plus a second restart — worth doing deliberately on a 1.9GB box where the frontend build already spills into swap. Backup at `.env.production.bak.20260808`. The OAuth client lives in Google Cloud project `erp71-709cf`, which was **created through the Firebase console** — a Firebase project *is* a GCP project, but a Firebase web app config (`apiKey`/`appId`/`messagingSenderId`) contains **no OAuth client id**, and Firebase Auth ID tokens would be rejected outright by `google-token.service.ts` anyway (`iss: securetoken.google.com/...`, `aud: erp71-709cf` fail the issuer and audience checks). The web OAuth client had to be created separately under APIs & Services → Credentials. **Verified:** backend up with no restart loop and `Nest application successfully started`; `/api/v1/health` → `{status: ok, db: ok, commit: 2e180ccf}` matching the merge commit; `/api/v1/auth/google/config` → `enabled: true` with the expected client id; `app.erp71.com/login`, `/blog`, `/whats-new`, `/blog/rss.xml`, `/sitemap.xml` all 200; `sync:role-permissions` granted the new blog permissions — `blog: granted 63 permission(s) across 21 role(s) and 36 member/store pair(s)` (21 tenants × VIEW/MANAGE/PUBLISH), the other five sync steps no-ops. A headless browser load of `/login` reported **0 console errors, 0 failed requests**, the GSI client script present and one `accounts.google.com` iframe mounted with the button rendered. **Not verified: no sign-in has been completed with a real Google account** — the button renders and the config is served, but the round trip (Google consent → ID token → `POST /auth/google` → session) has never run, so the consent-screen publish state and the authorised-origins list are still unexercised in the one way that would prove them. Also unexercised: no authenticated request was made, and no blog post has been authored, published or read through the new admin UI — route mounting and public page rendering are all that was checked — done 2026-08-08

- [x] **Google Sign-In on the login and signup pages** — new `POST /api/v1/auth/google` takes a Google Identity Services ID token and resolves it in three steps: known `google_id` → sign in; existing ERP71 account with the same address → link Google to it (so a password user switching to the button doesn't get a second, empty workspace); nobody → create the account. **The linking step is only safe because the token is rejected unless Google reports `email_verified: true`** — an unverified address is one the Google account holder never proved they own, and honouring it would let them claim someone else's workspace. An address that has moved to a *different* Google account is refused outright rather than relinked. 2FA is unaffected: Google proves identity, not possession of the second factor, so a TOTP account still gets the `requires_2fa` challenge after the Google step. **Token verification is hand-rolled against `node:crypto`** (`google-token.service.ts`) rather than pulling in `google-auth-library`, which adds ~835 transitive packages for one JWKS fetch and one RS256 verify — and, tried first, churned the lockfile enough to drop the `@next/swc-*` optional platform packages other developers' installs depend on. It pins `alg: RS256` before touching the signature (blocking `alg: none` and the HS256-signed-with-the-public-key confusion attack, both covered by tests), checks `iss`/`aud`/`exp`/`iat` with 60s skew, and caches Google's JWKS per its `Cache-Control` with an in-flight promise so a burst of logins triggers one fetch and an unseen `kid` forces a refresh. **`User.passwordHash` is now nullable** (migration `20260807120000_add_google_sign_in`, plus a unique `google_id`): a Google account has no password. Callers were audited — `login()` and `customerLogin()` already guarded null, `customerSignup()` did not and would have thrown on `bcrypt.compare(pw, null)`, and `changePassword()` now returns a clear 400 pointing at "Forgot password" (which works fine on a passwordless account and is the supported way to set the first one). `/auth/me` reports `has_password` and `google_connected` so the security page can adapt. **Configured by one backend env var, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`** (comma-separated to also accept the future mobile clients); the frontend reads it at runtime from `GET /auth/google/config`, because a `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` variable is baked into the image at build time and would make enabling Google a frontend rebuild rather than a restart. Unset means the buttons never render — a deployment without a client id behaves exactly as before. Google's own rendered widget is used (branding terms require it, and being an iframe it can't be restyled or click-intercepted), wrapped in `GoogleSignInButton.tsx` with `auto_select: false` so arriving on the page never silently signs anyone in. **Found and fixed en route:** the onboarding wizard sent `planCode: 'FREE'` to `POST /auth/setup-tenant`, which `provisionTenant` rejects with "The free plan is not available for new signups" — so the tenant-less path was already broken, it just had no way to be reached (every password signup requires an org name). It is reachable now: a Google sign-in from `/login` has no workspace details, returns `requires_workspace: true`, and lands in that wizard. The wizard now omits `planCode` and `setupTenant` falls back to the platform default plan. A Google *signup* from `/signup` carries the org name, plan and referral code with it and provisions in the same request, so referral attribution survives. Setup guide in `docs/ops/google-sign-in-setup.md`. **Verified:** 19 new cases in `google-token.service.spec.ts` (valid token, wrong audience, wrong issuer, expired, foreign signing key, tampered payload, `alg: none`, HS256 confusion, unverified email, malformed, JWKS outage, cache-hit/de-dupe/rotation), 8 in `auth.service.spec.ts` (passwordless creation, workspace provisioning, returning user, linking without overwriting an existing name, refusing a different Google identity, 2FA still challenged, invalid mobile, and no DB touch on a bad token), 4 in `GoogleSignInButton.test.tsx` (hidden when unconfigured, silent when the config request fails, credential forwarded, cancellation reported) — backend `npx jest src/auth/` 100/100 green; frontend login/signup/onboarding/localization suites green with locale parity intact across en/bn/ms. Backend and frontend `tsc --noEmit` at their known pre-existing baselines. **Not done: no sign-in has been performed against real Google credentials** — every test drives the verifier with locally-generated RSA keys and a stubbed JWKS endpoint, so the OAuth client, consent screen and authorised-origins configuration are unexercised — done 2026-08-07. **Update 2026-08-08:** the OAuth client now exists (GCP project `erp71-709cf`) and is live in production — `/auth/google/config` returns `enabled: true` and the button renders with no console errors — but the sign-in round trip itself is *still* unexercised; see the deploy entry above and the open item under OPERATIONAL → Deployment
- [x] **Gross-profit reports, on a corrected basis — three arithmetic bugs fixed, five reports added** — the reports sitting on top of the new weighted-average costing were computing margin three different wrong ways, each invisible until someone reconciled against the till. **(1) Uncosted lines were counted as free stock:** `unit_cost_at_sale !== null ? cost*qty : 0` in four places gave a line with no cost basis zero COGS, so it read as 100% margin and dragged every total up — while `sales-dashboard.service.ts` handled the same case correctly, so one tenant saw two different gross-profit figures depending on the screen. **(2) Returns reduced revenue but never reversed COGS**, so every refund overstated gross profit by the whole cost of the goods coming back; fixed with a new nullable `SalesReturnItem.unit_cost_at_return` (migration `20260807140000_add_sales_return_cost`), snapshotted from the original sale line where there is one and from the pool for a parentless return — goods go back at the cost they left at, so a refund books no profit or loss of its own. **(3) Invoice-level discounts never reached line-level margin:** `dto.discountAmount` and loyalty redemption reduce `Sale.total_amount` while `price_at_sale` stays at list, so Σ(line revenue) exceeded the invoice and margins were overstated on exactly the discounted sales worth examining; fixed at report time by a pro-rata `discountRatio`, which needs no schema change and self-corrects all history.
