All three now live in one place: new `apps/backend/src/sales-reports/gross-profit.utils.ts` reduces every revenue-bearing event — sale line or return line — to a **signed `MarginLine`**, so a return is a negative line and needs no special case downstream. `summariseMargin` excludes an uncosted line from *both* sides of the margin and returns **null rather than zero** when nothing has a basis ("we cannot say" ≠ "we broke even"), reporting a `coverage` block alongside every figure so a thin basis is visible rather than inferred. **Five reports** behind `VIEW_FINANCIAL_REPORTS` (cost is not the same thing as price and should not follow the same permission): gross profit **by product** (ranked by profit, not revenue), **by salesperson/counter** (`Sale.created_by`/`counter_id` were recorded and never aggregated, so commission had only revenue to work with — which rewards discounting; a refund is charged back to whoever made the original sale), **margin exceptions** (every line under a configurable floor, judged on the *discounted* price so a sale discounted below cost is caught, with a by-who-rang-it-up roll-up), **margin bridge** (volume/price/cost/mix, with mix as the residual so the four effects always sum exactly to the total change), and **cost coverage** (the list someone works through to make the other four defensible). Existing `by-customer` and `by-category` gained COGS, gross profit, margin and coverage — the customer one answers "which credit customer are we selling to at a loss", which nothing in the system could previously say. `getSalesSummary` keeps **invoice totals for the top line and allocated lines for attribution**, since the invoice is what matches the till; its daily rows now bucket in **Dhaka time** rather than UTC, which had been putting evening takings on the previous day. Five frontend pages under `sales/reports/gross-profit/` with a shared `CoverageNotice`/`MarginCell`/`ProfitCell` (a null margin renders as a dash, never 0%), en/bn/ms keys, `PageShell`/`PageHeader`, blue-600 only. **Verified:** 24 new cases in `gross-profit.utils.spec.ts` (discount allocation reconciling to the invoice, uncosted exclusion from both sides, return netting to zero, null-not-zero, bridge effects summing to the total, growth-from-nothing) plus 14 new service cases; backend 197/201 suites / 3280 tests green, frontend 207/207 suites / 2385 tests green, locale parity green, both typechecks at their known baselines. Also verified **against a real Postgres**: 22 assertions driving the real service over a scenario carrying all three bugs at once — a discounted sale, a partial return, and an uncosted product — every figure correct. **Not verified:** the four `test/*.spec.ts` HTTP integration suites still do not run in this sandbox (signup 500s on missing infrastructure, at baseline too) — done 2026-08-07

- [x] **Weighted-average (moving average) costing, replacing the price-list standard cost behind every gross-profit number** — a sale snapshotted `unit_cost_at_sale` from the newest `ProductPrice.cost`, a figure someone typed into the catalog that drifted from real buying prices the moment a supplier changed a rate; margins were computed against it. New `ProductCost` table (migration `20260807120000_add_product_cost_pool`) holds one running average per product per tenant — **tenant-wide, not per warehouse**, so a warehouse transfer is a pure quantity move with no cost effect and every report agrees on what a unit cost. `avg_cost` is `Decimal(12,4)`: rounding to paisa at each receipt compounds into visible drift over a few hundred movements. New `apps/backend/src/database/product-cost.utils.ts` holds all the rules as a **pure function** (`applyToPool`) plus a thin DB wrapper, called from `applyInventoryMovement` so all 36 stock-touching call sites maintain the pool through one chokepoint. Movement types are classified `REVALUE` (purchase receipt, initial stock, manufacturing output, purchase-return reversal/delete), `REVERSE_RECEIPT` (purchase return and its edit), or **`QUANTITY_ONLY` by default** — an unclassified type can move stock but never silently revalue the pool. Issues are stamped with the average they left at, and that stamp is the COGS the reports read. **Three callers were passing a selling price as a cost** and are fixed: `products.service.ts` INITIAL_STOCK used `dto.price` (new optional `cost` on `CreateProductDto`; omitted when unknown, since no basis beats a wrong one), `inventory-shrinkage.service.ts` used `product.price` (overstating every write-off by the whole margin), and both sale paths fell back to `priceAtSale`. **Manufacturing was inconsistent with itself** and is fixed too: jobs valued raw materials at the standard cost while the stock came out of a real one, and finished goods entered at the *output product's catalog cost* rather than what the run cost — `completeJob` now totals job costs before the output movement and books the goods in at `costPerUnit`. Per-tenant `InventorySettings.costing_method` (WEIGHTED_AVERAGE | LATEST_COST) exposed in Inventory → Settings with en/bn/ms keys; `resolveProductCosts` is the single resolver for both sales and manufacturing, falling back to `ProductPrice.cost` for products the pool has never seen and returning **nothing rather than zero** when there is no basis at all. New `npm run backfill:product-costs` (dry-run by default, `--apply`, `--tenant=`, `--no-restate`) replays movement history through the same `applyToPool` — no second implementation to drift — rebuilds the pools and **restates `unit_cost_at_sale` on past sales**, so historical margins change; safe because ERP71 is periodic-inventory (`posting-contract.ts:140`) and no COGS voucher is posted per sale, so nothing desyncs from the trial balance. The replay substitutes the price-list cost for historical INITIAL_STOCK movements, which recorded retail. **Verified:** 30 new unit cases in `product-cost.utils.spec.ts` (blending, quantity-weighting, negative/zero stock, missing cost, purchase-return round-trip netting to zero, no-drift over 300 receipts, atomic quantity increment, tenant scoping, method dispatch) plus manufacturing cases for average-cost consumption and production-cost output; full backend suite 196/196 unit suites / 3239 tests green; frontend typecheck and locale parity green. Also verified **against a real Postgres**: 10 assertions driving `applyInventoryMovement` end-to-end (receipt blending, sale stamped at average, shrinkage ignoring a passed retail price, transfer round-trip cost-neutral, purchase return, replay reproducing the live pool, pool quantity matching stock on hand), and the backfill restating a sale from a standard cost of 120 to the real average of 90, idempotent across two `--apply` runs. **Not verified:** the four `test/*.spec.ts` HTTP integration suites do not run in this sandbox — signup 500s on infrastructure the container lacks, at baseline as well as on this branch — done 2026-08-07

- [x] **Inventory → Demands: a branch can ask head office for stock it has run out of.** Every inventory document in the schema recorded something that had already happened — a transfer that moved, a count that was taken, shrinkage written off — and there was no way to record something that *hadn't*. A shop that ran out of rice sent a WhatsApp message. New `ProductDemand`/`ProductDemandItem` (migration `20260807120000_add_product_demands`) with the same state machine as `ExpenseClaim`, deliberately: DRAFT → SUBMITTED → APPROVED | REJECTED → FULFILLED, plus CANCELLED from either pre-decision state. **Nothing here moves stock**, which is the load-bearing decision: an approved demand is met by an ordinary warehouse transfer or purchase recorded through its own module, and `fulfil` only marks that this happened with a free-text note for the transfer or PO number. Posting stock from a demand would give one physical movement two records and let a request inflate a warehouse on its own. **An approval can be cut line by line** — `quantity_approved` is separate from `quantity_requested` and stays nullable until reviewed, because zero is a meaningful answer ("this line was declined, the rest went through") and null reads as "not decided yet"; an omitted review line is approved in full, so an approver who agrees with everything sends nothing but the status. **Cutting every line to zero is refused**: that is a rejection wearing an approval's clothes, and the requester deserves the reason a rejection carries. A rejection zeroes every approved quantity rather than leaving them null. **Two permissions, not one** — `CREATE_PRODUCT_DEMAND` and `APPROVE_PRODUCT_DEMAND` — and the split is the point: Cashier gets submit only, since the person at the counter is who notices the empty shelf and is exactly who should not sign it off; Manager gets both. Backfilled to existing tenants by a new `product-demands` group in `sync-role-permissions.ts`, which already runs in the container start chain. **Reads sit on `VIEW_PRODUCT_CATALOG`, not on either demand permission**, because `StorePermissionGuard` requires *every* listed permission — naming both on the controller class would have locked each side out of the other's list. Edit/submit/cancel are scoped to the raiser (OWNER exempt, since they bypass the guard anyway and refusing them would only inconvenience the one person it cannot stop); review and fulfil are deliberately unscoped, an approver acting on someone else's demand by definition. `PD-00001` numbering is allocated from the highest existing number rather than a row count — the house `count + 1` pattern reuses a number the moment a row goes away and the unique index would then reject the write. **UI** is one page for both sides of the request (`/inventory/demands`): the list is the same list, and which half you see is the approve action being hidden, not a second screen. `PageShell`/`PageHeader`/`ModalShell`/`ModalFooter` and the shared `Button`/`Field`/`Input`/`Select`/`Textarea`/`Alert`/`StatusBadge` primitives throughout, blue-600 accent, `hideOnMobile` on the five secondary columns, toasts through the global store and validation inline. Nav entry `inventory.demands`, hub tile, `routes.inventory.demands`, seven `api.*` methods, an AI help-content FAQ, and en/bn/ms catalog copy (parity test green). **Verified against a real Postgres, not by inspection:** built a DB on the pre-change schema with a tenant/store/warehouse/product/grant, ran the exact production boot step (`prisma db push --accept-data-loss`) — exit 0, both rows kept, both enum values present, both tables created — then exercised the new tables end to end (insert, approve a line, write a `CREATE_PRODUCT_DEMAND` grant, and confirm the FK cascade leaves no orphan items). The hand-written migration was separately applied in a single transaction to a fresh pre-change DB and `prisma migrate diff` against the new schema came back **empty**, so the migration and `db push` produce the same schema. Tests: 24 new backend cases, 10 new frontend cases, plus two assertions in `test/sync-role-permissions.spec.ts` narrowed from "every row in the table belongs to the manager" to "every row *of this group's permissions*" — they were written when no backfill group reached the cashier, and `product-demands` is the first that does (a new case pins exactly that: manager gets both, cashier gets submit only, accountant gets neither). Backend 196 suites / 3232 green with the 4 live-Postgres integration suites failing on a missing `DATABASE_URL` as they do on a clean tree; frontend 208 suites / 2395 green; both typechecks at their existing baselines. **Not done: never opened in a browser, and not deployed** — done 2026-08-07

- [x] **Deployed the HRIS/audit/referrals/messaging release to production (PR #468, commit `0bfefa7b`)** — merged 36 commits from `dev` to `main` with all CI green (Build, Lint, Backend, Frontend, Dependency Audit; E2E skipped as configured). Contents: HRIS phases 0-13 (employee identity and self-service portal, holiday calendar and work schedules, attendance capture with geofencing, overtime approval and frozen month snapshot, salary structure and bank details, payroll runs/payslips/adjustments, disbursement and export, expense claims, assigned assets and documents, leave policy engine, onboarding/offboarding/final settlement, Bangladesh statutory registers), tenant-action audit logging, the referral partner portal split into sub-routes with dashboard charts, and per-tenant email/WhatsApp sender identity. **The deploy failed on first run and took the API down for roughly 25 minutes** — `prisma db push` in the container CMD could not add the required `LeaveType.updated_at` column to a table with 4 existing rows, so the backend crash-looped while the frontend kept returning 200, making the site look healthy from outside. Recovered by backfilling the column directly against the prod database (add nullable → backfill from `created_at` → set not null) and restarting the backend; both follow-ups are filed under OPERATIONAL → Deployment. **Verified after recovery:** backend container up with no restart loop, `Nest application successfully started` in logs, `app.erp71.com` 200, `api/v1/auth/me` 401 (alive and guarded), `api/v1/products` 401 (no regression on pre-existing routes), and the new `hr/work-schedules`, `payroll/runs`, `expense-claims`, `employee-portal/me`, `attendance` routes all 401 rather than 404, confirming the modules mounted. Deployed commit on the VPS confirmed equal to `origin/main`. **Not verified:** no authenticated request was made, so no HRIS feature has been exercised end-to-end in production — route mounting is all that was checked. The six seed/sync steps in the container CMD (including the new `sync:work-schedules` and `sync:salary-components`) completed without error, but their effects were not inspected — done 2026-08-07

- [x] **Per-tenant sender identity for outbound email and WhatsApp, with the platform sender still the default** — every workspace shared one from-address (`PlatformSetting email.email_from`, default `notify@erp71.com`) and one WhatsApp Business number (`whatsapp.phone_number_id`), so a shop's CRM campaign arrived from the platform and **replies went to the platform, not the shop**. New `TenantMessagingIdentity` table (migration `20260806120000_add_tenant_messaging_identity`, one optional row per tenant) plus `TenantMessagingIdentityService`, which answers "does *this* tenant have its own sender, switched on?" — a null answer means the platform sender, which stays the answer for every tenant that has not been onboarded. **Per-channel switches**, so a tenant can brand email while still sending WhatsApp from the platform number, and turning a channel off falls back without losing the stored values. `EmailService.send()` and `WhatsAppService.sendMessage()` take an optional `tenantId`; `CrmCampaignsService.dispatchCampaign()` passes it. **Platform mail deliberately does not** — password reset, verification and billing pass no tenant id and are pinned to the platform address (covered by a test asserting `resolveEmailIdentity` is called with `undefined`). **Reply-to shipped alongside the from-address**, since a branded sender whose replies land in the platform inbox solves half the problem; it is plumbed through all three provider paths (Resend `reply_to`, Brevo `replyTo`, nodemailer `replyTo`). New `address.util.ts` splits/joins `Name <addr>` because Brevo's API takes the two parts separately while Resend and SMTP want them joined — this also **fixes the hardcoded `name: 'ERP71'`** the Brevo path stamped on every message regardless of `email_from`. **Access tokens are encrypted at rest** with the existing `platform-settings/crypto.util` and masked on read; the mask sent back on PATCH means "leave it alone", an empty string clears it. A tenant WhatsApp identity is **all-or-nothing** — its own number always sends with its own token, never the platform token, which belongs to a different WABA. **Platform-admin only, on purpose:** a from-address has to be verified at the provider and a phone number id has to belong to an onboarded WABA, so a tenant-facing version would let any workspace send as any address the provider accepts. Lives in Admin → Tenants → tenant detail (`TenantMessagingIdentityCard`), behind `PlatformAdminGuard` at `/admin/tenants/:tenantId/messaging-identity`. **Enabling a channel is validated, not merely stored** — email needs a from-address, WhatsApp needs both a phone number id and a token, or the PATCH is rejected; a half-set identity that somehow reaches send time logs a warning and falls back rather than dropping the message. Test-send endpoints per channel report **which sender actually went out** (`{ sender: 'tenant' | 'platform' }`), because a silent fallback to the platform sender is otherwise indistinguishable from success. **Verified:** 17 new cases in `tenant-messaging-identity.service.spec.ts` (enable/disable resolution, negative-result caching, encryption round-trip, mask-means-unchanged, empty-string-clears, partial-update preservation, validation refusals), 12 in `address.util.spec.ts`, 3 added to `email.service.spec.ts` (platform default, tenant override, platform mail untouched), and the two existing campaign dispatch assertions updated for the new `{ tenantId }` argument. Backend and frontend typechecks at their known baselines; locale parity green with keys added to en/bn/ms. **Not done:** no message has been sent through a real provider under a tenant identity — the provider request bodies are asserted in unit tests only — done 2026-08-06

- [x] **URL shortener Task 3: Short-link code generation and link service** — cryptographically random 7-character base62 code generator plus `ShortLinksService` with full link lifecycle (create manual/entity-scoped links with idempotency, resolve codes with optional click counting, list with tenant scoping, revoke with cross-tenant protection). Service integrates `isSafeTarget()` validation: rejects unsafe targets before DB touch, re-validates on read (defense in depth against stale rules), uses `BadRequestException` for invalid targets and `NotFoundException` for missing/revoked/stale-validated links. Collision handling retries with fresh codes up to 5 times before throwing `InternalServerErrorException`. All 14 test cases passing (14 in new `short-links.service.spec.ts` + 65 in existing sibling suite); no regressions. Commit `817984d8` — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **URL shortener Task 7: Public quotation view** — `apps/frontend/src/app/q/[token]/page.tsx` (server component, `force-dynamic`) fetches `GET /public/quotations/:token` from the backend's already-sanitized allow-list DTO and unwraps the `TransformInterceptor` envelope (`body?.data ?? body`); `apps/frontend/src/app/q/[token]/PublicQuotationView.tsx` renders it as a client component with a "Print / Save as PDF" button (`window.print()`, `print:hidden` on-screen controls, matching the existing internal quotation page's mechanism — no server-side PDF library). Every failure path — non-OK response (unknown token; a revoked token fails the backend's lookup the same way since `findByShareToken` clears `share_token` to `null`, so it 404s exactly like an unknown one rather than returning 200-with-empty-body), a 200 whose body has no `quote_number` (defensive fallback only, not an active path), a rejected `fetch` (network/DNS/timeout), and a non-JSON body — is caught in a `try`/`catch` around the fetch and JSON parse and renders the identical `Unavailable` component; the fetch/parse were originally unguarded, which let an outage or malformed response fall through to the root `error.tsx` and print a different page (red alert badge, raw error message, dashboard-link CTA) — fixed on code-review before merge. A regression test (`page.test.tsx`) calls the async Server Component directly, renders each of the five failure modes plus a valid-token case with `renderToStaticMarkup`, and asserts the four failure variants produce HTML **byte-identical** to the baseline unknown-token render (string equality, not text-content assertions) while the valid-token render differs and contains the quote number — guards the security property against silent regression. Every monetary value — unit price, line total, grand total — goes through `formatBDT` (found at `@/lib/format`, matching the brief's import exactly) rather than being rendered raw, which also hides the floating-point noise in the backend's computed `line_total`. The line-items table is wrapped in its own `overflow-x-auto` container (one deviation from the brief's literal snippet) so a long product name can't force horizontal scroll on the page body at 360px. UI rules followed: `blue-600` accent only, `min-h-touch` on the print button, `rounded-lg` (no `rounded-2xl`/`3xl`), no arbitrary hex classes. `tsc --noEmit` clean (only the two known pre-existing unrelated errors: a stale `.next` cache entry for the renamed `crm/tasks` page, and `contact/page.test.tsx` importing the uninstalled `@testing-library/user-event`); `npx jest` 183/183 suites, 2175/2175 tests green, no new failures. Commits `1b2a611b`, `f99044d0`, `75110f86` — done 2026-08-05
