Instead pre-checked permission via `api.getMe()` + `hasPermission`/`isOwner` from `@/lib/permissions`, the same building blocks `/crm/setup` pre-checks with, before ever mounting `ShortLinkManager` — so an unpermitted tenant user never fires the request at all, and sees an amber forbidden card (visually matching `/settings/audit-logs`'s) with a link back to `/settings`. Hub card: `apps/frontend/src/app/(app)/settings/page.tsx` — added `Link2` to the `lucide-react` import and a card to the `advanced` section with `key: 'urlShortener'`, matching Task 10's `settings.hub.links.urlShortener` key exactly (already present in all three locales) and the same `bg-primary-light text-blue-700 border-primary-border` accent used by the other blue-accent cards. Initial pass did not touch `ShortLinkManager.tsx` itself (see Review fix below for where that changed). **Verified:** `cd apps/frontend && npx jest --testPathPatterns='src/lib/localization' --testPathPatterns='src/app/\(app\)/settings' --testPathPatterns='src/components/short-links'` — 10 suites/85 tests green (plain positional-arg `npx jest a b c` silently drops all but the last pattern on this Jest version — confirmed via `--listTests` — so `--testPathPatterns` repeated per pattern is what actually ORs them; noting this since the brief's literal command form under-runs the suite); `npx tsc --noEmit` — only the two known pre-existing unrelated errors (stale `.next` cache entry for renamed `crm/tasks`, `contact/page.test.tsx` importing uninstalled `@testing-library/user-event`); full `npx jest` — 186/186 suites, 2191/2191 tests green, no new failures (the `user-event` import doesn't fail the actual test run, only `tsc`). Report: `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-04-url-shortener/task-12-report.md` — done 2026-08-05. **Review fix (2026-08-05):** two Important findings. (1) The permission pre-check's `.catch(() => setAllowed(false))` folded "confirmed denied" and "couldn't reach the backend" into one outcome, so a permitted shop owner on a flaky connection was told they lacked access with no way out but a reload — unlike `/crm/setup`'s same-shaped catch, which only disables an edit action rather than gating the whole view, so the blast radius here was much larger. Replaced the boolean `allowed` state with a `PermissionState = 'checking' | 'allowed' | 'denied' | 'error'` union; `'error'` renders a distinct amber card (`m.checkFailedTitle`/`m.checkFailedDescription`) with a `Try again` button that re-runs the same `getMe()` check, rather than the forbidden card. Added `settings.urlShortener.checkFailedTitle`/`checkFailedDescription`/`retry` to `en`/`bn`/`ms` `core.ts` with real Bengali and Malay copy. (2) With the component-modification restriction lifted for this specific defect, fixed `ShortLinkManager.load()` at the source: it now catches its own `fetchLinks()` failure into a `loadError` state instead of leaving an unhandled promise rejection, renders a red retry row in the table (distinguishable from the "no short links yet" empty state — the two look identical to a user otherwise) via new `components.shortLinkManager.loadError`/`retry` keys in all three locales, and never clears already-loaded `links` on a failed reload. Since `load()` no longer throws, also resolved a related Task-11 Minor for free: `revoke()`'s single `try` used to wrap both `revokeLink()` and the follow-up `load()`, so a successful revoke followed by a failed reload showed a misleading "could not revoke" toast; split into two steps so only an actual `revokeLink()` failure toasts, and a reload failure surfaces through the new inline `loadError` banner instead. Added two new `ShortLinkManager.test.tsx` cases (failed-initial-load-with-working-retry; reload failure after create leaves the prior row visible) — 11/11 in that suite. Re-verified the admin page at `/admin/url-shortener` is unaffected (no dedicated page test exists for it; it renders the same now-fixed `ShortLinkManager`, exercised by the same 11 component tests). **Verified after fix:** `npx jest --testPathPatterns='src/lib/localization' --testPathPatterns='src/app/\(app\)/settings' --testPathPatterns='src/components/short-links'` — 10 suites/87 tests green; `npx tsc --noEmit` — only the same two known pre-existing errors; full `npx jest` — 186/186 suites, 2193/2193 tests green.
- [x] **An expired session now lands on the login page instead of a shell that looks signed in but isn't** — reported from a screenshot: the sidebar, module tiles and avatar menu were all still there, but the user name showed `—`, the role fell back to `Staff` and every link was dead. **Root cause was a three-link chain, not one bug.** `requestWithAuth` in `apps/frontend/src/lib/api.ts` collapsed *every* non-OK response into a plain `Error(message)`, discarding the status — so no caller could tell an expired session from a validation failure. The app shell then swallowed it outright (`api.getMe().then(...).catch(() => null).finally(() => setHasResolvedUser(true))`), leaving `user` null while rendering the full chrome anyway; the `—` and `Staff` in the screenshot are literally `user?.name || '—'` and `userFallbackRole`. And there is **no middleware or route guard anywhere in the frontend** — the only paths to `/login` were the Sign Out button and select-account, so nothing ever redirected. **What made the fix clean:** every unauthenticated endpoint (login, 2FA verify, signup, demo, plans, invitations) uses raw `fetch`, and the public storefront has its own client — so a 401 out of `requestWithAuth`/`fetchBlobWithAuth` can only mean the staff session is dead. No endpoint allowlist needed. New dependency-free `src/lib/session-expiry.ts` (dependency-free on purpose: `api.ts` needs it and `auth-session.ts` imports `api.ts`, so anything heavier closes an import cycle) clears both storage backends and redirects to `/login?redirect=<path>&reason=expired`. **Hard `window.location.replace`, not a router push** — the shell holds stale user/tenant state across a dozen contexts and only a full navigation guarantees none survives; `replace` also keeps the dead page out of Back. A **once-per-page-load latch** matters more than it sounds: a dashboard fires ~11 requests that all 401 at once. `clearAuthSession` now delegates to the same key list so sign-out and expiry cannot drift. Login page shows an amber "Your session has expired" notice (en/bn/ms). **Verified:** 190 suites / 2,255 frontend tests green, including 18 new cases; and — the part that actually mattered — 5 new Playwright cases in `e2e/session-expiry.spec.ts` driving a real browser, since jsdom 26 makes `window.location` non-configurable so the navigation itself is untestable in Jest. **The e2e caught two false verifications:** port 3000 was serving a stale production build from the previous day, and a dev server on 3001 had every request killed by CORS before any 401 could occur — in both cases the spec "failed" against code that was never running. Confirmed the routing policy split (`resolveExpiredSessionRedirect`) is pure and unit-tested, and checked a valid session still reaches the dashboard. Three pre-existing `e2e/auth.spec.ts` failures (UI login + two signup cases) were confirmed identical with the fix stashed, so they are not from this change. **Not done:** a token that expires *while* a page sits idle is only caught on the next request — there is no proactive expiry timer; and network failures (backend unreachable) still leave the same stalled shell, which is a separate bug with the same symptom — done 2026-08-05
- [x] **Investor portal — implementation plan** — `docs/investor-portal-plan.md`. Design work only; no code. The recommendation is the **referral-partner pattern, not the storefront one**: same `/login`, same `app`-scope JWT, an `Investor.user_id` link, an `InvestorPortalGuard`, and portal routes inside the existing `(app)` shell gated by `active_context === 'investor'`. A separate token scope (the storefront approach) was rejected on two grounds — it duplicates the whole auth surface (lockout, throttling, 2FA, reset), and **a token carries one scope, so a shop owner who is also an investor would have to log out of their own shop to read their statement**, which is the common case for an SME partnership. Giving investors a `TenantUser` row was rejected outright: `VIEW_INVESTORS` lists every investor with their percentages and payouts, so partner A would see partner B's terms. Verified rather than assumed that an investor-only `app` token is inert against staff endpoints — no `TenantUser` row means `TenantInterceptor` never sets `request.tenantId` and `@Tenant()` throws — and audited the blast radius: 75 controllers take tenant via `@Tenant()`, only 3 (`auth`, `navigation`, `invitations`) read `req.tenantId` directly. **Three deliberate deviations from `RefereeGuard`** are written into the plan: no email-fallback linking (`Referee.email` is globally unique and admin-provisioned; `Investor.email` is free text a shop owner types, so a collision would hand a stranger a partner's financials), tenant resolved from the `Investor` row and never from `x-tenant-id`, and an `x-investor-id` context header because one person may invest in several tenants. Revocation is `portal_access = false` re-read per request rather than a token-version counter, so it cannot collaterally end the same person's staff session. Portal is read-only by design. **Open decisions recorded, not silently resolved:** whether the investor sees the company's net profit basis, and whether `EXITED` investors keep read access — done 2026-08-05
- [x] **Investors can be brought into a business on a share of monthly profit** — new `investors` module: capital in and out, a monthly profit run that declares each partner's share, and a payout that settles it. **The load-bearing decision is that the profit share posts as an equity distribution, not an expense** (`Dr Investor Profit Distribution / Cr Investor Profit Payable`, both new accounts under `3102`/`2105`). Booking it as an expense would put it inside net profit, so re-running a month would read a figure its own previous run had already reduced and the number would drift down on every recompute — the equity-contra treatment makes a run idempotent by construction, and it is also the correct treatment for a capital partner. The month's basis is read through `AccountingService.getProfitLoss`, so the figure investors are paid on and the figure on the P&L report cannot disagree, and it is **snapshotted onto the run** so a back-dated voucher cannot silently rewrite shares already paid. Idempotency is structural rather than checked: `InvestorProfitRun` is unique on `(tenant, year, month, scope_key)`, and **`scope_key` exists because a nullable `store_id` cannot carry it** — Postgres treats NULLs as distinct in a unique index, so a `store_id`-keyed constraint would happily accept two company-wide runs for the same month. **Losses carry forward per investor, never billed**: a loss month accrues nothing and banks each partner's slice against future shares, which the next profitable month pays down before any cash is declared. Rounding residual goes to the largest slice so the accrual vouchers sum exactly to the declared total rather than running a few paisa short every month. `PartyType.INVESTOR` makes Investor Profit Payable a control account, so each investor's statement comes out of `buildPartyLedger` with no new query code. Guards: active shares cannot exceed 100% of profit, a withdrawal cannot exceed the capital balance, an investor with ledger history is retired rather than deleted, a run with a paid share cannot be deleted, and the accrual is dated to the period end so the fiscal-period lock applies. Reads need `VIEW_INVESTORS`, writes `MANAGE_INVESTORS`. Page at `/accounting/investors` with a preview-then-declare run flow; i18n en/bn/ms. **Verified:** 35 new backend cases green (allocation arithmetic, carry-forward, rounding, posting event types and party tagging, run idempotency, payout guards); full backend suite 163/167 with only the four known DB-dependent integration suites failing; frontend 189 suites / 2,236 green; both typechecks at their known baselines; posting-contract and money-model-contract green with the new tuples registered. **Not done:** never run against a real database — no voucher from this module has reached a real ledger. **Partial payment of a month's share is not supported** (the payout's idempotency key is derived from the share id, so two partials would collide); a share is declared and settled as a unit. The new accounts and posting rules reach existing tenants only via `npm run sync:accounting -w @erp71/database` — until that runs, `autoPostFromRules` finds no rule and skips, posting nothing rather than posting something wrong — done 2026-08-05
- [x] **Stock on Hand report — quantity per warehouse in columns, valued at weighted average purchase cost** — asked for directly: "current qty of each product and value as per the average unit price... for a selected warehouse or all warehouses qty in columns". Neither half existed. The nearest report, `/inventory/reports/valuation`, values stock at `Product.price` — the **selling** price, not cost (stock-aging is explicit about this and stamps `valuationBasis: 'CURRENT_SELLING_PRICE'`) — and collapses every warehouse into one number, with the warehouse dropdown narrowing that sum rather than splitting it. Nothing in the app laid warehouses out as columns. **There is no stored average cost anywhere** — neither `Product` nor `ProductStock` has a cost column — so the basis is computed: `SUM(unit_cost x quantity_delta) / SUM(quantity_delta)` over each product's `PURCHASE`/`PURCHASE_RECEIPT`/`PURCHASE_RETURN` movements, one grouped raw query for the whole catalogue rather than a query per product. Purchase returns carry a negative delta **and their own `unit_cost`**, so they fall out of both sums with no special case. **`INITIAL_STOCK` is deliberately excluded even though it carries a `unit_cost`:** `products.service.ts:82` stamps the *selling* price there on the create path (the CSV import path uses real cost), so folding it in would drag every average toward retail and silently overstate inventory value — the most consequential call in this change. Products never purchased through the ledger fall back to the newest `ProductPrice.cost`, the same field sales uses for `unit_cost_at_sale`; products with neither are flagged `UNCOSTED`, valued at zero, and **counted in the summary** so the page warns the total is understated rather than presenting it as complete. A warehouse filter scopes the movements too, so a single-branch view reflects that branch's own buying rather than the tenant-wide blend. Per-warehouse value totals reconcile to the report total exactly, by construction and by test. **Verified against a real Postgres, which is what made this worth doing:** a scratch PG16 + `db push` + a 27-check script driving the service end to end caught that `::uuid` casts in the raw query are **wrong** — Prisma maps `String @id @default(uuid())` to a `text` column, so the cast failed with `42883 operator does not exist: text = uuid`. Mocked tests could never have found it. The same run confirmed INITIAL_STOCK and SALE movements stay out of the average (545, not the 550 a naive pool gives), tenant isolation, inactive-warehouse exclusion, and the fallback chain. **Suites:** backend 165 / 2,469 (+13 service cases), frontend 190 / 2,246 (+10 page cases); both typechecks at their known baselines (3 pre-existing backend test-file errors, 1 pre-existing frontend). The 4 `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites fail identically on a stashed clean tree — confirmed, not assumed. **Deploy step, easy to miss:** the new `inventory.reports.stock-on-hand` nav node needs `npx tsx prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=inventory.reports.stock-on-hand` for any tenant with a saved custom sidebar layout, since `resolveTenantSidebarLayout` returns saved layouts verbatim and never merges `NAV_REGISTRY`. **Also corrected:** the Valuation Report hub description said "Inventory value at cost", which was never true and would now collide with the new report — reworded to "current selling price" across en/bn/ms. **Not done:** never rendered in a browser and never run against production data; no export beyond the DataTable's built-in one; per-warehouse *average cost* is not its own column — value follows the tenant-wide (or filtered) average applied column by column — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **Referral commission payouts can now post into the platform's own books** — second of the two growth items chosen, and the one with a genuine modelling problem underneath it. `RefereePayment` was a standalone platform-level ledger with no link into the accounting module, so commission expense never appeared in the operator's P&L. **The obstacle is that the accounting module is tenant-scoped and the platform is not a tenant** — there is no workspace to infer, and no chart of accounts to guess at. So this is configuration rather than convention: a new `referral_accounting` platform-settings group naming the house tenant, the expense account to debit and the cash/bank account to credit. **All four keys are required and the whole thing is off by default**; with any of them unset the payout is recorded in the referral ledger exactly as before and nothing touches any chart of accounts, because guessing an account is worse than not posting. Enabled-but-incomplete logs a warning naming the missing keys rather than failing silently or half-posting. On a configured install, recording a payout writes a balanced `JOURNAL` voucher through the existing `AccountingService.createVoucher`, so it goes through the same validation, fiscal-period locks and audit trail as every other entry rather than reaching into the tables directly. It carries the admin's payment reference when there is one and falls back to `referee-payment:<id>`, so an entry can always be traced back. **The posting is caught and downgraded to a warning, deliberately:** reversing a real money movement because the bookkeeping leg failed — a locked period, a deleted account — would be worse than a missing journal entry an admin can add by hand, and a test asserts the payout and its `PAID` transition survive a rejected voucher. It posts the amount **actually recorded**, so a part-payment posts the part. **Verified:** backend 151 suites / 2,353 green (+6: off-by-default, balanced voucher into the configured workspace, reference fallback, incomplete-config warning, posting-failure survival, part-payment amount); `nest build` clean; typecheck at its known baseline. Checked statically that `ReferralsModule → AccountingModule` introduces no circular import — `AccountingModule` imports no modules at all and neither it nor `PlatformSettingsModule` reaches `AuthModule`. **Not done:** never posted to a real ledger, and there is **no admin UI for the four settings** — they have to be set through the platform-settings API or directly, which is the honest next step if this is switched on. Also unreconciled: a `REVERSED` commission that was already paid does not post a reversing entry, so the books and the referral ledger can disagree after a clawback — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **Referral links are tracked, and a click now survives long enough to be worth something** — asked for as part of "keep doing all PRs"; the first of the two growth items chosen over AIT withholding. Two separate gaps, and the second was the one that actually cost money. **Partners could see conversions but not traffic**, so a code shared widely that converted badly looked identical to one nobody ever clicked — there was no way to tell a bad landing page from a dead campaign. New `ReferralClick` table behind `/r/<code>`, a **server-side Next route handler** rather than a client page: the click is recorded before anything renders, so it does not depend on JavaScript and a visitor who bounces immediately still counts. It lives on the app domain rather than pointing partners at the API host, because these links get printed on cards and pasted into WhatsApp and an `api.*` URL reads as suspicious. Recording is **best-effort with a 2s abort** — every error path still redirects, since a tracking failure must never stop someone reaching signup. The public endpoint is throttled at 30/min and **always returns 204**, so an unknown code is indistinguishable from a recorded one and it cannot be used to enumerate which referral codes exist. **The table deliberately holds no IP address and no visitor id** — a partner's marketing reach does not justify storing anything that identifies the person who clicked; `referrer` and `user_agent` are kept because they are what make the number interpretable (bot traffic vs a real campaign) and are capped at 500 chars in both the DTO and the service. **The second gap: attribution did not survive the visit.** The code only ever existed as a query parameter, so someone who clicked on Monday and signed up on Thursday was attributed to nobody and the partner was simply not paid. `referral-attribution.ts` now remembers it for **30 days, last click wins**. localStorage rather than a cookie on purpose: the link, the signup page and the API are not guaranteed to share a registrable domain across environments, and a cookie set by the API host would be invisible to the app — the trade-off is per-browser attribution that dies with cleared site data, whose failure mode is "falls back to the un-attributed behaviour we already had", never a *wrong* attribution. It is **cleared on successful signup**, because leaving it would silently attach the same partner to an unrelated signup from that browser weeks later. **Stats plumbed through both audiences** — clicks and conversion rate on the partner portal, the admin list (as a sub-line under the click count) and the admin ledger. `conversion_rate` is **null rather than 0 when there are no clicks**: "0%" reads as "nobody converted" when the truth is "nobody has visited yet", and a partner should not be told they are failing before they have started. Click counts come from a second `groupBy` alongside the existing one, not a per-referee count, for the same reason the commission stats were degrouped earlier. **Verified:** backend 151 suites / 2,347 green (+4 click cases: normalisation, silent no-op on an unknown code, truncation, null-not-empty-string); frontend 181 suites / 2,160 green including 11 new attribution cases (window boundaries either side, last-click-wins, unparseable and half-written storage, empty code); `next build` clean with `/r/[code]` emitted as a dynamic route; locale parity green; both typechecks at their known baselines. **Not done:** never clicked in a browser and no click has ever been written to a real database — the redirect, the fetch timeout and the localStorage round-trip are all unit-tested or type-checked only. **Known limitation, deliberate:** no bot filtering, so `user_agent` is stored precisely so that can be added later without a migration — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **Referral commission is now a share of revenue actually collected, not of list price** — the last of the three money bugs, and the one that needed a policy call: gross vs net revenue share. Settled as **net**. `earnReferralCommission` was handed `emailAmount`, a local recomputed from `plan.monthly_price`/`yearly_price` **for the invoice email**, and used it as the commission base — which is why this reads as grab-what's-handy rather than a decision. With a 10% signup discount and a 10% commission on a ৳10,000 plan the platform collected ৳9,000 and paid out ৳1,000: an 11.1% effective rate that widened with the discount. Now the base is list price less that referral's own discount, so the same sale pays ৳900. **The discount is recomputed rather than the charged amount being plumbed through, and that is the load-bearing decision here:** it reuses the same formula and the same snapshotted `discount_pct` that `createCheckoutSession` used, so the two provably cannot drift; the statuses line up as well — checkout only discounts while `PENDING`, this only earns while `PENDING`, and status never moves backwards, so if a discount is applied here it was applied at checkout. Callers that never saw a checkout (SSL Wireless callbacks, manual admin plan changes, the webhook path) get the right answer without extra plumbing, which threading the amount through `applySubscriptionChange` would not have given. Add-ons are excluded on both sides, so the base is exactly plan revenue. A test pins the cent-level agreement with checkout's rounding (7.5% off 3999 → 3699.08 on both sides, not a re-rounding), and another covers a missing `discount_pct` resolving to no discount rather than `NaN` — the same class of bug that `SOURCE_WEIGHT` hit during the lead-taxonomy migration. **No backfill.** Commissions earned before today keep their list-price figures: retroactively cutting a commission a partner has already seen on their ledger is worse than a one-off inconsistency, so `plan_amount` now means net collected on new rows and list price on old ones — documented on the schema field and in Epic 06 rather than left for someone to trip over. **The characterisation test from the first referral PR was flipped, not deleted** — it asserted the list-price behaviour precisely so this change would show up as deliberate, and it now asserts the opposite. **Verified:** backend 151 suites / 2,343 green (+4 replacing the characterisation case: net base, no-discount passthrough, cent-level checkout agreement, missing-discount safety); `prisma validate` clean; typecheck at its known baseline of 3 pre-existing errors. **Not done:** never run against a real database or a real payment — the arithmetic is unit-tested only — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **Referral partners are now told when they earn and when they are paid, and the one-shot rule is written down** — sixth and last of the referral follow-ups that did not need a product decision. **Partners had no idea anything had happened unless they logged in and looked**: the module sent exactly one email ever, the login invite. Two new templates in `EmailService` — commission earned (names the referred business and the amount) and payment recorded (names the amount, method and reference) — fired from `earnReferralCommission` and `recordPayment` respectively. Both are **caught separately from the ledger write they follow** and downgraded to `logger.warn`: by the time the email is attempted the money movement is already committed, so a bounced SMTP connection must not read as "the commission failed to record". Tests assert exactly that — the `EARNED` update and the `PAID` transaction both still happen when the send rejects. The payment notification deliberately reports **the amount actually recorded rather than the one requested**, which matters now that an omitted amount means "settle exactly what is owed". `ReferralsService` gained `EmailModule` and a `Logger`. **The one-shot commission was the module's biggest undocumented behaviour** — both the discount and the commission are gated on `PENDING`, so renewals are charged full price and earn the partner nothing, and neither the epic nor any partner-facing screen said so. `docs/prd/epic-06-referral-affiliate-program.md` gained a **Commission rules (as built)** section: the one-shot rule spelled out in three concrete bullets, the full four-state machine as a table including `REVERSED` and why it is terminal, the self-referral guard, payout reconciliation, and a note that moving to a recurring revenue share is a data-model change (a commission row per billing period) rather than a config toggle. The **open list-price question is recorded there too**, so the doc does not read as though the current base were settled policy. Partner-facing, the portal now carries a one-line note above the commission table across en/bn/ms — the one-shot rule is the question partners ask most, and saying it beats letting them infer it from a renewal that earned nothing. **Verified:** backend 151 suites / 2,340 green (+6: earned and paid notifications, the amount-actually-recorded case, both still-succeeds-when-email-fails cases, and a referral with no linked partner address); frontend 180 suites / 2,149 green; locale parity green; both typechecks at their known baselines. **Not done:** no email has been rendered in a real client — the templates are plain HTML in the same shape as the existing referee invite, but nobody has looked at one — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **Referral screens follow the UI rules, and the admin referral page is finally in a menu** — fifth of the referral follow-ups; presentation only, no behaviour change. **`/admin/referrals` was in `NAV_REGISTRY` but in no layout at all**, so the platform admin referral screens were registered, rendered, routable and effectively hidden — reachable only through a card on `/admin/platform-settings`. Carried as a known follow-up since the 2026-07-20 nav reorg. Now in `DEFAULT_PLATFORM_ADMIN_NAV_LAYOUT` under `admin`, with `admin.platform-settings` shifted from sortOrder 6 to 7 and a test asserting the admin siblings hold no duplicate sort orders. **The default layout only covers installs that have not customised their admin sidebar**, because `resolveTenantSidebarLayout` returns a saved layout verbatim; `sync-nav-layout.ts` exists for exactly that gap but only ever handled `tenant_layout`. It now also syncs `platform_admin_layout` — which needed `addNavNodesToLayout` to be passed `DEFAULT_PLATFORM_ADMIN_NAV_LAYOUT`, since with the tenant defaults every admin node is skipped as "not in the default layout", i.e. it would have reported success while doing nothing. Both keys now come from the shared `NAV_LAYOUT_SETTING_KEYS` rather than being re-typed as literals. Run `npx tsx prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=admin.referrals --dry-run` before the live run. **UI-rule cleanup across all four referral surfaces:** every `৳{n.toFixed(2)}` replaced with `formatBDT()` (11 sites — the money rule is non-negotiable in CLAUDE.md and this module broke it everywhere, including a `৳` baked into the `payment.success` i18n string in all three locales, which had to move out of the translation so the symbol would not double up); the partner portal's page-local toast state, `setTimeout` and emerald banner replaced with the global `toast` store; and its hand-rolled `font-black uppercase tracking-widest` status pill replaced with the shared `StatusBadge`. **New finding, logged rather than fixed:** the shared `DataTable`'s own `<th>` carries the same three banned classes, so the new page test had to scope its assertion to `<td>`. That is one line to fix but changes the header of every table in the app, so it wants a deliberate visual call — logged under a new UI rules heading. **Verified:** frontend 180 suites / 2,149 green including a new `referrals/page.test.tsx` (3 cases: grouped-thousands formatting proving `formatBDT` is actually in the path, no bespoke pill in any body cell, no page-local toast banner); backend 151 suites / 2,334 green with 2 new nav-layout cases; `shared-types` `dist/` rebuilt so the backend and frontend both see the new layout; both typechecks at their known baselines. **Not done:** never opened in a browser, so the nav entry has not been seen in a real sidebar and the `sync-nav-layout.ts` platform-admin path has never run against a database — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **Referral list queries stop being N+1, archived partners stop masquerading as live ones, and a partner can no longer refer themselves** — fourth of the referral follow-ups; robustness rather than money correctness. **`listReferees` issued 1 + 3n queries** — one `count` and two `aggregate`s per referee, so 1,501 queries at 500 partners for a page that renders one row each. Now a single `groupBy(['referee_id', 'status'])` over `referral_signups` feeding a `Map`, with tests asserting `aggregate` and `count` are *not* called at all, so a regression back to per-row queries fails rather than merely getting slower. Stats gained `reversed_count`/`reversed_amount` while the shape was already being touched. **`listCommissions` is the one referral query with no natural bound** — it grows with total signups, not with one partner's history — so it pages, capped at 200 with a default of 50. The response is **wrapped rather than a bare array** specifically so it can report `total`/`has_more`: silent truncation reads as "that's all of them" when it isn't. Safe to reshape because `getAdminReferralCommissions` existed in `api.ts` but had **no caller anywhere in the frontend** — `GET /admin/referrals/commissions` has never been wired to a screen. `getLedger` is deliberately left unpaged: it is bounded by one referee's own history and the detail page needs the totals. **Archived referees were returned alongside active ones with no filter** — `listReferees` had no `where` clause at all, so a soft-deleted partner looked live apart from a badge. Now `deleted_at: null` by default with an explicit "Show archived" toggle on the admin list; the query DTO coerces `?include_archived=true|1|false|0` and **rejects anything else rather than falling back to false**, same principle as the `limit`/`offset` coercion, which 400s on `?limit=abc` instead of silently defaulting. **Self-referral is now refused at signup:** a partner using their own code would have collected commission on their own subscription *and* taken the signup discount on top. Checked two ways — `referee.user_id === userId` and a case-insensitive email match — because a referee created but not yet linked to a `User` has no `user_id` to match on. It throws rather than silently dropping the referral, so the person is told to remove the code instead of quietly losing a discount they think they are getting; the whole signup transaction rolls back, which is why the check sits next to the row it guards rather than in the controller. An unknown code still passes through harmlessly, as before. **Verified:** backend 151 unit suites / 2,332 green (+23: single-query stats, zero-signup and no-referee edges, the archived default and its override, page-window coercion and its four rejection cases, `has_more` on a full and a final page, and four signup cases incl. both self-referral detection paths); frontend 179 suites / 2,146 green; locale parity green across the new `showArchived` key; both typechecks at their known baselines. **Not done:** never opened in a browser, and the `groupBy` has only ever run against a mock — the query shape is the one thing here that a live database could still disagree with — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **A refunded tenant no longer leaves a payable referral commission standing** — third of the referral follow-ups, and the first that needed a schema change. `processRefund` recorded the refund and downgraded the tenant to FREE but never touched `referral_signups`, so pay → EARNED → refund left the platform owing a partner a commission on revenue it had just given back. **New terminal `REVERSED` state** on `ReferralCommissionStatus` plus `reversed_at` / `reversal_reason` / `reversed_after_paid` on `ReferralSignup`; migration `20260804090000_add_referral_commission_reversal`, additive only (one `ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS`, three `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, no `DROP`, no `ALTER COLUMN`) because production reconciles its schema with `prisma db push` on container start and never executes the migrations directory — the file exists to keep the history honest, not because it is the mechanism that ships. **The state machine reads the pre-refund status and branches on it:** `PENDING` is left alone (nothing was earned, and the tenant may yet pay); `EARNED` becomes `REVERSED` outright; `PAID` also becomes `REVERSED` but sets `reversed_after_paid`, because that money has already left the platform and cannot be un-sent. **`REVERSED` is terminal by design** — a referral does not fall back to `PENDING`, since the signup discount it granted was already consumed and re-earning would pay the same commission twice. **The netting falls out of the ledger arithmetic rather than needing a second mechanism:** `total_earned_amount` excludes `REVERSED` while the `RefereePayment` row still counts in `total_paid_amount`, so a commission reversed after payout turns into exactly the `overpaid_amount` credit added by the previous change, and that credit nets off the next payout — verified by a test that asserts a 300-earned / 200-reversed-after-paid / 200-paid ledger reads `balance_due` 300 → 100 with `overpaid_amount` 0. Summary also gained `reversed` and `total_reversed_amount`. The clawback runs **before** any downgrade and is fire-and-forget with the same log-plus-Sentry treatment as the earn path, so a bookkeeping failure cannot fail a refund the customer has already been given; a test asserts the refund still resolves when the update throws. **Separate money bug found and fixed in passing:** the admin referee *list* computed its balance column as `max(0, earned_amount - paid_amount)`, but `earned_amount` already sums only commissions still in `EARNED` (i.e. it *is* the outstanding balance) while `paid_amount` sums ones already settled — so it double-discounted and **disagreed with the detail page**: a referee owed ৳300 with ৳200 previously settled showed ৳100 in the list and ৳300 on their ledger. Now reads `earned_amount` directly. **Frontend:** `REVERSED` added to the status union, the admin badge (danger tone) and the partner portal badge (red), with `REVERSED` labels across en/bn/ms in both the admin and portal catalogues. **Verified:** backend 151 unit suites / 2,309 green (8 new clawback cases: earned reversal, paid-reversal flagging, PENDING left alone, no double reversal, never-referred tenant, reason fallback to the reference id, failure logging, plus a guard that a `REVERSED` referral is not re-earned on a later activation, and 2 new ledger cases); frontend 179 suites / 2,146 green; locale catalogue parity green; `prisma validate` clean and the client regenerates with the new enum member; both typechecks at their known baselines (backend 3, frontend 1 pre-existing test-file errors). **Not done:** never run against a real database — the migration and the `db push` path are both unexercised here, so a restore-a-dump rehearsal is the honest next step before this deploys; never opened in a browser — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **A referee payout is reconciled against the commissions it clears, instead of being two independent numbers** — second of the referral follow-ups. `recordPayment` took a free-form `amount` and *separately* flipped whichever `commission_ids` it was handed to `PAID`, with nothing tying the two together: a ৳1 payment could clear ৳50,000 of earned commission, and `getLedger`'s `Math.max(0, …)` then absorbed the difference so the ledger read "৳0 due" and pointed at nothing. **`amount` is now optional and defaults to exactly what the selected commissions are worth**, which is the only figure that keeps the ledger consistent and is more trustworthy than the admin form re-deriving it. A different figure is still allowed but has to say so via `allow_partial` — silence no longer means "trust me" — and the rejection names both numbers so the admin can see which side is wrong. **A second silent-wrong-behaviour fixed in passing:** commission ids that were not payable (already paid, another referee's, or mistyped) were filtered out by `earned.filter(s => ids.includes(s.id))` and the payout recorded as though they had been included; unknown ids are now named in a 400 rather than dropped on the floor. **`getLedger` gained `overpaid_amount`** — the mirror of `balance_due` — because `recordPayment` refusing to *create* drift does nothing about rows written before this change; `balance_due` stays clamped so the partner-facing number is never negative, and the drift is now carried in its own field, surfaced on the admin ledger **only when non-zero** (on a healthy ledger it is noise; when it is not zero it is the most important number on the page). All money sums go through a `round2` helper — comparing an `amount` against a float sum of Decimals would otherwise reject a legitimate payout over a rounding cent, and there is a test for exactly the `0.1 + 0.2` case. **Frontend follows the same contract rather than guessing:** the payment modal's amount field may now be left blank to mean "settle in full", gained a part-payment checkbox, and reports the amount **the server actually recorded** rather than the one typed — with the backend's reconciliation message surfaced inline. New i18n keys `amountHint`/`partialLabel`/`partialHint`/`summary.overpaid` across en/bn/ms. **Verified:** backend 151 unit suites / 2,299 green (11 new referral cases incl. default-to-owed, under- and over-payment rejection, explicit part-payment, float drift, and unknown-id naming); frontend 179 suites / 2,146 green including a new `RefereePaymentModal.test.tsx` (6 cases) covering blank-means-full, the part-payment flag and the server-error surface; locale catalogue parity green; frontend `tsc --noEmit` at its known baseline of 1 pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` error. **Note on the earlier characterisation test:** `getLedger`'s clamp test from the previous change was rewritten rather than deleted — it now asserts the drift is *surfaced* instead of asserting it is *absorbed*. **Not done:** never opened in a browser; the modal changes are covered by unit tests only — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **Referral commission ledger gets its first tests, strict query validation, and stops swallowing a failed commission write** — came out of a "what's the status of the referral system, and how does it work?" review. The module has been live and untouched since 2026-07-04, and it moves money to people outside the platform, so the finding that mattered most was that **`apps/backend/src/referrals/` had no `.spec.ts` at all** — and the one referral assertion anywhere in the backend was `billing.service.spec.ts` stubbing `referralSignup.findUnique` to `null`, which meant the discount and commission paths were not merely undertested but *never executed by a test*. Three new suites, 45 tests: `referrals.service.spec.ts` covers the PENDING → EARNED → PAID state machine, which commissions a payout actually clears, referee-code generation (including the short-name `padEnd` branch), the archive-vs-hard-delete split, and the four outcomes of `resolveActiveRefereeForUser`'s email-fallback auto-linking; `referee.guard.spec.ts` covers the portal's only access check including the decimal→number coercion; `referrals.dto.spec.ts` runs the **real `ValidationPipe` configuration from `main.ts`** rather than validating classes directly, following the `admin-tenants.dto.spec.ts` precedent, so a regression fails the way production would. 12 more tests added to `billing.service.spec.ts` for the discount at checkout and the commission on activation. **The validation fix is the same class of bug `ListLeadsDto` was introduced to fix on the CRM side:** `ListCommissionsQueryDto.status` was *typed* as a `'PENDING' | 'EARNED' | 'PAID'` union but *validated* with a bare `@IsString()`, so `?status=garbage` went straight into a Prisma `where` and surfaced as a 500 instead of a 400; same for a non-uuid `referee_id`. Now `@IsEnum` + `@IsUUID`, with `@Transform(emptyToUndefined)` so a cleared filter still works, and `RecordPaymentDto.commission_ids` gained the `@IsArray()` it was missing under `@IsString({ each: true })`. **`status` imports the Prisma-generated `ReferralCommissionStatus` rather than re-declaring the three values** — the `price-lists.dto.ts` precedent — because a hand-copied enum here is exactly the drift TODO.md already has a catalogue-guard spec for elsewhere. Verified the DTO spec is not tautological by reverting the decorators and confirming 4 of its tests fail. **Third fix:** `earnReferralCommission` was fired as `.catch(() => {})`, so a failed commission write vanished with no log, no retry and no alert — money owed to a partner that would simply never appear in any ledger. It now logs through a `Logger` and reports to Sentry with the tenant id and amount, while staying deliberately un-awaited (a bookkeeping failure must not fail an activation that has already been paid for); a test asserts both the log line and that the activation still returns. **Two tests deliberately characterise known gaps rather than endorsing them**, each commented and cross-referenced to its follow-up: the commission base being list price rather than the discounted amount actually charged, and `getLedger`'s `Math.max(0, …)` absorbing an overpayment instead of surfacing it — so when those are fixed the diff reads as an intentional behaviour flip. Also made the referral mock defaults explicit in the billing spec's `beforeEach`, since `jest.resetAllMocks()` was clearing the `mockResolvedValue(null)` declared on the mock and the existing tests only passed because `undefined` happens to be falsy too. **Verified:** backend 155 unit suites / 2,314 tests green; the 4 `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites fail identically before and after on a missing `DATABASE_URL` (no local Postgres); `tsc --noEmit` error count byte-identical to a clean `git stash` (3 known pre-existing test-file errors in `accounting.service.spec.ts` ×2 and `billing-scheduler.service.spec.ts` ×1). **Not done, and logged above instead:** the three money-correctness bugs this review surfaced — list-price commission base, unreconciled payout amounts, and no refund clawback — are behaviour changes needing a policy decision, so this change deliberately adds only the harness they will need — done 2026-08-04

- [x] **Task titles are editable in place, and tasks finally have a description you can read** — asked as "task title needs to be editable easily; add a description field with a little rich text, kept below the title with reasonable space". **Neither field had a UI at all**: `TaskDetailPanel` rendered the title as static text in the modal header and never rendered `description` — which the board card has been advertising with an `AlignLeft` marker since 3A, so a card could say "this has a description" while offering no way to read or write one. The backend needed nothing: `UpdateTaskDto` already accepted `title` and `description`, `update()` already wrote both, and a rename already recorded a `RENAMED` activity. **Title:** click the heading, it becomes an input; Enter or clicking away saves, Escape discards. A blank title is ignored rather than sent — the backend's `@MinLength(1)` would reject it anyway, and quietly erasing the one thing that names a card is worse than dropping the edit. **Escape needed `stopPropagation()`**: `ModalShell` listens for it on the document, so cancelling a field edit would otherwise close the whole card and take the rest of the edit with it (tested, in both editors). **Description:** sits directly under the title as the first thing in the body, above the hours metrics, with markdown rendered through the existing `Markdown` component — `skipHtml`, no `img`, so tenant-written text cannot make an outbound request or inject markup. **Stored as the text the user typed, not as HTML**: it stays legible in an export, in the API, and in a notification email, and there is no stored blob to sanitise on the way out. `react-markdown` is `lazy()`-loaded so the board does not carry it until a card opens. **The "little rich text" is a new shared `RichTextEditor`** (`components/ui`) — a textarea plus bold/italic/strike/code/bulleted/numbered/link, Ctrl+B and Ctrl+I, Ctrl+Enter to save. All the interesting behaviour is in `lib/markdown-format.ts` as pure functions over `{value, selectionStart, selectionEnd}`, because toggling a marker off again and prefixing a multi-line selection are string problems that deserve tests rather than a DOM: markers are removed whether they sit inside or just outside the selection, a list command takes whole lines even when half a line is selected, bullets↔numbers is one click, and **italic refuses to eat one star off a bold run** (`*` being half of `**` is the obvious trap; selecting inside `**bold**` and clicking I gives `***bold***`, not `*bold*`). Toolbar buttons `preventDefault()` on mousedown — a plain click blurs the textarea first and there is nothing left to wrap. The same editor is now the description field on the New Task modal, so a description can be written when the task is created rather than only afterwards. **One shared-component change:** `ModalHeader`'s title block got `min-w-0 flex-1` so a title that is itself a control fills the header instead of sitting in a column the width of its own text. **Verified:** frontend 178 suites / 2116 green — 24 new (14 on `markdown-format`, 10 on the panel: click-to-edit, save on Enter and on blur with trimming, Escape discarding, blank refused, the add-a-description affordance, markdown rendered as markup rather than source, save, clear-to-empty, a toolbar wrap end-to-end, and Escape in the editor not closing the card). Lint clean on every touched file; typecheck at its existing baseline (one pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` error in `contact/page.test.tsx`); en/bn/ms catalog parity green. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — the spacing and the toolbar's feel on a phone are the part unit tests cannot answer — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Released `dev` → `main` and deployed to the VPS** — PR #445, eleven commits: Projects Phase 3 (#442), tenant-editable conversation channels + CRM Setup (#443), storefront session scoping (#444). All CI green before merge (lint, backend, frontend, build, dependency audit; Playwright skipped as usual). `scripts/deploy.sh main` rebuilt both images and recreated the containers; `app.erp71.com` and `/api/v1/health` both 200 afterwards. **The thing worth knowing: production applies the schema with `prisma db push`, not `migrate deploy`** (`apps/backend/Dockerfile:91`), so this release's four migration files under `packages/database/prisma/migrations/` **were never executed against production** — the schema arrived by diff and the migration SQL, including the conversation-channel backfill, remains unexercised outside the local Postgres check in the CRM entry below. The boot-time `sync:lead-taxonomy` is what actually seeded channels, and it worked: **147 rows = 7 channels × all 21 tenants, and 0 `LeadConversation` rows left with a null `channel_id`** (there are no conversations in production yet, so the backfill path itself is still untested on real data). Verified against the live database, which also turned up a naming detail worth remembering for future ops queries — **this schema uses Prisma's default PascalCase table names** (`"ConversationChannel"`, `"LeadConversation"`) for models without an `@@map`, so unquoted lowercase SQL fails with "relation does not exist"; the Phase 3 project tables (`project_labels`, `project_task_labels`, `project_task_watchers`) do map to snake_case. **Not done: the `crm.setup` nav-layout sync** — logged under OPERATIONAL → Deployment above — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Project Management Phase 3L + 3D + 3K — board columns belong to a board, task attachments, and card covers** — the fifth batch of `docs/projects/project-management-phase-3.md`, leaving only 3J (archive) outstanding besides the postponed 3E and deferred 3G. **3L is the decision taken on 2026-08-03 made literal**: `project_task_statuses` gained a nullable `project_id` — NULL marks the tenant **template** (what Project Setup edits, and what a new project is seeded from), non-null is a board's own column and what a task's `status_id` points at — plus an advisory `wip_limit`. **`20260803160000_per_project_board_columns` is the only migration in this phase that moves data:** it copies the template onto every existing project and repoints that project's tasks at the copy, matched by name, which is safe because the pre-migration unique index made `(tenant_id, name)` unique. Nothing appears to move on the day it ships. **Five details worth keeping.** (1) **The over-limit count is taken from the unfiltered column** — a filter narrowing the view to two cards must not make a column of twelve look within a limit of three (tested). (2) **A status id from another board is refused on create and move** — it would not merely be wrong, it would put the card somewhere nobody on that board can see; template rows are still accepted since they belong to no project (mutation-checked: removing the guard fails that test and only that test). (3) **Name clashes and the default column are both scoped to the board** — two projects may each have a "Blocked", and one board's default is not another's. (4) **Postgres treats NULLs as distinct**, so the new `(tenant_id, project_id, name)` unique does not constrain template rows and the service checks those in code. (5) **The detail panel now loads the task's own board columns rather than the template** — it was reading the template, which after this change would have offered columns the card's board does not have. WIP limits are advisory: the column is marked over-limit, never blocked, because a limit is a prompt to talk about the queue and not a lock. New page at `/projects/[id]/columns` (add, reorder, WIP limit, delete), linked from the board header. **3D closes the Cloudinary leak logged at the top of this file, for this model at least.** `ProjectAttachment` had a model since Phase 1 and no API; the reason to build it carefully is that `AssetsService.uploadFile()` returns only a URL, which cannot be turned back into a `public_id`, so a row deleted that way strands its file and is billed forever. This path uses `uploadBuffer()` and stores a `storage_key`, and deleting an attachment purges the asset. **`uploadBuffer` and `deleteFile` gained an optional resource type**: a PDF through Cloudinary's image pipeline is rejected or mangled, so it is stored `raw` — and **a delete has to name the same resource type or Cloudinary removes nothing**, which is exactly how a PDF would linger after its row was gone (both default to `image`, so the existing contact-card caller is untouched, and there is a test for each direction). JPEG/PNG/WebP/PDF capped at 5 MB, checked on both sides — the client refuses before turning 20 MB into base64, the server refuses because a client check is not a control. The row is deleted before the asset: a broken link is worse than a few bytes of orphaned storage. File names are sanitised rather than trusted. `VoucherAttachment` still has the original problem and stays tracked separately. **3K** is a colour strip across the top of the card, reusing the label palette rather than adding a near-identical enum. **One shared primitive changed:** `StatusBadge` now spreads span attributes so a badge can carry an accessible name — a badge is often the only rendering of a state ("3/2" for an over-limit column), and without `aria-label` that reads as two numbers to a screen reader. **Verified:** backend 152 suites / 2254 green (35 new: attachment parse/type/size/sanitising, upload and delete ordering, raw-vs-image on both paths, the legacy row with no storage key, per-project column seeding and scoping, WIP limits, the cross-project column refusal, and cover set/clear/absent), with only the 4 known Postgres-needing integration suites failing as they do on the base commit. Frontend 177 suites / 2116 green (27 new). Two mutation checks, each landing on exactly its own test. `prisma validate` clean; en/bn/ms parity green; typecheck at baseline both apps; lint clean; `next build` emits the new `/projects/[id]/columns` route. **A late finding changed the shape of 3L: production applies the schema with `prisma db push`, never `migrate deploy` (`apps/backend/Dockerfile`), so the data migration would never have run there** — `db push` would have added `project_id`, left every task on a template row, and `board()` would have matched those against the project's own columns and found nothing, rendering **every existing board empty**. The backfill therefore lives in the code path that actually runs: `seedProjectColumns` adopts a project's tasks off the template as it copies the columns, and `board()` self-heals by comparing the loaded tasks' `status_id` against the loaded columns — a set lookup over rows already fetched, so no extra query — repairing once if any task is orphaned (mutation-checked: forcing the branch off fails that test alone). A task whose column name has no counterpart on the board is left where it is, because a visible gap beats a silent reassignment. The SQL migration is kept as belt-and-braces for `migrate deploy`. **Not done: not opened in a browser, and the production `db push` shape this defends against has not been exercised** — see the follow-up above — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Dual login for customer/owner users — storefront sessions are their own thing now.** The two logins shared a `User` row *and* a token shape: `StorefrontService.issueCustomerAuthResponse` signed the byte-identical `{sub, email, tv}` payload as `AuthService.generateAuthResponse`, and `JwtStrategy` only resolved the user, so **a token minted at `POST /storefront/:slug/auth/login` was a fully valid ERP token**. Containment was incidental — `TenantInterceptor` demands a `TenantUser` row, so a pure shopper got nothing from business endpoints — but anything guarded by `JwtAuthGuard` alone (`/auth/me`, profile update, avatar, change-password) was reachable, and the storefront path checked neither TOTP nor `REQUIRE_EMAIL_VERIFICATION`. An owner with 2FA enabled could hand over their password at *any* tenant's storefront and get an app session. Tokens now carry a `scope` claim (`app` / `storefront`); `JwtAuthGuard` rejects storefront-scoped ones and the new `StorefrontCustomerGuard` rejects app-scoped ones, so each surface only accepts its own. Storefront tokens are additionally bound to the shop that issued them via a `tid` claim, checked in `getCustomerProfile`/`getCustomerOrders`. **A missing `scope` is read as `app`** so live ERP sessions survive the deploy; storefront customers re-login once. TOTP is honoured on the storefront path too — login returns `{requires_2fa, user_id}` and `POST /storefront/:slug/auth/2fa/verify` completes it, with the code step on the existing sign-in page (signup hands off to it when an existing 2FA account registers at a new shop). **`Customer.user_id` was globally `@unique`**, so one `User` could link to one `Customer` platform-wide and the same person simply could not register at a second storefront — signup passed its per-tenant "already registered" check, then hit the constraint and surfaced as an unmapped Prisma 500. Now `@@unique([tenant_id, user_id])` (`User.storefrontCustomer` became `storefrontCustomers`). **Sessions revoke independently:** new `User.storefront_token_version` versions storefront tokens (`stv`) so an ERP logout no longer silently drops the same person's shopping sessions and vice versa; a password change or reset bumps both, since the storefront login accepts the same password. Storefront sign-out was client-only — `localStorage.removeItem` left the token live until expiry — and now calls a real revoke endpoint. Storefront login/signup/logout also emit audit events, which they never did. — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Dual login for customer/owner users — storefront sessions are their own thing now.** The two logins shared a `User` row *and* a token shape: `StorefrontService.issueCustomerAuthResponse` signed the byte-identical `{sub, email, tv}` payload as `AuthService.generateAuthResponse`, and `JwtStrategy` only resolved the user, so **a token minted at `POST /storefront/:slug/auth/login` was a fully valid ERP token**. Storefront login/signup/logout also emit audit events, which they never did. — **deployed to production 2026-08-03** (PR #445); the `scope`-missing-means-`app` fallback did its job, since no re-login was needed to reach the app after the container recreate — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Conversation channels are a tenant-owned list now, and every CRM list lives on one CRM Setup page** — asked as "make communication channel list dynamic … keep a list as seed data. This and other setting type items can be taken to make a new page CRM Setup. Tabs can be used for the lists." **The channel list was hardcoded in five places** — a Prisma enum-shaped DTO (`LeadConversationType`), two `CONVERSATION_TYPES` consts, two `typeIcons` emoji maps, and a `leadConversations.types` block in three locales — so a shop that talks to customers on Telegram or Imo had to log it as "Other" forever. It is now a `ConversationChannel` table, built to exactly the shape `LeadSourceOption` already established: immutable `code`, tenant-editable `name`, `is_system` rows that deactivate rather than delete (the idempotent tenant sync would otherwise resurrect them), and reassign-on-delete. Seeded with the seven old enum members, `icon` carrying the emoji the lists used to hardcode. **`LeadConversation.type` is kept alongside the new `channel_id`, deliberately.** Every filter, sort and `groupBy` on that table reads `type`, and the existing `(tenant_id, type, created_at)` index serves them directly; resolving a code to an id on each request would buy nothing. So the service resolves the client's `type` — id *or* code, per `resolveByIdOrCode` — and writes both, always the resolved code so an id-sending client is still visible to filters. The one place that could drift is reassignment, and `remove()` moves `type` with the FK for that reason, with a test pinning it. **The three lists share one service rather than getting a module each.** `CrmLeadTaxonomyService` gained a `CHANNEL` kind and a small `CONSUMERS` map naming the table and FK each list is counted against — the only structural difference between them. `usage()` and `remove()` read that map instead of hardcoding `db.lead`. **One channel-specific rule:** the last *active* channel cannot be deactivated or deleted. Sources and categories have no such floor (a lead can be created without either), but an empty channel list means the log-conversation form has nothing to offer and conversations stop being loggable at all. **CRM Setup** (`/crm/setup`) is four tabs — Channels, Lead Sources, Lead Categories, Custom Fields — over one `CrmListPanel` used three times, so the tabs cannot drift apart in behaviour. The tab is in the URL so a list can be linked to (`?tab=channels`) and survives a reload. The two old pages redirect into it rather than being deleted, and the sidebar's separate `crm.lead-taxonomy` / `crm.custom-fields` nodes collapse into one `crm.setup`; layouts a tenant customised before this still name the old ids, which `buildNavModulesFromLayout` already drops silently, so **`npx tsx prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=crm.setup` is needed after deploy** for those tenants. **Labels come from the row, not from i18n** — same rule the lead taxonomy set: a tenant renaming a channel sees exactly that in every locale, and the stored `code` is the fallback so a conversation logged against a since-deleted channel still renders rather than showing a blank cell. The AI drafter's channel picker now sends the channel's *label* rather than its code, because it goes straight into the prompt where "WhatsApp" reads as intended and "ONLINE_MEETING" does not. **Verified against a real Postgres 16, not just mocks:** the migration was applied to a database built at the pre-change schema and holding two tenants, four conversations and one `type` value the old enum never allowed — it seeded 7 channels per tenant, gave the stray value its own non-system row, and backfilled all four rows with zero left unmatched. Then the sync script was run twice on it (no-op both times, as intended, since the migration had done the work) and separately against a `db push` database with no channel rows at all, which is the path production actually takes — it seeded, reconciled the odd value, backfilled, and no-opped on the second run. 22 new tests (channel resolution and rejection of unknown/retired channels on create, the FK-plus-code reassignment, both last-active guards, usage counted against conversations, the seed reaching signup through the CommonJS mirror, and four on the conversations page proving labels, the icon fallback and the filter all come from the tenant list). Backend **143 suites / 2120 green**, frontend **172 suites / 1953 green**, both typechecks at their pre-existing baseline, lint clean, frontend build clean. **Not done: never opened in a browser** — the tab strip, the emoji column and the setup page's mobile layout are verified by tests and the build, not by eye — **deployed to production 2026-08-03** (PR #445); the boot-time sync seeded 7 channels for all 21 tenants (147 rows) with no conversation left unbackfilled, confirming the `db push` path the entry above predicted production would take. **The nav sync this entry called for has *not* been run**, so tenants with a customised sidebar cannot see CRM Setup in the menu yet — logged under OPERATIONAL → Deployment — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Project Management Phase 3C + 3I — task comments, an activity feed, and watchers that actually notify** — the fourth batch of `docs/projects/project-management-phase-3.md`; migration `20260803140000_add_task_activity_and_watchers` is additive (`project_task_activities`, `project_task_watchers`, one enum). **3C closes a Phase 1 loose end**: `ProjectComment` had a model *and* a `CreateCommentDto` written for it and **no service and no controller behind either** — while `_count.comments` was already selected and, since 3A, already rendered on the card, so the count was visible before the thread existed. Comments now list, create, and edit or delete **your own only** — the feed is an audit trail and nobody rewrites anybody else's line in it; that rule lives in the service rather than in a permission, so it cannot be granted away (mutation-checked: removing the ownership check fails both refusal tests and nothing else). **3I** adds an append-only `ProjectTaskActivity` and a `ProjectTaskWatcher` join. **`data` holds the before/after payload rather than a rendered sentence, so the line is composed client-side and therefore translates** — a stored English string would be permanently English in a trilingual app; `describeActivity` lives in `task-activity.ts` with 17 tests including the unknown-type fallback (a newer server must not render a blank line) and the missing-value dash (never "undefined"). **There is deliberately no `COMMENTED` activity type**: comments are their own rows and the panel merges the two streams by timestamp, so a row per comment would draw every comment twice. **Watchers actually do something, which is what separates this from bookkeeping** — they fan out to the existing in-app `Notification` table when a task moves, is reassigned, or gets a comment, and **nobody is ever notified of their own action** (mutation-checked: dropping the actor filter fails that test alone), because being told what you just did is how a notification bell stops being read. You are subscribed automatically on creating a task, being assigned one, or commenting on one — a watch list nobody is ever added to is a feature nobody uses. **Everything in the activity service is best-effort and never throws**: a task move that succeeded must not report failure because an audit row would not write or a notification would not send, so `record`, `watch` and `notifyWatchers` all catch and log (three tests assert the swallow). **One ordering detail with a test on it: a comment notifies *before* it subscribes the author** — the other way round, the author is in the watcher set by the time the fan-out reads it and gets told about their own comment; the actor filter alone would cover it, but the ordering makes the intent survive a refactor of that filter. Activity is recorded **per field that actually changed**, not once per save, so the feed reads as a list of edits rather than "updated the task" forever — a field submitted unchanged records nothing, and dropping a card back into the column it came from is likewise not news (both tested). **Permissions: comments, watching and the feed are gated on `VIEW_PROJECTS` rather than `MANAGE_PROJECT_TASKS`** — if you can see the board you can discuss it and subscribe to it — which avoids a new permission and the backfill migration one would need. The panel gained one timeline section (composer, merged feed, watch toggle) that **loads independently of the panel and says so when it fails**, rather than showing a broken feed as an empty one — the `useServerList` trap in miniature. **Verified:** backend 149 suites / 2185 green (32 new across two new spec files plus 10 in the tasks spec), frontend 177 suites / 2089 green (29 new). Two mutation checks, both landing on exactly the intended tests. `prisma validate` clean; en/bn/ms parity green with ~25 new keys per locale; typecheck at baseline both apps; lint clean; `next build` clean. **Not done: not opened in a browser, and neither of this phase's two migrations has been run against a real Postgres.** `@mentions` deliberately left out and logged above — the notification path now exists, so it is parsing plus a recipient list — **deployed to production 2026-08-03** (PR #445); `project_task_activities` and `project_task_watchers` confirmed live, though **by `db push`, not by running this phase's migration** — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Project Management Phase 3F + 3H — task labels and a start date** — the third batch of `docs/projects/project-management-phase-3.md`, and the phase's first schema change: migration `20260803130000_add_project_labels_and_start_date` is purely additive (one nullable `project_tasks.start_date`, the `ProjectLabel`/`ProjectTaskLabel` tables, and a `ProjectLabelColor` enum), so it deploys ahead of the application without breaking the running one. **Colour is an enum, not a hex string** — the UI rules forbid arbitrary hex, and a free colour field is how a board ends up with twelve indistinguishable greys; each value maps to a Tailwind class pair **written out in full**, because Tailwind scans source text and `bg-${color}-100` compiles to nothing (there is a test asserting every palette entry resolves to a real written-out pair). **Labels are tenant-scoped, not per-board, which is a deliberate departure from the per-project-columns decision**: a label is a vocabulary ("Blocked", "Client waiting") and the cross-project Tasks page and tenant-level sprint board are only useful if it is shared — per-board labels would fragment it exactly the way per-project statuses would. Columns are structural and belong to a board; labels are descriptive and belong to the workspace. Adding `project_id` later is additive if a board ever needs private labels. **Two things in the write path worth keeping:** the endpoint **replaces** a task's whole label set rather than patching it, so a toggle sends everything that should remain and there is no add/remove pair to keep in step (`undefined` still means "leave alone", `[]` means "remove them all" — both tested); and **every label id is checked against the tenant before writing**, because the join row carries a `tenant_id` that nothing else validates, so an unchecked id would let one tenant tag with another's label (mutation-checked: removing the guard fails that test and only that test). Ids are de-duplicated so a repeated id cannot violate the composite primary key. **Deleting a label in use is allowed rather than refused**, unlike a board column which still blocks on tasks — retiring "Blocked" from the vocabulary should not mean untagging fifty cards first; the join rows cascade, the tasks are untouched, and the response reports how many were untagged. Surfaces: chips on the board card (above the badge row, since colour is what the eye scans a column by), a fourth board filter that only appears once the tenant has labels, toggle chips in the detail panel, and management in `/projects/settings` beside the board columns. **3H** adds `start_date` alongside `due_date`, both editable in the panel and saved on change. **Clearing a date needed a backend fix worth knowing about: `@IsOptional()` skips only `null` and `undefined`, so an empty string reaches `@IsDateString()` and 400s — while PATCH reads `undefined` as "leave alone". Without a `@ValidateIf`, "no due date" was not expressible at all.** The same pattern very likely affects `UpdateProjectDto.customerId` and the Phase 2 project edit form — logged as a follow-up above rather than fixed blind. **Also fixed in passing:** `Field` only ties its label to its control when given `htmlFor`, so the new date inputs had no accessible name until they passed one — the general case is logged above; and an unused `Field` import was removed from the settings page. **Verified:** backend 147 suites / 2153 green (13 new: label set replacement, the empty-array clear, absent-field no-op, the cross-tenant refusal, tenant-scoped lookup, de-duplication, tagging on create, and the three date behaviours), with only the 4 known Postgres-needing integration suites failing as they do on the base commit. Frontend 176 suites / 2062 green (23 new: label filtering including cards carrying several labels and the no-label case, the palette-to-class mapping and its grey fallback, chips on the card, the filter hidden when a workspace has no labels, the board surviving a failed labels request, and the panel's toggle/add/remove/clear payloads plus date display, save, clear-with-empty-string and the start-after-due warning). `prisma validate` clean and the client regenerates; en/bn/ms parity green; typecheck at baseline; lint clean; `next build` clean. **Not done: not opened in a browser, and the migration has not been run against a real Postgres** — only validated and hand-written to match the schema — **deployed to production 2026-08-03** (PR #445); `project_labels` and `project_task_labels` confirmed live, but **the migration file still has not run anywhere** — production took the `db push` path, so it remains unexercised — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Project Management Phase 3M + 3N — add a card without leaving the board, filter it, and drag it on a phone** — the second batch of `docs/projects/project-management-phase-3.md`, with 3E (subtasks) postponed on request. **The headline is that the board was read-only on a phone and nobody had noticed:** HTML5 `draggable` produces no drag events from touch input on iOS Safari or Android Chrome, so a phone user could see the board and could not move a card on it. Dragging is now **pointer-based** — one code path for mouse, touch and pen. The awkward part is that touch has to serve two gestures on the same surface, scrolling a column and picking up a card, and making the whole card touch-draggable would have cost the scroll: so the **card body arms a drag only for a mouse** (with a 6px threshold, below which the gesture is the tap that opens the card — a hand is never perfectly still on a click), and **touch drags start from a grip** that opts out of `touch-action` while the card stays `touch-pan-y`. A ghost follows the pointer, because on touch there is no cursor to tell you the card has been picked up, and a blue bar shows where it will land. Releasing over the page chrome is a no-op rather than a move to somewhere arbitrary. **The geometry lives in `board-drag.ts` as pure functions** (`movedFar`, `resolveDropTarget`, `toFullIndex`) precisely because jsdom has no layout — a component test cannot answer "which card is the pointer over", but handing those functions rectangles directly can. **Filters** (assignee / priority / due: overdue, today, this week, no date) apply client-side over the board already in memory, so no new request and no new query parameter; the **assignee list is built from the cards present rather than the project roster**, since you can only usefully filter by someone who holds a card here, and it keys users and employees apart so the two assignee columns cannot contradict each other. **The trap in filtering a drag surface: the server reorders against the whole column, not the filtered one** — dropping "above the second visible card" while a filter is on would otherwise land it at position 1 of twenty, jumping it over every hidden card, so `toFullIndex` maps the visible position back onto the full column (tested). A filtered-empty column says "Nothing matches the filters", not "Nothing here", and **empty columns are kept rather than dropped** — the board's shape must not change as you type, and you have to be able to drop into them. **3M** is a composer at the foot of every column: type a title, Add, keep typing; it stays open with an empty field so a column can be filled in one go, keeps the text if the save fails, and closes on Escape. **A test-infrastructure fix came out of this and is worth knowing about: jsdom implements no `PointerEvent`, and Testing Library's fallback silently drops `pointerType`, `pointerId` and `button`** — a handler branching on any of them sees `undefined` and takes a path it would never take in a browser. The first version of the touch tests passed for exactly that wrong reason; `jest.setup.ts` now polyfills `PointerEvent` over `MouseEvent`, which is what made the mouse and touch paths genuinely distinguishable, and any future pointer-driven UI inherits it. **Verified:** frontend 176 suites / 2039 green, 59 new — 42 on the two pure modules (due-date buckets including today-is-not-overdue and completed-is-not-overdue, assignee keying, filter combinations, the drag threshold measured diagonally, midpoint drop resolution, the dragged card excluded from its own index math, and the filtered→full index mapping) and 17 on the page (tap opens, a 2px twitch still opens, a drag does not, a drop outside every column is a no-op, **touch on the card body does not drag while touch on the grip does**, a tap on the grip does not move anything, the add-card payload and trim and reload and failure path, and the filter behaviours). The touch-body guard was **mutation-checked** — removing it fails that test and only that test. `next build` clean, lint clean, typecheck at baseline, en/bn/ms parity green. **Not done: not opened on a real phone**, which is where the remaining risk sits, and **edge auto-scroll while dragging is not implemented** — on a narrow screen you must scroll to the target column before dragging. Both logged as follow-ups above — **deployed to production 2026-08-03** (PR #445); still not opened on a real phone, which remains the open risk on this one — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Project Management Phase 3A + 3B — the board card opens, says something, and checklists finally have a UI** — asked as "task cards should have Trello-like features"; scoped in `docs/projects/project-management-phase-3.md` and built after both open questions were answered (**per-project board columns** for the later 3L, **single assignee stands** so 3G is deferred). **The finding that shaped the work: most of "Trello-like" was already in the database with no UI over it.** The board endpoint returns due date, priority, `checklistItems`, `_count.comments`, `_count.subtasks`, description, milestone and sprint, and the card rendered **three** of them — title, remaining hours, assignee — while `priority` was declared in the page's own TS interface and never drawn. **3A:** the card is now clickable by mouse, touch and keyboard (`role="button"` + Enter/Space) and opens the `TaskDetailPanel` that had existed since Phase 1 and was wired into `/projects/tasks` and `/projects/[id]` but **never into the board**, so the board had no way to open a task at all; closing reloads the board so a status changed in the panel shows in the columns.
