Badges added: due date with overdue/due-today/due-soon/done tones, HIGH and URGENT priority only (**MEDIUM and LOW stay quiet** — a badge on every card is not a signal), checklist progress, comment and subtask counts (**zero counts are omitted**, not printed as `0`), description marker, remaining hours, initials avatar. **Two correctness details worth keeping:** `due_date` is compared as a `YYYY-MM-DD` **string**, not a `Date` — it is a `@db.Date` serialised at UTC midnight, so `new Date(due) < new Date()` marks a task due *today* overdue for every timezone east of UTC, which is all of them here; and a drag that ends where it began reads as a click, which would open the card you just dropped, so the card tracks whether a native drag started and swallows the click that follows. The card also now reads **both** assignee fields — Phase 2 made an employee without a login assignable and the old card read only `assignee`, so those tasks rendered as unassigned. **3B:** checklists went from complete-and-invisible (model, three endpoints, tenant-scoped service methods, and even the api-client methods `addTaskChecklistItem`/`updateTaskChecklistItem`/`deleteTaskChecklistItem` — **no component in the codebase called any of them**) to a working section: progress count and bar, tick/untick, click-to-edit rename with Enter to save and Escape to discard, delete, and move up/down. Reorder needed **one new endpoint**, `PATCH /project-tasks/:id/checklist/reorder`, taking **the whole order** and re-sequencing it in a single `$transaction` — the obvious client-side shortcut of PATCHing two `sortOrder`s to swap a pair is wrong, because a half-applied swap leaves two items sharing a position and `checklistItems` orders by `sort_order` alone, so the list would reshuffle on every subsequent read; the endpoint rejects an order that omits an item, repeats one, or names an item from another task. Two dead i18n keys (`task.checklist`, `task.addChecklistItem`) were replaced by a real `checklist` section in en/bn/ms. **Verified:** backend 25 tests in `project-tasks.service.spec.ts` (7 new, **mutation-checked** — disabling the reorder guard fails exactly the three rejection tests and no others), full backend 147 suites / 2143 green with only the 4 known `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites failing, which **fail identically on the base commit** (no Postgres in the container — confirmed by stashing). Frontend 174 suites / 1980 green including 30 new across two new files: the board card (opens on click and on Enter, **does not** open when the click ends a drag, badge rendering, zero-count omission, a task due today not being called overdue, a completed task not being called overdue, MEDIUM staying quiet, the employee-assignee path, and that dragging a card between columns still moves it) and the checklist (progress, all-done, tick/untick payloads, trimmed add, blank add refused, rename on Enter, discard on Escape, no-op rename not saved, **reorder sending the whole order rather than the pair**, first/last move buttons disabled, delete, reload-after-change, empty state, and a failed save surfacing a toast without an optimistic tick to roll back). en/bn/ms catalog parity green; both apps typecheck at their existing baselines (frontend 1 pre-existing, backend 3 pre-existing); lint clean; `next build` emits all ten project routes. **Not done: neither has been opened in a browser** — see the follow-up above, which is where the risk actually sits — **deployed to production 2026-08-03** (PR #445), still unopened in a browser — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Dashboard quick actions point at the statements, and the three financial statements print** — asked as "Drop Expenses, Loans, Journal from Quick Links… Add Balance Sheet, Cash Book, Bank Book" plus "add print option in P&L, Balance Sheet, Trial Balance reports". The quick-action swap is what it says: the accounting row is now Voucher Entry, Ledger, P&L, Balance Sheet, Cashbook, Bankbook. **Printing is one `lib/statement-printer.ts` behind a shared `ReportPrintButton`, and it prints what is on screen rather than re-fetching** — so the scope, detail level, as-of date, approved-only and hide-zero choices made in the toolbar are exactly what comes out of the printer, which is the whole point of a filed copy. Three decisions worth recording. **The toolbar choices are printed as context lines** (`Branch: Dhaka · Level: Account · Approved vouchers only`), because the same date range gives different numbers per branch, per level, and with approval filtering on or off — a statement filed without them is ambiguous, and the approval line is omitted entirely for tenants who do not run voucher approval rather than printing a meaningless "All vouchers". **Hide-zero is honoured but totals are not recomputed**: the printed rows are the visible ones, and the footer stays the server's figure, so hiding a nil row can never make a statement stop tying. **Print is disabled, not hidden, in branch-comparison scope** — the compare view is an N-column matrix that this printer does not render, and a control that vanishes reads as a bug where one that explains itself on hover does not. The header comes from the tenant's print template (`LIST_REPORT`), resolved on click rather than on mount so the fetch only happens for people who actually print. Two incidental fixes: the balance sheet had five hardcoded English labels ("Total Assets", "Total Liabilities + Equity", …) in a trilingual app, now keyed and translated in en/bn/ms so screen and print cannot drift; and `SalesByCategoryDonut` gained the `ariaLabel` override the accounting dashboard needed. **Verified:** 25 new tests — 11 on the printer itself (escaping a name containing markup, formatBDT rather than bare numbers, the group-level shape that has totals but no rows, empty-section and empty-table copy, the context-line rules) and 14 across the three report pages (what actually reaches the printer for each, a loss printing as a positive figure under the Net Loss label, out-of-balance carried onto the page, hide-zero filtering rows but not totals, print disabled while loading). Frontend **172 suites / 1950 green**, typecheck at baseline, lint clean, en/bn/ms parity green. **Not done: nothing has been through a real print dialog** — the printer is verified by asserting on the HTML handed to `document.write`, so pagination of a long trial balance across pages is unproven — **deployed to production 2026-08-03** (PR #441, released together with #440); backend container restarted, `/api/v1/health` ok, `app.erp71.com` and `/login` both 200 — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Every tenant can now choose its dashboard, and accounting subscribers get one built for the ledger instead of the retail one with holes punched in it** — asked as a design suggestion, then "can we have a settings in subscription plans? which dashboard to show", then "any subscription tenant may choose". **The choice resolves through three layers, in one shared function so backend and frontend can never disagree.** Plan default: a new boolean entitlement `accountingDashboard`, `true` on the ACCOUNTING plan — one registry entry, no migration (`defaultPlanFeatures()` backfills, `upsertPlan` adds missing keys only so it reaches the live row without clobbering admin edits), and no admin-UI work since `EntitlementField` already renders booleans. Tenant choice: a new `Tenant.dashboard_preference` column (`AUTO` | `RETAIL` | `ACCOUNTING`) behind `GET`/`PATCH /tenants/dashboard-settings`, restricted to OWNER/MANAGER because it is workspace-wide. **It is deliberately a column and not a `features_json` key**: entitlements are capabilities, and `mergeAddonFeatures` ORs booleans in, so an add-on carrying the key would silently *replace* a retail tenant's dashboard rather than add anything. Then two guard rails that are real failure modes, not theory — the whole `AccountingController` sits behind `@RequiresFeature('premiumAccounting')` **and** the `VIEW_LEDGER` *store* permission, so a tenant opting in would hand every cashier a dashboard where all twelve panels 403; `resolveDashboardVariant` therefore falls back to retail per-user, using the `permissions` array already in `/auth/me`. In the other direction an `accountingOnly` tenant has no retail routes to fall back to, so the plan stays the floor and no preference can escape it. **The dashboard itself is a separate component, not more guards.** The old page carried six `!accountingOnlyMode` branches and gave a ৳749 subscriber four KPI tiles and one chart; it is now `RetailDashboard` / `AccountingDashboard` behind a thin switcher, because every panel added to either side made the interleaved version worse. The accounting one shows position as **balances** (cash & bank, receivable, payable, net worth) with no sparkline or delta — comparing a closing balance against a prior window reads as a trend it is not — then period flows with deltas, a books-health strip (trial balance out-by, vouchers awaiting approval, failed postings, recurring due, closed months still unlocked), cash flow, an expense-mix donut, an AR/AP aging table and recent vouchers. The renewal reminder is back: it was the one non-retail item in the old attention strip, and accounting-only tenants never saw it because the whole section was hidden. **One backend endpoint, not seven.** `GET /accounting/dashboard/overview` replaces what would have been parallel calls to `ar-aging`, `ap-aging`, `trial-balance`, `financial-ratios`, `cashbook`, `bankbook` and `profit-loss` — each of which independently pulls the tenant's whole `voucherDetail` set. It is two grouped aggregates (cumulative-to-date, in-period) plus one row-level read restricted to the receivable and payable accounts, which are the only figures needing individual voucher dates. Also fixed the retail wording that used to leak through: "Sales" is "Revenue", and "Cash in hand" was bound to `net_cash_movement`, a period flow labelled as a balance. **Verified:** 33 new tests — 6 on the overview endpoint (position vs period derivation, aging buckets by voucher date including that a settlement credit is not itself an aged item, unbalanced trial balance, the null-account path that skips the row-level read entirely), 6 on the tenant setting's role guard, 6 on the accounting dashboard's panels, 7 on the resolver, 4 on the settings page, plus the variant-selection cases on the dashboard page. Frontend **169 suites / 1928 green**, backend **143 suites / 2110 green**, both typechecks at their pre-existing baseline, lint clean on every new file, en/bn/ms parity green. **Not done: never opened in a browser.** The two things jsdom cannot answer are whether the four-column aging table scrolls inside its own container rather than the page body, and whether six-digit taka figures fit the KPI tiles at 360px — both listed above — **deployed to production 2026-08-03** (PR #440, released together with #441); backend container restarted, `/api/v1/health` ok, `app.erp71.com` and `/login` both 200 — done 2026-08-03

- [x] **Card edge detection failed on exactly the desks people use, and the camera feed now outlines the card live** — reported as "edge detection is not working", plus a request for the live-feed overlay. **I first told the user no edge detection existed, which was wrong** — `card-dewarp.ts` had shipped that morning; my grep for `edge` was drowned by "l**edge**r" hits and truncated by `head -20` before reaching it. Corrected before any code was written, and a redundant second detector I had begun was deleted rather than landed alongside the real one. **The bug was not a wrong constant but a wrong instrument.** Detection ranked pixels by gradient strength and took the convex hull of the strongest, which assumes the sharpest things in a photo belong to the card's border. They do not: a frame holds up to four populations — flat surface, desk texture, the card's border, and the card's *printing* — and black print on white card is routinely a **stronger** edge than that card against a desk. Every threshold rule tried failed on some ordinary photo, and this was established by measurement rather than argument: a throwaway harness rendered ten photograph-like frames and reported which gate rejected each. At the shipped `EDGE_KEEP_RATIO = 0.12`, wood grain put the hull at **96% of the frame** (the card is 37%), edge-support caught it, and detection returned null — so the cropper fell back to the untouched photo and the feature looked dead. Tightening to 0.03 fixed grain and broke printed cards, which then locked onto the **text block** (area 0.18 against a true 0.37) — a confident wrong answer, worse than none. A strength-relative cutoff fixed print and grain separately but not together: grain-only needed the bar above 0.40 of the strongest edge, grain-plus-print below 0.55, and noise closed the gap. Otsu alone split the two *largest* populations — flat card face against grain — putting the cut below the texture. **The fix is scale, not strength.** The 3×3 box blur became a separable box blur of radius 2 run twice (~Gaussian σ≈3): a border is a step between two surfaces, so blurring spreads it but leaves the total change across it intact, while print strokes and desk grain are narrower than the blur and average away against their own surroundings. Once the populations no longer overlap, a single Otsu finds the cut with **no tuning constant at all** — `EDGE_KEEP_RATIO` and `EDGE_MIN_RELATIVE` are both gone. Second change: edge points are now the **outermost** strong pixel per scanline from all four directions instead of every strong pixel. Printing is always inside the border, so it can never be the first edge a ray from outside meets — that removes it as a category rather than by threshold. Incidentally this deleted a 49,000-element sort per frame, which matters now the same code runs eight times a second. **The live overlay** (`LiveCardOutline`) samples the feed at 8fps, draws the quad in `blue-600` with dimmed surround, and tells the user "Card detected" before they commit to a photo rather than letting them find out on the crop screen. It holds the last outline across 3 dropped frames so a hand tremor does not make it strobe, eases each detection at 0.35 so it stops shimmering, and maps detection coordinates through the video's `object-contain` letterbox — drawing against the element's own box would sit the outline off the picture by the width of the bars. Capture is untouched: the overlay is a framing aid, and what gets scanned and stored is still settled after the shutter where the corners are draggable. **Verified:** 8 new detection tests on photograph-like frames (grain, print, grain+print, dense print, low contrast, drop shadow, angle, and a no-card frame that must still return null), **confirmed to fail 3/10 against the shipped code and pass 10/10 against the fix** — so they catch the reported bug rather than restate the new behaviour; plus 12 for the overlay (dropout hold, give-up, unmount, unreadable frame, letterbox maths). The shadow case asserts a tolerance of the shadow's own width and says why: a shadow hugging the card is a real edge just outside it, so the outline comes out a few pixels large, which is the honest answer and what draggable corners are for. All 39 pre-existing card tests still pass untouched. Frontend **166 suites / 1907 green**, typecheck at baseline, lint clean, build clean, en/bn/ms parity checked directly. **Not done: not opened in a browser, and every frame tested is synthetic** — no real photograph has been through this, which is the whole remaining risk on a change that is entirely about how real photographs behave — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Platform admin's plan and add-on edits no longer vanish on every deploy** — reported as "the tenant features option I set are lost after every deploy" and "Subscription Plans modifications lost after each new deploy". **`db:seed:platform` runs on every container start** (it is in the Dockerfile CMD chain, and it has to be: an empty catalog is a broken deployment and a plan added in code must reach production without anyone hand-inserting rows). Its `upsertPlan`/`upsertAddon` `update` blocks rewrote **name, description, monthly_price, yearly_price, is_active, features_json and marketing_features_json** with the values hardcoded in the file — so every price, rename and entitlement a platform admin set through Admin → Subscription Plans was reverted at the next release. The admin UI was effectively a lie: the edit saved, then quietly disappeared. Proven on production before touching anything — every live plan row was byte-identical to the constants in `seed-platform.ts` — and then **reproduced end to end on a throwaway Postgres**: seed, hand-edit STANDARD to `1299 / "Standard (BD)" / inactive / maxUsers 12` as an admin would, re-run the *old* seed, and it came back `999 / "Standard" / active / maxUsers 10`. **The fix reframes the file from "the values these rows are held at" to "the values a row is born with".** On create, the full definition as before. On a row that already exists, it now only ever **adds entitlement keys that are missing** — which is what still carries a newly-coded flag to production — and never rewrites an existing key, nor any of the pricing or copy. Verified on the same scratch database: the admin's four edits survived a redeploy untouched, a deliberately deleted `premiumCrm` was re-added on the next run with `maxUsers 12` and the custom name still intact, and a second consecutive run wrote nothing at all. **The stated cost, in the file's header:** changing a price or an existing entitlement *in code* no longer reaches an environment where that plan already exists — the admin UI is now the source of truth for live values, which is the only way both can be true at once. **The other half of the report did not reproduce.** The platform-wide toggles on Admin → Platform Settings → Tenant Features live in `platform_settings`, which nothing in the deploy chain writes: production's rows were last touched **2026-07-25 and 2026-07-04** and have survived every deploy since, and all `Tenant.feature_overrides` are `{}`, which is the column default rather than evidence of a wipe. What was almost certainly being lost is the **plan entitlements** those features are read from — same root cause, same fix. 8 new tests pinning the rule (an existing key is never overwritten even when code disagrees, a deliberately `false` value is not treated as absent, a null/non-object column is treated as empty, a second run is a total no-op, and a new key still propagates). Backend 142 suites / 2106 green; typecheck at its existing baseline. **Action needed after this deploys: re-apply the plan edits once.** Production currently holds the code defaults because they have been reset repeatedly; from this release on they will stick — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Business card scanner detects the card's edges and flattens it square-on, with draggable corners before anything is applied** — asked as "can we make it smart to detect the edges and then optimize to transform to a proper rectangle?". **The honest framing first, because it changes what this is worth:** the card goes to a *vision model*, not to classical OCR, and those read a tilted card perfectly well — so deskewing buys little extraction accuracy on its own. The real wins, in order, are **cropping** (a card on a desk is maybe a third of the frame, so cropping spends the whole 1600px budget on the card and roughly triples the pixels on its smallest line of text), then **the stored image** (`7e2b11c2` keeps the card so the AI can be checked against it, and a square-on card is far easier to check), and only then the deskew itself. **Hand-rolled, not OpenCV.js** — that is ~8 MB of WASM for one screen, and the repo already refused a chart library and hand-rolled SVG for the burndown. `lib/card-dewarp.ts` is greyscale → box blur → Sobel → convex hull → largest quad → DLT homography → bilinear inverse warp, all plain arithmetic over `ImageData` so it tests without a browser. **The design decision that matters is that detection is allowed to say "no".** A confidently wrong quad smears the card into a parallelogram that extracts *worse* than the raw photo, so `detectCardQuad` returns null unless the outline clears four independent checks: area between 15% and 98% of frame, the quad explaining ≥72% of the hull, every corner between 45° and 135°, and — the one that actually catches noise — **edge support**, the mean gradient along the quad's own sides against the frame mean. Uniform noise clears the first three (any quad through it looks as good as any other) and fails only this one. Two bugs were found by the tests rather than by reasoning: the ratio-based edge cutoff collapsed to zero on a clean photo where *only* the border has gradient, so every flat pixel counted as an edge (fixed with a floor relative to the frame's strongest); and noise was being detected as a card until edge support was added. **The corners are always draggable, never a read-only preview of the detector's guess** — it will sometimes lock onto a table edge or a notebook underneath, so a human has to be able to fix it, or bail out entirely with "use full image". Degradation is total: no confident quad, a degenerate warp, a detector that throws, or no canvas context all fall through to the untouched photo, which is exactly what ships today. The crop step sits after capture, so the camera **and** the file-pick path get it — the file path is the desktop fallback and must not be the poorer one. **Verified:** 21 new tests on the maths (homography round-trip and its null on a degenerate quad, corner ordering on a steep trapezoid where angle-sorting fails, hull, the four detection refusals, border clamping) and 7 on the cropper (including the "found nothing" copy not claiming a detection, and the fall-back-to-original paths). Four existing scanner tests were updated for the new step rather than worked around. Frontend 164 suites / 1885 green, typecheck clean at baseline, `next build` succeeds, en/bn/ms parity green. **Deployed to production 2026-08-02** (PR #437); `app.erp71.com`, `/crm/contacts` and `/crm/contacts/new` all 200, zero errors in the backend log. **Not done: never tested against a real photograph** — every detection test uses a synthetic image, so the thresholds (`EDGE_MIN_RELATIVE`, `MIN_EDGE_SUPPORT`, `MIN_QUAD_FILL`) are unproven on a real card in real lighting, which is the one thing that actually matters here. Expect to tune them once a real card has been through it — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **The scanned business card is now kept as an attachment on the contact** — asked for as "when a business card is captured via camera or an image uploaded, keep the original image as an attachment". Until now the scan deliberately stored nothing: the DTO comment said so in as many words ("the card never lands on disk … there is no uploaded image to store, serve, or later have to delete"), so this reverses a documented decision rather than filling a gap, and that comment was rewritten rather than left to contradict the code. **Which image gets kept was the user's call** — offered as true originals (4–12 MB per phone photo), a 2400px copy, or the 1600px JPEG the scan already sends; **the 1600px scan image was chosen**, so the card costs *nothing extra over the wire* and roughly 0.25 MB of storage. **The timing is what keeps storage clean:** the upload happens only after `createContact` returns an id, so a scan the user walks away from never leaves a file behind — no orphan sweeper needed, which the alternative (upload during the scan, when no contact exists yet) would have required forever. It is also deliberately *not* folded into contact creation: an upload to a third party can hang or fail, and a contact the user has already typed must not be lost because a CDN was having a bad minute, so a failed attach toasts and the contact still saves. **Storage already existed and is live** — `AssetsService` (Cloudinary) with all three env vars set in production and no "not configured" warning in the boot log — but `uploadFile` returns only `secure_url`, and **a URL cannot be turned back into a `public_id`**, so everything ever uploaded through it is undeletable. Added `uploadBuffer()` returning `{url, publicId, bytes}` and stored the `public_id` as `storage_key`, which is what makes deletion actually work; `VoucherAttachment`/`ProjectAttachment` predate this and still leak, logged below. **Two leak paths were closed that the schema hides:** `onDelete: Cascade` removes the *rows* when a contact is deleted but Cloudinary knows nothing about it, so `remove()` and the bulk `DELETE` both purge the files first — without that, every deleted contact is billed for its card forever. Deletion order is row-then-file on purpose (a stray file beats a row pointing at nothing). New `crm_contact_attachments` table following the `VoucherAttachment`/`ProjectAttachment` shape, plus `storage_key` and a plain-string `kind` (matching `LeadConversation.type`, so a second kind needs no schema change). The list→new-contact hand-off carries the photo in **its own sessionStorage key**, so a photo too large for the quota cannot cost the user the extracted fields as well. **Verified against a real Postgres, not by inspection:** a scratch database was built on the *pre-change* schema, seeded with a tenant and a BUSINESS_CARD contact, then put through the exact production boot command (`prisma db push --accept-data-loss`) — **no data-loss warning at all** (purely additive), the contact came out untouched, the `kind` default applied, a bogus `contact_id` was refused by the FK, and deleting the contact cascaded its attachment to zero rows. The **hand-written migration was checked to agree with Prisma rather than assumed to**: applied to a second pre-change database and then diffed against the schema — **"No difference detected"**. Two FK targets were wrong in the first draft (`"tenants"`/`"users"`); the real table names are `"Tenant"`/`"User"`, caught by checking an existing migration instead of trusting the snake_case convention. Both scratch databases dropped. **`prisma format` was reverted on sight** — it reflowed the whole schema into a 1252-line diff, so the change is 39 lines instead. 8 new backend tests (upload path, cross-tenant refusal, non-image rejection, unconfigured storage vs. a failed upload as *distinct* errors, file-with-row deletion, and the purge-before-delete leak) and 4 new frontend tests; **two of the frontend tests were confirmed to fail when the behaviour is mutated** — swallowing the attach error, and skipping the sessionStorage drain — so they guard the rules rather than restate them. Backend 144 suites / 2115 green, frontend **162 suites / 1857 green**, both apps build, lint clean, and all 8 new i18n keys confirmed present in en/bn/ms (bn/ms are *not* type-checked against en, so a missing key there fails silently at runtime — checked directly rather than assumed). The four `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites still fail, **confirmed identical on a stashed tree** and unrelated. **Deployed to production 2026-08-02** (PR #436). The schema change was verified where it counts: `crm_contact_attachments` exists in the live database with **every** constraint, index and the `kind` default intact, and the boot's `db push` left the existing data alone — 2 contacts and 21 tenants still there, 0 attachments as expected. Backend health reports `commit 0f9d184` with `db: ok`, all three `/crm/contacts/:id/attachments` routes are mapped (GET, POST, DELETE), no `Nest can't resolve` or error-level lines in the log, and no "Cloudinary env vars not set" warning. **The Cloudinary round-trip — the one link every test mocked — was then driven for real inside the production container** using the exact options `uploadBuffer` passes: the upload returned `public_id=retail/_deploy-check/roundtrip`, and `destroy()` on that same handle returned `{"result":"ok"}`, which proves the `storage_key` being persisted is precisely the handle deletion needs. Test asset destroyed. `app.erp71.com` 200, `/crm/contacts` 200, and the served chunks carry the new "Business card" string and the detail page's attachment code. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — no card has been scanned and saved through the real UI, so the end-to-end flow on screen is the remaining risk — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Contacts → Scan Business Card → "Take photo" opened a file dialog instead of the camera** — reported as "not bringing up the camera". The button's only mechanism was `<input type="file" accept="image/*" capture="environment">`, and **`capture` is a hint, not a request**: it is honoured on mobile Safari/Chrome, ignored outright on every desktop browser, and ignored on any mobile browser that declines it — in which case the input falls back to its default behaviour, which is the file picker. So on a laptop the button could never have done anything else. Fixed by opening a real stream with `navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({ video: { facingMode: { ideal: 'environment' } } })` and rendering a live `<video>` viewfinder with a Capture button, drawing the frame to a canvas at capture time. **`facingMode` is `ideal`, not `exact`** — `exact` throws `OverconstrainedError` on any laptop, which has no environment-facing camera, and that is precisely the machine this bug was reported from. **The `capture` input is kept as the fallback and is still the only path on three occasions:** no `mediaDevices` at all, the user denying permission, and an insecure origin (`getUserMedia` is undefined on plain HTTP, so a LAN-IP dev server keeps working). The existing resize path was factored into `drawScaled(source, w, h)` so a captured video frame goes through the same `MAX_EDGE_PX` downscale and JPEG re-encode as a picked file — a phone's 4–12 MB frame must not reach the API either way. **Two things worth naming.** `stopCamera()` is called from `reset()`, from the viewfinder's own Cancel and from an unmount effect, because a `MediaStream` whose tracks are never stopped **holds the camera indicator light on after the modal is gone** — the kind of thing users read as spyware. And Capture is disabled until `onLoadedMetadata` fires: `videoWidth` is 0 before then, so an early click drew a zero-size canvas and silently did nothing. `play()` is normalised through an `instanceof Promise` check rather than `.catch()`ed directly, since older engines return `undefined`. New strings `capture` / `openingCamera` in en/bn/ms. **Verified:** 4 new tests — the stream opens with the expected constraints and renders the viewfinder, tracks are stopped when it is dismissed, a rejected `getUserMedia` falls through to the capture input, and Capture stays disabled until metadata lands. Frontend 161 suites / 1853 green, typecheck at its existing baseline (the pre-existing `user-event` import error only), lint clean. **Deployed to production 2026-08-02** (PR #435): verified at the level that actually proves it shipped rather than merely built — the served chunk contains **`facingMode:{ideal:"environment"}`**, so the constraint that makes this work on a laptop is in the bundle, and the new `Opening camera` string is in the messages chunk. Backend health reports `commit 91ead96` with `db: ok`; `app.erp71.com` 200 and `/crm/contacts` 200; no runtime errors in the backend log. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — the reporter's own machine is the real confirmation, and the permission prompt is the one part of this that no test and no bundle check exercises — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Project Management Phase 2 — all seven requested changes, including the schema change that makes sprints tenant-level** — scoped in `docs/projects/project-management-phase-2.md` after four decisions were taken up front, then built in one pass ("do all, go yolo"). **(1) Menu** renamed Projects → Project Management across en/bn/ms. **(2) Projects list** gained an actions column (edit/delete) with a `ConfirmDialog` whose copy says what *survives* — the delete is soft, so tasks and the hours logged against them stay. **(3) My Tasks → Tasks**, now a real `DataTable` + `useServerList` list of every task in the workspace with project/assignee/status filters, defaulting to "assigned to me" so nobody loses their daily view; `/projects/my-tasks` is kept as a redirect stub because people bookmark these. `ListTasksDto` gained `statusId`, `statusCategory` (filters on what a column *means*, so "open tasks" is one parameter rather than the caller enumerating this tenant's board columns) and `assigneeEmployeeId`. **(4) Project edit** — the new-project form was extracted to `ProjectForm` taking `mode: 'create' | 'edit'` rather than copied, so the two cannot drift a field apart; `code` is absent from both modes because it is allocated server-side and printed on documents. Edit deliberately sends `''` rather than `undefined` for optional links, since `undefined` means "leave alone" to PATCH and clearing a customer has to be expressible. **(5) Backlog page deleted**; the project's task list is the backlog — a task with no sprint *is* backlog — and now shows it, alongside sprint/assignee/priority/estimate/remaining/due. **(6) Sprints are tenant-level.** `Sprint.project_id` was **dropped**: `assertSprint`'s "That sprint belongs to a different project" was the exact thing blocking the request, `assertNoOtherActive` re-scoped from project to tenant, `list()` now filters by *participation* (sprints holding a task from that project) rather than ownership, and which projects a sprint spans is derived from its tasks. **Deleting a project no longer cascades into sprints** — it detaches its tasks first, in one transaction, because a shared sprint must not carry phantom rows inflating its committed hours; same reasoning as `removeMilestone`. New `/projects/sprints` and `/projects/sprints/[id]`, the latter being the planning surface that replaces the backlog, with a **project filter on the backlog pane** (unusable without one when drawing from every project) and the burndown relabelled, since a chart captioned with a project name would now lie. **(7) Project team**, with the open question answered as **Assignable**: `ProjectMember` takes a user *or* an employee with no login (mirroring the nullable pair `ProjectTimeEntry` has carried since Phase 1 for exactly this), and `ProjectTask.assignee_employee_id` lets such a person actually hold work — a team you cannot give work to is half a feature. Exactly-one-of is enforced in the service because Prisma cannot express it. The picker merges both directories and **de-duplicates on `Employee.user_id`**, adding a linked person as the *user* so they keep their permissions. `removeMember` re-keyed from `userId` to the member row, since an employee member has no user id. **Verified against a real Postgres, not by inspection:** a database was built on the *pre-Phase-2* schema, seeded with a sprint, a member and a task attached to that sprint, then put through the exact production boot command (`prisma db push --accept-data-loss`). It warned about the column drop, succeeded without the crash-loop that caught the account-code rollout, and left every row intact — the sprint kept its identity, the member kept `employee_id` NULL, and **the task kept its `sprint_id`**, so nothing was orphaned; a second push was a clean no-op. Backend 141 suites / 2090 green (9 new for members and project deletion; three old tests that asserted the per-project sprint rule were **rewritten to assert the new contract**, including one that now proves a cross-project task assignment succeeds where it used to throw). Frontend 161 suites / 1849 green (8 new: the Tasks page's default filter and its refusal to fetch before the user id resolves — which would flash everyone's tasks — plus the picker's dedup, its employee-without-login path, and the roster marker). `next build` emits all nine project routes with `/projects/[id]/backlog` gone; both apps typecheck at their existing baselines; en/bn/ms parity green. **Not done: not opened in a browser, and the nav additions need `sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=projects.tasks,projects.sprints` after deploy** for any tenant on a saved sidebar layout — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Board and Backlog were dead ends: no breadcrumbs, nothing naming the project you were in, and no route back to it** — reported as "no way to go back to the project page". Both screens are titled only "Board" / "Backlog" and are reachable **only** from a project, so nothing on either page said which project you were looking at. The project detail page linked *out* to both; the board linked on to the backlog; **the backlog linked nowhere at all** — once there, the sidebar's All Projects was the only way out, and it lost your place. **The root cause is that the module never registered with the breadcrumb system:** `projects` was missing from `ModuleKey`/`moduleRoots` in `page-breadcrumbs.ts`, so none of the four project screens could produce a trail even though `PageHeader` has taken a `breadcrumbs` prop all along and every other module uses it. Added the module key, gave the detail page `Home › Projects › PRJ-0001 · Project 1`, and added a shared `projectChildBreadcrumbs()` for the two children so they render `Home › Projects › PRJ-0001 · Project 1 › Board`, with the project segment linking back to it. **The project segment is dropped, not faked, when the project is unknown** — both children paint before their fetch resolves and that fetch is allowed to fail without blocking the page, so a placeholder crumb would be a link to nowhere; the trail degrades to `Home › Projects › Board`, which still gets you out. Neither child had the project's name to show, so both now fetch it — deliberately in **its own effect**, not folded into the board's sprint/mode reload or the backlog's `load()`, since those re-run on every sprint edit and the project name will not have moved. Also gave the backlog a **Board** button: the board already linked to the backlog, so the two sibling views were a one-way trip. 3 new tests on the trail builder (shape with the project, the degraded shape without it, and that the tail is never a link while Projects always is); frontend 159 suites / 1841 green, typecheck at its existing baseline. **Deployed to production 2026-08-02** (PR #434); `app.erp71.com` 200, `/projects` 200, no errors in the backend log, and `sync:role-permissions` reported `projects: every system role already reconciled. Nothing to do.` — the second production run of the reconciler, which is the idempotency claim confirmed on real data rather than on a scratch database. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Project Management rendered near-black with invisible field values on any dark-mode machine — 59 stray `dark:` classes, the only ones in the app** — reported with a screenshot as "getting weird color when I go to a project". **`tailwind.config.js` sets no `darkMode`, so Tailwind 3 falls back to the `media` strategy**: a `dark:` class activates from the viewer's OS setting alone, with no toggle and no opt-in. The projects module was the *only* place in the codebase emitting them (7 files — the detail, board, backlog, my-tasks and settings pages plus `BurndownChart` and `TaskDetailPanel`), so on a dark-mode machine those pages flipped to `bg-gray-900` cards inside an otherwise light app while every other module stayed light. **The invisible values are the same bug, not a second one:** the paired classes only ever set background and border, never text colour, so `Stat`'s value (`text-sm font-medium`, colour inherited) and `Row`'s `<dd>` (`truncate text-right`, likewise) rendered near-black on near-black — which is why the screenshot shows Progress/Estimated/Logged/Remaining and Status/Priority/Manager/Target end/Budget as bare labels with nothing beside them. All 59 were paired with a light sibling already present, so stripping them leaves the light styling untouched; the diff is 41 lines, every one a class-list shortening. Fixed by removal rather than by adding `darkMode: 'class'`: the app has no dark theme, no toggle, and `docs/ui-design-guidelines.md` describes a single light system — a real dark mode is a deliberate project, not a side effect of one module's markup. **Guarded, because this is invisible to a reviewer on a light-mode machine** — that is exactly why it survived code review and shipped. `src/lib/ui-guardrails.test.ts` walks all app source and fails on any `dark:` variant, naming the offending files; **confirmed to fail when one is reintroduced**, not merely to pass now. Frontend 158 suites / 1838 tests green, typecheck at its existing baseline. **Deployed to production 2026-08-02** (PR #434): verified at the only level that actually proves it — **both served CSS bundles contain zero `prefers-color-scheme:dark` rules**, so the dark styling is not merely overridden but absent from the build. `app.erp71.com` 200, `/projects` 200. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — the reporter's dark-mode machine is the real confirmation — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Project Management works for non-owners: the six `projects.*` permissions now reach existing tenants, and the module is gated on holding one** — asked as "let's fix the bypassing issue. it should work for non-owner also", after the empty-list diagnosis turned up **zero** `%PROJECT%` grants in `UserStorePermission` platform-wide. **The bypass is not the bug; it is what hid the bug.** `StorePermissionGuard` short-circuits `if (userRole === 'OWNER') return true`, which is correct and stays — but it means the owner, who is always the first to try a new module, never touches the grant tables. So the module looked fine to the only person who tested it while being a 403 for everyone else. **The actual defect is that `ROLE_DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS` is read exactly twice** — at tenant creation (`seedDefaultTenantRoles`) and at role assignment (`syncMemberPermissionsFromRole`) — so a permission added afterwards reaches nobody who already exists, in either the `TenantRole` template or the materialized per-member `UserStorePermission` copies. This is the **third** occurrence (lead taxonomy, `APPROVE_VOUCHER`, now projects), so the fix is a general reconciler rather than a fourth one-off: **`prisma/sync-role-permissions.ts`**, in the container start chain as `sync:role-permissions` (production runs `prisma db push` and **no migrations**, so a migration-based grant would reach nobody — the trap from commit `409234e`). **Which role gets which permission is never decided in the script**; it reads `ROLE_DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS`, so it cannot drift from the matrix it backfills. In practice that means MANAGER gets all six and Cashier/Accountant get none, exactly as the matrix says. **The design problem worth naming: absence is ambiguous.** An owner can edit a system role (`updateRoleTemplate` has no `is_system` guard), so a missing row means either "predates the permission" or "the owner took it away", and a naive grant-anything-missing would silently undo the second on every deploy — forever, since this runs on every boot. Hence **groups**: a group is reconciled onto a role only when the role holds **none** of it; holding even one proves the group already landed, so a later gap is deliberate and is left alone. Same explicit-list discipline as `sync-nav-layout.ts`. A **renamed** system role is skipped and reported rather than run through `resolveBaseUserRole`, which falls back to CASHIER and would have quietly reconciled it as one; the warning is loud but deliberately **not** a non-zero exit, because this sits in the Dockerfile `&&` chain and a renamed role must not stop the backend booting. **Frontend, so the 403 is never reachable:** `canAccessProjects` now requires `owner || hasPermission(perms, 'VIEW_PROJECTS')` on top of the platform flag, and `/projects*` redirects home without it — the same pattern and the same reasoning as the premium-CRM paths ("the API 403s either way, but without this the page shell still renders"). Without it a cashier would have seen the module and hit a blank page behind every link, since `useServerList` turns a 403 into an empty table — logged above as its own item, because it affects every list in the app, not just this one. **Verified against a real Postgres, not by inspection:** built two tenants in the pre-projects state (system roles seeded from the matrix *minus* the projects group, members materialized from that), then drove the reconciler — dry run reports 12 grants and writes nothing; live run gives each Manager all six and materializes 6 member/store rows; **re-run is a clean no-op**; deleting `MANAGE_SPRINTS` from one Manager afterwards and re-running leaves it deleted (5, not 6); and renaming a system role to "Branch Lead" leaves it at 0 and reports it unmapped. `npm run sync:role-permissions` was then run as the container invokes it. 8 new backend tests cover the same rules against a Prisma stand-in — including cross-tenant isolation (a user in two tenants must not gain the permission on the other tenant's stores) — and **were checked to fail (2 of 8) when the group-skip rule is removed**, so they guard the rule rather than restate it. Backend 140 suites / 2079 green plus the new spec; frontend 157 / 1837 green; both apps typecheck at their existing baselines (the same 3 backend spec errors and the frontend `user-event` import as before). Scratch database dropped, throwaway driver deleted. **Deployed to production 2026-08-02** (PR #433): the boot chain's `sync:role-permissions` reported `projects: granted 126 permission(s) across 21 role(s) and 72 member/store pair(s)` — 21 tenants × 1 Manager role × 6 permissions, and 12 Manager members × 6 — with **no unmapped-role warnings**, so every production system role still carries its seeded name. Confirmed against the live database: `TenantRolePermission` holds 126 project grants and `UserStorePermission` 12 per permission including `MANAGE_SPRINTS` (which the obvious `LIKE '%PROJECT%'` check misses — it is the one permission in the group without "PROJECT" in its name, worth remembering when auditing this). `app.erp71.com` 200, `/projects` 200, API 401 unauthenticated, zero errors in the backend log. **Not done: never opened in a browser** — no non-owner login was driven through the module, which is where the remaining risk sits — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Project Management lists were always empty — the four list surfaces read `.items` off a response the api client had already unwrapped to a bare array** — reported as "created project. not showing on the list", straight after the feature flag was switched on. Nothing about the flag: the rows were being created and then thrown away client-side. **`TransformInterceptor` reshapes any `{ items, total, page, limit, pages }` service result into `{ data: items, meta: {…} }`.** `fetchWithAuth` unwraps `.data` and drops `meta`, so it hands back the **array**; `fetchPaginated` exists precisely to read `meta` back into an `{ items, total, … }` envelope, and every other server-paginated list in the app uses it. All three paginated `projects` endpoints called `fetchWithAuth`, so `useServerList`'s `result?.items ?? []` and the three pages' `(page as { items?: Task[] })?.items ?? []` each read `undefined` off an array and fell to their empty default. **This silently emptied four surfaces, not one** — the projects list, My Tasks, the task list on the project detail page, and the sprint backlog — and `getProjectTimeEntries` was wired the same way with no caller yet. Switched all three to `fetchPaginated`. **Diagnosis, since every layer looked correct in isolation:** the backend service was driven for real — `ProjectsService.create` then `.list` against a throwaway Postgres built by `db push` — and returned the created row under the exact query string the frontend sends, so the query, the `deleted_at`/status filters, the `include`s and the `created_at` sort were all cleared; production was then checked directly and held **3 projects, none deleted**, so the data existed too; and rendering the list page with a mocked `{ items, total }` produced the row, which isolated the fault to the shape crossing between them. Also ruled out on the way: the permission guard (`UserStorePermission` holds **zero** `%PROJECT%` grants platform-wide, but the tenant's OWNER bypasses the guard entirely — worth knowing before a non-owner tries the module, and logged separately above), route ordering, and DTO validation. **Verified:** the new `api.projects-list.test.ts` feeds all three methods a real TransformInterceptor-shaped payload and asserts an `{ items, total }` envelope comes back — **confirmed to fail on the pre-fix code**, not just pass on the new. Frontend 157 suites / 1837 green, typecheck at its existing baseline. Both throwaway diagnostics and the scratch database were removed. **Deployed to production 2026-08-02** (PR #433) alongside the permission backfill above; `app.erp71.com` 200, `/projects` 200, zero errors in the backend log. **Not done: never opened in a browser** — the three projects sitting in production were not confirmed on screen — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Voucher approval, finished: per-report approved-only toggles, bulk approve, a sidebar queue badge, and the whole flow driven in a real browser** — the four gaps left open by the morning's landing. **(1) Per-report toggle.** `useApprovedOnly()` in `accounting-report-scope.ts` resolves the tenant's `reports_approved_only` setting, lets the user override it, and remembers the override in `localStorage` — the same shape as the existing scope/level/hide-zero helpers, except this one is a *server* filter, so it is sent on the request rather than applied to the response. `ApprovedOnlyParams` + `appendApprovedOnly()` went onto all 16 report methods in the api client; **`false` is a real instruction ("include pending") and is deliberately not dropped as falsy** — only `undefined` falls through to the tenant setting. The shared `<ApprovedOnlyToggle>` renders **nothing** unless the tenant actually requires approval, since with approval off every voucher is APPROVED and the control could only ever be a no-op. Wired into all 13 surfaces: `ReportScopeBar` covers P&L / balance sheet / trial balance, and the nine standalone report pages plus the ledger got the toggle in their own filter bars. Every page holds its first fetch on `approvalReady` so a report is never generated against the wrong value and then silently corrected. **(2) Bulk approve.** `POST accounting/vouchers/bulk-approve` and `/bulk-reject` (`BulkVoucherApprovalDto`, 1–200 uuids). Vouchers already in the target state come back as `skipped` rather than failing the batch — **a stale selection is the normal case when two people work the queue and it must not lose the other rows** — and ids from another tenant are reported as `notFound`. One `updateMany`, one audit row, and no write at all when nothing is actionable. The UI uses `DataTable`'s own `enableRowSelection` + `bulkActions`. **(3) Sidebar badge.** `GET accounting/vouchers/pending-count` **short-circuits to 0 without touching the vouchers table when the tenant does not require approval**, and `usePendingVoucherCount()` stops polling once it learns that — so a tenant with the feature off pays one request per session, not one per minute. Approve/reject anywhere in the app dispatches `erp71:voucher-approval-changed` so the badge updates without waiting for the 60s poll. **(4) Browser verification — the actual point of the exercise, and it found a real bug.** Brought the stack up on a scratch Postgres (`db push` + seed) on isolated ports (backend 4100, frontend 3200) because Docker holds 3000/4000 and another `next-server` holds 3100; drove it with Playwright. Found and fixed: the voucher list rendered **two stacked "N selected" bars**, because `DataTable` already renders a `BulkActionBar` internally once `bulkActions` is passed and the page was rendering a second one itself — invisible to jsdom, obvious in a screenshot. Also learned `POST /auth/login` is throttled to 5/min, which makes iterating on a UI script through the login form impossible; the driver injects the session into `localStorage` instead. Verified end to end: settings page saves all three flags and keeps auto-approve disabled until approval is required; three manually-created vouchers landed **PENDING**; the sidebar showed **Vouchers 4**; the queue filtered to Pending; select-all → "Approve selected" cleared all four and the badge disappeared; and the approved-only toggle is **provably wired to the server, not decorative** — the same trial balance reads ৳500.00 with it on and ৳8,277.00 with it off (a deliberately unapproved ৳7,777 voucher), balanced both ways. Scratch DB dropped and both scratch services stopped afterwards; the user's own stack on 3100 was left alone. **(5) The deploy path, caught before shipping.** `apps/backend/Dockerfile` reconciles schema with `prisma db push` and applies **no migrations** — so the `20260802121000` permission backfill would have reached production never, and existing tenants' Accountants would silently have been unable to approve anything. Same trap as the account-code rollout (`409234e`). Reproduced against a throwaway Postgres populated with vouchers and an Accountant role: `db push` succeeds (my column carries a default, unlike the account-code case that crash-looped), existing vouchers come out `APPROVED`, and the grant count is **0**. Fix follows the precedent — `prisma/sync-approve-voucher-permission.ts`, called from `sync-accounting.ts` which is already in the container CMD chain and documented as additive-and-idempotent. It grants the system Accountant role AND materializes the permission onto its current members (`userStorePermission` holds a per-member copy written at role assignment, so granting the role alone would leave every existing Accountant without it). Verified on that same database: dry-run reports 1 role + 1 member, live run grants both, re-run is a no-op. The migration is kept for local `migrate deploy` and cross-references the script. **Verified:** backend 140 suites / 2079 green (+8 new: bulk partial/stale/no-op/reason paths, pending-count short-circuit), frontend 156 / 1834 green (+8 new: the hook's poll-stop and event-refresh, the toggle's hidden state and both flip directions, bulk approve and the nothing-pending guard); both apps typecheck at their existing baselines, lint clean, en/bn/ms parity green — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Voucher approval (maker-checker): a per-tenant switch, an `APPROVE_VOUCHER` permission, an approval queue, and an approved-only mode for every report** — asked for after confirming the feature had never been built. **The whole thing is off by default and inert until switched on.** `Voucher.approval_status` defaults to `'APPROVED'` at the column level, so existing rows and every tenant that never opens the settings page behave exactly as before; `approvalVoucherFilter(false)` returns `{}`, a genuine no-op spread, so no report gains a filter it did not have. **Three flags, on a new `AccountingSettings` row** (`GET`/`PATCH accounting/settings/accounting`, PATCH gated `@TenantRoles('OWNER')` because the user asked for the owner to decide): `require_voucher_approval` (manual vouchers and opening-balance imports land PENDING), `auto_approve_system_vouchers` (**default true** — sales, purchases, payments and payroll post their own vouchers and holding those back would stall day-to-day operations behind an approver; turning it off routes them into the same queue), and `reports_approved_only`. The read deliberately does **not** create the row — no settings row is the defaulted state, which keeps "never configured" and "configured to the defaults" indistinguishable and correct. **One decision function**, `initialApprovalStatus(settings, 'manual' | 'system')` in `voucher-approval.util.ts`, is the only place the status is decided, called from all three creation paths: `createVoucher`, `importOpeningBalances`, and `autoPostFromRules` in `posting.utils.ts` (which loads the settings off its own `tx`, since it runs inside the caller's transaction). **Editing a voucher resets it to PENDING and clears the earlier sign-off** when approval is required — an edit invalidates the approval it had. `POST vouchers/:id/approve` and `.../reject` (with an optional trimmed reason) are gated `@RequireStorePermission(VIEW_LEDGER, APPROVE_VOUCHER)`; both are listed explicitly because `getAllAndOverride` makes method-level metadata *replace* the class-level `VIEW_LEDGER`, not add to it. **Reports:** the approved-only filter is applied at 30-odd query sites across P&L, balance sheet, trial balance (all three plus their compare variants), ledger, cashbook, bankbook, AR/AP aging, comparative P&L, VAT, financial ratios, cash flow, budget-vs-actual, cost-center P&L, the dashboard KPI/trend queries, bank reconciliation, and the chart-of-accounts balance column — that last one because the number there has to agree with the ledger it links to. Every report DTO now extends `ApprovedOnlyQueryDto`, so `?approvedOnly=true|false` overrides the tenant setting per request. **Party ledgers are deliberately excluded** (`party-ledger.util.ts` untouched): a customer's outstanding due should not move because an accountant has not signed off. **Migrations, both replayed against a real Postgres:** `20260802120000_add_voucher_approval` (enum value, four voucher columns, index, `accounting_settings`) and a *separate* `20260802121000_backfill_approve_voucher_permission`, separate because Postgres forbids using an enum value in the transaction that added it. The backfill grants `APPROVE_VOUCHER` to existing tenants' system **Accountant** role and materializes it onto its members — Managers and Cashiers are left out on purpose, since letting whoever entered a voucher also approve it defeats the control. Verified on a scratch DB that it hits Accountant and not Manager, and that re-running both files inserts nothing. **UI:** new `/accounting/settings` page (owner-editable, read-only notice otherwise, auto-approve disabled until approval is actually required); the voucher list gains an approval column, a status filter — `?approvalStatus=PENDING` turns it into the approval queue rather than adding a second list page — and inline approve/reject on pending rows; the detail page gains an approval panel showing the rejection reason. Both surfaces resolve the caller's `APPROVE_VOUCHER` grant on mount. en/bn/ms all carry the new keys. **Verified:** backend 140 suites / 2073 green including 25 new (the four `initialApprovalStatus` branches, override precedence in both directions, the create/update/approve/reject transitions and their guards, and the report filter on and off); frontend 154 suites / 1826 green including 4 new; both apps typecheck at their existing baselines and lint is clean. **Not done: not opened in a browser, and no per-report `approvedOnly` toggle is wired in the UI yet — the backend accepts it, only the tenant-wide setting is exposed** — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Project Management was unreachable: the `projects` platform switch could never be turned on platform-wide** — reported as "we added a basic project management module. but not seeing any options." Everything downstream was correctly wired — `ProjectsModule` is in `app.module.ts`, all seven `(app)/projects` routes exist, the `projects` module and its three links are in `NAV_REGISTRY` and `DEFAULT_TENANT_NAV_LAYOUT`, `Sidebar` gates on `module.key === 'projects'`, and en/bn/ms all carry the labels. The break was one missing line: **`projects_enabled` was never added to `SETTINGS_SCHEMA.general`** in `platform-settings.service.ts`. `getPlatformFeatures()` builds its result by iterating `Object.keys(schema)`, so a flag absent from the schema is not merely defaulted off — it is **unreadable**, pinned to `false` no matter what row sits in `platform_settings`. The admin page for it (`admin/platform-settings/tenant-features`) also had no toggle in `FEATURE_TOGGLES`, so there was nothing to click and nothing to persist. Fixed both. **`external_import_enabled` was broken the same way and is fixed alongside** — the frontend already rendered its toggle, but the key was likewise missing from the backend schema, so saving it wrote a row `getPlatformFeatures()` would then ignore; that toggle had been inert since it shipped. A comment now says on the schema why every `PlatformFeatures` key needs an entry here. The **per-tenant override path was never broken** — `resolveTenantFeatures` merges `tenant.feature_overrides` on top of the platform defaults and `TenantDetailModal` iterates `PLATFORM_FEATURE_KEYS`, so Admin → Tenants → *tenant* → Feature access → Project Management → On has worked all along and is the way to switch it on for one workspace. **Second gate to remember when enabling it:** a tenant on a *saved* nav layout (platform `navigation.tenant_layout` or a `TenantNavLayout` row) is served that layout verbatim, so the projects nodes need `npx tsx prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=projects,projects.list,projects.my-tasks,projects.setup` — flipping the feature flag alone is not enough for those tenants. **Verified:** backend `platform-settings` 2/2 and frontend admin tenants + platform-settings 28/28 green; both apps typecheck at their existing baselines (the 3 known backend accounting/billing spec errors and the frontend `user-event` import are unchanged). **Not done: not opened in a browser, and the toggle has not been flipped on production** — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Codes made load-bearing across the accounting UI: code-first lists, a searchable code-ordered account picker, and a hide-zero toggle on the statements** — asked as four observations after the hierarchical-code rollout landed. **(1) Accounts list.** The code column already led the table but carried a second `was 1010` line under it and was `hideOnMobile`, with the name cell repeating the code as a mobile subtitle. The legacy number is now dropped from the UI entirely (the column is still in the database, so nothing is lost), the code column shows at every viewport, and the duplicated subtitle is gone — one code, one place. `coa.legacyCode` and the now-unused `coa.noCode` were removed from en/bn/ms. **(2) Reports.** Two changes, one of them a backend change: `bucketForAccount` returned `code: null` for every group and subgroup bucket, so at `subgroup`/`group` detail level a report had no code to print — those buckets now carry their row's real code, and the synthetic unassigned bucket takes the reserved `<group>00` slot that its accounts are actually coded into. With codes present, ordering by name became indefensible: `sortLevelEntries` now orders by code (falling back to name for anything uncoded, and still sinking the unassigned bucket last), and `rollUpByLevel` sorts at *account* level too rather than passing the query's order through — so Revenue (41) precedes Expenses (51) instead of sorting alphabetically. On the frontend the near-identical `BSSection`/`AccountSection` copies in the balance sheet and P&L collapsed into one `StatementSection`, which prints the code in a fixed-width column to the *left* of every group and account name; `CompareMatrixTable` and the trial balance got the same treatment (the trial balance gained a real Code column rather than a grey suffix). **Hide-zero is client-side on purpose:** the totals stay the server's, so suppressing a row can never change what a statement adds up to. `hideZeroGroups` drops zero rows and then the groups they leave empty, but **keeps a group whose rows all net to zero while its total does not** — the total must stay reachable. Trial balance filters on closing balance, which is safe because only the two balance columns are footed. Sub-cent amounts count as zero (rounding dust, not a row). The toggle lives in `ReportScopeBar` next to the detail-level control and persists to `localStorage` like scope and level. **(3) Groups and subgroups.** Both create modals now lead with a Code field prefilled from the `next-code` endpoints that already existed but were only wired to the account modal; on edit the field is read-only, because the service forbids the operations that would strand the children coded beneath it. The subgroup modal's parent-group dropdown also shows codes. **(4) Account selection.** A native `<select>` cannot be filtered and a real chart of accounts runs to hundreds of rows, so `AccountSelect` is a listbox with its own search field: rows are `code · name` in code order, search matches code *or* name, and arrow keys/Enter/Escape work. **The popup is positioned `fixed` off the trigger's rect, not `absolute`** — voucher entry mounts one inside a scrolling table and an absolutely-positioned panel is clipped by it; it re-positions on scroll and resize. Wired into voucher entry, ledger, opening balances, recurring journals, recurring vouchers, voucher templates, bank reconciliation, cashbook and bankbook — nine `<select>`s replaced, three of which did not show the code at all. **Verified:** backend 141 unit suites / 2058 green (the 4 `test/*.spec.ts` live-Postgres suites fail identically with and without this branch — the pre-existing issue logged elsewhere here); frontend 153 suites / 1822 green including 16 new (11 for the picker: code ordering with uncoded accounts sunk last, filtering by code and by name, the empty state, click/Enter/Escape, the trigger label, the clear entry; 5 for the statement helpers: the zero-row and zero-group drops, the offsetting-group case, sub-cent dust, and code-before-name ordering in the DOM). The three test files that drove account `<select>`s through `fireEvent.change` now go through a shared `selectAccount` helper. Both apps typecheck at their existing baselines; en/bn/ms parity green. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — done 2026-08-02

- [x] **Hierarchical codes for account groups, subgroups and accounts** — asked as "Account Group, Sub Group, Account should have codes. sub-group code should have parent's code as prefix. same for account". Groups and subgroups had **no code column at all**, and `Account.code` was an optional, non-unique free-text field holding flat 4-digit numbers no prefix rule could hold over (`1050` Fixed Assets is a *Non-Current* Asset but sits inside the `10xx` Current Assets block; `1060` Staff Advances is nowhere near its `1030` subgroup peer). **Scheme: fixed-width `2 / 4 / 6`** — `<type digit><group serial>` / `+<subgroup serial>` / `+<account serial>`, so `110101` is Cash in Hand under subgroup `1101` under group `11`. The leading digit is the account type (1 asset … 5 expense), which makes an account's type readable off its code and means **a plain lexicographic sort is hierarchy order** — `findAccounts` now orders by `code` alone, and the existing `contains` search doubles as a subtree filter (typing `1101` returns exactly that subgroup). Fixed width is what buys that, so **serials spill into base-36 at the same width rather than widening** (… 98, 99, A0, A1 … ZZ): `'9'` is `0x39` and `'A'` is `0x41`, so the seam stays correctly ordered, and allocation is decimal-first so a normal tenant never sees a letter. That is 35 groups per type and 1,035 children elsewhere; exhaustion throws a 400 naming the parent instead of wrapping. Widening the field was rejected as the primary answer because it only moves the wall — the spill rule is needed at any width. **Allocation is monotonic (`max + 1`)** so a deleted account's code is never recycled onto a different account (last year's printed ledger keeps meaning what it said); holes are only backfilled once the decimal phase is full, and only then does it go alpha — so one hand-typed `…90` costs a few slots rather than pushing everything into letters. Codes are **suggested, never demanded**: three `next-code` endpoints prefill the form, and the allocator always runs server-side, so a client-supplied code is validated (width, charset, parent prefix, uppercase-normalised — lowercase sorts after `Z` and would break ordering) but never trusted. **Re-parenting an account re-codes it**, which is safe because vouchers reference accounts by id and never by code — the modal warns with the exact before/after first. Groups and subgroups never re-code, because the service already forbids the two operations that would strand them (a group's `type` is immutable, a subgroup cannot be re-parented). **The deployability problem, found by testing the real deploy path rather than reasoning about it:** the first cut put the backfill in the migration, which production would never have run — the backend container reconciles the schema with `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` on boot and runs no migrations. Worse, the schema as written made `db push` **exit 1** ("cannot add a required column to a populated table"), and because the container CMD is an `&&` chain that would have stopped `db:seed:platform`, `sync:accounting` and `node main.js` from ever running — a crash loop with `restart: unless-stopped`, i.e. the site down, and not one code changed. Reproduced on a throwaway Postgres before it could ship. So the change was restaged: the migration is **DDL only**, the columns ship as loose as `db push` accepts (`NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` for the two new ones; `accounts.code` stays nullable, since db push refuses to make an *existing* column required even with a default), the unique indexes are deferred, and **the backfill moved into `prisma/backfill-account-codes.ts`, called from `applySync` — which already runs on every boot in that same chain and is built for exactly this drift.** That leaves **one** backfill implementation reusing `account-code.ts`, instead of the codec restated in PL/pgSQL where the two could drift; the phase-B tightening is logged above. The backfill runs *before* the bootstrap (which hangs new child codes off their parent's) and that ordering is asserted by a test. It pins the template rows by name so an existing tenant lands on exactly the codes a fresh tenant gets, **in two passes** — reserving every pinned code before allocating anything, because a single interleaved pass let a tenant-made subgroup ("Petty Cash Floats") take `1102` and push "Receivables" off its canonical code, silently breaking the property the pinning exists for. A template account the tenant had *moved* is deliberately not pinned; it is allocated under its actual parent so the prefix invariant holds rather than the code lying about where it sits. **Verified against a real Postgres, not by inspection:** the full production boot sequence — build a DB on the old schema, load two tenants of legacy data covering the awkward paths, then run the exact Dockerfile `db push` followed by `sync:accounting` — with a moved template account correctly landing under its real parent, an account with no subgroup in the reserved `00` slot, a tenant-created group pushing the template's onto the next code, all five invariants at zero violations, no duplicate or blank codes, **byte-identical results to a freshly bootstrapped tenant**, and a second boot confirmed a no-op. 28 new codec tests including the full-range sort-order property, round-tripping every serial, the spill boundary and exhaustion — plus a **twin-parity test that was checked to actually fail on drift** (the `.ts` and `.js` copies are hand-maintained and the package resolves to the `.js` at runtime). `seed.ts` also allocated rather than hardcoding its demo overhead accounts, verified by running the seed twice. Backend 2058 passing / 69 pre-existing failures vs base 2020 / 70 (the live-Postgres integration suites, confirmed by running base with a matching generated client); frontend 151 suites / 1806 green; both apps typecheck at their existing baselines; en/bn/ms catalog parity green. **Deployed to production 2026-08-01** after a 39 MB pre-write `pg_dump` (`/opt/erp71/backups/pre-account-codes-20260801-174127.sql`): the boot chain's `db push` succeeded, the backfill re-coded **all 503 accounts across all 21 tenants** to 6-char codes with 0 blank and 502 legacy codes preserved, and all five invariants query at **zero violations** on live data — including no duplicate codes, which the old flat scheme did not hold (`Due from Branches` and `Stock on Hand` both sat on `1040` for one tenant; they are now `110401` and `110501`). Production also had exactly one NULL `accounts.code`, confirming the nullable-column decision was load-bearing rather than cautious. Site verified up: `app.erp71.com` 200, `/accounting/coa` 200, API 401 unauthenticated, zero errors in the backend log. **Not done: never opened in a browser** — done 2026-08-01

- [x] **Designable print headers — every printed document now carries a tenant-designed letterhead (logo, business details, fonts, sizes, colours), configurable per paper size and per document type** — asked as "for all prints (various sizes), need an option to design a header. Should be possible to include logo, font size/styles etc." Delivered in four phases. **The precondition was that there was no print pipeline to add a header to.** Printing lived in five HTML-string builders under `lib/`, seven pages that hand-rolled `window.open` + `document.write` inline, three React invoice pages that print via CSS, and `DataTable`'s list print — each with its own `@page` rules, its own escaping, and its own header markup; `sales-invoice-printer.ts` literally fell back to the string `'RETAIL STORE'`, and list reports had no header at all. **Phase 1** put one shell under all of it: `lib/print/` — `header.ts` (pure `renderHeaderHtml`/`headerCss`, DOM-free so the print windows, the React pages and the settings preview all render from the same function), `print-window.ts` (`openPrintWindow` owning `window.open`, the `@page` rules and the print trigger), `document-styles.ts`. The five printer libs were refactored onto it with their output unchanged. **`printWhenReady` fixes a real bug class:** the old code called `print()` from `win.onload` or an inline `window.onload`, which does not wait for images — a Cloudinary logo regularly misses the print snapshot and the header prints with an empty box. It now waits on `document.images`, with a 3s timeout so a dead CDN cannot hang the dialog, and prints synchronously when there is nothing to wait for. **Multi-page invoices keep their letterhead** via a `<table><thead>` wrapper, because Chrome repeats table headers and `position: fixed` does not survive pagination. **Phase 2** added `PrintTemplate` (migration `20260731120000_add_print_templates`) — tenant-scoped, `name`, `is_default`, `doc_types[]`, and a **versioned `PrintHeaderConfig` JSON blob** rather than 25 columns, so "add a footer" is a type change and not a migration. `apps/backend/src/print-templates/` is CRUD + `GET /resolve?docType=`, which returns the template assigned to that document type, else the tenant default, else **a config derived from the tenant's existing branding** — so every tenant gets a logo on their prints without creating anything, and no data migration was needed. Single-default is enforced in a transaction (Prisma cannot express a partial unique index), and deleting the default promotes the oldest remaining template rather than leaving a tenant with none. The config is **validated field-by-field with class-validator nested DTOs**, not stored as opaque JSON: colours must be hex, sizes are clamped, and the logo URL must be `http(s)` — the renderer independently re-sanitises anyway (`cssColor`, `safeImageUrl`, everything HTML-escaped), because the print window is a document the tenant's own text lands in. **Phase 3** is `/settings/print-templates`: form on the left, **live preview in a sandboxed iframe rendered at true paper width and scaled to fit**, driven by the same `renderHeaderHtml` the printers use, so what is previewed is what prints. Logo upload reuses `POST /assets/upload`. Layout, per-line font size/weight/italic/alignment/colour, a `{{token}}` line editor, divider, typography, and per-paper-size overrides. **Tokens drop their own line when empty** — a tenant with no phone number does not print a bare "Tel:". **Thermal is coerced, not just styled:** side-by-side layouts become stacked and logo/type sizes are capped at 80mm and again at 58mm, since a two-column letterhead does not exist on a 58mm roll. **Phase 4** migrated the seven inline `document.write` sites and `DataTable`'s list print onto the shell, deleting ~200 lines of duplicated CSS. **Three latent bugs fixed in passing:** `sales/orders/[id]`, `sales/returns/[id]` and `purchases/returns/[id]` printed the literal text `{t.shared.print.salesOrder}` in their footers (and the purchase return printed `{t.common.product}` as four table headers) — `{...}` inside a template literal is not interpolation, so those pages had been shipping brace-wrapped i18n keys onto paper. **One design correction mid-way:** `DataTable` first resolved the template on mount, which put a request on every list page in the app and broke 24 test suites whose api mocks predate it; it now resolves lazily on the Print click (`usePrintHeader(type, { eager: false })`), which is both cheaper and the honest scope — printing a list is a rare action. **Verified:** frontend 151 suites / 1804 tests green, including 46 new (renderer: token substitution and empty-line dropping, escaping, `javascript:` logo rejection, thermal coercion, per-paper overrides, colour sanitising; print window: `@page` per size, header ordering, `thead` repetition, image-wait and the double-print guard; the hook: branding fallback, cache-per-doc-type, failure path; 8 for the settings page). Backend 138 unit suites green including 14 new (`print-templates.service.spec.ts` — tenant scoping, default promotion/demotion, doc-type resolution precedence, branding fallback). Both apps typecheck clean at their existing baselines; en/bn/ms catalog parity passes with ~90 new keys per locale. The 4 `test/*.spec.ts` backend integration suites fail identically with and without this branch — the pre-existing "needs a live Postgres" issue logged above. **Not done: never opened in a browser and nothing has been sent to a real printer** — see the follow-ups above, which is where the risk actually sits — done 2026-07-31
- [x] **Chart of Accounts list shows a Balance column** — asked as "In Accounts list, add a column to show current balance". The data did not exist in the response: `GET /accounting/accounts` returned the account rows only, so this needed a backend change. `findAccounts` now follows the `findMany` with **one grouped aggregate for the whole page** (`voucherDetail.groupBy` by `account_id`, scoped through `voucher: { tenant_id }`, summing debit and credit) rather than an aggregate per account, and returns `balance` + `balance_side` per row. The balance is derived with the same `calculateSignedBalance`/`presentBalance` pair the ledger and trial balance use, so **the number on the list ties back to the account's ledger** instead of being a second, subtly different definition — credit-normal types (liability/equity/revenue) net credit-minus-debit, the amount is presented positive with the side it sits on, and an account overdrawn onto the *opposite* side reports that side rather than a negative. Consistent with those reports, it is the **all-time closing balance with no date or posting-status filter** — the same rows the ledger shows for an account with no range applied. An account with no voucher lines is a flat `0` with side `neutral` and renders without a Dr/Cr label. The query is skipped entirely when the filters match no account, so an empty chart of accounts costs one query, not two.
