Frontend adds a right-aligned `tabular-nums` money cell via `formatBDT` (never a literal `$`) with the localized Debit/Credit label under it, reusing the existing `t.accountingShared.debit`/`credit` keys; only the new `coa.columns.balance` header needed adding, across en/bn/ms. **Verified:** 2 new backend tests (per-account balance including a liability sitting on the debit side and an account with no lines at all; the exact `groupBy` args including tenant scoping; and the no-accounts short-circuit asserting the aggregate is never issued) — 44/44 in `accounting.service.spec.ts` green; 1 new frontend test asserting the formatted amount and its side label — 6/6 in `coa/page.test.tsx`; message-catalog parity green; both apps typecheck at their existing baseline. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — verified by tests, not by eye — done 2026-07-31
- [x] **Vouchers list shows a Narration column** — asked as "Vouchers list should show column: Narration". The list already had the data: `serializeVoucher` spreads the whole voucher row, so `description` (what the detail page and the printed voucher both label "Narration") was in every `GET /accounting/vouchers` response and simply unrendered — no backend change needed. Added between Reference and Amount, `truncate` inside a `max-w-[22rem]` with the full text on `title` (narrations run long and would otherwise push Amount and the row actions off-screen), `hideOnMobile` per the wide-table rule, `—` when empty like the Reference column beside it. Header reuses the existing `t.accountingShared.narration` key, so all three locales were already covered. **Verified:** the page's `DataTable` mock was a stub that rendered only `voucher_number` and ignored `columns` entirely — no column in this table had ever been under test — so it now renders the headers and runs each accessor column's `cell`, and the spec asserts both the "Narration" header and the row's narration text; 1/1 green, frontend typecheck clean — done 2026-07-31
- [x] **Accounting > Vouchers > Edit no longer blanks the line accounts** — reported as "Edit not loading the line accounts correctly. Also, showing error (select account…)". **Two independent causes, both in `apps/frontend/src/app/(app)/accounting/vouchers/new/page.tsx`** (the edit form is this page with `?edit=<id>`). **(1) A reconcile `useEffect` keyed on `[accounts, voucherType]`** cleared any `row.accountId` not present in that row's allowed options — but it also ran *while the accounts request was still in flight*, when the options list is empty and therefore matches nothing. The edit loader sets `voucherType` from the fetched voucher, which retriggers the effect; whenever `getVoucher` resolved before `getAccounts` (the common case — one row by id versus the whole chart of accounts) every account the voucher had just supplied was wiped, leaving blank selects and "Select an account for this row." on every line. The effect is deleted; the reconcile now happens inside `handleVoucherTypeChange`, so **only a deliberate type switch may drop an account**, never a load. **(2) The category rules were row-positional** — row 0 of a cash/bank voucher was filtered to CASH/BANK accounts only. The API enforces no such thing (`validateVoucherTypeRules` requires *at least one* cash/bank line, anywhere), and it stores no line order at all, so a perfectly valid saved voucher routinely came back with its cash leg on row 2 — where the rule then rejected whatever sat on row 1. Rules are now voucher-wide and mirror the backend: `getVoucherTypeError` blocks submit with the same wording the API would return ("Cash vouchers require at least one cash account line."), shown in the summary panel; only FUND_TRANSFER still restricts *every* row to cash/bank, which is non-positional and matches the server. The type-helper strings were reworded from "Use the first row for…" to "At least one row must be…". **(3) Also fixed while in here: editing a voucher silently reassigned its branch.** The form defaulted `storeId` from `localStorage`/the first store and never read the voucher's own `store_id`/`attribution`, so saving an edit moved the voucher to whatever branch the user was last in. The edited voucher's scope is now captured on load and applied once the store list arrives (they load in parallel, hence a small effect rather than an inline set), with `loadStores` no longer defaulting over it in edit mode; COMPANY attribution restores the "Company-wide" option. **Backend:** every voucher-detail read (`getVoucherInclude`, the list query, the recurring-voucher post, the ledger export) now orders `[created_at asc, id asc]` — the lines of a voucher are inserted in one transaction and share a `created_at`, so without the tiebreak Postgres could return them in a different order on every read. That makes the order *stable*, not *authored*; a real `line_number` column is logged as a follow-up above. **Verified:** 4 new frontend tests — accounts survive a `getVoucher`-before-`getAccounts` resolution with the cash leg on the second line, the posted branch is restored, an edit saves both lines without re-selecting anything, and a cash voucher is flagged when no line is cash then clears once cash is chosen *on row 2* — 10/10 in `vouchers/new/page.test.tsx`, 17 accounting frontend suites / 104 tests green, backend 16 accounting suites / 243 tests green. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — verified by tests, not by eye — done 2026-07-31
- [x] **Chart of Accounts rebuilt as a standard list UI, account create/edit moved into a modal, and account groups/subgroups split onto their own menu and page** — asked as "make it a standard list UI, New Account should come as modal, make separate menu for account groups, subgroups". The old `/accounting/coa` page stacked **three always-visible inline create forms** (Group, Subgroup, Account) above the table plus two scrolling "hierarchy summary" cards, and broke several house UI rules (ad-hoc amber/sky badges, `bg-primary` on two buttons but `bg-blue-600` on a third, an amber "Bootstrap active" banner occupying the `PageHeader` actions slot). **CoA is now list-only**: `PageShell` + `PageHeader` with a single blue-600 **New Account** action opening `AccountFormModal` (`ModalShell`, so it is a bottom sheet on mobile), a Group/Subgroup/Type/Category filter bar, and one `DataTable` with **Code promoted to its own column** (it was previously squeezed as grey subtext under the name — a chart of accounts should read as a coded list), plus Edit/Delete row actions. Badges moved to the shared `StatusBadge`; all primary actions are `blue-600`. **A real bug fixed by the modal:** `createAccount` rejects any account whose `type` differs from its group's (`accounting.service.ts`), but the old form let you pick a group and then freely change Type — a guaranteed 400. Type is now derived from the selected group and rendered read-only, and `UpdateAccountDto` **omits `type` entirely** so the server always derives it. **New `/accounting/account-groups` page** — a master–detail rather than two disconnected tables, because a subgroup is meaningless without its parent: a left rail lists groups **sectioned by account type** (Assets/Liabilities/Equity/Revenue/Expenses) with per-group `n subgroups • n accounts` counts and hover Edit/Delete, and the right pane is a `DataTable` of the selected group's subgroups. Registered in the nav catalog (`accounting.setup.account-groups`, `packages/shared-types/navigation.ts`) with its layout node and the following setup links renumbered; `routes.accounting.accountGroups` and an `accountGroups` key in `accounting-nav.ts` added; the CoA hub link icon changed `FolderTree` → `BookOpen` so the two entries are distinguishable. **Backend: the six missing CRUD endpoints** — the module previously had only `GET`/`POST`, so row-level edit/delete had nothing to call. Added `PATCH`/`DELETE` for accounts, groups, and subgroups with **referential guards, since the database would not have stopped any of this**: deleting an account is refused when any of seven relations reference it (voucher details, posting rules, budgets, recurring journal lines, recurring voucher lines, voucher template lines, payment records) with every blocker named in one message; a group is refused while it holds accounts or subgroups; a subgroup while it holds accounts. **`PaymentRecord.account_id` is `onDelete: SetNull`**, so Postgres would have accepted that delete and silently dropped the payment's accounting link — it is guarded explicitly and has its own test. Bootstrap-seeded accounts need no special-casing: they are all wired into posting rules, so the posting-rule guard already protects them. **Update is deliberately narrower than create** — group and subgroup accept a rename only (changing a group's `type` would reclassify every account under it between the P&L and balance sheet; re-parenting a subgroup would strand its accounts under a group that no longer owns it, which `createAccount` forbids), and moving an account to a group of a *different* type is refused once it has postings, since that re-signs every entry already made against it. **Verified:** 22 new backend tests in `account-crud.spec.ts` (all guard paths, cross-type move blocked-vs-allowed, name-uniqueness including "may keep its own name", cross-tenant `NotFoundException`) plus the existing 221 accounting tests green; 11 frontend tests across the rewritten `coa/page.test.tsx` and a new `account-groups/page.test.tsx` (type-derived create, edit asserting no `type` in the payload, delete-after-confirm, rail grouping, pane swap, group rename asserting type is untouched). Frontend 145 suites / 1735 green except a pre-existing `admin/tenants` failure from unrelated in-progress addon-modules work (its api mock lacks `getAdminAddonModules`). Both apps typecheck clean; en/bn/ms message parity test passes — the `coa` block was restructured and a new `accountGroups` block added across all three locales, with ~20 now-dead keys from the removed inline forms deleted. **Not deployed** — local implementation only — done 2026-07-31
- [x] **CRM Contacts — an address book with basic fields, plus a business-card scanner that fills the create form from a photo** — asked for as "In CRM, add contact feature with basic fields. Also, add feature to scan business card to generate contact." **Why a new model rather than reusing `Lead`:** a lead carries a pipeline (status, score, next step, source/category taxonomy, a `lost_reason` that becomes mandatory) because it is being *worked*; the person whose card you took at a trade fair is not, and forcing them through a pipeline stage is what makes an address book feel like a chore. `CrmContact` is deliberately thinner — name, company, designation, mobile, office phone, email, address, website, LinkedIn, notes, owner — with `capture_source` (MANUAL / BUSINESS_CARD / IMPORT) recording how the row arrived, which matters because OCR-derived values are the ones most worth re-checking later. `@@unique([tenant_id, mobile])` mirrors `Lead`, and a blank mobile is normalised to **NULL rather than `''`** in the service: Postgres treats NULLs as distinct, so any number of contacts may have no number at all, where a second empty string would collide on the index. Migration `20260730120000_add_crm_contacts`. **Scanning is extraction only — it never writes a contact.** The card goes to a vision model and the fields come back into an editable form for a human to press save on; a photo is a lossy source (glare, a number split across two lines, a Bangla company name transliterated three ways) and silently creating records from it would put unreviewed OCR straight into the CRM. `AiService.scanBusinessCard` reuses the existing OpenRouter plumbing — API-key resolution, `enforceCredits`, `logUsage` under feature `business_card_scan` — so a scan bills against the tenant's AI credit ceiling like every other AI call, and gets its own `ai.vision_model` platform-setting knob (blank falls back to the default, which is already vision-capable). The prompt is instructed to **omit** anything it cannot read rather than guess, and `normalizeBusinessCard` keeps only non-empty strings for keys the schema actually has, so an invented value never reaches the form quietly. **The merge rule is that a scan never overwrites what a human typed** (`applyScannedCard`): the card fills empty fields only. Nothing is stored server-side — no image upload, nothing to serve or later delete — the photo travels as base64 and is discarded. **Three details did real work.** The client re-encodes through `createImageBitmap` + canvas to a ≤1600px JPEG before sending: phone cameras produce 4–12 MB frames, a card needs far less, and the re-encode is also what makes an iPhone HEIC pick work. `createImageBitmap` specifically, not an `Image` element — it *rejects* on a format the browser cannot decode, where `img.onerror` may simply never fire and leave the scan hanging forever with no way out (this was caught by the jsdom test, which is exactly the "decoder never answers" case). The server accepts either a bare base64 string or a full `data:` URL, because `FileReader.readAsDataURL` produces the latter and making every caller strip the prefix by hand is how one of them eventually forgets. And a card the model could read *nothing* from raises a 400 rather than returning `{}`, so the user does not end up staring at a blank form unsure whether the request even ran. **Scanning from the contacts list** (where there is no form to fill) hands the result to `/crm/contacts/new` through `sessionStorage`, drained on mount, so a scan always lands in front of a human. `POST /crm/contacts/scan-card` is throttled 10/min/user on top of the credit ceiling — every call is a paid round-trip carrying a multi-megabyte image. **Also shipped:** full CRUD, server-side search across name/company/designation/both numbers/email/notes, owner + origin filters, allowlisted sorting via `resolveOrderBy`, bulk delete/assign, CSV import (blank cells mean "not supplied", not "erase" — a re-import that only fills in emails must not wipe every other column), the `crm.contacts` nav node and CRM-hub tile, the `/crm/contacts` premium-path guard, and en/bn/ms copy (parity-tested). **One pre-existing break fixed because it blocked everything:** `prisma validate` failed on `dev` — migration `20260729160000_optional_return_parents` made `SalesReturn.sale_id` / `PurchaseReturn.purchase_id` nullable in SQL and in the scalar fields, but left the `sale` / `purchase` **relation** fields required, which Prisma rejects. `prisma generate` therefore failed repo-wide, so nothing that touches the DB client could be typechecked at all. Two `?`s, exactly what the migration already intended; the backend typechecks clean afterwards, so no code was relying on those relations being non-null. **Verified:** backend 30 new tests (service CRUD/tenant-scoping/dedupe/import + the vision leg: wire shape of the image part, data-URL handling, blank/extra-key stripping, fenced-JSON unwrapping, credit logging, mime and size rejection before paying for a round-trip) — 129 unit suites green, the same 4 `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites failing identically before and after (they need a live Postgres this container has none of). Frontend 146 suites / 1744 tests green including 17 new, `next build` clean with `/crm/contacts`, `/crm/contacts/new` and `/crm/contacts/[id]` emitted, `nest build` clean, both apps typecheck at their existing baseline (2 pre-existing accounting/billing spec errors, 1 pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` error). **Not done: never opened in a browser, and no real business card has been through the scanner** — the prompt, the model's Bangla handling and the mobile camera capture are so far exercised only by tests and a stubbed `fetch`. Photographing a few real cards (English, Bangla, and a bilingual one) is the honest next step before this is announced — done 2026-07-30
- [x] **The AI assistant can now answer product how-to / feature questions from the Help Center, not just report on the tenant's data** — asked as "now do enhancement a. Use latest help pages from dev branch" (the follow-up to the deep-link work below). Until now the assistant was a read-only *data* chatbot: ask it "how do I record a return?" and it had no tool for it, so it could only name a page in prose. **New knowledge base:** `apps/backend/src/ai/help-content.ts` mirrors the user-facing Help Center (`apps/frontend/src/lib/localization/messages/en/help.ts`, the 13-section FAQ rewritten in #420) as structured `HELP_SECTIONS`, plus a `searchHelp()` keyword search (scored token overlap — question hits > title > answer, a phrase-match bonus, a tiny synonym map like staff→employee; the corpus is ~50 entries so no index/embeddings). **New tool:** `search_help` (`tools/help.tools.ts`) — same weakest permission as the other always-available meta tools (`VIEW_PRODUCT_CATALOG`), no module gating (the docs cover every module and say which are Premium), returns the matching FAQ entries or, on no match, the section titles plus a "don't invent behaviour, point to Help Center/support" note. Wired into `CHAT_TOOLS` near the front. **Prompt:** the intro now says it answers *two* kinds of question (data + how the app works), and a new "ANSWERING HOW DO I… AND FEATURE QUESTIONS" block tells it to call `search_help` and never answer app how-to from memory (the product changes; the tool is the current source) — and to link to the relevant page from the (b) "Pages you can link to" list, so a how-to answer arrives with a working deep link. Both the prompt block and the intro line are gated on the tool actually being present. **Cross-app drift guard:** the backend can't import the frontend, so `help-content.spec.ts` reads the Help Center file and fails if it grows a section the assistant's knowledge base doesn't cover — a new documented feature can't silently become unanswerable. Only English is mirrored; the assistant translates from it exactly as it does tool results. **Frontend:** `search_help` maps to `/help` in the Sources panel `TOOL_ROUTES` (a how-to answer cites the Help Center), `/help` added to the backend nav catalog, and the empty-state copy — which claimed "I can **only** read your data" — was corrected across en/bn/ms to "look up your data **and explain how to use the app**", with a new how-to suggestion chip ("How do I record a customer return?"). **Verified:** backend `help-content.spec.ts` (new, incl. the drift guard), `chat.service.spec.ts` (2 new: prompt teaches `search_help`; a how-to turn runs the tool and answers from its result) and `chat-tools.spec.ts` all green — 87 tests across the three AI specs; the one failing backend spec (`anomaly-detection.service.spec.ts` › store filter) fails **identically on clean `origin/dev` with these changes stashed**, so it is pre-existing and unrelated. Frontend `AiChatWidget.test.tsx` (updated for the reworded capability copy) and the en/bn/ms catalog-parity tests green. Both areas typecheck clean. **Scope:** this is enhancement (a) — product knowledge — building on (b)'s route map; the two compose (a how-to answer explains *and* links). **Not done: not opened in a browser** — verified by tests and typecheck, not by eye — done 2026-07-30
- [x] **Project Management module, Phase 1 — projects, tasks, hour logging with history, kanban, and scrum with a burndown** — scoped in `docs/projects/project-management-phase-1.md`, then built. Thirteen Prisma models in migration `20260730130000_add_project_management`, `apps/backend/src/projects/` (4 controllers, 7 services, a nightly cron), seven frontend routes under `(app)/projects`, a `projects` platform feature (per-tenant overridable, off by default), six `projects.*` permissions, and en/bn/ms throughout. **The remaining-hours discipline is the substance.** `RemainingHoursService` is the only writer of `ProjectTask.remaining_hours`: it updates the column and writes a `ProjectTaskRemainingLog` row in one transaction carrying previous, new, delta, a `source`, and the sprint the task was in *at that moment* (frozen, so a later sprint move cannot rewrite where hours burned). A test scans every `projectTask` write in the module and fails if anything else assigns the column — with two control cases proving the scanner catches a real bypass and does not trip on a `select` projection; writing that control caught a genuine bug in the scanner itself, where `\s*` backtracked to zero-width and let a `true` lookahead slip past. **`source` is what makes the log worth keeping:** logging time defaults remaining to `max(0, remaining − hours)` but the caller may override, and an override is recorded as `RE_ESTIMATED` rather than `TIME_LOGGED`, so scope growth cannot hide inside what looks like progress. Completing burns to zero, reopening restores `estimate − logged`, deleting a time entry gives the hours back. **`SprintSnapshot` is a cache, not the source of truth** — the nightly cron (`50 17 * * *`, i.e. 23:50 Dhaka so the row lands on the day it describes, registered in `JOB_NAMES` and wrapped by `JobTrackerService`) writes today's point, and `computeFromLog` replays the log to rebuild any day, so a missed night is repairable rather than a permanent hole; a test builds a snapshot both ways and asserts they agree. Two figures are honestly weaker on the replay path and the code says so: task counts and completion are inferred, because the log covers hours only. **Burndown:** ideal steps down on working days alone (skipping the Fri/Sat Bangladesh weekend), is anchored to the opening commitment so later scope reads as overrun instead of moving the goalposts, reaches zero on the last working day, and leaves snapshot-less days null rather than carrying forward. Chart is hand-rolled SVG with straight segments — the scope doc said to confirm the library rather than assume, and the confirmed answer was that this repo has none; a spline would draw hours that were never measured. One board component serves both modes: kanban is every task by column, scrum filters to the active sprint and adds the burndown. **Migration verified against a real Postgres**, not just `prisma generate`: applied to a baseline built from the pre-change schema, then `prisma migrate diff` against the target reports no difference; `prisma/seed.ts` also runs clean on the new schema. **Fixed in passing, pre-existing and blocking:** `SalesReturn.sale` and `PurchaseReturn.purchase` were required relations over nullable scalars, so `prisma validate` failed on `dev` — confirmed by stashing this branch. **Verified:** backend 132 suites / 1919 tests and frontend 144 suites / 1729 tests green (91 new backend tests); both typecheck at baseline (the 3 known accounting/billing spec errors and the frontend `user-event` import are unchanged); `next build` succeeds with all seven `/projects` routes emitted. **Not done: never opened in a browser**, and the four `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites still fail for a reason unrelated to this work, now logged above — done 2026-07-30

- [x] **The AI assistant can now deep-link to app feature pages, and its "point to the right page" advice is a clickable in-app link instead of prose** — asked as "can it give link for specific app feature?" followed by "implement b". Before this the model was never told any app routes, so the two grounding rules that already said "suggest which report page to open" and "point to the right page" could only name a page in words; the only real links in the panel were the frontend's `TOOL_ROUTES` "Sources" list, keyed off which report tools ran, not chosen by the model. **New:** a module-gated page catalog (`apps/backend/src/ai/app-navigation.ts`) — path + label + one-line description for ~35 real routes under `apps/frontend/src/app/(app)` (every path verified to exist), each tagged with the same `ChatToolModule`s the tools use. `buildNavigationSection(modules)` renders a "Pages you can link to" block into the system prompt, and the two grounding rules now say to link with a markdown link to one of those exact paths. **Gating mirrors the tools:** `resolveTools` now returns `{ tools, modules }` so `buildSystemPrompt` gets the resolved module set, and an accounting-only tenant is offered only accounting pages — never `/inventory/products` or `/sales/new` — the same boundary that already hides retail tools from it. The model is told to link *only* to listed paths and never invent one, so a wrong path can't become a dead in-app link. **Frontend rendering:** `Markdown.tsx` now splits links by target — a path starting with `/` renders as a `next/link` client-side navigation (same tab, blue-600), while external web citations keep `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"`; a `//host` protocol-relative link is treated as external. Following an in-app link fires `onNavigate`, which the widget wires to close the panel (on mobile it's a full-screen sheet that would otherwise cover the page navigated to). react-markdown already strips unsafe protocols before the anchor sees the href, so the single-leading-`/` test is safe against `javascript:`. **Scope:** this is question (b) only — giving the model a route map. It does **not** add app-feature/how-to *knowledge* (question a); the assistant is still a read-only business-data chatbot that now knows where the pages are, not what every feature does. **Verified:** backend `chat.service.spec.ts` green, 24 tests incl. 3 new (catalog reaches the prompt; sample paths present; accounting-only tenant excludes retail/inventory pages) — `resolveTools` call sites updated for the new return shape; frontend `Markdown.test.tsx` green, 9 tests incl. 2 new (in-app path → same-tab link + `onNavigate` fires; `//host` treated as external) and `AiChatWidget.test.tsx` green (16). Both changed areas typecheck clean; `next lint` shows only the file's pre-existing `_node` unused-var warnings. **Not done: not opened in a browser** — verified by tests and typecheck, not by eye — done 2026-07-30

- [x] **"CRM Tasks & Follow-ups" renamed to "Follow-ups" (freeing "Task" for the upcoming Project Management module), and its two automated triggers — previously dead on arrival — actually work now** — asked as "what is the purpose of Tasks & Follow-Ups in CRM?" followed by a request to rename it and fix whatever was needed to make it useful. Investigation found the feature had **zero rows in production across all 20 tenants, ever**, despite both auto-generation crons running successfully 28 times each. Not neglect — both triggers were structurally incapable of firing: `Customer.birthday` had no input anywhere in the UI (only `createCustomer` accepts it; nothing renders the field), and the dormant-customer cron's `last_contacted_at: { lt: cutoff }` excludes SQL NULL, so a customer nobody has ever logged contact with — the single strongest "reach out" signal there is — was invisible to it, and `CustomerInteraction` (the only writer of `last_contacted_at`) had zero rows tenant-wide anyway. **Rename:** `CrmTask` → `CrmFollowUp` (model, table, both FK relations, migration `20260729120000_rename_crm_task_to_follow_up` — a clean drop+create, safe only because production held zero rows, verified against the live DB before touching the schema), module `crm-tasks` → `crm-follow-ups`, route `/crm/tasks` → `/crm/follow-ups`, the `FOLLOW_UP` enum member → `GENERAL` (kept so a "follow-up" container doesn't hold a "follow-up" type), API client methods, the nav registry entry and its default-layout node, and en/bn/ms copy throughout (`crmHr.ts`, `customers.ts`, `core.ts`, `sales.ts`) — parity-tested. The legacy `/sales/crm/tasks` redirect stub now points at the new URL rather than being deleted. **`JOB_NAMES.CRM_REORDER_TASKS`/`CRM_BIRTHDAY_TASKS` deliberately keep their persisted string values** (`crm.reorder-reminder-tasks`, `crm.birthday-tasks`) though the TS constant keys became `_FOLLOWUPS` — `job-names.ts`'s own comment warns these are persisted in `JobRun` and must stay stable, and the live table already has SUCCESS rows under the old names. **The birthday cron was rewritten to filter in SQL** (`$queryRaw` with `EXTRACT(MONTH/DAY FROM birthday)` gated by `birthday IS NOT NULL`) instead of `customer.findMany({ where: { deleted_at: null } })` pulling every customer on the platform into Node to test month/day there — correct today at 1,286 customers, unbounded before. **The dormancy check is now `OR: [{ last_contacted_at: { lt: cutoff } }, { last_contacted_at: null, created_at: { lt: cutoff } }]`**, falling back to `created_at` so a customer created yesterday isn't instantly flagged dormant. **Both auto-generated follow-up types now notify the tenant owner in-app the moment they're created** (`NotificationsService.create`, type `CRM_FOLLOW_UP`, linking to `/crm/follow-ups`) — wrapped so a notification failure never blocks the follow-up itself from being recorded. This is scoped deliberately to the two automated triggers, which were being created into a void with literally no way to discover them short of opening the CRM hub; a due-today digest for manually-created follow-ups is listed separately above, not built here. **A structural gap fixed in passing: lead-linked follow-ups had never been creatable from the UI at all**, even though the backend has validated and supported `lead_id` (including refusing LOST/CONVERTED leads) since the feature shipped — only the customer detail page had a create form, hardcoding `customer_id`. Rather than duplicate that ~120-line form a second time, it became a shared `FollowUpPanel` component (`components/crm/FollowUpPanel.tsx`) used by both the customer detail page (inside its existing tab) and a new card section on the lead detail page. **Also added: a birthday date field on the customer create form** (`AddCustomerModal`) — the backend DTO already validated `birthday` as an `IsDateString`, only the input was missing. This unblocks the trigger only for customers created from now on: **there is no customer-edit UI anywhere in this app** (`api.updateCustomer` has zero frontend callers), so an existing customer can never retroactively gain a birthday short of a database edit — logged separately above as its own gap, out of scope to fix here. **Verified:** backend 120 unit suites / 1770 tests green (11 new, covering the exactly-one-of-customer/lead validation, the LOST/CONVERTED refusal, the OR-with-NULL dormancy where-clause shape, the SQL-level birthday filter, dedup against a same-day duplicate, the owner notification firing and its failure being non-fatal, and the summary counts) — the same 4 `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites fail identically before and after (need live Postgres), and one additional failure (`circuit-breaker.spec.ts`, a real-timer test) was confirmed to be CPU-contention flakiness from running concurrently with the frontend suite, not a regression — it passes standalone. Frontend 144 suites / 1727 tests green (unchanged; no new frontend tests were added for the follow-up panel — it follows the exact untested pattern of the customer/lead detail pages it lives in). Both apps typecheck clean and `next build` succeeds with `/crm/follow-ups` emitted and `/crm/tasks` gone. **Not done: the routing/model rename has not been deployed** — this entry covers the local implementation only — done 2026-07-29

- [x] **CRM lead sources and categories are tenant-managed master data instead of Postgres enums** — asked for as "how can we make Lead Sources and Lead Categories list dynamic? Like master data. Some values can be seeded in the beginning." Both were `enum` types in `schema.prisma`, re-declared a second time as TypeScript enums in `crm-leads.dto.ts` (for `@IsEnum`) and a third time as frozen tuples in `lead-form-fields.tsx`, with nothing cross-checking the three. A shop that sells through TikTok or Bikroy.com had to file it under "Other". They are now rows in `LeadSourceOption` / `LeadCategoryOption`, one set per tenant, managed at **CRM → Settings → Lead Sources & Categories**. Categories are flat by explicit decision — not nested like `CustomerGroup`. **Shape follows the existing precedent rather than inventing one:** `Lead.source_id` → `LeadSourceOption` mirrors `Customer.customer_group_id` → `CustomerGroup` and `Customer.territory_id` → `Territory`, with a real FK and `onDelete: Restrict` so an orphaned lead is structurally impossible rather than prevented by an app-layer check. Each row carries an immutable `code` **separate from the editable `name`**, which is the detail everything else depends on: the backfill and the CSV importer join on `code`, so a tenant renaming "Facebook" to "Meta Ads" mid-migration cannot break the match or silently re-bucket historical leads. **The rollout is expand → backfill → contract, and that is forced by the deploy pipeline, not by taste.** Production has no migration runner — `apps/backend/Dockerfile:78` and `scripts/deploy-erp71.sh:99` both reconcile the live schema with `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` on container start, and `packages/database/prisma/migrations/` is never executed there (`CLAUDE.md` also names a `npm run db:migrate` script that does not exist). Running `prisma migrate diff` on an enum→String/FK change emits **`DROP COLUMN` + `ADD COLUMN`, not an in-place cast**, so the naive edit would silently reset every lead's source to `OTHER` and every category to `NULL` on the next restart. So this release is additive *only*, and that was verified rather than assumed: diffing the pre- and post-change schema emits exactly 2 × `CREATE TABLE`, 8 × `CREATE INDEX`, 4 × `ADD FOREIGN KEY` and 1 × `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN`, with zero `DROP` or `ALTER COLUMN` — `db push` is *incapable* of losing lead data here regardless of how the shell chain is wired. The legacy enum columns stay and are dual-written on every create/update so a rollback finds sane data; dropping them is a separate release gated on `SELECT count(*) FROM "Lead" WHERE source_id IS NULL` reading zero. Full runbook in `docs/crm/lead-taxonomy-rollout.md`. **Backfill** is `prisma/sync-lead-taxonomy.ts`, joining the start chain next to `sync:accounting` (the only mechanism that reaches already-provisioned tenants — the transactional seed fires at tenant creation only). Per tenant it seeds defaults, then **reconciles codes actually in use** before backfilling, then reports residual unmatched rows. That middle step is what makes the last one meaningful: without it the backfill would have to bucket unmatched leads into `OTHER`, destroying the provenance signal and hiding the exact condition the contract release must check for. It guards on `information_schema` rather than assuming the legacy columns exist, so it degrades to seeding-only after they are dropped instead of erroring — it sits in the `&&` chain ahead of `node main.js`, where an error is a full outage rather than a failed script. For the same reason it **warns rather than exiting non-zero** on residual rows. **`SOURCE_WEIGHT` was the hard part of the migration.** `lead-scoring.util.ts` keyed a closed `Record<LeadSource, number>` off the enum, so a tenant-defined source yields `undefined + number = NaN`, which passes straight through `Math.max(0, Math.min(100, NaN))` into an `Int` column — a 500 that also rolls back the enclosing conversation transaction. The weight moved onto the row, and the scoring input field was **renamed from `source` to `sourceWeight` specifically so every call site becomes a compile error** rather than relying on a grep to find all four (three in `crm-leads.service.ts`, one in `crm-lead-conversations.service.ts`); a `Number.isFinite` guard inside the util covers the path a cast could still sneak through. The six legacy weights are carried over verbatim (`REFERRAL: 25`, `WEBSITE: 20`, …) so **no existing lead is rescored on migration day**, and a test asserts exactly that. **CSV import changed behaviour deliberately.** `resolveEnum` upper-cased and exact-matched, falling back to `OTHER` — so a spreadsheet holding the *display* label `Walk-in` (the label the app itself renders) imported as `OTHER` and reported success. It now resolves against the tenant's own rows by id, code **or** name, and an unrecognised value **fails that row** with `unknown source "…" — add it under CRM → Settings → Lead Sources, or correct the spreadsheet`. Silently rewriting a lead's provenance and calling it a success is worse than a row error naming the cell to fix. The lookup is built into a `Map` once per import rather than per row, since the importer accepts up to 5000. **Two latent bugs fixed in passing, both found by the work rather than reported:** `GET /crm/leads` took `status`/`priority` as raw `@Query` strings straight into a Prisma `where`, so a stale bookmarked filter 500'd instead of 400'ing — there is now a `ListLeadsDto`; and `Lead` had no `[tenant_id, source]` index despite the source filter already existing in the service. **The one that would have shipped broken:** `packages/database/package.json` sets `main: ./index.js` and `types: ./index.ts`, so the backend type-checks against the `.ts` and *loads* a hand-maintained CommonJS mirror. The new seed compiled clean and was `undefined` at runtime — every tenant signup would have died on `seedDefaultLeadTaxonomy is not a function`. Caught by a spec, not by review. There is now a `lead-taxonomy.seed.js` mirror, and `lead-taxonomy-catalogue.spec.ts` fails if the two copies drift. **Deletion is a real flow rather than a refusal.** Removing a source in use shows the lead count and requires choosing where those leads move; seeded rows deactivate instead of deleting (the idempotent sync would otherwise resurrect them next restart); and the `OTHER` source can be renamed but never deleted or deactivated, since lead creation, CSV import and the backfill all fall back to it. **Frontend:** the settings page is reachable from a new **Settings** section on the CRM hub, which also un-orphans the pre-existing Custom Fields page — `routes.crm.customFields` was referenced nowhere outside `routes.ts`, so that screen had been reachable only by typing the URL. The leads list gained the source filter and column it never had (the backend filter and the `allSources` copy both already existed, unused), and both taxonomy columns use explicit TanStack `id`s because `DataTable` emits the column id as `sortBy` and `resolveOrderBy` falls back silently on a mismatch. The lead form keeps showing a since-deactivated row on a lead already using it, or editing an unrelated field would silently reassign the lead. Tenant-entered names are shown **verbatim and never translated** — only the seeded defaults ship with English names, which is the honest answer once the list is user-owned. Defaults are Bangladesh-retail shaped: Walk-in, Phone Call, Facebook, WhatsApp, Instagram, Marketplace (Daraz/Bikroy), Referral, Website, Other. **Verified:** backend 119 unit suites / 1752 green (22 new across the taxonomy service, the catalogue drift guard, lead provisioning and CSV resolution), frontend 144 suites / 1727 green, `next build` clean with `/crm/settings/lead-taxonomy` emitted, en/bn/ms catalog parity green, `prisma validate` green, and both apps typecheck at their existing baseline (2 pre-existing accounting/billing spec errors; 1 pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` error). The 4 `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites fail identically before and after, needing a live Postgres. **Not done: never opened in a browser, and the backfill has never run against real data** — the local dev DB is not migrated, and prod applies `schema.prisma` via `db push`, so the schema edit plus `sync-lead-taxonomy` are the load-bearing halves and both are so far only exercised by tests. A restore-a-prod-dump rehearsal before the first deploy is the honest next step — done 2026-07-28

- [x] **CRM conversations were only visible one lead at a time — there is now a filterable cross-lead list at `/crm/conversations`** — asked for as "see the list of conversations (with various leads), with necessary filtering conditions". Most of the feature already existed and was unreachable: `LeadConversation` has stored every touchpoint since the CRM module shipped, and `GET /crm/lead-conversations` has always returned **cross-lead** paginated rows with `lead` and `creator` joined and ordered newest-first. It accepted `leadId`, `page` and `limit` and nothing else, and its only caller passed `leadId` — so the one query the data supported ("what did my team do this week?") could not be asked through the product. **Backend:** `findAll` gained `search` (summary/outcome/lead name/lead mobile), `type`, `direction`, `createdBy`, `mine`, `dateFrom`/`dateTo`, `leadStatus`, `leadAssignedTo` and `sortBy`/`sortDir` through the existing injection-safe `resolveOrderBy` allowlist, plus a new `GET /crm/lead-conversations/summary` (total, last 7 days, leads contacted, counts by type) declared **above** `@Get(':id')` so Nest's declaration-order matching does not route `/summary` into `findOne`. **The two share one `buildWhere`**, so the stat tiles describe the filtered set the user is looking at rather than the tenant as a whole — tiles and rows cannot disagree about what is being counted. **Four details are the actual substance.** `leadStatus` and `leadAssignedTo` both target the same to-one relation and are merged into a single `where.lead` accumulator; two separate `where.lead = {…}` assignments silently discard the first with no error. A bare `dateTo=YYYY-MM-DD` parses to 00:00Z, so `lte` would drop everything logged during the final day — it is advanced to the next midnight and compared with `lt` instead. `getSummary`'s 7-day count **intersects** any explicit date filter via an `AND` clause rather than overwriting `created_at`, or "last 7 days" could exceed the total printed above it. And `leadsTouched` uses `groupBy(['lead_id'])` rather than `findMany({distinct})`, because Prisma applies `distinct` in memory *after* fetching every matching row — the convenient spelling would pull the whole conversation table to render one number. **Filters are validated at the edge by a new `QueryLeadConversationsDto` rather than parsed from raw `@Query` strings** (which is what the other CRM controllers do). This is a deliberate divergence: `Lead.status` is a Prisma enum, so an unvalidated `leadStatus` string reaches the driver and 500s, and `page=0` produces a negative `skip`. `@IsEnum`/`@Min(1)` turn both into a 400 at the boundary. The global pipe runs `forbidNonWhitelisted: true`, so all 14 params are declared and every string one carries `emptyToUndefined` — `@IsOptional()` does not skip the empty string — and `page`/`limit` carry `@Type(() => Number)` since implicit conversion is off repo-wide. The in-service `Math.min(limit, 100)` clamp is kept, since no CRM controller binds `PaginationDto`. **`api.getLeadConversations` moved from `fetchWithAuth` to `fetchPaginated`**, which is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: `fetchWithAuth` unwraps `.data` and drops `meta`, so `total` was being thrown away and `DataTable`'s server-mode footer would have sat at zero rows with the empty state rendering over real data. The existing lead-detail caller was simplified in the same pass, since its `data?.items ?? data ?? []` fallback became unreachable (and, once typed, a tsc error). **Frontend:** the page drives `useServerList`, which already owns the page-1 reset on filter change, the rows-per-page reset and the out-of-order response guard. Column ids are pinned to the backend's `CONVERSATION_SORTABLE` keys (`created_at`, `type`, `direction`, `lead`, `creator`) because `DataTable` sends the TanStack column id as `sort.id` and `resolveOrderBy` falls back silently for anything unlisted — a mismatch reads as a dead header rather than an error, so Summary/Outcome/Mobile are explicitly `enableSorting: false` and `summary` was removed from the allowlist (sorting free text alphabetically is not a useful operation). Type renders as emoji + plain label with no invented per-type colour map; only `direction` gets a `StatusBadge`, being a genuine two-value status. Reached from a CRM hub tile **and** the sidebar (`crm.conversations`, `premiumOnly`), and `layout.tsx`'s premium URL guard was widened to an array — `/crm/conversations` is a sibling of `/crm/leads`, not a child, so `startsWith(routes.crm.leads)` did not cover it and the shell would have rendered for a non-premium tenant even though the API 403s. Full en/bn/ms copy (26 keys × 3 catalogs; parity is enforced by both `catalog.test.ts` and `tsc`, since `MessageDictionary` derives from the English catalog). **Verified:** 23 new backend tests and 15 new frontend tests; backend 115 unit suites / 1719 green, frontend 144 suites / 1727 green, both apps typecheck at their existing baseline (3 pre-existing accounting/billing spec errors; 1 pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` error). The 4 `test/*.spec.ts` integration suites fail identically before and after — confirmed by running them on a stashed tree — as they need a live Postgres. **Two tests were checked by mutation rather than trusted:** removing the summary sequence guard, and reducing `clearFilters` to a single `setTypeFilter('')`, each turn exactly one new test red. An adversarial review pass raised 37 candidate defects across five dimensions; 34 were refuted against the real code and the 2 distinct survivors are both fixed here — the stale-summary race and the clear-filters test that asserted a guarantee it did not check. **Not done: never opened in a browser** — verified by tests, typecheck and Prisma schema validation, not by eye. The migration is written but unapplied locally; prod deploys via `prisma db push` from `schema.prisma`, so the schema edit is the load-bearing half — done 2026-07-27

- [x] **Lists now open at 10 rows per page, and a chosen page size survives navigation on server-paginated tables** — requested as "keep the default to 10 rows/page in all list UIs for new tenants". The default lived in three places that had drifted apart: `dataTableDensity.comfortable` said 20, `compact` said 25 (which is what produced the off-list dropdown bug above), and `useServerList` hardcoded its own 20 for the server-paginated tables, which do not read the density value at all. All three now come from one exported `DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE = 10`, so the two pagination modes open on the same number of rows. **Scoping is worth being precise about, because it is not what "new tenant" implies:** table preferences are a single `localStorage` key (`data-table-preferences`) keyed by `tableId` — per browser profile, not per tenant, not per user, not server-side. So the new default reaches any browser with nothing stored, an existing user on a fresh browser or incognito window gets 10, and a genuinely new tenant signing in on a machine where someone previously used the app inherits that browser's 20/25. It also means table preferences already carry across a tenant switch on the same browser, which predates this change. Anyone who has opened a list page keeps their stored size, which is the intended half of the request. Keying the store by tenant id is possible but resets everyone's saved preferences, so it is listed under ROADMAP rather than done silently. **A related defect fixed at the same time:** the four server-paginated tables owned their page size in `useServerList` and never consulted the preference store, so picking 50 on the products page and navigating away reset it to the default on return — the setting appeared not to stick. `useServerList` now accepts the `tableId` and seeds from the store on mount, while `DataTable` stays the single writer. That writer also had to change: it persisted its local client-mode `pageSize`, which in server mode is an unread default, so every mount of a server-paginated table would have overwritten the viewer's real choice with 10. It now persists `effectivePageSize` — the size actually in use in whichever mode is active. **Verified:** frontend 143 suites / 1712 tests green, 3 new plus 5 rebased. The new ones cover opening at 10 with no stored preference (asserting the select value, the reported range and that row 11 is *not* on page 1), the same under compact density, and the server-mode persistence assertion — `setPageSize` called with the viewer's 50 and explicitly never with the local 10. The 5 rebased are the client-pagination tests from the previous fix, which asserted the old default of 20; the off-list-page-size test now drives 25 from a saved preference, since compact no longer supplies it. `tsc` at baseline — done 2026-07-26

- [x] **Client-side tables were frozen on page 1, and the rows/page control displayed a size it wasn't using** — reported as "showing 1–11 of 11 while rows/page says 10". Two separate defects in `DataTable`, both in the pagination block. **The control was lying about its own value.** The `<select>` renders `value={effectivePageSize}`, but its options come from a fixed `PAGE_SIZE_OPTIONS = [10, 20, 50, 100, 500]` — and `compact` density (which the accounting module and every `CompactUiContext` page use) defaults to `defaultPageSize: 25`. **A `<select>` whose value matches no `<option>` silently renders the first one**, so the control read "10" while the table paged at 25 — hence 11 rows on screen under a footer that had computed `endRow = min(1 × 25, 11) = 11`. The footer and the table were consistent with each other and only the dropdown was wrong, which is why it read as "the selected value isn't working". A saved `pageSize` preference could fall off-list the same way. Options are now the presets unioned with the size actually in use, so the value always has an option to render. **The larger defect underneath: `pagination` was supplied in `state` — making it fully controlled — while `onPaginationChange` was `undefined` in client mode**, so `setPageIndex`/`nextPage`/`previousPage` had nowhere to write and the state never moved. `pageIndex` was additionally hardcoded to `serverPageIndex`, which is `0` whenever `serverPagination` is absent. Every client-paginated table was **permanently on page 1**: the next/prev/numbered buttons rendered, enabled, and did nothing, and `startRow` was always 1. **This was mostly unreachable until the previous change** — while every list endpoint was truncated to 20–100 rows and page sizes were 20–25, almost every table fit on a single page, so page 2 was rarely there to click. Loading complete result sets is what surfaced it. Client mode now owns a real `clientPageIndex`, and `onPaginationChange` is wired in **both** modes through one handler that branches at the end. A page-size change resets to page 1 rather than holding the index — row 40 is on a different page at 20/page than at 50/page, so carrying it lands somewhere arbitrary or past the end; server mode already did this via `useServerList` and client mode now matches. `handlePageSizeChange` routes through `table.setPageSize` alone instead of also calling `setPageSize` directly, so there is one path rather than two that must agree (the direct call also wrote a client page size that nothing reads in server mode). **Verified:** frontend 143 suites / 1709 tests green, 10 new and all 10 written against the un-fixed component first — the four client-mode tests failed on behaviour (`Row 21` never rendered after clicking next) rather than on a missing selector, and the selector test failed with an empty value against `compact`. Coverage: advancing a page, the reported range following the page actually viewed, jumping to a numbered page, the reset-to-page-1 on size change, the off-list page size displaying correctly, and five server-mode assertions proving that path is unchanged — real total not page length, `onPageChange(2)` on next, size change reporting a size and *not* a page change, a caller-supplied page 3 producing "41–60", and the `SERVER_MAX_PAGE_SIZE` filter still dropping 500 from the options. `tsc` at baseline — done 2026-07-26

- [x] **List pages reported the page size as the total row count — "Showing 1–100 of 100" on a table with thousands of rows** — reported as a wrong count; the count was the honest part. `DataTable` in client mode computes `totalRows` as `table.getFilteredRowModel().rows.length`, so the footer faithfully reported the rows it was handed — and it was handed one page. **The rows beyond that page were not merely uncounted, they were unreachable**, with no control in the UI hinting they existed. The cap varied by endpoint and none of it was visible from the page: `getProducts`/`getCustomers`/`getEmployees` sent a hardcoded `limit=100`; `getPurchases`, `getOrders`, `getQuotations`, `getReturns`, `getStockTakes` and the purchase-side equivalents sent nothing at all and took the backend `PaginationDto` default of **20**; `/sales` is cursor-paginated and also defaulted to 20; the inventory ledger was a bare `take: 200`. The backend knew the real total in every paginated case — `paginate(items, total, page, limit)` returns it and `TransformInterceptor` puts it in `meta` — and the frontend threw it away, because every one of these helpers ended in `.then(r => r?.items ?? r)`, which keeps the rows and drops the envelope. `fetchPaginated` (which reads `meta`) already existed and had exactly one caller, `getLeads`. **The fix is split by table volume, deliberately, rather than applying one recipe 64 times.** Four high-volume tables got true server pagination — products, customers, suppliers and the inventory ledger — because pulling every row just to count them is the thing pagination exists to avoid. Everything else now fetches its complete result set, which makes the count truthful and every row reachable without a page rewrite. **The expensive part of server pagination is not the paging, it is that every client-side search, sort and filter preset has to move to the server at the same time** — a `columnFilters` preset applied to page 1 of 40 silently filters 1/40th of the data and looks like it worked. So the products page's IN/LOW/OUT presets became a real `stockStatus` query param, and the customers page's VIP/At-Risk/Organizations presets became `segment`/`customerType`; both pages also gained debounced server-backed search boxes and `showSearch={false}`. **The stock-status filter mirrors the badge exactly rather than approximating it:** the badge reads `stocks[0]` and `productInclude()` orders stocks by `warehouse.is_default desc`, so it is the *default warehouse's* quantity and not the sum across warehouses — `stockStatusWhere()` filters on that same relation, and OUT uses Prisma `none` rather than `some { quantity: 0 }` so a product with no stock row at all still counts as out. A filter and the badge beside it disagreeing would be worse than the original bug. **Sorting goes through the existing `resolveOrderBy` allowlist** on all four, so a client-supplied `sortBy` can only ever select a key the endpoint published. **New shared pieces, so the remaining pages are a small mechanical change rather than a rewrite each:** `useServerList` owns page/pageSize/sort/total, resets to page 1 whenever a filter changes, and drops out-of-order responses via a sequence guard — a filter change while on page 7 fires a stale-page request *and* a reset-to-page-1 request, and without the guard the slower stale response wins and overwrites the correct rows. `fetchAllPages` walks page/limit to the end and **degrades to exactly one request against an endpoint that does not paginate**, which is what made it safe to apply uniformly; `fetchAllCursorPages` does the same for `/sales`. Both are capped at 100 iterations as a runaway backstop. **The same truncation was silently in the dropdowns**, which is the half nobody reported: `getSuppliers`, `getBrands`, `getTerritories`, `getCustomerGroups`, `getPriceLists`, `getProductGroups`, `getProductSubgroups`, `getDiscountCodes` and `getTeamMembers` all sent `limit=100`, so a tenant with 400 suppliers picked from 100 in every supplier picker with nothing indicating the other 300 existed. All now fetch the full set. **Two callers were deliberately left bounded**, because fetching everything there would be the bug: the POS customer typeahead (new `searchCustomers`, capped at 8) and the POS recent-sales panel (new `getSalesPage`, one cursor page of 50). **Verified:** frontend 143 suites / 1699 tests green (6 new — `fetchAllPages` walking, single-request degradation, query-param preservation, early stop on an empty page, cursor following, and `getProductsPaged` surfacing a 4,213 total against a 1-row page); backend at baseline — the same 4 suites / 69 tests fail before and after (`test/*.integration.spec.ts` and the demo-data generator need a real DB), with 1,706 passing vs 1,700, the 6 added covering the ledger's total/skip/allowlisted-sort and the products search + stock-status translation. Both apps typecheck at their existing baseline (the pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` and `accounting`/`billing` spec errors unchanged). **Not done, and listed under ROADMAP:** ~55 pages now show correct counts over complete data but still page client-side, so they trade a wrong number for a larger payload — each is a candidate for `useServerList` as its table grows. The journal, vouchers, reconciliation and audit-log pages were found to already drive their own server pagination and read the server total, and were left alone — done 2026-07-26

- [x] **Per-tenant feature overrides were ignored by four backend checks, so a tenant switched on individually got the UI and none of the function** — reported against the AI assistant: with `aiChat` off platform-wide and overridden **on** for one tenant, the chat panel rendered and every message came back "The AI assistant is not available." **The two sides disagreed about what "enabled" means.** The frontend resolves features from the JWT, which `auth.service.ts` builds with `resolveTenantFeatures(features, membership.tenant.feature_overrides)` — override-aware, so `layout.tsx` correctly mounted the widget. `ChatService.assertEnabled()` called `platformSettings.isFeatureEnabled('aiChat')`, the **platform-wide** lookup, which never reads `feature_overrides` at all. Every one of the six platform features is per-tenant overridable, so that switch is a *default*, not the answer, and the tenant-aware `isFeatureEnabledForTenant(feature, tenantId)` had existed alongside it the whole time — `PlatformFeatureGuard` already used it correctly. **The same defect was in four places**, all in controllers that already had `@Tenant()` in scope: AI chat (`chat.service.ts`), voice (`ai.controller.ts`), feedback and support. All four now resolve against the caller's tenant. Fixing only the reported one would have left three features that behave identically wrong the moment anyone pilots them on a single tenant — which is the entire purpose of the override. Worth noting the failure is silent in the worst way: the override does exactly what it promises in the admin UI, the effective-features column shows the feature on, and the only symptom is at the point of use. **Verified:** 116 backend suites / 1700 tests green, including 2 new — one asserting `chat()` passes the caller's tenant id to the resolver, one proving a tenant overridden on still answers while the platform default is off. The 4 `test/*.integration.spec.ts` suites fail identically on a clean tree (they need a real DB; pre-existing, the dev DB is missing `feature_overrides`), and `tsc` is at baseline — done 2026-07-26
- [x] **The chat panel now says what it can actually reach, and Platform Settings takes any OpenRouter model** — two fixes from one report ("web search isn't working"). **The panel was claiming the wrong thing.** `AiChatWidget` hardcoded "Answers come from your own data. Check the sources under each answer." and "I can only read your data" with no reference to what the backend had actually granted, so the copy was wrong in both directions: it told a user with web search enabled that the assistant could not look anything up, and it gave a user with it *disabled* no way to tell that the missing capability was a switch rather than a defect. `GET /ai/chat/tools` and its typed helper `api.getAiChatTools` had existed since Phase 5 with **zero callers** — the same shape as the chat-history bug below. The widget now probes it on first open (not on mount: the panel is closed on most page views) and swaps the disclaimer and empty-state description when `web_search` is in the list. **A failed probe keeps the data-only wording** — being wrong in the direction of promising less is the only safe way to be wrong here, since the alternative is advertising a tool the model was never handed. Full en/bn/ms copy. **Platform Settings → AI: three models added and a manual escape hatch.** `moonshotai/kimi-k3`, `qwen/qwen3.7-plus` and `openrouter/free` (all three slugs verified against openrouter.ai, not assumed), plus an "Other — enter a model slug…" branch, because OpenRouter ships models faster than a hardcoded array gets edited. A stored slug that is not in the list — hand-typed, or predating the list — now loads back into the text field instead of silently snapping to Haiku, which is the bug that version of the feature would otherwise have shipped with. Save validates `provider/model` shape inline per the UI rules, since a malformed slug breaks every AI call for every tenant and surfaces only as an opaque OpenRouter 400. Matching `MODEL_RATES` rows were added backend-side: tenant credits are token-based so billing was never at risk, but without them the platform's own `cost_usd` fallback would have recorded Haiku rates for a model that cost $0. **Caveat carried in the UI copy:** `openrouter/free` picks a different free model per request and is rate-limited (~50/day, 1,000 with $10 in credits) — well under `chat_daily_turn_cap`'s default of 200 turns, each of which is up to 8 round-trips — so it is a trial setting, not a production one. **Verified:** 16 frontend widget tests green (3 new — data-only wording, web wording, and the fallback when the probe fails; the `openPanel` helper now awaits the probe so the new state update lands inside `act()` for every test), 5 backend `src/ai` suites / 167 tests green, both apps typecheck at baseline (the pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` and `accounting`/`billing` spec errors unchanged). **Not done: the root cause was not fixed, because it is not code** — `web_search_enabled` is almost certainly still `false` on the environment that was tested; see the open item above — done 2026-07-26
- [x] **AI assistant chat history was stored but unreachable — the panel now shows it** — `AiConversation`/`AiMessage` have persisted every thread since the chatbot shipped, and `GET /ai/chat/conversations`, `GET /ai/chat/conversations/:id` and `DELETE /ai/chat/conversations/:id` have been live the whole time, with typed `api.ts` helpers already written for all three. **The helpers had zero callers.** `AiChatWidget` kept the thread in React state alone, so a full page reload orphaned the conversation permanently: still in Postgres, still counted against the tenant's credits, unreachable through the product. Its only history-adjacent control was "New chat", which *discarded* the conversation id. Closed with frontend work only — no migration, no new endpoint, no backend change. **The pane replaces the message list in place rather than opening a dialog over it:** on mobile the panel is already a bottom sheet, and `ConfirmDialog`/`ModalShell` stacked on top would have buried the dismiss affordance under two overlapping surfaces at the same `z-modal` layer. So the header swaps its bot glyph for a back arrow and its title for "Past conversations", and the composer is unmounted while the list is up — there is nothing to type into a thread you have not chosen yet. **Deleting is a two-step in-row confirm** (trash → "Delete?" / "Keep") for the same reason, and because `window.confirm` is barred by the UI rules. **Three behaviours that are the actual substance:** the list is refetched on every open rather than cached, so a thread created by the question just asked appears at the top without a reload; opening a thread restores `conversationId` as well as the messages, so the next question *continues* it server-side instead of forking a new one — restoring messages alone would have looked right and silently split every resumed thread in two; and deleting the thread currently on screen clears the local `conversationId`, since the next question would otherwise post to a conversation the backend no longer has. The active thread is outlined in `blue-600` in the list. Rows show title (the backend already truncates the first user message into `title`, with an "Untitled chat" fallback), `formatDate` in the user's locale, and message count. Failures split by blast radius per the house pattern: list/open errors render inline in the pane where the user is looking, delete failures go through the global `toast`. Full en/bn/ms copy; ≥44px targets; titles truncate rather than widening the 380px panel. **Verified:** 13 frontend widget tests green (5 new — empty state, restoring a thread's messages, the resumed-thread `conversationId` assertion, the two-step delete, and an inline list-load failure), `i18n` suite green, `tsc` clean at baseline (the pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` error unchanged), `next lint` clean. **Not done: not looked at in a running browser** — verified by tests and typecheck, not by eye — done 2026-07-25
- [x] **The AI assistant can now find bad transactions, not just report good ones** — every one of the 33 existing tools answers "what happened"; none could answer "is anything wrong". The gap was not the model, it was **granularity**: the only per-transaction tool, `list_documents`, returns document headers (number, date, party, amount), so the assistant could see "INV-1042, ৳8,000, Karim Traders" and had no way to see that the line inside it sold rice at ৳40 against a ৳75 norm. It could spot a revenue swing with `top_movers`; it could not spot the invoice that caused it. **The detection is SQL, not the model, and that is the whole design.** An LLM asked to scan transactions for "anything unusual" invents patterns it likes the sound of, misses the ones it does not, and neither failure is visible to the person reading the answer — so `AnomalyDetectionService` does the arithmetic in six Postgres queries and the model gets a ranked list to explain and prioritise, which is the half it is actually good at. **Baselines are medians, via `percentile_cont`, not means** — retail price and quantity distributions are long-tailed and the outlier being hunted is itself in the sample, so a mean moves toward the thing it is meant to catch and one ৳50,000 keying error drags "normal" up far enough to hide the next one. **14 checks in 6 queries:** sold below its own `unit_cost_at_sale`; sold at ৳0; unit price above/below the product's 90-day median; line quantity ≥5× the median (with an absolute floor of 5 units, because in a shop where the median line is one unit a multiple alone flags every three-item basket); unit cost above/below the median; bought above the shelf `Product.price`; zero-cost purchase lines; the same named customer or supplier billed the identical amount twice in one day; sales dated >7 days from when the row was written, in either direction; and refunds exceeding the invoice they are against — aggregated across every return on that sale, because three partial refunds each under the total but summing over it is the version that actually happens and a per-return check misses it entirely. **Every flag is ranked by taka at stake**, not by how far from normal it is: a 90%-off ৳40 item and a 20%-off ৳40,000 one are not the same message, and severity is derived once in a single `finish()` helper (impact thresholds, deviation, plus a per-rule floor) so "high" means the same thing across fourteen rules. **Two design decisions worth naming.** Filtering happens in SQL rather than over fetched rows — a month of a busy shop is tens of thousands of line items and the interesting ones are a handful, so pulling them all across the wire to discard 99% was never viable. And a failing detector is caught per-detector rather than thrown — five working checks are worth returning, and the chat loop has no way to retry a partial failure — but a caught failure is **named in `failedDetectors` and surfaced to the model**, because an empty row list from a query that errored is byte-identical to one from a query that found nothing, and "nothing is wrong" is the worst possible answer for an audit that did not run. When a scan comes back empty with failures, the tool states what did not run and explicitly withholds the reassurance. **Tenant isolation is structural as elsewhere** — `tenantId` is a service argument, never a tool parameter, and it appears in the baseline CTEs as well as the outer queries, with a test asserting both, because a baseline computed across tenants would leak one shop's prices into another's definition of "normal" without ever returning a row of foreign data. A branch filter likewise applies to CTE and outer query together. **Prompt work carries as much weight as the code:** a new `REPORTING ANOMALIES` section requires the assistant to quote what was observed, what it was compared against and how many past transactions the comparison came from; to offer the ordinary explanation first (most flags are data-entry slips); to treat a clean scan as a real answer rather than a failed lookup; and — the load-bearing rule — to **never name a person**, since `enteredBy` is who keyed the record and not who caused the problem, and to never call anything theft or fraud. The tool result repeats that guidance where the model reads it. Sensitivity is one `high`/`normal`/`low` preset rather than six numeric knobs the model would have to invent values for. **Verified:** backend 114 suites / 1686 tests green, including 27 new service tests and 9 new tool tests — rule precedence (a below-cost line is *not* also reported as below-median, or one problem reads as two), the baseline-sample floor, the absolute quantity floor, sensitivity presets changing what trips, impact arithmetic on every rule, ranking order, the `minImpact` filter, a scan surviving one dead detector, tenant and store ids reaching both CTE and outer query, row-capping keeping the full counts, and a model-invented check name being dropped rather than passed through (with an empty filter falling back to a full scan — passing `[]` through would scan nothing and report a clean bill of health for a scan never run). One real hole was found by the tests and fixed: a zero-price line on a product with no price history had impact 0 and was silently dropped by `minImpact`, so impact now falls back median → recorded cost → catalogue price. `tsc` at baseline (the 3 pre-existing `accounting`/`billing` spec errors unchanged). **Not done: the SQL has never been executed** — the suite stubs `$queryRaw`, so `percentile_cont`, the day-gap sign and the over-refund `HAVING` are unverified against a real database; thresholds are a considered guess and not measured against real data; there is no scheduled scan, no endpoint and no page, so it only runs when someone asks the chatbot. Follow-ups are listed above — done 2026-07-25
- [x] **Production deploys were silently half-failing — the frontend image build ran out of V8 heap** — `./scripts/deploy.sh main` fast-forwards the repo *before* it rebuilds, so when `next build` died with `FATAL ERROR: Reached heap limit — JavaScript heap out of memory` (exit 134) the VPS was left with `/opt/erp71` at the new commit while the containers kept serving the previous image. Nothing looked wrong from outside — the site returned 200 and `/api/v1/health` said `ok` — and the only tell was `health.commit` still reporting the old SHA and `docker ps` showing containers hours old. Caught by checking the commit the *running* backend reports rather than trusting the deploy script's exit. **Cause:** `apps/frontend/Dockerfile` pinned `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=1536` and the build outgrew it; the box has only 1.9GB RAM but ~7GB of swap, and because this is a V8 ceiling rather than physical exhaustion the swap was never reachable. Raised to 3072 so the build can spill into swap — slower, but it completes. **Worth doing next, and listed under OPERATIONAL:** the deploy script should verify the built image actually started before reporting success, and 1.9GB is thin for building two Node images on the same box that runs them — a build cache or an off-box build would be the real fix — done 2026-07-25
- [x] **Accounting reports can now be read at account, subgroup or group level** — the trial balance, P&L and balance sheet only ever rendered one row per account, so a tenant with a few hundred accounts had no way to see the shape of the business; "what did we spend on operating expenses" meant reading thirty rows and adding them up. A new `level` query param (`account` | `subgroup` | `group`, defaulting to `account`) is **orthogonal to the existing `scope`** — scope decides *which vouchers* are counted, level decides *how coarse the rows are*, and the two compose, so compare-scope × group-level gives group totals per branch. **The roll-up is one generic primitive, not three implementations:** `report-level.utils.ts` maps an account to its level bucket and folds account-grained rows into buckets with a caller-supplied merge, so at `account` level every account is its own bucket and the output is order-preserving and byte-identical to before — one code path, no `if (level === …)` scattered through the reports. On top of it, `buildLevelledGroups` (single-column) and `buildLevelledCompareSection` (per-column) replaced the four near-duplicate inline group builders in `accounting.service.ts`. **The trial balance is where this could quietly go wrong:** balances must be netted *signed* and only then presented on a debit or credit side — summing the already-presented debit/credit columns would report a bucket holding offsetting balances gross on both sides and it would stop tying back to the account-level view. So a Sales Returns contra account shows as a 300 debit on its own row but nets away inside the Sales subgroup, and the grand totals legitimately **shrink** at coarser levels (3,600/3,600 → 3,300/3,300 in the fixture) while `is_balanced` still holds — surprising enough that the UI prints a one-line explanation under the totals whenever the level is not `account`. **Two edge cases decided rather than ignored:** accounts with no subgroup (`subgroup_id` is nullable) roll into a synthetic per-group `<Group> — Unassigned` bucket sorted last within its group, because dropping them would silently break the totals; and bucket keys are prefixed with the account *type*, since the schema does not stop a group from holding mixed types and netting an asset against an expense is meaningless — a mixed group splits into one row per type instead. At `group` level the group header *is* the row, so `rows` comes back empty rather than repeating the header. P&L/balance-sheet groups renamed `accounts` → `rows` (it stopped being a list of accounts); the AI finance tools were checked first and read only `group.name`/`group.total`, so nothing else consumed it. **Frontend:** the level control lives in the shared `ReportScopeBar` behind optional props, so the non-COA reports (cashbook, aging, VAT) that render the same bar are untouched; the choice persists in `localStorage` like the scope does, because an accountant who thinks in groups should not re-pick it on every page. `level` is **not** a new permission — it is a presentation choice over data the user can already see. **Verified:** backend 113 suites / 1650 tests and frontend 143 suites / 1683 tests green, including 35 new backend tests and 8 new frontend ones. The load-bearing assertions are the invariants: group totals, net profit, total assets and `is_balanced` are identical at all three levels, and the compare `total` column is recomputed rather than carried forward. Both apps typecheck at their existing baseline (the 3 pre-existing `accounting`/`billing` spec errors and the frontend `@testing-library/user-event` import are unchanged) and `next lint` is clean. **Not done: not looked at in a running browser**, and the 4 `test/*.integration.spec.ts` suites could not run — the local dev database is missing a `feature_overrides` column and fails before reaching any assertion, which is pre-existing and unrelated. Follow-ups (the remaining three reports, and expandable drill-down) are listed under ROADMAP — done 2026-07-25
- [x] **AI assistant can now search the web — as a tool it chooses, not a flag on every message** — the assistant could only ever see the tenant's own database, so market prices, NBR/VAT rules, brand specs and exchange rates were unanswerable. **Why not OpenRouter's `web` plugin directly:** it is a *request-level* flag, so attaching it to the agent's own calls searches on every message — and almost every message here asks about the shop's own numbers. That is a per-search fee (Exa: $0.005/request incl. 10 results) spent on questions the report tools already answer, plus web results injected into answers that must come from the ledger. Instead `AiService.callOpenRouterWithWebSearch` isolates the plugin in a **separate one-shot completion**, and `web_search` is an ordinary entry in the tool registry: the model reaches for it only when a question leaves the business, and nothing is billed otherwise. Citations come back from `message.annotations[].url_citation` and are the load-bearing part — the summary text is secondary. **`fetch_web_page` is the "deep research" half:** it reads one page as text so the model can follow a promising hit instead of answering from a snippet. Its allow-list is the security boundary — only URLs a `web_search` returned this turn, or ones the user typed into their own message, and everything else is refused before a socket opens. Behind that: scheme and port restricted to http/https on 80/443, **every** resolved address checked against a `net.BlockList` of loopback/private/link-local/CGNAT/multicast ranges (169.254.169.254 is the cloud metadata endpoint — a page fetch reaching it is a credential leak), a host with one public and one internal address refused rather than gambled on, each redirect hop re-validated because the target was chosen by the page and not by the search that vouched for it, a 2MB read budget, and a content-type allowlist so a PDF fails cleanly.
