HTML→text is regex-based rather than a new parser dependency: script/style/nav contents are dropped *before* tags are stripped, block boundaries survive as newlines, and the output is consumed by a model that tolerates imperfect segmentation better than the tool-result budget tolerates boilerplate. **Three layers keep it off internal-data questions**, which was the explicit requirement: a `THE WEB` prompt section whose first rule is "the default is NO"; a deterministic pre-flight refusal for any query pairing a first-person possessive with an internal-data noun ("our sales last month"), which returns guidance to the model *without* spending a search — a web query naming "my" business is nonsense by construction, since the open web has no record of it; and a per-tenant `web_search_daily_cap` (default 50) counted off the existing usage log, far tighter than `chat_daily_turn_cap` because a chat turn costs tokens and a search costs cash. **Billing was a real hole:** the search sub-call is invisible to the agent loop's usage accounting, so a new `toolCredits` accumulator on `ChatToolContext` folds it into the turn's total — without it every web answer under-reports its own cost. **New `featureFlag` dimension on `ChatTool`**, orthogonal to the existing permission and module filters (this user / this subscription / this *deployment*): `web_search_enabled` defaults to false, and off means the tools are absent from the tool list, so the model never offers a capability nobody paid for. Platform Settings → AI gets the toggle plus engine (Exa/Parallel/Perplexity/native, priced in the labels), results-per-search and daily cap; the two pre-existing purple accents on that page moved to `blue-600` per the UI rules. **Audit trail extended rather than bypassed:** `AiChatToolCall` gains `urls`, so the sources line under an answer links web claims to the pages they came from by domain — an internal figure links to its report, and an external one has nowhere else to point. A refused search is marked in the trace and drops out of the sources list, since it produced no data. The prompt requires inline `[domain](url)` citation with dates, and states plainly that internal report figures are authoritative and web figures are not. **Verified:** 4 backend `src/ai` suites / 129 tests and 143 frontend suites / 1676 tests green, including ~50 new tests — the first-person guard proving it refuses "our sales last month" *without* calling the model and still allows "wholesale rice price Bangladesh July 2026"; each SSRF range refused with no socket opened; redirect re-validation; allow-list enforcement; URL canonicalisation (tracking params stripped so two spellings cannot smuggle a third past the check); HTML extraction dropping nav/script/style; the daily cap and its per-tenant scoping; feature-flag gating in `resolveTools` including a settings-lookup failure treated as disabled; and the sources line rendering a web link with `rel="noopener"`. Both apps typecheck at baseline and `next lint` adds no new warnings. **Not done: nothing has run against a real OpenRouter key** — the plugin request shape and annotation response shape come from OpenRouter's docs, so the first live call is the real test; see the open follow-ups above — done 2026-07-25
- [x] **Chat answers now render as markdown instead of literal syntax** — the system prompt has always asked for markdown ("use a compact markdown table…"), but `MessageBubble` dropped the model's reply into a `whitespace-pre-wrap` div, so users read raw `## Top Customer`, `**LifeTech Medical**` and pipe-delimited table rows. New `components/ui/Markdown.tsx` wraps `react-markdown` + `remark-gfm` (GFM is what makes tables work) with a components map tuned for the 380px panel: every heading level renders at one size, tables sit in an `overflow-x-auto` bordered container so a wide table scrolls inside the bubble rather than widening the panel, and links are `blue-600` per the UI rules. **Security kept where it was:** the bubble's old "rendered as text, never as HTML" guarantee holds — `skipHtml` plus no `rehype-raw` means raw HTML in model output stays inert, `img` is in `disallowedElements` so tenant-controlled strings cannot trigger an outbound request, and react-markdown's default `urlTransform` neutralises `javascript:` hrefs. **Lazy-loaded** via `React.lazy` behind a `Suspense` fallback that shows the raw text, so the ~144KB markdown chunk downloads only when an answer needs it — verified in the build output that it is its own chunk and not in the 225KB shared bundle. Deliberately **not** re-exported from `components/ui/index.ts`, since the barrel would drag it into every page. **The Jest config needed a real fix, not a tweak:** `next/jest` prepends its own `transformIgnorePatterns` and only lets a custom config *append*, so an appended rule can never re-include a package the first pattern already excluded — the config now awaits `createJestConfig(...)()` and replaces the list outright with a prefix-matched allowlist of the ~85 ESM packages in the unified/micromark/mdast tree. Doing it there rather than via `next.config`'s `transpilePackages` keeps the production build from transpiling 85 packages webpack handles natively. Backend prompt tightened to match what the panel can show: three-column table cap, `###` only when an answer has two or more sections, bold the figure asked for. **Verified:** 143 frontend suites / 1674 tests and the 3 backend `src/ai` suites / 77 tests green, including 7 new renderer tests (headings/emphasis/lists, GFM table structure and column alignment, raw-HTML and image suppression, `javascript:` neutralisation, and that the mdast `node` prop is not spread onto the DOM — it was, on first pass); `tsc` clean apart from the pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` error; `next build` green. **Not done: not looked at in a running browser** — no live model was called in this session, so the styling is verified by tests and the build, not by eye — done 2026-07-25

- [x] **AI assistant Phase 5 — widened the data tool surface from 8 tools to 29, and built the report APIs behind them** — the v1 chatbot could only answer one-aggregate/one-window/one-dimension questions, so anything needing a second period, a second dimension or a specific document was either impossible or exhausted the 5-round-trip budget. **New report service methods, each with a REST endpoint as well as a tool** (the point was APIs, not just chat): `SalesReportsService.getSalesTrend` (day/week/month buckets, gap-filled, `compareTo`), `.getSalesBreakdown` (one `groupBy` covering product/category/brand/branch/customer/payment_method/staff/hour_of_day/day_of_week, replacing the idea of one endpoint per dimension), `.getTopMovers` (period-over-period gainers and decliners — the "why did it change" report), `.getReturnsAnalysis` (refunds as a **rate** against revenue, by reason/product/branch), `.getCustomerRetention` (new vs returning vs lapsed); `InventoryReportsService.getStockAging` (dead/slow-moving stock, last-sold from the movement ledger so a warehouse filter actually narrows it); `PurchaseReportsService.getPurchaseTrend`. **Three shape fixes carry most of the value:** `compareTo` computes the prior window in the report layer so the model never does date arithmetic; `groupBy` collapses what would have been six endpoints; and every breakdown states its `revenueBasis` (`sale_line_items` / `invoice_totals` / `payment_records`), because those three do not agree when invoices carry discounts and the model was previously free to present the mismatch as a contradiction. **New `resolve_entity` tool + `ChatDataService`**: previously only branch ids were resolvable (they are pasted into the system prompt), so `warehouseId`/`productId` filters were unusable and the model's fallback was inventing a uuid the filters accept silently. Also new: `list_documents` (drill-down to invoices/POs/vouchers behind an aggregate — what makes an answer auditable), `describe_capabilities` (date coverage per record type, so "no data for 2019" and "zero sales" get different answers), `open_pipeline` (open sales orders/POs/quotations/leads/deliveries — every other tool reports what already happened), `payables_aging` (the mirror of receivables, previously absent entirely), `financial_statement` (P&L/balance sheet/cash flow/trial balance/ratios via `AccountingService`, so ledger figures match the accounting pages), `cash_position`, `budget_vs_actual`, `vat_tax_summary`, `workforce_summary`, `loyalty_summary`, `stock_movements`, `shrinkage_summary`, `supplier_lookup`, `customer_purchase_history`, `customer_segments`. `top_products` was **removed** rather than kept alongside `sales_breakdown(groupBy: product)` — duplicate tools degrade routing. **Plumbing:** `MAX_TURNS` 5 → 8 (entity resolution is now a first-class step, and comparison questions were failing at the cap); tool calls within a turn run via `Promise.all` but are appended in the model's call order so the stored trace stays readable; `limit`/`offset` paging on every list tool with `hasMore`, so "show me the next 20" works instead of re-running the same query. **Module filter** added alongside the permission filter — an accounting-only tenant no longer gets inventory tools it can only answer with an empty report (closes a known TODO). `chat-tools.ts` split into `src/ai/tools/{types,meta,sales,inventory,purchasing,parties,finance,operations}.ts` with the old file as the barrel. **Bug found and fixed along the way:** `buildWindow`/`buildDateWindow` in sales-, purchase- and inventory-reports parsed a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` upper bound as midnight, so `lte` excluded every transaction on the final day — a 30-day report was really 29 days plus an instant, and "sales this month" never included today. A date-only bound now means end-of-day; explicit timestamps are unchanged. This was pre-existing and affected the report pages too, but the new trend buckets and period comparisons are built on it, so it could not be left. **Verified:** 108 backend suites / 1524 tests and 142 frontend suites / 1661 tests green, including ~90 new tests (period arithmetic incl. leap-year clamping and Dhaka-local hour bucketing; every new report method; the tool projections, row caps and paging; the module filter; concurrent-tool ordering; and tenant-scoping assertions on every `ChatDataService` query). Both apps typecheck at baseline (the 3 pre-existing `accounting`/`billing` spec errors and the frontend `@testing-library/user-event` import are unchanged). The eval set was rewritten from 25 to 45 questions with efficiency criteria (taking the long way is now a fail) plus an accounting-only module spot-check. **Not done: still nothing has run against a real model**, and no frontend page renders the new endpoints yet — API client helpers exist, pages do not — done 2026-07-25
- [x] **Store-setup wizard kept reappearing after the owner skipped it** — skipping setup only wrote `localStorage.onboarding_complete`, so the dismissal was per-browser and, worse, `clearAuthSession()` deleted the key on logout; a new browser, a second device, a private window or any teammate's first login bounced straight back into `/dashboard/onboarding`. Moved the decision to the workspace: new nullable `Tenant.onboarding_dismissed_at` (migration `20260723120000_add_tenant_onboarding_dismissed_at`), a JWT-guarded `POST /auth/onboarding/dismiss` that resolves the caller's membership (explicit `x-tenant-id`, else their single tenant) and sets the timestamp via `updateMany ... where onboarding_dismissed_at: null` so it is idempotent and re-dismissing never rewrites the original time; `/auth/me` now returns `onboarding_dismissed` per tenant membership. `(app)/layout.tsx` gates both the auto-redirect and the blue setup banner on `activeTenant.onboarding_dismissed || localStorage`, and the banner's ✕ and the wizard's "Skip setup"/"Go to dashboard" both fire the API call — fire-and-forget with a swallowed rejection, so a flaky network never blocks navigation and the next dismiss retries. **Deliberately no backfill in the migration:** keying it off "tenant has a store" would have silently retired the wizard for every existing tenant, since signup already provisions a store — existing tenants see it at most once more and the next dismissal sticks. **The wizard is still reachable by URL** (demo login and the banner's "Start setup" navigate there on purpose), so the page keeps only its original localStorage self-guard; the tenant flag governs the automatic prompt, which is the reported bug. **Verified:** backend `src/auth` 52 tests green; both apps typecheck at baseline (pre-existing `accounting`/`billing` spec errors and the frontend `@testing-library/user-event` import unchanged). **Not done: the migration has not been applied to any database** — no Postgres was reachable in this session, so `npm run db:migrate` still needs running locally; the deploy script applies it in prod — done 2026-07-23

- [x] **Sales by category donut — fixed clipping, sizing and the stranded percentages** — reported from a screenshot: the total read `5 339,042.00` because `formatBDT` emits two decimals and the full string overflowed the ~60px hole, clipping the `৳`; the donut was small in a card the grid stretches to match the cash-flow card beside it, leaving a large void; and the category name and its share sat at opposite ends of a wide row because the share was pushed out with `ml-auto`. Donut grew 132→168px (radius 52→66, stroke 19→22) so the hole clears ~110px; the page now passes a 0-decimal `totalLabel` plus a new `totalTitle` prop carrying the exact figure on hover, so precision is moved rather than lost. **The share now sits immediately before the name**, right-aligned in its own `2.25rem` column — capping the legend width alone did not fix it, because a short name like "Heat & Cold Therapy" still stranded its percentage; putting the figure first keeps the numbers aligned for scanning while never separating them from what they label. **Verified by actually looking at it this time:** the component was server-rendered with `react-dom/server`, wrapped in the real dashboard card/grid chrome, styled with a real `tailwindcss` build of `globals.css`, and screenshotted with Playwright at 1280px and 360px. That caught two things unit tests did not — a first attempt at vertical centering used `h-full`, which on the auto-height mobile card pushed the legend **5px past the card's own padding** (measured, not eyeballed: card bottom minus list bottom = −5 against a 12px `p-3`; now +13 with `lg:h-full`), and a **JSX syntax error I introduced** by putting a `{/* comment */}` as a sibling before the root element inside `return (` — esbuild rejected it, but an earlier `>/dev/null 2>&1` on the render step had hidden the failure and the screenshot tooling silently reused stale HTML for two rounds. Full suite 142 suites / 1661 tests green, lint clean, `tsc` clean apart from the known pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` error. **Worth a separate look:** in the reported data "Other" is **50%** — the single largest slice is the catch-all bucket, which makes the chart mostly uninformative; that is the API's top-N grouping, not the chart — done 2026-07-21

- [x] **Dashboard charts rebuilt — axis/tooltip on cash flow, validated palettes, period-over-period deltas** — the dashboard's three charts each had a defect that made them decorative rather than readable. **Cash flow** was 28 paired `<div>` bars with no y-axis, no gridlines and no tooltip, so a bar's height was the only encoding and there was nothing to measure it against; it nested an `h-40` bar row inside an `h-40` track and then added a label row below, so the plot overran its own box; its `showLabel` rule was a chain of `||` ending in `point.cash_inflow !== 0`, which on any active month rendered ~30 date labels into ~12px each. It also spent `success`/`danger` on series identity — paying a supplier is not an error — leaving no colour free for a genuinely bad number. It is now `components/dashboard/CashFlowChart.tsx`: layered smooth areas for money in/out plus a **net curve on the same axis** (never a second y-scale), a y-axis whose ticks step outward from zero in both directions so the zero line lands exactly on a gridline and a negative-net day is never clipped, a crosshair tooltip with all three figures, and **exactly four date labels chosen as evenly-spaced indices up front** rather than "every Nth, plus the last" — the latter yields five on a 30-day range with the final pair colliding, which is what the test caught. **Curves are monotone cubic (Fritsch–Carlson), not Catmull-Rom** (`lib/charts/smooth-path.ts`): a plain spline overshoots around turning points, which on a cash chart draws a dip below zero on a day that never went negative. **Sales by category** kept its donut but was rebuilt as SVG arcs with a validated six-hue palette; the previous palette was verified as failing — `#e2e8f0` sits outside the lightness band (L 0.929) and below the chroma floor (C 0.013), i.e. a gray that vanishes on the white card, and three hues fell under 3:1 contrast. The replacement passes every gate (worst adjacent pair ΔE 9.1 simulated-CVD, 19.6 normal-vision); three of its hues are still sub-3:1 on white, so the legend always prints the share as text and colour never carries meaning alone. Arcs are separated by a 2px surface gap rather than a drawn border. **Sparklines** were a bare 2px stroke with no reference, so a rising and a falling line looked equally fine; they now carry an area fill, a mean reference hairline and an emphasized endpoint dot, toned to the delta direction. **The delta itself was the worst of it:** `trendDelta()` compared the first and last point of the current series, so one quiet Friday flipped a KPI from ▲40% to ▼40% with nothing changed in the business. Replaced by `lib/dashboard-delta.ts` + `previousWindow()` in `lib/dashboard-range.ts` — the dashboard now fetches `getFinancialKpis` for the preceding window of equal length and compares like for like, labelled `vs last week`/`vs yesterday`/`vs last month` so the reader knows what the number means. `periodDelta` **returns `—` rather than a percentage when the previous period was zero**, because percent-change from zero is undefined and "100%" would be an invention; it also measures against `Math.abs(previous)`, so a loss of 100 improving to a loss of 50 reads ▲50%, not ▼. Receivables is a balance rather than a flow and gets no delta at all. `tailwind.config.js` gained exactly one new token, `series.2` (`#eb6834`) — `primary` is the single accent and the status tokens are reserved for state, so a two-series chart had no second chart-safe hue; `warning #f59e0b` could not serve (2.15:1 on white, and reserved). Inflow uses `success.text #047857` rather than `success #059669`: against the new orange the lighter green measures CVD ΔE 6.6, inside the warn band, while the darker step clears at 8.1 — same token family, no new colour. Also removed the arbitrary hex `#059669`/`#dc2626` from `HealthKpiTile` in favour of `text-success-text`/`text-danger-text`. 6 new i18n keys × en/bn/ms. **Verified:** built test-first throughout — 27 new tests across `smooth-path` (including an assertion that a V-shaped series never overshoots its data range), `Sparkline`, `HealthKpiTile`, `SalesByCategoryDonut`, `CashFlowChart` (empty state, smooth path, zero line, negative-net axis range, the four-label cap at 30 days, hover, mouse-leave) and `dashboard-delta`/`dashboard-range`; full frontend suite **142 suites / 1660 tests green**, `next lint` clean on all changed files, `tsc --noEmit` clean apart from one pre-existing unrelated error (`src/app/contact/page.test.tsx` imports `@testing-library/user-event`, which has never been in `package.json`). The nine new Tailwind classes were confirmed present in generated CSS rather than assumed. **Not done:** no table-view twin per chart yet, and the charts have not been looked at in a running browser — verification is unit tests, typecheck and generated-CSS only — done 2026-07-21

- [x] **Per-tenant feature ON/OFF in the tenant edit UI** — the six `PlatformFeatures` switches (feedback, support, help, voice, manufacturing, aiChat) were platform-wide only: `/admin/platform-settings/tenant-features` wrote `PlatformSetting` rows that applied to every tenant at once, so piloting Voice with one shop or pulling AI Chat from one abusive tenant was impossible without flipping it for everybody. Added a **tri-state override per tenant** rather than a second set of booleans: new `Tenant.feature_overrides` JSON column (migration `20260721130000_add_tenant_feature_overrides`, default `{}`) where a missing key means *inherit the platform default* and an explicit boolean pins the feature for that tenant. A plain per-tenant boolean would have frozen every tenant at whatever the platform default was on migration day, so future changes to the platform switch would stop reaching anyone — inherit-by-default keeps the existing page meaningful. Shared-types gained `PLATFORM_FEATURE_KEYS`, `TenantFeatureOverrides`, `parseTenantFeatureOverrides()` (narrows the untrusted JSON blob to known keys and boolean values) and `resolveTenantFeatures(platform, overrides)`. **Backend:** `GET`/`PATCH /admin/tenants/:tenantId/features` (super-admin guarded, audit-logged as `tenant.features.update`) returning `{platform_defaults, overrides, effective}`; `null` for a feature clears its override, an omitted key is left alone. `PlatformSettingsService.getFeaturesForTenant()`/`isFeatureEnabledForTenant()` layer the override on the cached platform group, and `PlatformFeatureGuard` now resolves the caller's tenant before deciding — **guards run before interceptors**, so `request.tenantId` is not yet populated there and it falls back to the `x-tenant-id` header; trusting that header is safe because `TenantInterceptor` rejects a tenant the caller isn't a member of moments later, so a forged value only buys a feature check against a tenant whose data the request can never reach. `/auth/me` now returns `platform_features` **per tenant membership** (effective, overrides applied) instead of only account-wide, which also fixes a latent bug for multi-tenant users: the shell previously gated on one global set regardless of which workspace was active. **Frontend:** new "Feature access" panel in `TenantDetailModal` — one Inherit/On/Off segmented control per feature, each row showing what Inherit currently resolves to, saved in a single PATCH; `(app)/layout.tsx` prefers `activeTenant.platform_features` and keeps the account-level set as the fallback outside a workspace. The platform page's hint was reworded from "applies to every tenant immediately" (no longer true) to name it as the default that per-tenant overrides sit on top of; the six feature labels are reused from `platformSettings.tenantFeatures` rather than re-translated, with 14 new `tenants.featureControls` keys × en/bn/ms. A note on the panel states plainly that switching a feature on here does **not** grant the plan entitlement it also requires — platform switch and plan entitlement remain ANDed, so this is a kill switch and a pilot lever, not a way to hand out Premium. **Verified:** frontend 138 suites / 1623 tests green, backend `src/` green (the 5 DB-backed integration suites fail identically on a clean `git stash`); both apps typecheck at baseline. **Not done: the migration has not been applied to any database** — no Postgres was reachable in this session, so `npm run db:migrate` still needs running locally and the deploy script will apply it in prod — done 2026-07-21

- [x] **AI data chatbot — natural-language questions over the tenant's own data** — new `POST /ai/chat` runs a tool-calling agent loop over 8 read-only tools, each delegating to the *same* service method its REST endpoint uses (`SalesReportsService.getSalesSummary`/`getSalesByProduct`, `InventoryReportsService.getReorderSuggestions`/`getInventoryValuation`, `CustomersService.findAll`+`getAnalytics`/`getDueAgingReport`, `ExpensesService.getSummary`, `PurchaseReportsService.getPurchaseSummary`), so answers cannot drift from what the report pages show. **Tool calling, not text-to-SQL:** `tenantId` is never a tool parameter — it comes from the JWT via `ChatToolContext`, making tenant isolation structural rather than prompt-dependent, with a test asserting no tool schema exposes a tenant-ish property. **Permission filtering happens before the tool list is sent** (`hasStorePermission` per tool), so a tool the caller cannot use is absent rather than refused — the model never learns it exists; a cashier gets exactly `low_stock` + `stock_on_hand`, an owner gets all 8, a user with no grants gets none. Every loop iteration writes an `AiUsageLog` row (`feature: 'data_chat'`) — billing only the final round-trip would have under-charged a multi-lookup question by most of its cost — on top of `enforceCredits` and a new per-tenant daily turn cap (`ai.chat_daily_turn_cap`, default 200). The loop caps at 5 round-trips and **withholds the tool list on the last one**, forcing a text answer instead of failing the question outright; a failing tool returns `{error}` to the model rather than 500ing the turn. Tool results are projected down to scalars and capped at 20 rows with `{truncated, totalRows}` so the model can say "the top 20 of 143" — a raw report row carries whole Prisma entities and is re-billed as input tokens on the next iteration. Prior tool results are **never replayed** into later questions in a thread for the same reason. New `AiConversation`/`AiMessage` models (migration `20260721120000_add_ai_chat_conversations`) store threads per user, with a tool-call *summary* — name, args, rowCount, ms — and deliberately no result payloads, since those are a second copy of tenant business data; 180-day purge added to the existing retention cron and to `docs/data-retention-policy.md`. System prompt is assembled per request with today's date in `Asia/Dhaka` (without it, "last month" is whatever the model guesses), the tenant's branch list so branch names resolve without a lookup tool, `৳` formatting, and a grounding rule forbidding any number that did not come from a tool result. A model-supplied `storeId` outside the tenant's own branches is dropped and the model told the result is unfiltered. **Frontend:** header-mounted `AiChatWidget` (docked right panel on desktop, bottom sheet on mobile — not an FAB, per the UI rules), `blue-600`, ≥44px targets, en/bn/ms, four starter questions, and a collapsed **sources line** under each answer linking to the real report — the affordance that makes an answer auditable. Assistant text renders as text, never HTML, since it is built partly from tenant-controlled product and customer names. Gated by a new `PlatformFeatures.aiChat` kill switch (Platform Settings → Tenant Features) plus the existing `premiumAi` entitlement; optional `ai.chat_model` override; `POST /ai/chat` throttled 20/min/user. **Verified:** 33 new backend tests (tool projection/rounding/row-caps, the per-role permission filter, agent-loop dispatch, iteration cap, tool-error and unknown-tool paths, per-iteration billing, no-tool-replay, prompt contents) + 6 frontend widget tests; full unit suites green — backend 106 suites / 1418 tests, frontend 138 / 1623; both typecheck clean. The 4 `test/*.integration` suites still fail without a live DB, as they did before this branch. **Not yet done: nothing has run against a real model** — the manual eval set at `apps/backend/test/fixtures/ai-chat-eval.md` (25 questions + a cashier permission spot-check) is written but unrun, so routing quality is unverified. Plan: `docs/ai-data-chatbot-plan.md` — done 2026-07-21

- [x] **Sales Return / Quotation / Order entry rebuilt as Sales-Entry-shaped pages** — the three were `ModalShell` dialogs that each re-implemented the customer picker (a plain `<select>` of every customer), the product picker (`api.getProducts()` pulled wholesale into memory and filtered client-side, top 8) and the item table, in `rounded-xl` + `font-bold uppercase tracking-widest` styling the UI rules forbid. They are now `sales/quotes/new`, `sales/orders/new` and `sales/returns/new`, sharing the entry frame with New Sale and the sale detail page; the list pages link to them instead of opening a modal, `CreateQuotationModal`/`CreateOrderModal`/`IssueReturnModal` are deleted, and `alert()` + inline error divs give way to the global `Toaster`. **This landed alongside the view/edit work above, which had independently extracted a `SaleEntryLayout` for the same reason — the two were reconciled rather than merged.** `SaleEntryLayout` is a sale-specific composition (~30 props, three modes, hardcoded `PaymentSection`/`ProductSearch`/`entryType="sale"`) and could not serve a quotation or a return without another eight booleans, but both render the identical frame. So the frame itself became **`DocumentEntryLayout`** — slots for meta bar, customer picker, picker, table, note, panel and actions — and `SaleEntryLayout` now composes it with its public API byte-identical, so `/sales/new`, `/sales/[id]` and all 1631 of their tests were untouched by the change. The other three pages fill the same slots with what they actually need: no `PaymentSection` anywhere, and the return's picker is a serial lookup rather than a product search. **Fields an API cannot store are hidden rather than shown-and-dropped:** no adjustments block or per-line Disc % on quotations/orders, no note box on orders (`CreateSalesOrderDto` has no notes field), and no editable document date on any of the three since all three APIs assign it server-side — via new opt-outs on the shared components (`showDiscount`, `showAvailable`, `readOnlyPrice`, `maxQuantityOf`, `emptyMessage`, `showAdjustments`, `totalLabel`, and `docLabel`/`showRefNumber`/`showDate`/extras-slot on `SalesHeader`), all composing with the existing `readOnly`. Returns seed their lines from the sale through the existing `loadCart` (no second seeding path was added) **keyed by sale-item id rather than product id** — one sale can carry the same product on two lines and the cart identifies rows by `productId`, so keying by product would have collapsed them and corrupted the per-line caps; `LineItem` gained `sourceLineId`/`maxQuantity` for this. Fully-returned lines are dropped, price is read-only, and each line is capped at what is still returnable — but seeded *at* that cap where the old modal seeded 0, which is faster for the common case and is filed above as a thing to watch. `routes.ts` gained `quoteNew`/`orderNew`/`returnNew`. **Verified:** 137 suites / 1617 tests green — the deleted `CreateOrderModal.test.tsx` was 24 modal-mechanics cases (`isOpen` null-render, the X button, `<select>` interaction) with nothing left to test, replaced by 19 cases across the three new pages covering both behaviour and the deliberate omissions, and the 9 modal open/close cases on the list pages became 3 link assertions (1631 − 24 − 9 + 19 = 1617, checked rather than assumed since the removals were scripted); `tsc --noEmit` clean apart from one pre-existing unrelated error; `next build` compiles all four entry routes. — done 2026-07-21
- [x] **Storefront typography matched to the app; shop product cards shrunk to square tiles** — the storefront was already inheriting the app's Inter/Noto-Bengali stack from the root layout (`fontFamily.sans` in `tailwind.config.js`), so the "different font" was **weight and tracking**, not family: the store pages leaned on `font-black tracking-tighter`, which CLAUDE.md bans in app code. Normalized to `font-bold tracking-tight` at all six sites — the `StorefrontHeader` wordmark, the signin/signup wordmarks, the home footer wordmark, the home trending price, and the shop `<h1>` — and toned the hero from `text-5xl→7xl font-black leading-[0.95]` down to `text-4xl→6xl font-bold leading-tight` so it reads as the same type system as the dashboard. **Shop cards:** image swapped from `aspect-[4/5]` to `aspect-square`, grid from 1/2/3 columns to **2/3/4** (2-up on mobile, which is what "smaller cards" means on a 360px phone), card padding `p-5`→`p-3`, radius `rounded-2xl`→`rounded-xl`, name/category/price down to `text-sm`/`text-xs` with `line-clamp-2` on the name, sale badge shrunk, and the Add-to-Cart button reduced to `text-xs` but kept at `min-h-touch` so the ≥44px target survives the density pass. The home page's trending cards were left at 4/5 — the ask was scoped to the shop grid, and those are a deliberately larger hero-adjacent treatment. Class-string changes only; no logic, no locale keys — done 2026-07-21
- [x] **Header mic wrongly blamed the user's internet** — the toast came from the Web Speech `network` error code, which the widget mapped straight to "Voice recognition needs an internet connection." That code does **not** mean the device is offline: Chromium streams the audio to Google's speech backend (Safari to Apple's), so a VPN, ad blocker, DNS filter, corporate firewall, or a Chromium build shipped without speech API keys (Brave, plain Chromium, Electron shells) produces `network` on a perfectly healthy connection. `VoiceNavWidget` now (a) retries up to `MAX_NETWORK_ATTEMPTS` (3) on `network` — first falling through the locale chain to `en-US`, then one plain retry 500 ms later, since Chromium routinely throws one spurious `network` on the first attempt after page load — keeping the button in its listening state across the retry, and (b) only shows the offline wording when `navigator.onLine === false`, otherwise the new `serviceUnreachable` string naming the real suspects. New `isBrowserOffline()` in `lib/voice-nav.ts`; `serviceUnreachable` added to en/bn/ms (catalog parity test green). Frontend `tsc` unchanged from baseline — done 2026-07-21
- [x] **Sales view/edit now IS the New Sales Entry screen, plus Delete on the list and detail pages** — `/sales/[id]` was a completely separate card-stack layout (summary tiles, a Line Items card, a Payment Records card, a Note card) that shared nothing with `/sales/new`, so the same sale looked like two different documents depending on how you reached it. **The seven entry-form components moved from `sales/new/components/` to `sales/components/`** (`git mv`, so history follows) and each grew a `readOnly` prop; a new **`SaleEntryLayout`** holds the whole screen — meta strip, customer + product picker, line-item table, note, and the right-hand totals/payment/actions panel — and both pages now render it, so view, edit and create are the *same* markup with different props and a different `actions` slot. `new/page.tsx` kept every behaviour it had (voice entry, staged product row, Save Draft, paper-size print menu) and shrank by ~130 lines; the shared `computeSaleTotals` is now one function instead of an inline `useMemo` per page. `useNewSaleCart` gained `loadCart()` so the detail page seeds the identical cart state from a fetched sale rather than maintaining a parallel `editItems`/`editPayments` pair. `readOnly` freezes fields *without* changing layout: inputs become spans, the product search and the payment-method picker disappear entirely (and both skip their now-pointless `getCustomers`/`getPaymentMethods` fetches), and `TotalsFooter` shows only the rows that carry a value instead of a column of zeroes. Status moved into a banner — a badge when viewing, a select when editing, still locked to `['DRAFT']` for drafts so a draft can only leave DRAFT via Complete Sale. **The honest-totals problem, which drove two of the changes:** a `Sale` row stores `total_amount` and nothing else — there is no column for the entry form's discount %, VAT, transport, labour or rounding — so those figures are unrecoverable on reload. Rather than invent a plausible split, the gap between the line subtotal and the stored total is carried as a single row labelled **"Adjustment"** (new optional `roundingLabel` on `TotalsFooter`), and VAT is rendered at rate 0 so nothing is double-charged. That exposed a **pre-existing data-loss bug in `update()`**: it recomputed `total_amount` as `Σ(qty × price)` unconditionally, so *any* edit to a sale — even just fixing a typo in the note — silently erased its VAT and delivery charges. Fixed with an optional `totalAmount` on `UpdateSaleDto` which, when supplied, wins over the recomputed sum; the detail page always sends it, so a no-op re-save is now genuinely a no-op. Also fixed **payment round-tripping**: `paymentsToAmounts` matched on name *and* canonical type, but a reloaded payment carries only the canonical string (`'Mobile Wallet'`), so an amount taken via a tenant method named "bKash" came back blank on the edit form — it now falls back to the first method of that classification. **Delete** — new `DELETE /sales/:id` → `SalesService.remove()`, which **refuses outright** when a sales return, warranty claim or delivery order references the sale (those must be reversed on their own terms, or the books lose their anchor) and otherwise undoes everything the sale posted, in one transaction: stock back via `SALE_DELETE_REVERSAL` movements, warranty serials returned to `IN_STOCK`, net loyalty points decremented and their `LoyaltyTransaction` rows dropped, the `CREDIT_SALE` row deleted with its amount taken off `due_balance`, `total_spent` decremented, and **both** accounting legs voided — the main voucher and the credit sale's separate `'paid'` down-payment leg, which needed a new optional `legKey` on `voidAutoPostedVoucher` (it hard-coded a leg-less idempotency key and would have orphaned that voucher). A `DRAFT` posted nothing, so it is simply removed. UI: a red trash action in the list's Actions column and on both detail modes, `window.confirm` first, and the backend's own refusal text surfaced as an error toast. Detail page also dropped its hand-rolled `window.open` print (whose footer emitted the literal string `{t.shared.print.thankYouPurchase}`) in favour of the shared `printSalesInvoice` with the same paper-size menu as New Sale — which incidentally removes ~10 `rounded-xl` / `font-black uppercase tracking-widest` violations of the CLAUDE.md UI rules. 3 new locale keys × en/bn/ms (`shared.confirm.deleteSale`, `shared.errors.deleteSale`, `sales.detail.delete`/`deleting`/`deleted`). Tests: `[id]/page.test.tsx` rewritten for the shared layout (24 cases — read-only assertions, the adjustment carry, save payload including `totalAmount`, the bKash reload, drafts, and all four delete paths) and 9 new backend cases covering the reversal, the three referential refusals, the draft short-circuit, the walk-in path and `totalAmount` precedence. Frontend 135 suites / 1631 green; backend `src/` 106 suites / 1401 green (the 4 DB-backed integration suites fail identically on a clean `git stash`); `tsc` byte-identical to baseline on both apps — done 2026-07-21
- [x] **Draft sales convert into real sales** — the follow-up to the entry below, which left drafts save-and-view only. `POST /sales/:id/finalize` (`FinalizeSaleDto`, all fields optional) posts a parked draft **through the exact same code path as a direct sale**, which is the point of the refactor it required: `create()`'s body was split into `prepareSale()` (warehouse, product costs, warranty serials, totals, loyalty preview, credit headroom — everything validated *before* a row is written) and `applySalePostings()` (sale lines, stock movement, serials, loyalty, `due_balance`, accounting voucher), so `finalizeDraft()` is `prepare → swap the parked lines/payments → flip the row to COMPLETED → apply postings` with **no second implementation of the sale rules to drift**. Behaviour-preserving: the pre-existing 115 backend sales tests passed untouched across the refactor. The draft's **serial and reference number survive** (it's an update, not a re-create) and the whole thing runs in one transaction, so a stock failure rolls the status flip back with nothing posted. Optional overrides let the user adjust items/payments/customer/date on the way out — and **warranty serials can only arrive here**, since `validateWarrantySerials` demands one serial per unit and a draft never captured them. One subtlety worth recording: a draft stores whatever total the entry screen sent, with no discount breakdown, so finalize **re-derives `discountAmount` as `subtotal − storedTotal`** — that keeps the shared total check consistent instead of rejecting every draft the moment it posts. **Two latent bugs in `update()` fixed while here**, both from drafts holding no stock: editing a draft's items ran the `SALE_EDIT_REVERSAL` loop over lines that were never decremented (which would have *inflated* stock) and adjusted `total_spent` that was never incremented — both now skipped for `status === 'DRAFT'`; and flipping a draft to COMPLETED via `PATCH` is refused outright ("Finalize this draft instead") so it can't bypass the posting path. **UI:** `/sales/[id]` shows an amber "nothing posted yet" banner and a blue **Complete Sale** action for drafts, toasts the backend's own reason on failure (e.g. insufficient stock) and reloads; in edit mode a draft's status select offers only DRAFT. New `api.finalizeSale`; `DRAFT` label added to `shared.statuses.sale` + 5 detail strings across en/bn/ms (catalog parity test green). Tests: 6 new backend cases (posts stock/lines/payments/spend/voucher, honours edited items, refuses a non-draft, rolls back on insufficient stock, plus the two `update()` guards) and 5 new frontend cases. Backend 104 suites / 1382 green; frontend 135 suites / 1638 green; `tsc` unchanged from baseline on both — done 2026-07-21
- [x] **New Sales Entry: staged product row (unit price / qty / available stock), editable line price, keyboard picking, previous due, Save as Draft** — five asks on `/sales/new`. (1) **Picking a product no longer adds it blind:** `ProductSearch` now stages the selection into an inline row showing the product, its **available stock summed across warehouses** (the search endpoint already `include`s `stocks`; new exported `availableQtyOf` helper), plus editable **Unit Price** (prefilled from the product) and **Qty**; Add/Enter commits, Escape cancels, focus returns to the search box. Available stock also shows on every dropdown row, and the committed line carries `availableQty` (new optional field on `LineItem`) into a new "Avail" table column that turns amber when qty exceeds it. Voice entry passes `{quantity}` and leaves `availableQty` undefined rather than claiming zero stock, since those products come without stock rows. (2) **Line price is now an input** in `LineItemsTable`, same as Disc %/Qty (floored at 0). (3) **↑/↓ + Enter + Escape** on both `ProductSearch` and `CustomerSelection`, with the highlighted row scrolled into view and mouse hover keeping the highlight in sync. (4) **Previous Due** renders under Total in `TotalsFooter` from `customer.due_balance` (already returned by `GET /customers`) — informational only, deliberately **not** rolled into the sale total, which would have broken the backend's total-vs-lines check. (5) **Save as Draft** — new optional `isDraft` on `CreateSaleDto` routes `SalesService.create` to a separate `createDraft` path that stores the sale (`status: 'DRAFT'`), its lines and the intended payments and **nothing else**: no inventory movement, no total/overpayment/credit-limit validation, no loyalty, no `due_balance`/`total_spent` change, no accounting voucher, no receipt email/SMS. Safe because **every `sales-reports` query already filters `status: 'COMPLETED'`**, so drafts stay out of revenue figures; `total_amount` is stored as sent (not recomputed) since a draft is parked as entered. Frontend requires only "at least one item"; the backend still verifies the products exist. Sales list gets a grey DRAFT badge + a Draft filter preset (label added to en/bn/ms). **Follow-up shipped same day — see the draft-conversion entry above.** Tests: 4 new frontend cases (staging shows summed stock + prefilled price, edited price/qty reach the cart, arrow-key pick, draft posts `isDraft: true` with no payment) — the existing sale-date test gained the new Add step — and 2 backend cases (draft stores lines and posts nothing; unknown product rejected). Frontend `sales/` 28 suites / 369 green; backend `src/sales` 6 suites / 115 green; `tsc` unchanged from baseline on both apps — done 2026-07-21
- [x] **Sidebar reorg across Purchase, Accounting, Inventory and HR; "Advanced" badges deleted platform-wide** — third and largest pass of the nav cleanup, same registry-driven pattern as the two Sales entries below (`NAV_REGISTRY` + `DEFAULT_TENANT_NAV_LAYOUT` in `packages/shared-types/navigation.ts`). **Five subgroups dissolved** — `purchase.order-flow`, `purchase.payables`, `accounting.reconciliation`, `hr.organization`, `hr.operations` — with their 12 links re-parented to the module root. As with the earlier passes the **registry entries and child ids were deliberately left intact** (`purchase.order-flow.orders` etc.): ids are opaque keys and renaming them would invalidate any stored `tenantNavLayout` / platform override. **Moves:** `inventory.products` → into Inventory Setup (first slot), `inventory.labels` → into Inventory Reports (last slot). **Renames across en/bn/ms** (5 keys × 3 locales): `purchases.hub.setup`→'Purchase Setup', `sidebar.sections.accountingReports`→'Accounting Reports', `sidebar.sections.accountingSetup`→'Accounting Setup' (was 'Settings'), `inventory.hub.setup`→'Inventory Setup', `sidebar.items.categories`→'Product Categories'; three of those keys are shared with the `/purchases`, `/accounting` and `/inventory` hub pages, whose section headers follow suit. **Advanced badges removed globally by deleting the two render sites in `Sidebar.tsx`** rather than by touching flags — this was the key call: in Sales/Purchase the *subgroup* carries `advancedOnly` so link-level flags are decorative, but in **Inventory and Accounting the group is ungated and only individual links are flagged**, so stripping those flags would have exposed Reorder/Shrinkage/Valuation and Comparative P&L/Budget-vs-Actual/Cash-Flow/Financial-Ratios to tenants without the entitlement, while hoisting them to the group would have hidden Stock Ledger and the 8 basic accounting reports. Deleting the JSX leaves every gating flag untouched, so visibility is provably identical — and it also removes two `font-black uppercase tracking-widest` spans that violate the CLAUDE.md UI rules. The second badge site was **dead code** (`mod.key === 'reports'`, a module key that has never existed in the registry). **Also:** added the missing `sales.storefront.orders` ('Online Orders', `/storefront`) to the Sales sidebar — it was registered but in *no* layout, reachable only from the `/sales` hub page; and all four setup subgroups (sales/purchase/accounting/inventory) now use the `Layers` icon instead of a gear, establishing "setup group = Layers" and finishing the gear-in-gear fix from the entry below. **Deliberately not done:** a Warehouses link — there is no `/inventory/warehouses` route, warehouses are a section *inside* `/inventory/settings`, so the requested serial ships as Products / Product Categories / Brands / Inventory Settings. Tests updated: `nav-resolver.test.ts` (accounting labels + a new Bank-Reconciliation-is-ungrouped assertion), `Sidebar.test.tsx` (6 assertions repointed off the dissolved Payables/Reconciliation groups and the renamed accounting groups; the accordion test now pairs Accounting Setup against Accounting Reports), `accounting/page.test.tsx` ('Settings'→'Accounting Setup'). Frontend 135 suites / 1629 green; backend nav specs 2/2; `tsc` error count byte-identical to a clean `git stash` (3 known pre-existing test-file errors); `shared-types` `dist/` rebuilt. **Known follow-ups, surfaced but not actioned:** Account Settings shows 4 of its 20 registered pages in the sidebar (rest are hub-only), `admin.referrals` and `sales.new` are in no layout at all, and Sales Reports still holds Customer Ledger / Due Aging / Loyalty Points — done 2026-07-20
- [x] **Sales sidebar: "Sales Settings" group → "Sales Setup", storefront link disambiguated, gear icons de-duplicated** — follow-up to the entry below, which had left a "Sales Settings" group containing a "Sales Settings" item. The *other* confusing item turned out to be a pre-existing bug, not new: `sales.storefront.settings` uses `sidebar.items.storefrontSettings`, whose value was literally `'Settings'` — fine back when it sat inside an "Online Storefront" subgroup that supplied the missing noun, but that subgroup isn't in `DEFAULT_TENANT_NAV_LAYOUT`, so the link has hung context-free off the setup group for a while. Renamed the **group** `sales.hub.setup` → 'Sales Setup' (bn 'বিক্রয় সেটআপ', ms 'Persediaan Jualan') so the item keeps the more precise 'Sales Settings' name, and the **storefront link** → 'Storefront' (bn 'স্টোরফ্রন্ট', ms 'Kedai Dalam Talian' — reusing the wording already on `sidebar.modules.storefront` for consistency). Chose this over renaming the item because `sidebar.items.salesSettings` is **shared with `account-settings.sales`**, where 'Sales Settings' is exactly right — renaming it would have needed a new key rather than an edit. **Icons were half the confusion and got fixed too:** the group, the storefront link, and Sales Settings were all gears (`Settings`/`Settings`/`Cog`); group → `Layers` (unused elsewhere in Sales), storefront → `Globe` (matches how the storefront is iconed elsewhere), leaving `Cog` uniquely on the real settings page. - [x] **Customers: Customer Code + Owner Name fields, and the CSV/Excel import actually imports** — two asks, one root cause between them. **The import bug:** `Customer.phone` was `NOT NULL` while `IMPORT_FIELDS` on `/sales/customers` marked Phone `required: false`, so the wizard happily let you map only Name, preview, and import — and then *every* row died in `runImport`'s per-row catch with a raw multi-line "Invalid prisma.customer.create() invocation … Argument phone is missing." dump. Reproduced against the local stack before touching anything (3 rows in → `created: 0` + 3 Prisma dumps) and confirmed a file *with* phones already imported fine and already skipped bad rows — so the row-skipping the user asked for was structurally present all along; it was the blanket phone failure that made "import is not working" look total. Fixed the way `Lead.mobile` was made optional on 2026-07-12 (same shape, deliberate precedent): `phone String?` + migration `20260720120000_customer_owner_name_optional_phone` (`DROP NOT NULL`; the `@@unique([tenant_id, phone])` index stays valid because Postgres treats NULLs as distinct). **New fields:** `owner_name String?` (nullable, new column) and `customer_code` promoted from purely auto-generated to user-supplied-or-generated — both added to `Create/UpdateCustomerDto`, the Add Customer modal, the list table (Owner column, `hideOnMobile`), the profile header, `findAll` search, and `IMPORT_FIELDS`; import now also dedupes on `customer_code` **first** (then phone, then email), which is what makes a code-keyed shop list upsert correctly. **Code allocation reworked** now that users can type their own: `generateCustomerCode` scopes its `orderBy` to `startsWith: 'CUST-'` so a hand-entered `SHOP-42` can't derail the series (previously it took the lexical max over *all* codes, and a non-matching regex silently reset the counter to 1 → guaranteed unique-constraint collision), and a new `withCustomerCode` helper pre-checks explicit codes for a friendly `Customer code "X" is already in use.` and retries generated ones on P2002 — closing the long-standing read-then-write race that bulk import made much more likely. **`describeRowError` in `import.util.ts`** collapses Prisma dumps to their trailing sentence and names the conflicting fields on P2002/P2003/P2025 — this improves error text for **all 12 importers**, not just customers. **Ripple from nullable phone, checked by hand because `strictNullChecks` is off in both tsconfigs so the compiler catches none of it:** `crm-campaigns.resolveTargetCustomers` now filters `phone: { not: null }` (every channel writes a `CrmCampaignRecipient`, whose `phone` is NOT NULL, and the SMS/WhatsApp senders call `.replace()` on it), and `CustomerSelection.tsx`'s `c.phone.includes(query)` → `c.phone?.includes(query)`. Remaining `.phone` reads audited and are display-only or already guarded (`sales.service` gates on `customer?.phone`). Locale keys added to **en/bn/ms** (`columns.ownerName`, `modal.customerCode`/`customerCodeHint`/`ownerName`, 2 placeholders) — `catalog.test.ts` enforces parity — and `modal.addFailed` reworded off "Phone might already exist." Verified end-to-end against a real backend + Postgres: the exact original failure now returns `created: 3`; a mixed batch returns `created: 3, skipped: 1, errors: ["Row 3: missing required field(s): name"]`; `SHOP-001/003` and `CUST-00018..21` coexist in the DB with the series intact; upsert-by-code, duplicate-code rejection, and owner-name search all confirmed. 9 new backend tests (4 import/allocation in `customers.service.spec.ts`, 5 `describeRowError`); backend unit suites 1384 green — the 4 failing DB-backed integration suites are pre-existing, confirmed byte-identical on a clean `git stash`. **Note:** the local dev DB is `db push`-managed with no `_prisma_migrations` table, so the migration was verified by applying its SQL directly; `migrate diff` against the full history is blocked by an unrelated pre-existing break at `20260510182137_warranty_claims` — done 2026-07-20
- [x] **Expenses split out of Accounting into its own sidebar module; Loans kept in Accounting** — the sidebar tree is registry-driven (`NAV_REGISTRY` + `DEFAULT_TENANT_NAV_LAYOUT` in `packages/shared-types/navigation.ts`), so the split is a registry change, not a Sidebar edit. New top-level `expenses` module (root sortOrder 4, between Accounting and Inventory; inventory→11 renumbered) with `expenses.list`/`.categories`/`.reports`. **Loans stays under Accounting** as a direct child (`accounting.loans`, slot 5) rather than moving to HR: the `Loan` model is business financing (`direction` PAYABLE/RECEIVABLE, free-text `counterparty`, no `employee_id`) — bank/director borrowings, not employee advances, which `SalaryPayment` already handles as instalments/advances. The now-single-item `accounting.transactions` subgroup was dissolved. **URLs unchanged** (`/accounting/expenses/*`) — deliberately nav-only; the route move is a filed follow-up. `expenses` is gated on `canAccessAccounting` in both normal and `accountingOnlyMode` paths of `Sidebar.tsx`, preserving today's access exactly (and `isAccountingOnlyBlockedPath` still permits the pages since they're under `/accounting`). Locale work: added `sidebar.modules.expenses` and renamed `accounting.hub.transactions`→`accounting.hub.financing` ('Loans & Financing') across **en/bn/ms** — `catalog.test.ts` enforces key-identical catalogues. Hub page's `ACCOUNTING_TRANSACTION_LINKS` split into `ACCOUNTING_FINANCING_LINKS` (loans) + `EXPENSE_LINKS`. **Also deleted two stale parallel definitions found in the process:** `buildAccountingSidebarChildren` (+ its `AccountingSidebarNav*` types and its test) in `accounting-nav.ts` — a second hand-rolled sidebar tree that nothing but its own test imported since the registry took over, and which still described the pre-split layout; and `packages/shared-types/navigation-updated.ts`, a comments-only scratch file proposing a *different* (subgroup-inside-accounting) version of this same change. Tests updated: `nav-resolver.test.ts` (new case asserting the three expense hrefs hang off the `expenses` module), `Sidebar.test.tsx` (2 assertions repointed off 'Transactions & Funds' to Reconciliation/Reports; 2 new cases for the Expenses module's visibility gate). Frontend 135 suites / 1629 tests green; shared-types + frontend `tsc` clean (only the 3 known pre-existing test-file errors). Container boot auto-ran `sync:accounting` LIVE across 19 tenants (added the missing `sale/payment_mode/{bkash,nagad,credit}` + supplier/salary/depreciation/asset/fund-transfer rules — the fix for the original "some Skipped, some Posted" bKash/Nagad report); verified idempotent via follow-up `--dry-run`. Then `backfill:vouchers` (posted **1373** historical rows, 0 failed, idempotent) and `repair:vouchers` (deleted 1 fabricated voucher + 38 harmful fallback rules). `reconcile:balances` run **report-only** — 48 mismatches remain (seed-denorm drift + Grocery Demo AP where purchases aren't yet backfilled), so **`--rebuild` deliberately NOT run**. - [x] **Fixed-asset acquisition posts a voucher** (gap found by the Phase 7 census) — `createFixedAsset` only registered the asset row; the acquisition (Dr Fixed Assets / Cr Cash|Bank) never reached the GL, so the cash outflow was invisible and Fixed Assets on the balance sheet stayed empty. New `asset_acquisition` event (Prisma enum + migration `20260718140000`, `PostingEventType`, `VOUCHER_TYPE_BY_EVENT`=CASH_PAYMENT) + rules `asset_acquisition/payment_mode × cash/bank/bkash/nagad` → Dr Fixed Assets / Cr \<mode\> (both `bootstrap-accounting.ts` and `.js`); 4 `POSTING_CONTRACT` entries. `createFixedAsset` now runs in a `$transaction`, posts via `classifyPaymentMode(dto.paymentMethod ?? 'cash')`, dated to the purchase date (fiscal-period guard applies). New optional `paymentMethod` on `CreateFixedAssetDto` (frontend field is a follow-up; API defaults cash). **`FixedAsset` reclassified `gap`→`postsVia: 'asset_acquisition'`** in `money-model-contract.ts` (gap set now just `OrderDeposit`). 3 unit tests + e2e verified against real DB (Dr 1050 Fixed Assets / Cr 1010 Cash in Hand, balanced). `sync:accounting` applied the 4 rules to existing tenants. Backend 104 suites / 1358 green; `tsc` only the 3 known pre-existing. **Follow-up:** per-asset `asset_account_id`/`depreciation_account_id` overrides still ignored (separate TODO) — done 2026-07-18
- [x] **GL-derived party ledgers + reconciliation** (Phase 8 cont. of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — the ledger UI now reads the **general ledger**, not the parallel `CustomerCreditTransaction`/`SupplierCreditTransaction` tables. New `accounting/party-ledger.util.ts` (`buildPartyLedger`) builds a party's ledger from its tagged control-account voucher lines in the account's natural sign (AR debit−credit / AP credit−debit), with opening/closing/running balances and `from`/`to` windowing. New `GET /customers/:id/gl-ledger` + `GET /suppliers/:id/gl-ledger` (thin service wrappers) return the **same shape** as the credit-transaction endpoints — debit/credit mapped to the `CREDIT_SALE`/`CREDIT_PURCHASE`/`PAYMENT` labels the pages already render — so both ledger pages swapped their data source (`api.getCustomerGlLedger`/`getSupplierGlLedger`) with **no rendering change**. Credit-transaction endpoints kept **alongside** for diffing. Plus `npm run reconcile:balances` (`packages/database`, report-only default, `--rebuild` to overwrite `due_balance` from the GL) — the "diff the two on real data before deleting anything" step; a mismatch isn't auto-a-bug (GL and `due_balance` legitimately diverge on over-returns / before backfill). 6 `buildPartyLedger` unit tests (mutation-checked: flipping the asset/liability sign fails) + real-DB smoke (700 sale → bal 700, 300 payment → bal 400); reconcile verified e2e (due 999 vs GL 700 reported, `--rebuild` → 700). Backend 104 suites / 1350 green; frontend `tsc` clean (only pre-existing unrelated errors). Deferred: retiring the parallel tables once the diff is clean on prod — done 2026-07-18
- [x] **Historical voucher backfill script** (Phase 8 core of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — `sync:accounting` fixed the posting *rules* but not history: a bKash sale booked on a pre-2026-07-16 tenant, or any supplier payment booked before the `supplier_payment` event existed, still has no voucher. New `apps/backend/scripts/backfill-vouchers.ts` (`npm run backfill:vouchers`, `--dry-run`, `--tenant=`) re-posts those rows. **Safe by construction:** `autoPostFromRules` is idempotent (a posted row short-circuits — verified: 2nd run posts 0), a row whose historical date is in a **locked** fiscal period is left alone and reported (never force-posted), `--dry-run` writes nothing (verified) — the per-tenant review gate. Two reposters: **sales** (reconstructs credit-vs-mode from the row + first payment, reusing `classifyPaymentMode`/`creditDueAmount` to stay in lock-step with `sales.service`; partial paid-portion dropped to match live, not Phase 6) and **supplier payments** (PAYMENT→pay / PAYOUT→receive, tagged with the supplier party). Extensible `Reposter` framework (`findCandidates`) for the rest. Uses `tsx`, not `ts-node` (backend only has `tsx`; `seed:demo`'s `ts-node` is separately broken — noted). **Verified end-to-end against the real dev DB:** inserted a skipped bKash sale directly (no posting event), dry-run reported it + inferred `bkash` and wrote nothing, live posted Dr 1015 bKash Account / Cr 4010 Sales Revenue (balanced 500=500), re-run was a no-op; test data cleaned up. Backend suite 103 suites / 1344 tests green; `tsc` clean on the script. **NOT run against any prod DB** — that dry-run + per-tenant review is tracked as an open item. Remaining Phase 8: more reposters (customer/depreciation/salary/cashier), repoint ledger UI at the GL-derived view, rebuild `due_balance` — done 2026-07-18
- [x] **Money-model contract — the anti-recurrence guard** (Phase 7 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — the backstop that makes this whole bug class (a model that moves money and never posts) impossible to add silently. `POSTING_CONTRACT` starts from call sites that CAN hide; this starts from the data model that CANNOT. New `money-model-contract.ts` classifies all **65** models with a `@db.Decimal` field as `postsVia: <eventType>` (15), `exempt: '<reason>'` (48), or `gap: '<reason>'` (2); `money-model-contract.spec.ts` parses `schema.prisma`, asserts every money model is classified (no unclassified, no stale, no dupes), every `postsVia` names an event type `POSTING_CONTRACT` knows, every reason is non-empty, and pins the gap set. **Mutation-verified both directions:** dropping a classification fails, and appending a new `@db.Decimal` model to the schema fails the build until classified — the exact recurrence property. Added a third `gap` state (beyond the spec's postsVia/exempt) so known-unwired money is tracked, not hidden: `OrderDeposit` (Phase 4/5 deposits) and **`FixedAsset` — a newly-found gap** (asset acquisition isn't posted; `createFixedAsset` only registers the row), now its own TODO item. Same drift-guard idiom as `database-exports.spec.ts`. Backend suite 103 suites / 1344 tests green; `tsc` only the 3 known pre-existing errors — done 2026-07-18
- [x] **Payroll accrual + payment — employees get a GL payable ledger** (Phase 5b / D4=IN, two commits, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — the flow the user asked for: "salary posted every month to the payable account, payments adjusted from there." **(A) Accrual:** new `SalaryAccrual` model (mirrors `AssetDepreciationEntry`), `Salary Payable` (2050, marked `party_type EMPLOYEE` — the third party-control account) + `Salary & Wages` (5020), `salary_accrual` event, rule `salary_accrual/none` → Dr Salary & Wages / Cr Salary Payable [party]; `runMonthlyAccrual` (`POST /salary-payments/run-accrual`, OWNER/ACCOUNTANT) mirrors `runDepreciation` (one `$transaction`, idempotent per (employee, period), dated to period end for the fiscal-period guard, voucher_id written back), skips+reports employees with no `basic_salary`, expense leg untagged / payable leg carries the employee party. **(B) Payment settlement:** `salary_payment` event, rules `salary_payment/payment_mode × cash/bank/bkash/nagad` → Dr Salary Payable / Cr \<mode\> [party] via `classifyPaymentMode`; wired into `salary-payments.create` (now in a `$transaction`, posts + returns `posting_status`/`voucher_id`), `update` void-and-reposts, `remove` voids. **Dropped the `@@unique([tenant_id, employee_id, pay_period])` constraint** (migration `20260718130000`, plain index instead; removed the `ConflictException` duplicate checks in create/update) — a deliberate product behaviour change so a period can be paid in instalments/advances; the payable balance, not a row count, is the source of truth. All accounts + rules in `bootstrap-accounting.ts` AND `.js`. `sync:accounting` applied 2050/5020 + 5 rules to existing tenants. **E2e verified against a real DB (both commits):** accrual posts Dr 5020 / Cr 2050 [party] dated to month-end; accrue 30000 then pay 10000 leaves the employee's Salary Payable at 20000 credit (`accrual − Σ payments`, GL-derived). Scope limits (documented in spec): flat `basic_salary` only (no allowances/deductions), no attendance→pay link (loss-of-pay out of scope), raises don't retro-adjust posted accruals. Backend suite 102 suites / 1336 tests green; integration 9/9; `tsc` only the 3 known pre-existing errors — done 2026-07-18
- [x] **Cashier PAYOUT/LOAN posts a voucher — till cash-out reaches the GL** (Phase 1+4 slice of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — `addCashTransaction` created a `CashTransaction` (cash out of the till) but posted nothing, so petty payouts and staff loans never hit the GL. New `Staff Advances` (1060, plain account in the Receivables subgroup) + `cash_transaction` event type (Prisma enum + migration `20260718110000_add_cash_transaction_event_type`, `PostingEventType` union, `VOUCHER_TYPE_BY_EVENT`=CASH_PAYMENT); rules on the existing `reason_type` condition key: `PAYOUT` → Dr General Operating Expense / Cr Cash in Hand, `LOAN` → Dr Staff Advances / Cr Cash in Hand — both in `bootstrap-accounting.ts` AND `.js` (verified via node load); 2 `POSTING_CONTRACT` entries. `addCashTransaction` rewrapped in a `$transaction`; posts only `PAYOUT`/`LOAN` (a `POSTABLE_CASH_TYPES` set) — **`DROP` and `OTHER` deliberately don't post** (drawer→safe and misc are Cash in Hand on both sides). `amount` is signed (negative for cash-out) so the voucher takes `Math.abs`. 4 new tests (mutation-checked: posting all types fails the DROP/OTHER tests) + **e2e verified against the real DB**: PAYOUT → Dr 5010 / Cr 1010, LOAN → Dr 1060 / Cr 1010, both balanced cash_payment vouchers. `sync:accounting` applied 1060 + 2 rules to existing tenants. Backend suite 102 suites / 1327 tests green; integration 9/9; `tsc` only the 3 known pre-existing errors. **Note:** 1060 is a plain account (no party) because a cashier session links to a `user_id`, not an `Employee`; the party tension with Phase 5b employee salary advances is documented in the spec's Phase 5b section — done 2026-07-18
- [x] **Depreciation posts a voucher — fixed assets reach the P&L** (Phase 1+4 slice of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — `runDepreciation` recorded an `AssetDepreciationEntry` and incremented `FixedAsset.accumulated_depreciation` but **never wrote a voucher**, so depreciation never hit the P&L and profit was overstated. The schema proved the intent: `AssetDepreciationEntry.voucher_id` was declared and never set. New **Non-Current Assets** account group + `Fixed Assets` (1050), `Accumulated Depreciation` (1055, contra-asset), `Depreciation Expense` (5030); new `depreciation` event type (Prisma enum + migration `20260718100000_add_depreciation_event_type`, `PostingEventType` union, `VOUCHER_TYPE_BY_EVENT`=JOURNAL); rule `depreciation/none` → Dr Depreciation Expense / Cr Accumulated Depreciation, in **both** `bootstrap-accounting.ts` and `.js` (parity verified via node/tsx load); `POSTING_CONTRACT` entry. `runDepreciation` **rewrapped in a single `$transaction`** so a posting failure rolls back the entry + the accumulated increment together (the old non-transactional version could leave them out of sync); posts per entry with `sourceId = entry.id` for idempotency, dates the voucher to the **last day of the depreciation month** (`Date.UTC(year, month, 0)`) so it lands in-period and the fiscal-period guard (free via `autoPostFromRules`' date arg) blocks running into a locked month; writes `voucher_id` back to the entry. 5 unit tests (mock `posting.utils`) + **end-to-end verified against the real DB**: created a 120k/60mo asset, ran the posting, confirmed a balanced JV (Dr 5030 2000 / Cr 1055 2000) dated 2026-03-31 with the entry linked. `sync:accounting` applies the 3 accounts + rule to existing tenants (verified: reported all local tenants behind, then added them). Backend suite 102 suites / 1321 tests green; demo-data integration 9/9; `tsc` only the 3 known pre-existing errors. **Follow-up:** per-asset `FixedAsset.asset_account_id`/`depreciation_account_id` overrides (captured at creation, still read by nothing) — same shape as the `PaymentMethod.account_id` follow-up; the rule-based default ships now — done 2026-07-18
- [x] **Party dimension — subsidiary ledgers for customers/suppliers/employees** (Phase 3 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — turns the single Accounts Receivable / Purchase Payable control accounts into per-party ledgers without an `Account` row per party (which `autoPostFromRules` couldn't resolve and would bloat the chart to thousands of rows). New `PartyType` enum + `Account.party_type` (marks a control account) + `VoucherDetail.party_type`/`party_id` (polymorphic, **not** an FK, indexed) mirroring the `cost_center_id` dimension; migration `20260717170000_add_party_dimension`. Bootstrap marks AR=`CUSTOMER`, Purchase Payable=`SUPPLIER` via the upsert **update** clause (both `.ts` and `.js`), so `sync:accounting` applies it to existing tenants on next deploy — verified by stripping `party_type` to NULL and watching sync restore it. `autoPostFromRules` gains optional `partyType`/`partyId`, stamped **only** on the leg whose account's `party_type` matches (never the cash/revenue side; no-op if neither matches or types differ). Wired into sales (credit), customer payments, purchases, purchase orders, sales/purchase returns, supplier payments, and the demo generator. **Retires the suppliers party debt** — `recordCreditPayment` now passes the supplier party. Party ledger = `voucher_details WHERE party_id = X`; historical backfill + UI repoint are Phase 8. **Two pre-existing generator bugs surfaced (not caused) and fixed:** (1) `maybePurchaseReturn` recorded the full `-lineTotal` in the supplier credit ledger while flooring `due_balance` at 0, diverging on an over-return (intermittent ~1-in-3 reconciliation failure) — now clamps both to `min(lineTotal, due)` matching `purchase-returns.service`; (2) `simulate.ts` stamped today's sales at a random 10–20h, landing some in the future (time-of-day flake in the date-window invariant) — now clamped to `now`. **Corrected my own first attempt:** the initial "Σ per-party == control balance" invariant was tautological, and a GL-vs-`due_balance` check is unsound (GL posts the full return, the credit tracker floors at zero — they legitimately diverge). Shipped invariants: every control-account line has a `party_id` (completeness), and every `party_id` resolves to a real party of the matching type (cross-contamination — the failure the unit tests can't see across modules). 5 unit tests (mutation-checked: removing the party_type match fails 3) + 2 integration invariants. Backend suite 101 suites / 1315 tests green; demo-data integration 9/9 stable across repeated runs; `tsc` only the 3 known pre-existing errors — done 2026-07-17
- [x] **Read paths report the real posting status** (Phase 7b of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — the sales list showed **"Skipped"** for postings that had actually **failed**. Eight read sites across sales/purchases/sales-returns/purchase-returns inferred status from voucher existence (`posting_status: voucher ? 'posted' : 'skipped'`), collapsing four states into two: a `failed` posting (misconfigured rule that threw) rendered identically to a deliberate no-op, so a real accounting failure looked routine and was invisible in the UI; `pending` likewise. New `apps/backend/src/accounting/posting-status.util.ts` (`loadPostingSummaries`/`loadPostingSummary`/`NO_POSTING_EVENT`) reads `PostingEvent` — the actual record of what the engine decided — and applies to all 8 sites as one shared helper rather than the same fix pasted eight times. Hits the existing `@@index([tenant_id, source_module, source_type, source_id])`, one query per list not per row. Also surfaces **`posting_error`**, so "why was this skipped" is answerable (`POSTING_RULE_NOT_CONFIGURED` vs `AUTO_POSTING_AMOUNT_INVALID`) instead of a mystery. Sources with no `PostingEvent` row (pre-auto-posting rows; rows whose event `voidAutoPostedVoucher` removed) still report `skipped` — indistinguishable after the fact, and what they have always reported, so the fix does not silently reclassify history. **No frontend change needed**: `PostingBadge` already maps all four statuses (amber Pending, red Failed) and was simply never sent them. 10 new tests, mutation-checked (reverting the util to the old voucher-existence logic fails 3). Backend suite 100 suites / 1310 tests green — done 2026-07-17
- [x] **Supplier payments post a voucher — Purchase Payable finally clears** (Phase 4/suppliers of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — purchases credited Purchase Payable on every tenant but `suppliers.service.recordCreditPayment` never called `autoPostFromRules`, so nothing ever debited it: the payable grew forever and the balance sheet overstated liabilities. New `supplier_payment` event type (Prisma enum + migration `20260717160000_add_supplier_payment_event_type`, `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS`, following `20260616000000_add_loan_posting`), `PostingEventType` union, `VOUCHER_TYPE_BY_EVENT` (CASH_PAYMENT default, CASH_RECEIVE for the `receive` direction), 2 rules in `DEFAULT_POSTING_RULES` (**both `bootstrap-accounting.ts` AND the hand-maintained `.js`** — that drift has shipped 5×; the contract spec imports via `index.js` so it validates the `.js` side), 2 `POSTING_CONTRACT` entries, and the call wired into `recordCreditPayment` with the result surfaced as `posting_status`/`voucher_id`/`voucher_number`. **Correction to the spec:** keyed on `payment_direction` (pay/receive), NOT `payment_mode` — `SupplierCreditTransaction` has no `payment_method` column, so there is no mode to read; mirrors `customer_payment`, which hardcodes Cash in Hand for the same reason. `pay`/`receive` mirror `dueDelta` so the voucher and `due_balance` cannot diverge. Rules live in `DEFAULT_POSTING_RULES` rather than a lazy `ensure*` helper because the bootstrap creates Purchase Payable + Cash in Hand unconditionally. 6 new tests in `suppliers.service.spec.ts` asserting the **arguments and the returned voucher**, not merely that a call happened (the weakness logged against `customers.service.spec.ts`); mutation-checked by inverting the direction mapping (2 tests fail, pass on restore). Also proved Phase 0's value end-to-end: adding these rules made `sync:accounting --dry-run` report all 3 existing tenants as behind — without it they would have silently skipped every supplier-payment posting, exactly the original bug. Backend suite 99 suites / 1300 tests green; `tsc` only the 3 known pre-existing errors. **Does not yet pass party context** (landed ahead of Phase 3) — tracked above — done 2026-07-17
- [x] **Accounting defaults sync — `npm run sync:accounting`** (Phase 0 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-voucher-coverage-and-party-ledgers-design.md`) — the fix for the "some sales Skipped, some Posted" report. Root cause: `bootstrapDefaultAccountingForTenant` runs **only** at tenant creation and nothing ever re-runs it, so when `9ffb067` (2026-07-16) added the bkash/nagad/credit rules *and* fixed `classifyPaymentMode` to stop collapsing wallets into `bank`, every pre-existing tenant was left with a bKash sale that classifies as `bkash`, matches no rule, and posts nothing — while cash/card sales post fine. New `prisma/sync-accounting.ts` (`--dry-run`, `--tenant=<uuid>`; additive-only + idempotent) and `prisma/sync-accounting.utils.ts` (testable core, split the way `repair-fabricated-vouchers.utils.ts` is), plus `apps/backend/src/accounting/sync-accounting.spec.ts` (9 tests). **Two corrections to the spec's plan, found while building:** (1) it hooks the **backend Dockerfile CMD**, not `scripts/deploy.sh` — there is no migration step there; this project uses `prisma db push` from the container CMD, so sync joins that chain (`db push && db:seed && sync:accounting && node main.js`), inside the `&&` deliberately because a stale rule set must fail loudly, and `tsx` is present in prod (`npm ci --include=optional`, no `--omit=dev`); (2) re-running the bootstrap is **not sufficient** — it calls `ensureLoanPostingSetup`/`ensureInterBranchAccounts` but **not** `ensureCustomerPaymentPostingSetup`, which is otherwise only provisioned lazily on a tenant's first customer payment, and which returns *silently* without an `Accounts Receivable` account — so `applySync` calls it explicitly and second, both pinned by tests. **Finding: all 3 local tenants had no `customer_payment` rules at all** — an independent gap, unrelated to the bkash/nagad regression, invisible until sync enumerated it. Dry run performs a real sync in a transaction and rolls it back rather than name-diffing `DEFAULT_POSTING_RULES` (which under-reported the ensure\*-provisioned rules and promised "nothing to do" before a live run that wrote); deltas sorted so dry/live outputs diff cleanly. Also added the missing `ensureCustomerPaymentPostingSetup`/`ensureLoanPostingSetup` entries to the `database-exports.spec.ts` drift guard — both are runtime-imported from `@erp71/database` by `customers.service.ts`. Verified against a real DB by simulating a pre-2026-07-16 tenant: dry run named exactly the 2 accounts + 6 rules missing and touched only the drifted tenant, DB confirmed unchanged after it, live output matched dry byte-for-byte, second run a clean no-op, exit codes 0/1 correct for the `&&` chain. Backend suite 99/1294 green; `tsc` only the 3 known pre-existing errors. **Not yet run against production** — tracked as an open item above — done 2026-07-17

New routes `POST /admin/tenants/:id/demo-data` (202) + `GET /admin/tenants/:id/demo-data/status` on the `PlatformAdminGuard`-protected controller, via `AdminTenantsService.loadDemoData` (audit-logged `tenant.demo_data.load`) / `getDemoDataStatus`.
