- [x] **Help Center audited against the whole product and rewritten across all three locales** — asked as "Are Help pages updated for all the latest changes?" The Help Center (`apps/frontend/src/app/(app)/help/page.tsx` + `lib/localization/messages/{en,ms,bn}/help.ts`) was a 7-section FAQ (Getting Started, POS, Inventory, Accounting, Billing, Storefront, Security) that had drifted well behind the app's 21 `(app)` modules and carried several outright-wrong claims. Six read-only research agents verified module behaviour before any copy was written, which surfaced the inaccuracies: POS receipts were described as printing BIN + VAT breakdown (tax is hardcoded to 0, no BIN/VAT on the receipt); POS split-payment listed "Cash, Card, bKash, Nagad" (the till only offers Cash/bKash/Card — Nagad/bank are accounting-only); POS discount was described as a free-form percent/amount box (it's Discount Codes + loyalty redemption only); product CSV import said matching SKUs are "updated" (they're **skipped**); billing "yearly saves 20%" (actually 10× monthly ≈ **16.7%**, two months free); expiry "locked to read-only mode" (actually PAST_DUE → downgraded to **Free** after a ~7-day grace, data kept); and 2FA / data-export / deletion were pointed at "Settings → Account" (they live on **Profile → 2FA** and **Profile → Data & Privacy**). The storefront "inventory deduction is on the roadmap" FAQ was **verified still accurate** (`placeOrder` validates but never decrements stock; `updateOrderStatus` only flips the enum) and deliberately kept. **Rewrite:** expanded to **13 sections** — added Sales/Returns & Customers, Purchases & Suppliers, CRM & Leads, Manufacturing, HR & Payroll, and AI Assistant — covering the recently-shipped work the question was really about (returns with the now-optional original document — noting the UI still requires looking up the sale by serial, so the parentless path is API/importer-only; CRM revamp — leads/stages/convert, tenant-managed Sources & Categories, Follow-ups incl. auto birthday/reorder triggers, the read-only cross-lead Conversations list; the read-only AI assistant panel with its capability/anomaly-detection tools and plan-allowance AI credits vs prepaid SMS credits; server-side sales paging; Manufacturing being inventory-only with no GL posting; HR salary payments posting Dr Salary Payable/Cr mode with no payslips yet). `page.tsx`'s `sections` array widened to all 13 keys. **Localization:** `en` rewritten by hand from the verified facts; `bn` and `ms` fully re-translated to match (the old `bn` file had Bengali titles but **English FAQ bodies** — now proper Bengali throughout; `ms` was already full Malay and stays so), preserving every structural key, all 13 icons, identical per-section faq counts `[4,4,4,4,4,4,4,3,3,3,4,3,4]`, and verbatim tokens (route paths, plan codes, bKash/Nagad/Tally/QuickBooks/SSL Wireless, POS/SMS/AI/QR/CSV/BIN/VAT/2FA/JSON/BOM/PO/CRM/HR/SKU, CSV column names, numbers). The inline contextual help (`lib/help/contextual-help.ts`: COA, Posting Rules, Stock Takes) was reviewed and left unchanged — still accurate. **Verified:** `catalog.test.ts` locale-parity green (all three locales share the section structure), `help/page.test.tsx` 16 green (updated the assertions whose FAQ copy changed), `HelpTooltip`/`ContextualHelpPanel` green — 26 tests total; frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean for all help files (the lone remaining error is the pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` missing-types issue in `contact/page.test.tsx`, unrelated). **Not done: not opened in a browser this session.** Known product gaps the audit surfaced but did not fix (each already tracked or out of scope): no persisted product barcode field (labels derive from SKU), no customer-edit UI (limits the birthday follow-up trigger to newly-created customers), and the HR monthly-accrual endpoint has no frontend entry point — done 2026-07-30

- [x] **Audit logging was only covering a fraction of tenant activity — closed both gaps** — the question "is audit logging all events for a tenant admin?" turned out to be no, for two independent reasons. **(1) Coverage.** Only five modules ever called `AuditService.log` (team, accounting vouchers, SMS credits, billing, auth); none of the ~75 business modules did, so there was no record of who voided an invoice, changed a price, adjusted stock, or rotated an API key. Fixed with a global `AuditInterceptor` (`apps/backend/src/audit/audit.interceptor.ts`, registered as an `APP_INTERCEPTOR` in `app.module.ts`) that records every successful tenant-scoped `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`, deriving `entity` + a dotted `action` from the route (`POST /sales/:id/payments` → `sales.payments.create`) via the pure helpers in `audit-route.util.ts`. Coverage is now the default: a new module is audited the moment its routes exist. Two ordering facts it depends on, both pinned by tests — it must be registered *after* `TransformInterceptor` so it sits inside the response envelope and sees the raw controller result, and it must read `request.tenantId` inside the `tap` rather than before `next.handle()`, because `TenantInterceptor` is controller-scoped and therefore runs *inside* the global one. Bodies are redacted by key (`password`, `token`, `apiKey`, … matched case- and separator-insensitively) and oversized bodies collapse to a `_truncated` marker so a bulk import can't bloat the table. Modules that already write richer rows opt out with a new `@NoAudit()` decorator (class-level on `TeamController`/`AuditController`, method-level on the four accounting voucher/posting-rule handlers, billing `confirm`/`refund`, and SMS-credit `confirm`) — otherwise the same action lands twice. Audit failures are swallowed at two levels so a logging problem can never turn a successful business write into a 500. **(2) Rows written but invisible.** `GET /audit-logs` filters strictly on `tenant_id`, and `AuditService.log` silently writes `null` when the caller omits it — so every account-level event was unreachable by the tenant admin who needed it most. Added `AuditService.logForUserTenants()`, which fans a row out across the user's live tenant memberships (a user in two tenants gets two rows; each tenant sees only its own; a membership-less user still gets one unscoped row for the platform view). Applied to `USER_LOGIN`, `LOGIN_FAILED`, `USER_LOGOUT`, `USER_SIGNUP`, `PASSWORD_CHANGED`, and all three password-reset events. **Three related gaps found while in there:** 2FA sign-ins produced *no* `USER_LOGIN` row at all (`login()` returns early for 2FA users, so `completeTwoFactorLogin` was the only place it could be recorded, and it recorded nothing); no auth event captured IP or user agent despite the table having the columns and the UI rendering `ip_address`, now threaded through via `extractRequestMeta(req)` from the controller; and platform-admin actions *against* a tenant — `tenant.impersonate`, `tenant.suspend`, `tenant.delete`, addon grant/revoke, payment/refund recording, credit sales — put the tenant id in `entity_id` but left `tenant_id` null, so a shop owner could not see that they had been impersonated or suspended. All now scoped to the tenant; the genuinely platform-scoped `user.platform.*` and `user.promote`/`demote` rows deliberately stay unscoped. `STOREFRONT_CUSTOMER_LOGOUT` likewise now resolves the shopper's `Customer` rows so it lands in the same view as the matching login. **(3) Frontend 403 handling.** `/settings/audit-logs` detected permission denial with `error.message.includes('OWNER or MANAGER')`, but the backend throws `'You do not have permission to view audit logs'` — the string never matched, so a denied user got a silently empty table instead of the forbidden panel. Rather than re-matching new copy, `api.ts` now throws a typed `ApiError` carrying the HTTP status and the page branches on `error.status === 403`, which can't rot when wording changes. **Verified:** backend `npx jest src/` — 168 suites / 2692 tests green, including 43 new tests across `audit-route.util.spec.ts` (route derivation, redaction, payload capping, request meta), `audit.interceptor.spec.ts` (opt-out, missing tenant, handler-throws, late tenant resolution, failure isolation) and `audit.service.spec.ts` (per-tenant fan-out); frontend `npx jest` — 205 suites / 2362 tests green, including a new `audit-logs/page.test.tsx` pinning the 403-vs-500 branch. `tsc --noEmit` on both apps shows only the four pre-existing backend spec errors and the pre-existing `@testing-library/user-event` frontend one — confirmed identical on a stashed tree. The four `test/*.integration.spec.ts` suites fail for want of `DATABASE_URL`, also confirmed pre-existing. **Not done: not exercised against a running server or in a browser.** Retention was checked and needs no change — `notifications.service.ts` already prunes `audit_log` at 90 days, matching the UI copy, though the interceptor will grow that table considerably faster than before and the cutoff may be worth revisiting — done 2026-08-06
- [x] Blog feature — implementation plan written to `docs/blog-feature-plan.md`. Scoped deliberately to a **platform-authored** blog (staff write; anonymous visitors and signed-in tenant users read) and explains why the tenant-authored-storefront-blog reading is a separate feature rather than a variant — it is tenant-scoped, needs a `MANAGE_BLOG` store permission and a moderation story, and shares no schema. Four phases (content spine → public surface → in-app "What's new" → editorial polish), with the decisions argued rather than asserted: all copy in `BlogPostTranslation` including `en`, a `BlogPostSlug` history table so renames 301 instead of 404, markdown-plus-preview rather than a WYSIWYG, and a per-user `blog_last_seen_at` for the unread dot instead of fanning `Notification` rows out to every user of every tenant. Planning surfaced three pre-existing gaps, each filed above as its own item: no `sitemap.ts`, no server-rendered public pages, and `AuditInterceptor` silently skipping every platform-admin mutation — done 2026-08-07
- [x] **Blog — platform blog (phases 1 & 2)** — `BlogPost`/`BlogPostTranslation`/`BlogCategory`/`BlogPostSlug` with no `tenant_id`; `BlogModule` split the way short-links is (unguarded public controller, `PlatformAdminGuard` admin controller, pure helpers for slug/reading-time/status). All copy including English lives in the translation table, so there is one answer to "what is this post's title in locale X" — verified live: a `bn` request returns the Bangla title, an `ms` request falls back to English. Every read goes through one `visibleWhere()`; a draft was confirmed invisible on the public list before publish and visible after. Renaming a slug returns `{redirect_to}` instead of 404ing, verified end to end. `published_at` is set once and never moved by a later edit. Public surface at `/blog`, `/blog/[slug]`, `/blog/category/[slug]`, `rss.xml` — real server components with `generateMetadata`, JSON-LD and a permanent redirect on rename. `ArticleMarkdown` is a sibling of `Markdown.tsx`, not a reuse: it allows `img` and a real heading hierarchy, which the 380px chat renderer deliberately does not, while keeping `skipHtml` and no `rehype-raw` — done 2026-08-08
- [x] **Blog — storefront blog (tenant-authored)** — separate tenant-scoped model set and service; `VIEW_BLOG`/`MANAGE_BLOG`/`PUBLISH_BLOG` split writing from speaking in the shop's public voice. Public surface at `/store/[slug]/blog`. Two switches gate it — the storefront and the blog — and both were verified: a published post stayed 404 while `TenantBlogSettings.enabled` was false, and another shop's slug does not resolve under this shop. Managed from `/settings/blog` — done 2026-08-08
- [x] **In-app "What's new"** — `/whats-new` reading the `IN_APP` audience, with the unread dot driven by a per-user `blog_last_seen_at` rather than a `Notification` fan-out, which would have been one row per user of every tenant per post — done 2026-08-08
- [x] **Platform-admin mutations are now audited** — `AuditInterceptor` records platform-scoped actions with a null tenant, admitted by the guard-set `isPlatformAdmin` flag rather than the mere absence of a tenant (storefront customers are tenant-less too). Verified live: `blog.posts.create`, `blog.posts.publish.create` and `blog.posts.update` all land with `tenant=null, _scope=platform`. Retrofits short-links, tenant admin, platform settings and plan edits at the same time; `GET /admin/audit-logs` reads them back, since `GET /audit-logs` filters strictly on `tenant_id` and would never show them. **Found while fixing it:** stripping the `admin` prefix so those rows keep a usable entity exposed a latent trap — `OPAQUE_ID_RE` matches any `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}`, and `platform-settings` is 17 chars of it, so as an entity segment it read as an opaque id and `resolveAuditTarget` bails on those. `PUT /admin/platform-settings/email` would have gone unrecorded rather than mislabelled. An all-lowercase hyphenated word is now never an id — done 2026-08-08
- [x] **`sitemap.ts` and server-rendered marketing metadata** — added `apps/frontend/src/app/sitemap.ts` (marketing, legal, blog index, categories and posts; `/q/` and `/s/` excluded, pinned by a test that reads the disallow list out of `robots.ts` rather than restating it), and `robots.ts` now points at it. **The "no public page is server-rendered" item was overstated** — `/q/[token]` and `/store/[slug]/p/[productId]` already were, and `publicApiBase()` exists for them. The real gap was that all seven marketing pages are `'use client'`, so none could export `metadata` and every one shipped the layout's default title with no description. Each is now a server `page.tsx` exporting metadata around an unchanged client component — done 2026-08-08
- [ ] `@testing-library/user-event` is imported by `apps/frontend/src/app/contact/page.test.tsx` but is not a declared dependency anywhere — `tsc --noEmit` reports it as an unresolved module. Pre-existing (present at `HEAD` before the blog work) and the suite currently passes, so it is resolving transitively through another package's tree, which is exactly the kind of thing that breaks on an unrelated dependency bump. Add it to `apps/frontend` devDependencies. Surfaced 2026-08-08 while typechecking the blog changes.
- [ ] Wire on-publish revalidation for the blog — the public pages are ISR at 300s and the plan assumed publishing would call a revalidation route so a new post appears at once. It does not yet: publishing takes up to five minutes to show. A `revalidateTag('blog')` route secured by a shared secret, called from `BlogService.publish()`, is the remaining piece. Surfaced 2026-08-08.
- [ ] Preview tokens for unpublished posts — phase 4 of `docs/blog-feature-plan.md`. Staff can only see a draft through the admin editor's preview pane today; sending a draft to someone for review needs the short-lived signed token the plan describes (`?preview=<token>`, always `noindex`). Surfaced 2026-08-08.

- [x] **Admin → Social Media, with one-click publishing to Facebook (and any other connected page) via Buffer** — a platform-owned composer for ERP71's own marketing posts, modelled on the platform blog rather than on anything tenant-scoped: `SocialMediaPost` has no `tenant_id`, and the admin controller is guarded by `PlatformAdminGuard` alone, because these posts belong to no shop and there is no tenant permission that could sensibly gate them. **Why Buffer and not the Facebook Graph API:** page access tokens expire and need re-consent, and Meta's app review is a per-app hurdle worth clearing to post as a *customer's* page but not to post as our own. Buffer already holds the page connection, so the credential stored is one API key rather than a token per page per network — and the same key reaches LinkedIn, Instagram and X without a second integration. Implemented against Buffer's **GraphQL** API (`https://api.buffer.com`, `Authorization: Bearer <key>`): a `channels(input: { organizationId })` query to resolve a page name into the channel id `createPost` needs, and the `createPost` mutation for the handover. **The contract detail that would have made this silently broken:** Buffer models rejections as *union members* on `PostActionPayload`, not as GraphQL errors — a mutation selecting only `... on PostActionSuccess` gets HTTP 200 with an empty `createPost` and looks like it worked. Every mutation therefore also selects `... on MutationError { message }`, and that path has its own test. **`PUBLISHED` here means "handed to Buffer", not "live on Facebook"** — Buffer owns the queue after that and there is no webhook back, so the status vocabulary refuses to claim more than the handover, and a post in that state is read-only (Duplicate, not Edit) because editing the copy here would not change what is sitting in Buffer's queue. Pushes are recorded one row per channel (`SocialMediaPostPush`) rather than as a status column on the post: pushing to three pages can half-fail, and one field cannot say which page rejected it. Channels are pushed in sequence, not in parallel — a partial failure has to be attributable, and Buffer rate-limits per key. A partial success counts as `PUBLISHED` (something did go out) with the per-channel errors kept visible in the push history; only an all-channel failure is `FAILED`, and that is re-pushable after an edit. Deletes are soft, because the push history is the record of what the platform said in public while the post itself stays live on Facebook. Credentials live in a new `social_buffer` platform-settings group (key encrypted at rest via the existing `is_secret` path) with a settings page at `/admin/platform-settings/buffer` whose "Test connection" runs the cheapest authenticated read there is — `channels` — so a wrong key fails loudly and a right key with nothing connected is distinguishable from both. The list page's setup banner only appears on a definite `configured: false`; a *failed* probe deliberately shows nothing, so one flaky request cannot put a setup prompt in front of a working install. Nav entry `admin.social-media` added to the registry and to both default layouts — existing saved layouts need `npx tsx prisma/sync-nav-layout.ts --nodes=admin.social-media` (see the script's header for why this is not done on boot). i18n complete in en/bn/ms. **Verified:** backend `npx jest src/social-media src/platform-settings` 4 suites / 44 tests green (Buffer client against a stubbed `fetch`, incl. the union-error case, the `dueAt`-only-for-`customScheduled` rule and network-failure wording; the service's partial-failure, link-append, default-channel and soft-delete behaviour; the status lifecycle); frontend `npx jest social-media` 7 green (list, the two banner branches, no-Edit-on-sent, default channel preselected, and partial-push reported as an error rather than a success); nav suites green on both sides; locale parity 3/3; both typechecks at the known pre-existing baseline. **Not done: not exercised against a real Buffer account** — every Buffer call in the tests is stubbed, so the first live push is the first real check of the mutation's field names — done 2026-08-08
- [ ] Exercise the Buffer integration against a real account once an API key exists — the GraphQL request shapes (`createPost` input fields, the `assets: [{ image: { url } }]` form, the `mode` enum values `addToQueue`/`now`/`next`/`customScheduled`) are built from Buffer's published docs and covered only by stubbed-`fetch` tests. A single test push to a sandbox channel would confirm them; a field-name mismatch surfaces today as a `MutationError` in the push history, which is visible but only after someone tries. Surfaced 2026-08-08.
- [ ] Scheduled social posts do not publish themselves — `SocialMediaPost.scheduled_for` is honoured only when someone pushes with "At an exact time", which forwards the date to Buffer; a post left in `SCHEDULED` with nobody pressing Push simply sits there. Either add a cron that pushes due posts to the default channel (the blog's publish cron is the precedent) or drop the local date field and let Buffer's own queue be the only scheduler. Surfaced 2026-08-08.
- [ ] **Nothing reads `ShortLinkClick` yet** — the table is written on every click but no endpoint or screen surfaces it, so the data is currently write-only. The shortener list still shows a bare click count. Smallest useful next step is a per-link detail view: clicks over time, top referrer hosts, channel split, device split, and the bot/human toggle (`is_bot` is stored precisely so a report can exclude preview fetches without losing the signal that a link was shared). Scope it as tenant-scoped for `/settings/url-shortener` and platform-scoped for `/admin/url-shortener`, matching how `list()` already splits. Surfaced 2026-08-13.
- [ ] **`ShortLinkClick` has no retention policy** — unlike `ReferralClick`, it stores `ip_address`, which makes these rows personal data, and nothing prunes them. Two decisions to make deliberately: how long raw clicks are kept before being rolled up into daily aggregates, and whether the IP should be truncated or hashed after the geo lookup that justifies keeping it. Neither is urgent at current volume, but both get harder once there is a year of rows and a report reading them. Surfaced 2026-08-13.
- [ ] **`country`/`city` on `ShortLinkClick` will stay null until something fills them** — the redirect handler reads `cf-ipcountry`, `x-vercel-ip-country`, `cloudfront-viewer-country` and friends opportunistically, but Caddy sets none of them today, so geo is captured only if a CDN is ever put in front. The columns exist so a geo-IP backfill has somewhere to write; doing that lookup at redirect time would need a local database (MaxMind GeoLite2 or similar) rather than a network call, since it sits in the path of a redirect someone is waiting on. Surfaced 2026-08-13.
- [ ] The URL shortener design docs still describe the removed interstitial — `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-url-shortener-design.md:171` and `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-04-url-shortener.md` both list `s/[code]/leaving/page.tsx` as a file to create. They are historical records of the original build rather than living specs, so they were left alone, but anyone reading them as current will look for a file that no longer exists. Surfaced 2026-08-13.
- [ ] Image URLs for social posts have to be pasted by hand — Buffer fetches assets from a public URL and has no upload API, so the composer takes a URL string. `AssetsService.uploadBuffer()` already returns a public Cloudinary URL with a `storage_key`, so an upload control here is small and would remove the one step in the flow that requires leaving the page. Surfaced 2026-08-08.
